*Logical*, I thought. If you aren’t prepared for something, it just won’t happen. There’s an awful lot of that sort of thinking going on. As I remember my Old Terran history, it was even indulged in by governments, at one time. None of them exists now.

“You know what all crawls into the Bottom Level,” I reminded him. “If you don’t, ask Mr. Murell, here. One sent him to the hospital.”

Dad nodded; I had a point there. The abandoned sections of Bottom Level are full of tread-snails and other assorted little nasties, and the heat of the fire would stir them all up and start them moving around. Even aside from the possibility that, having started the fire, Steve Ravick’s gang would try to take steps to keep it from being put out too soon, a gun was going to be a comforting companion, down there.

===Chinese===
*真有逻辑*，我心想。只要对一件事毫无准备，它就压根不会发生。这种思路如今遍地都是。据我所记得的古特拉历史，当年连各国政府都好这一口。如今那些政府可是一个都不剩了。

「您知道底层都爬满些什么玩意儿，」我提醒他。「您要是不知道，就问问这儿的穆雷尔（Murell）先生。其中一样就送他进过医院。」

爸爸点点头；这一点上我占理。底层的废弃地段满是履带蜗牛（tread-snail）和形形色色招人嫌的小东西，大火的热气会把它们全激起来，闹得四处乱窜。且不说还有一层可能——火既是史蒂夫·拉维克那帮人放的，他们还会设法阻挠，别让火太早扑灭——单说底下那种地方，身边有杆枪做伴总归叫人踏实。

===Original===
“Well, stay out of any fighting. Your job’s to get the news, not play hero in gun fights. I’m no hero; that’s why I’m sixty years old. I never knew many heroes that got that old.”

It was my turn to nod. On that, Dad had a point. I said something about getting the news, not making it, and checked the chamber and magazine of the Sterberg, and then slung my radio and picked up the audiovisual outfit.

===Chinese===
「总之，任何打斗都别掺和。你的差事是采访新闻，不是在枪战里逞英雄。我就不是英雄；所以才能活到六十岁。我认识的英雄里，能活到这岁数的可没几个。」

这回轮到我点头了。在这一点上，爸爸说得在理。我说了句什么只采访新闻、不制造新闻的话，检查了斯特贝格的枪膛和弹匣，随后背上无线电，拎起那套视听设备。

===Original===
Tom and Joe Kivelson had left already, to round up the scattered Javelin crew for fire fighting. The attack on the Municipal Building and on Hunters’ Hall had been postponed, but it wasn’t going to be abandoned. Oscar and Professor Hartzenbosch and Dad and a couple of others were planning some sort of an observation force of a few men for each place, until the fire had been gotten out or under control. Glenn Murell decided he’d go out with me, at least as far as the fire, so we went down to the vehicle port and got the jeep out. Main City Level Broadway was almost deserted; everybody had gone down below where the excitement was. We started down the nearest vehicle shaft and immediately got into a jam, above a lot of stuff that was going into the shaft from the First Level Down, mostly manipulators and that sort of thing. There were no police around, natch, and a lot of volunteers were trying to direct traffic and getting in each other’s way. I got some views with the jeep camera, just to remind any of the public who needed reminding what our city administration wasn’t doing in an emergency. A couple of pieces of apparatus, a chemical tank and a pumper marked **salamander volunteer fire company no. 3** came along, veered out of the jam, and continued uptown.

===Chinese===
汤姆（Tom）和乔·基维尔森（Joe Kivelson）早已动身，去把散在各处的标枪号（*Javelin*）船员召集来救火。进攻市政大楼和猎手会堂的计划是推迟了，但绝不打算作罢。奥斯卡、哈岑博施教授（Professor Hartzenbosch）、爸爸，还有另外几位，正筹划组织某种监视队，每处派上几个人守着，直到大火扑灭或得到控制。格伦·穆雷尔（Glenn Murell）决定跟我一道出去，至少到火场那儿，于是我们下到车辆港，把吉普车开了出来。主城层的百老汇大街几乎空无一人；人都下到底下凑热闹去了。我们驶进最近的车辆竖井，立刻堵了个正着——底下大批东西正从下第一层（First Level Down）往竖井里涌，多是些操纵机（manipulator）之类的家伙。周围照例连个警察的影子也没有，一群志愿者忙着指挥交通，互相碍手碍脚。我用吉普车上的摄影机拍了几组画面，好提醒公众里那些需要提醒的人：紧急关头我们的市政当局都干了些什么——或者不如说，没干什么。一两件装备——一辆化学药剂罐车和一台水泵车，车身漆着**蝾螈志愿消防队第三分队**（salamander volunteer fire company no. 3）的字样——驶了过来，拐出堵塞的车流，继续朝城那头开去。

===Original===
“If they know another way down, maybe we’d better follow them,” Murell suggested.

“They’re not going down. They’re going to the lumber plant, in case the fire spreads upward,” I said. “They wouldn’t be taking that sort of equipment to a wax fire.”

“Why not?”

===Chinese===
「他们要是知道别的下行道，咱们兴许还是跟着他们好，」穆雷尔提议。

「他们不是往下走。他们是去木材厂，以防火势向上蔓延，」我说。「要是去扑蜡火，他们就不会带那种装备了。」

「为什么不会？」

===Original===
I looked at him. “I thought you were in the wax business,” I said.

“I am, but I’m no chemist. I don’t know anything about how wax burns. All I know is what it’s used for, roughly, and who’s in the market for it.”

“Well, you know about those jumbo molecules, don’t you?” I asked. “They have everything but the kitchen sink in them, including enough oxygen to sustain combustion even under water or in a vacuum. Not enough oxygen to make wax explode, like powder, but enough to keep it burning. Chemical extinguishers are all smothering agents, and you just can’t smother a wax fire. And water’s worse than useless.”

===Chinese===
我瞧了他一眼。「我还以为你是干蜡这一行的，」我说。

「我是干这行的，可我不是化学家。蜡怎么燃烧，我一窍不通。我只大致知道它派什么用场，还有市面上谁要买它。」

「那么，那些巨无霸分子你总知道吧？」我问。「里头什么都有，就差一个厨房水槽——连维持燃烧的氧气都自备，哪怕泡在水下、搁在真空里都烧得起来。氧气倒没多到让蜡像火药那样爆炸，但让它烧个不停是绰绰有余。化学灭火器清一色是窒息式灭火剂，可蜡火压根儿闷不灭。水就更帮倒忙了。」

===Original===
He wanted to know why.

“Burning wax is a liquid. The melting point is around 250° Centigrade. Wax ignites at 750°. It has no boiling point, unless that’s the burning point. Throw water on a wax fire and you get a steam explosion, just as you would if you threw it on molten metal, and that throws the fire around and spreads it.”

“If it melts that far below the ignition point, wouldn’t it run away before it caught fire?”

“Normally, it would. That’s why I’m sure this fire was a touch-off. I think somebody planted a thermoconcentrate bomb. A thermoconcentrate flame is around 850° Centigrade; the wax would start melting and burning almost instantaneously. In any case, the fire will be at the bottom of the stacks. If it started there, melted wax would run down from above and keep the fire going, and if it started at the top, burning wax would run down and ignite what’s below.”

===Chinese===
他追问缘由。

「燃烧的蜡是液体。熔点在 250 摄氏度左右。蜡到 750 度着火。它没有沸点——除非就把着火点当沸点。往蜡火上泼水，会引发蒸汽爆炸，就像泼到熔化的金属上一样，火被炸得四下飞溅，越烧越散。」

「既然熔点比着火点低了那么多，它着火之前岂不是早就流跑了？」

「正常情况下是会。所以我敢断定这场火是人为引爆的。我看是有人放了一枚集热剂炸弹（thermoconcentrate bomb）。集热剂火焰约有 850 摄氏度；蜡几乎会在顷刻间熔化并燃烧起来。而且无论如何，火头必定在蜡垛底部。要是火从底下起的，熔化的蜡从上面淌下来，把火一直喂下去；要是从顶上起的，烧着的蜡往下淌，把底下的也点着。」

===Original===
“Well, how in blazes do you put a wax fire out?” he wanted to know.

“You don’t. You just pull away all the wax that hasn’t caught fire yet, and then try to scatter the fire and let it burn itself out. … Here’s our chance!”

===Chinese===
「那么，见鬼，蜡火到底该怎么灭？」他非要问个明白。

「灭不了。只能把还没着火的蜡统统拖开，再设法把火分散开来，让它自己烧完。……瞧，咱们的机会来了！」

===Original===
All this conversation we had been screaming into each other’s ears, in the midst of a pandemonium of yelling, cursing, siren howling and bell clanging; just then I saw a hole in the vertical traffic jam and edged the jeep into it, at the same time remembering that the jeep carried, and I was entitled to use, a fire siren. I added its howls to the general uproar and dropped down one level. Here a string of big manipulators were trying to get in from below, sprouting claw hooks and grapples and pusher arms in all directions. I made my siren imitate a tail-tramped tomcat a couple of times, and got in among them.

===Chinese===
这一路对话，我们都是扯着嗓子朝对方耳朵里吼的，四下里人喊声、咒骂声、警笛声、铃铛声乱成一团；恰在此时，我瞧见那道垂直车流的堵阵里裂开一个空当，便把吉普车蹭了进去，同时想起这辆吉普车装有消防警报器——而且我有权使用。我便让它也嚎进这片大合唱，往下降了一层。这儿一串大型操纵机正从底下往上挤，浑身朝四面八方支棱着爪钩、抓斗和推臂。我让警报器学了两声被踩了尾巴的公猫叫，挤进了它们中间。

===Original===
Bottom Level Broadway was a frightful mess, and I realized that we had come down right between two units of the city power plant, big mass-energy converters. The street was narrower than above, and ran for a thousand yards between ceiling-high walls, and everything was bottlenecked together. I took the jeep up till we were almost scraping the ceiling, and Murell, who had seen how the audiovisual was used, took over with it while I concentrated on inching forward. The noise was even worse down here than it had been above; we didn’t attempt to talk.

===Chinese===
底层的百老汇大街乱得吓人，我这才发觉，我们恰好降落在城市电厂两大机组之间——那是两台硕大的质能转换器。街道比上面的窄，在齐天花板高的两壁之间绵延一千码，所有东西都挤作一团。我把吉普车往上开到几乎蹭着天花板，穆雷尔已看会了那套视听设备的用法，接手操作，我则专心致志地一点一点往前蹭。这底下的噪音比上面还要糟；我们索性不作交谈的打算了。

===Original===
Finally, by impudence and plain foolhardiness, I got the jeep forward a few hundred yards, and found myself looking down on a big derrick with a fifty-foot steel boom tipped with a four-clawed grapple, shielded in front with sheet steel like a gun shield. It was painted with the emblem of the Hunters’ Cooperative, but the three men on it looked like shipyard workers. I didn’t get that, at all. The thing had been built to handle burning wax, and was one of three kept on the Second Level Down under Hunters’ Hall. I wondered if Bish Ware had found a way for a gang to get in at the bottom of Hunters’ Hall. I simply couldn’t see Steve Ravick releasing equipment to fight the fire his goons had started for him in the first place.

===Chinese===
最后，全凭脸皮厚外加浑胆大，我把吉普车往前拱了几百码，发觉自己正俯瞰着一座大型吊车——五十英尺长的钢吊臂，顶端装着四爪抓斗，前面像炮盾似的挡着钢板。车上漆着猎手合作社（Hunters' Cooperative）的徽记，可上面的三条汉子看着像造船厂工人。这我完全闹不明白。这玩意儿本是专为搬运燃烧的蜡造的，一共三台，存放在下第二层猎手会堂底下。我暗自嘀咕，莫非比什·韦尔（Bish Ware）真给哪路人马找到了从猎手会堂底部潜入的门路？我实在没法想象，史蒂夫·拉维克肯放出装备去扑一场火——那火本来就是他的打手们替他放的。

===Original===
I let down a few feet, gave a polite little scream with my siren, and then yelled down to the men on it:

“Where’d that thing come from?”

“Hunters’ Hall; Steve Ravick sent it. The other two are up at the fire already, and if this mess ahead doesn’t get straightened out. …” From there on, his remarks were not suitable for publication in a family journal like the *Times*.

===Chinese===
我降低了几英尺，用警报器客客气气地小叫了一声，然后朝底下吊车上的汉子们喊话：

「这玩意儿是哪儿来的？」

「猎手会堂；史蒂夫·拉维克派来的。另外两台已经上火场了，要是前头这团乱子再理不顺……」再往后的话，就不宜刊在《时报》这种老少咸宜的家庭报纸上了。

===Original===
I looked up ahead, rising to the ceiling again, and saw what was the matter. It was one of the dredgers from the waterfront, really a submarine scoop shovel, that they used to keep the pools and the inner channel from sanding up. I wasn’t surprised it was jammed; I couldn’t see how they’d gotten this far uptown with it. I got a few shots of that, and then unhooked the handphone of my radio. Julio Kubanoff answered.

“You getting everything I’m sending in?” I asked.

“Yes. What’s that two-em-dashed thing up ahead, one of the harbor dredgers?”

===Chinese===
我朝前望去，车流又涨到天花板了，也看出了症结所在。那是港滨开来的一艘挖泥船——其实就是一台水下铲斗挖泥机，平日用来防止水湾和内航道淤沙。它堵在那儿我毫不意外；我是奇怪他们怎么会把它开到城里这么远的地方来。我拍下了几个镜头，然后摘下无线电的手持话筒。接的是胡利奥·库巴诺夫（Julio Kubanoff）。

「我发回去的东西你都收到了？」我问。

「收到了。前头那个见鬼的大玩意儿是什么，港口的挖泥船吗？」

===Original===
“That’s right. Hey, look at this, once.” I turned the audiovisual down on the claw derrick. “The men on it look like Rodriguez & Oughourlian’s people, but they say Steve Ravick sent it. What do you know about it?”

“Hey, Ralph! What’s this Walt’s picked up about Ravick sending equipment to fight the fire?” he yelled.

Dad came over, and nodded. “It wasn’t Ravick, it was Mort Hallstock. He commandeered the Coop equipment and sent it up,” he said. “He called me and wanted to know whom to send for it that Ravick’s gang wouldn’t start shooting at right away. Casmir Oughourlian sent some of his men.”

===Chinese===
「正是。嘿，来瞧瞧这个。」我把视听设备转下来，对准那台爪式吊车。「上面的人看着像罗德里格斯-乌古尔良（Rodriguez & Oughourlian）公司的人，可他们说是史蒂夫·拉维克派来的。你们那边知道什么内情？」

「嘿，拉尔夫（Ralph）！沃尔特这边听说拉维克（Ravick）派装备来救火，是怎么回事？」他嚷道。

爸爸走了过来，点点头。「不是拉维克，是莫特·霍尔斯特克（Mort Hallstock）。他征用了合作社的装备，派了上来，」他说。「他还给我来了电话，想知道派谁去领，拉维克那伙人不会立马朝人家开枪。卡斯米尔·乌古尔良（Casmir Oughourlian）派了几个手下过去。」

===Original===
Up front, something seemed to have given way. The dredger went lurching forward, and everything moved off after it.

“I get it,” I said. “Hallstock’s getting ready to dump Ravick out the airlock. He sees, now, that Ravick’s a dead turkey; he doesn’t want to go into the oven along with him.”

“Walt, can’t you ever give anybody credit with trying to do something decent, once in a while?” Dad asked.

“Sure I can. Decent people. There are a lot of them around, but Mort Hallstock isn’t one of them. There was an Old Terran politician named Al Smith, once. He had a little saying he used in that kind of case: ‘Let’s look at the record.’”

===Chinese===
前方似乎有什么松了道。挖泥船踉跄着往前一冲，后头所有东西都跟着动了起来。

「我明白了，」我说。「霍尔斯特克这是准备把拉维克扔出气闸了。他如今看出来了：拉维克是只死定了的火鸡；他可不想跟着一块儿进烤炉。」

「沃尔特，你就不能偶尔也承认一回，人家是在想法子做件正经事？」爸爸问。

「当然能。正经人。这种人到处都有，可莫特·霍尔斯特克不在其中。古特拉从前有位政客，名叫阿尔·史密斯（Al Smith）。他应对这种场合有句小口头禅：『咱们来看看政绩记录。』」

===Original===
“Well, Mort’s record isn’t very impressive, I’ll give you that,” Dad admitted. “I understand Mort’s up at the fire now. Don’t spit in his eye if you run into him.”

“I won’t,” I promised. “I’m kind of particular where I spit.”

*Things must be looking pretty rough around Municipal Building*, I thought. *Maybe Mort’s afraid the people will start running Fenris again, after this. He might even be afraid there’d be an election.*

===Chinese===
「这个嘛，莫特的记录确实不太拿得出手，这我认账，」爸爸承认道。「听说莫特这会儿在火场上。你要是撞见他，可别当面啐他。」

「不会的，」我应承道。「往哪儿啐这件事，我还是挺讲究的。」

*市政大楼那边的局面想必相当难看了*，我心想。*也许莫特是怕经此一遭，老百姓又要重新自己当家治理芬尼斯（Fenris）。他甚至可能怕会来一场选举。*

===Original===
By this time, I’d gotten the jeep around the dredger—we’d come to the end of the nuclear-power plant buildings—and cut off into open country. That is to say, nothing but pillar-buildings two hundred yards apart and piles of bagged mineral nutrients for the hydroponic farms. We could see a blaze of electric lights ahead where the fire must be, and after a while we began to run into lorries and lifter-skids hauling ammunition away from the area. Then I could see a big mushroom of greasy black smoke spreading out close to the ceiling. The electric lights were brighter ahead, and there was a confused roar of voices and sirens and machines.

And there was a stink.

There are a lot of stinks around Port Sandor, though the ventilation system carries most of them off before they can spread out of their own areas. The plant that reprocesses sewage to get organic nutrients for the hydroponic farms, and the plant that digests hydroponic vegetation to make nutrients for the carniculture vats. The carniculture vats themselves aren’t any flower gardens. And the pulp plant where our synthetic lumber is made. But the worst stink there is on Fenris is a tallow-wax fire. Fortunately, they don’t happen often.

===Chinese===
到这时，我已把吉普车绕过挖泥船——核电厂房也到头了——拐进了开阔地带。所谓开阔地带，也不过是每隔两百码一根的柱状楼，外加一堆堆供给水培农场的袋装矿物养料。前方想必是火场的地方，望得见一片耀眼的电灯，过了一阵，我们开始不断遇上正往外拖运弹药的卡车和升举滑车（lifter-skid）。接着我看见一大团油腻腻的黑烟，像蘑菇一样贴着天花板摊开。前方的电灯更亮了，人声、警笛、机器混成一片乱哄哄的轰鸣。

还有一股恶臭。

桑多尔港四处恶臭不少，好在通风系统大多能在它们窜出各自地盘之前抽走。比如，把污水再生处理、为水培农场提取有机养料的那座工厂；把水培植物消化掉、给肉培培养槽制造养料的那座工厂。肉培培养槽本身也说不上是什么花园。还有制造我们合成木材的那座木浆厂。但全芬尼斯顶顶难闻的臭，还得数脂蜡火灾。所幸这种事不常有。

## Darkness and Cold / 黑暗与严寒

===Original===
The next time I woke, Tom Kivelson was reciting the Mayday, Mayday incantation into the radio, and his father was asleep. The man who had been praying had started again, and nobody seemed to care whether he wasted oxygen or not. It was a Theosophist prayer to the Spirit Guides, and I remembered that Cesário Vieira was a Theosophist. Well, maybe there really were Spirit Guides. If there were, we’d all be finding out before long. I found that I didn’t care one hoot which way, and I set that down to oxygen deficiency.

Then Glenn Murell broke in on the monotone call for help and the prayer.

===Chinese===
再醒来的时候，汤姆·基维尔森正对着无线电一遍遍念诵「梅戴、梅戴」那套咒语，他父亲倒睡着了。先前祷告的那个汉子又祷告起来了，也没人在乎他费不费氧气。那是一篇神智学者（Theosophist）向灵界向导（Spirit Guides）祈求的祷文，我想起塞萨里奥·维埃拉正是个神智学者。好吧，说不定灵界向导真有其灵。若真有，我们大伙儿很快就要见分晓了。我发现自己两样都毫不在乎，我把这归咎于缺氧。

这时，格伦·穆雷尔的声音打破了单调的呼救声与祷告声。

===Original===
“We’re done for if we stay down here another hour,” he said. “Any argument on that?”

There wasn’t any. Joe Kivelson opened his eyes and looked around.

“We haven’t raised anything at all on the radio,” Murell went on. “That means nobody’s within an hour of reaching us. Am I right?”

“I guess that’s about the size of it,” Joe Kivelson conceded.

===Chinese===
「再在这底下待上一个钟头，我们就全完了，」他说。「有谁有异议吗？」

没有人有异议。乔·基维尔森睁开眼睛，环顾四周。

「无线电里什么都没叫通，」穆雷尔接着说。「这就是说，谁也不在我们一小时航程之内。我说得对不对？」

「我看，事情大概就是这么个局面，」乔·基维尔森承认道。

===Original===
“How close to land are we?”

“The radar isn’t getting anything but open water and schools of fish,” Abe Clifford said. “For all I know, we could be inside Sancerre Bay now.”

“Well, then, why don’t we surface?” Murell continued. “It’s a thousand to one against us, but if we stay here our chances are precisely one hundred percent negative.”

“What do you think?” Joe asked generally. “I think Mr. Murell’s stated it correctly.”

===Chinese===
「我们离陆地有多远？」

「雷达上除了开阔的海面和鱼群，什么都没有，」艾布·克利福德说。「天晓得，我们这会儿说不定已经进了桑塞尔湾（Sancerre Bay）。」

「那么，我们何不浮出水面？」穆雷尔接着说。「上去是千分之一的机会，可待在这儿，我们的胜算恰恰是百分之百的没有。」

「大伙儿怎么看？」乔向众人问道。「要我说，穆雷尔先生讲得没错。」

===Original===
“There is no death,” Cesário said. “Death is only a change, and then more of life. I don’t care what you do.”

“What have we got to lose?” somebody else asked. “We’re broke and gambling on credit now.”

“All right; we surface,” the skipper said. “Everybody grab onto something. We’ll take the Nifflheim of a slamming around as soon as we’re out of the water.”

===Chinese===
「死亡是不存在的，」塞萨里奥说。「死不过是一场转变，转变之后是更多的生。你们爱怎么办就怎么办，我无所谓。」

「我们还有什么可赔的？」另有人问。「我们如今已经输光老本，全靠赊账在赌了。」

「好；浮上去，」船长说。「人人都抓牢点什么。一出水面，我们就得挨上一顿尼弗尔海姆级的猛烈摔打。」

===Original===
We woke up everybody who was sleeping, except the three men who had completely lost consciousness. Those we wrapped up in blankets and tarpaulins, like mummies, and lashed them down. We gathered everything that was loose and made it fast, and checked the fastenings of everything else. Then Abdullah Monnahan pointed the nose of the boat straight up and gave her everything the engines could put out. Just as we were starting upward, I heard Cesário saying:

“If anybody wants to see me in the next reincarnation, I can tell you one thing; I won’t reincarnate again on Fenris!”

===Chinese===
我们把睡着的人全都叫醒，只有三个完全昏死过去的除外。那三个人，我们用毯子和防水油布裹起来，像木乃伊似的，再牢牢捆好。散着的东西都收拢来固定住，其余物件上的系固也逐一查过。然后，阿卜杜拉·莫纳汉（Abdullah Monnahan）把艇首垂直朝上，引擎能出的力气全都使了出来。就在我们开始上冲的当口，我听见塞萨里奥在说：

「下辈子谁要是还想见我，有一件事我可以奉告；我绝不再投生到芬尼斯来了！」

===Original===
The headlights only penetrated fifty or sixty feet ahead of us. I could see slashers and clawbeaks and funnelmouths and gulpers and things like that getting out of our way in a hurry. Then we were out of the water and shooting straight up in the air.

It was the other time all over again, doubled in spades, only this time Abdullah didn’t try to fight it; he just kept the boat rising. Then it went end-over-end, again and again. I think most of us blacked out; I’m sure I did, for a while. Finally, more by good luck than good management, he got us turned around with the wind behind us. That lasted for a while, and then we started keyholing again. I could see the instrument panel from where I’d lashed myself fast; it was going completely bughouse. Once, out the window in front, I could see jagged mountains ahead. I just shut my eyes and waited for the Spirit Guides to come and pick up the pieces.

===Chinese===
前灯只能照进我们前方五六十英尺的水域。我看得见砍杀兽（slashers）、钩喙鱼（clawbeaks）、漏斗嘴鱼和囫吞鱼之类，正慌慌张张地给我们让路。随后我们破水而出，笔直地射向空中。

上一回的那一幕又重演了，还加倍地凶，只是这一回阿卜杜拉没去硬抗；他只管让艇往上升。接着艇一次又一次地翻起跟头。我看我们大多数人都昏了过去；我自己肯定昏了一阵。最后，与其说靠本事，不如说靠运气，他让艇转了过来，风落到了我们身后。这样撑了一阵，接着我们又兜起大圈来。从我把自己捆牢的地方望得见仪表板；上面的指示已经完全乱了套。有一回，透过前面的舷窗，我看见前方群山嶙峋。我索性闭上眼睛，专等灵界向导前来收拾我们的残骸。

===Original===
When they weren’t along, after a few seconds that seemed like half an hour, I opened my eyes again. There were more mountains ahead, and mountains to the right. *This’ll do it*, I thought, and I wondered how long it would take Dad to find out what had happened to us. Cesário had started praying again, and so had Abdullah Monnahan, who had just remembered that he had been brought up a Muslim. I hoped he wasn’t trying to pray in the direction of Mecca, even allowing that he knew which way Mecca was from Fenris generally. That made me laugh, and then I thought, *This is a fine time to be laughing at anything.* Then I realized that things were so bad that anything more that happened was funny.

===Chinese===
那几秒钟像半个钟头一样漫长，灵界向导终归没有来，我又睁开了眼睛。前方是更多的山，右手边也是山。*这下完了*，我想，同时纳闷爸爸要过多久才会得知我们的遭遇。塞萨里奥又祷告起来了，阿卜杜拉·莫纳汉也祷告起来了——他刚刚想起自己从小受的是穆斯林教养。我倒希望他没在朝麦加的方向祷告，就算退一步，承认他大体知道从芬尼斯往麦加是哪一头好了。这让我笑出了声，接着我又想，*都什么时候了，还有心思笑话任何事情。*随即我明白过来：事情已经糟到这个地步，再发生什么都只显得可笑了。

===Original===
I was still laughing when I discovered that the boat had slowed to a crawl and we were backing in between two high cliffs. Evidently Abdullah, who had now stopped praying, had gotten enough control of the boat to keep her into the wind and was keeping enough speed forward to yield to it gradually. That would be all right, I thought, if the force of the wind stayed constant, and as soon as I thought of that, it happened. We got into a relative calm, the boat went forward again, and then was tossed up and spun around. Then I saw a mountain slope directly behind us, out the rear window.

A moment later, I saw rocks and boulders sticking out of it in apparent defiance of gravitation, and then I realized that it was level ground and we were coming down at it backward. That lasted a few seconds, and then we hit stern-on, bounced and hit again. I was conscious up to the third time we hit.

===Chinese===
我还笑着，忽然发觉艇已经减到爬行般的速度，正倒退着往两道高崖之间进去。显然，这会儿已经住口的阿卜杜拉把艇控制得够好了：既让艇顶着风，又保持着足够的前进速度，好缓缓向风让退。我想，只要风力一直不变，这样就成；而这念头刚一冒出来，事情就来了。我们进入了一片相对平静的地带，艇又朝前一蹿，随即被抛起来打了个转。接着，从后窗望出去，我看见正后方是一道山坡。

再过一瞬，我看见岩石和巨石从坡面上突兀而出，全然无视重力；随即我明白过来，那是一片平地，我们正倒着朝它降落。这样持续了几秒钟，然后我们尾部撞了地，弹起来，又撞了一下。到第三次撞击为止，我一直清醒着。

===Original===
The next thing I knew, I was hanging from my lashings from the side of the boat, which had become the top, and the headlights and the lights on the control panel were out, and Joe Kivelson was holding a flashlight while Abe Clifford and Glenn Murell were trying to get me untied and lower me. I also noticed that the air was fresh, and very cold.

===Chinese===
接下来我知道的事，是自己被捆带吊在艇的一侧——那一侧如今成了顶——前灯和控制板上的灯全灭了，乔·基维尔森举着手电，艾布·克利福德和格伦·穆雷尔正动手解我身上的绳子，把我放下来。我还注意到，空气很新鲜，也非常冷。

===Original===
“Hey, we’re down!” I said, as though I were telling anybody anything they didn’t know. “How many are still alive?”

“As far as I know, all of us,” Joe said. “I think I have a broken arm.” I noticed, then, that he was holding his left arm stiffly at his side. Murell had a big gash on top of his head, and he was mopping blood from his face with his sleeve while he worked.

===Chinese===
「嘿，我们落下来啦！」我说，倒像是在告诉谁什么他们不知道的事似的。「还活着的有几个？」

「据我所知，全都活着，」乔说。「我大概是断了条胳膊。」这时我才注意到，他把左臂僵直地拢在身侧。穆雷尔头顶开了道大口子，一边干活一边用袖子抹着脸上的血。

===Original===
When they got me down, I looked around. Somebody else was playing a flashlight around at the stern, which was completely smashed. It was a miracle the rocket locker hadn’t blown up, but the main miracle was that all, or even any, of us were still alive.

We found a couple of lights that could be put on, and we got all of us picked up and the unconscious revived. One man, Dominic Silverstein, had a broken leg. Joe Kivelson’s arm was, as he suspected, broken, another man had a fractured wrist, and Abdullah Monnahan thought a couple of ribs were broken. The rest of us were in one piece, but all of us were cut and bruised. I felt sore all over. We also found a nuclear-electric heater that would work, and got it on. Tom and I rigged some tarpaulins to screen off the ruptured stern and keep out the worst of the cold wind. After they got through setting and splinting the broken bones and taping up Abdullah’s ribs, Cesário and Murell got some water out of one of the butts and started boiling it for coffee. I noticed that Piet Dumont had recovered his pipe and was smoking it, and Joe Kivelson had his lit.

===Chinese===
他们把我放下来以后，我环顾四周。另有一人正拿手电往船尾四处照，那里已经砸得稀烂。火箭储柜居然没被引爆，算是奇迹；但头一号的奇迹是，我们全体——哪怕能有一个人——还活着。

我们找到几盏还能点的灯，把大伙儿全都搀扶收拢起来，把昏迷的人一一弄醒。有多米尼克·西尔弗斯坦断了一条腿。乔·基维尔森的胳膊果然如他所料是断了，另有一人手腕骨折，阿卜杜拉·莫纳汉觉得自己断了两根肋骨。其余的人肢体都还囫囵，可个个带伤挂彩。我只觉得浑身没一处不疼。我们还找到一台能用的核电热器，把它点上了。我和汤姆张起几块油布，把破裂的船尾隔开，挡住寒风里最凶的那股。等断骨复位上了夹板、阿卜杜拉的肋骨也缠好胶布之后，塞萨里奥和穆雷尔从一只储水桶里舀出些水来，烧上了准备煮咖啡。我注意到皮特·杜蒙找回了自己的烟斗，正抽着；乔·基维尔森的那只也点上了。

===Original===
“Well, where are we?” somebody was asking Abe Clifford.

The navigator shook his head. “The radio’s smashed, so’s the receiver for the locator, and so’s the radio navigational equipment. I can state positively, however, that we are on the north coast of Hermann Reuch’s Land.”

===Chinese===
「那么，我们这是在哪儿？」有人在问艾布·克利福德。

领航员摇了摇头。「无线电砸坏了，定位信标接收器砸坏了，无线电导航设备也砸坏了。不过有一点我可以言之凿凿：我们是在赫尔曼·罗伊希之地的北海岸上。」

===Original===
Everybody laughed at that except Murell. I had to explain to him that Hermann Reuch’s Land was the antarctic continent of Fenris, and hasn’t any other coast.

“I’d say we’re a good deal west of Sancerre Bay,” Cesário Vieira hazarded. “We can’t be east of it, the way we got blown west. I think we must be at least five hundred miles east of it.”

===Chinese===
这话引得大家都笑了，唯独穆雷尔不笑。我只好向他解释：赫尔曼·罗伊希之地是芬尼斯的南极大陆，除此之外再没有别的海岸。

「依我说，我们在桑塞尔湾往西好远的地方，」塞萨里奥·维埃拉揣测道。「我们不可能是它的东面——我们是一路往西被吹过来的。我想我们至少在它东面五百英里。」

===Original===
“Don’t fool yourself, Cesário,” Joe Kivelson told him. “We could have gotten into a turbulent updraft and been carried to the upper, eastward winds. The altimeter was trying to keep up with the boat and just couldn’t, half the time. We don’t know where we went. I’ll take Abe’s estimate and let it go at that.”

===Chinese===
「别自己骗自己了，塞萨里奥，」乔·基维尔森对他说。「我们说不定卷进了一股湍急的上升气流，被带到了高空向东的风里。高度计有一半时间拼命想跟上艇的动静，就是跟不上。我们不知道自己到过什么地方。我认艾布的估算，这事就到此为止。」

===Original===
“Well, we’re up some kind of a fjord,” Tom said. “I think it branches like a Y, and we’re up the left branch, but I won’t make a point of that.”

“I can’t find anything like that on this map,” Abe Clifford said, after a while.

===Chinese===
「反正我们是在某条峡湾的上游，」汤姆说。「我看它分岔像个 Y 字，我们上的是左边那条岔，不过这一点我不敢咬死。」

「这张地图上，我找不到任何像这样的东西，」过了半晌，艾布·克利福德说。

===Original===
Joe Kivelson swore. “You ought to know better than that, Abe; you know how thoroughly this coast hasn’t been mapped.”

“How much good will it do us to know where we are, right now?” I asked. “If the radio’s smashed, we can’t give anybody our position.”

===Chinese===
乔·基维尔森骂了一句。「艾布，你不该抱这种指望；这条海岸没有被测绘得多彻底，你是再清楚不过的了。」

「眼下我们知道自己身在何处，又能顶什么用？」我问。「无线电要是砸坏了，我们就没法把方位告诉任何人。」

===Original===
“We might be able to fix up the engines and get the boat in the air again, after the wind drops.” Monnahan said. “I’ll take a look at them and see how badly they’ve been banged up.”

“With the whole stern open?” Hans Cronje asked. “We’d freeze stiffer than a gun barrel before we went a hundred miles.”

===Chinese===
「等风势减弱，说不定能把引擎修一修，让艇重新上天，」莫纳汉说。「我这就去看看它们，瞧砸坏了多少。」

「整个船尾都敞着口？」汉斯·克龙涅问。「不出一百英里，我们就得冻得比枪管还硬。」

===Original===
“Then we can pack the stern full of wet snow and let it freeze, instead of us,” I suggested. “There’ll be plenty of snow before the wind goes down.”

Joe Kivelson looked at me for a moment. “That would work,” he said. “How soon can you get started on the engines, Abdullah?”

===Chinese===
「那我们就把船尾塞满湿雪，让它去冻，别冻着我们，」我提议道。「等风停之前，雪有的是。」

乔·基维尔森端详了我片刻。「这法子行得通，」他说。「阿卜杜拉，你多快能动工检修引擎？」

===Original===
“Right away. I’ll need somebody to help me, though. I can’t do much the way you have me bandaged up.”

“I think we’d better send a couple of parties out,” Ramón Llewellyn said. “We’ll have to find a better place to stay than this boat. We don’t all have parkas or lined boots, and we have a couple of injured men. This heater won’t be enough; in about seventy hours we’d all freeze to death sitting around it.”

===Chinese===
「马上就能动手。不过得有人给我搭把手。你们把我包扎成这副模样，我干不了多少活。」

「我看我们最好派两队人出去，」拉蒙·卢埃林说。「我们总得找个比这条艇强些的容身之处。我们不是人人都带着风雪大衣和夹里靴子，还有两三个伤员。这台热器不够劲；再过七十来个小时，我们围着它坐，全得冻死。」

===Original===
Somebody mentioned the possibility of finding a cave.

“I doubt it,” Llewellyn said. “I was on an exploring expedition down here, once. This is all igneous rock, mostly granite. There aren’t many caves. But there may be some sort of natural shelter, or something we can make into a shelter, not too far away. We have two half-ton lifters; we could use them to pile up rocks and build something. Let’s make up two parties. I’ll take one; Abe, you take the other. One of us can go up and the other can go down.”

===Chinese===
有人提到了找山洞的可能。

「我看悬，」卢埃林说。「我从前随一支探险队下来过这一带。这儿全是火成岩，多半是花岗岩。山洞不多。不过不远的地方也许有什么天然遮蔽处，或者什么能改造成遮蔽处的东西。我们有两台半吨级的升举器；可以用它们垒石头，搭出个东西来。就编成两队吧。我带一队；艾布，你带另一队。一队往上游去，一队往下游去。」

===Original===
We picked parties, trying to get men who had enough clothing and hadn’t been too badly banged around in the landing. Tom wanted to go along, but Abdullah insisted that he stay and help with the inspection of the boat’s engines. Finally six of us﻿—Llewellyn, myself, Glenn Murell, Abe Clifford, old Piet Dumont, and another man﻿—went out through the broken stern of the boat. We had two portable floodlights﻿—a scout boat carries a lot of equipment﻿—and Llewellyn took the one and Clifford the other. It had begun to snow already, and the wind was coming straight up the narrow ravine into which we had landed, driving it at us. There was a stream between the two walls of rock, swollen by the rains that had come just before the darkness, and the rocks in and beside it were coated with ice. We took one look at it and shook our heads. Any exploring we did would be done without trying to cross that. We stood for a few minutes trying to see through the driving snow, and then we separated, Abe Clifford, Dumont and the other man going up the stream and Ramón Llewellyn, Glenn Murell and I going down.

===Chinese===
我们分头编队，尽量挑衣裳穿得够、着陆时又没被摔得太狠的人。汤姆也想跟着去，可阿卜杜拉非要他留下，帮忙检查艇上的引擎。最后我们六个——卢埃林、我、格伦·穆雷尔、艾布·克利福德、老皮特·杜蒙，还有另一名汉子——从艇的破船尾钻了出去。我们带了两盏便携泛光灯——侦察艇上装备向来带得多——卢埃林提一盏，克利福德提一盏。雪已经下了起来，风顺着我们降落的那条窄峡谷直灌上来，卷着雪往我们身上打。两壁岩崖之间横着一道溪流，被黑暗降临前的那几场雨灌得满满的，溪里溪边的岩石都裹着一层冰。我们看了一眼便摇了头。要探路，也不能指望横渡那条溪。我们站了几分钟，想透过扑面的风雪看清点什么，随后便分了手：艾布·克利福德、杜蒙和那名汉子沿溪上行，拉蒙·卢埃林、格伦·穆雷尔和我沿溪下行。

===Original===
A few hundred yards below the boat, the stream went over a fifty-foot waterfall. We climbed down beside it, and found the ravine widening. It was a level beach, now, or what had been a beach thousands of years ago. The whole coast of Hermann Reuch’s land is sinking in the Eastern Hemisphere and rising in the Western. We turned away from the stream and found that the wind was increasing in strength and coming at us from the left instead of in front. The next thing we knew, we were at the point of the mountain on our right and we could hear the sea roaring ahead and on both sides of us. Tom had been right about that V-shaped fjord, I thought.

===Chinese===
在艇以下几百码处，溪水从一道五十英尺高的瀑布上跌下去。我们顺着瀑布旁边往下爬，发现峡谷渐渐开阔起来。眼前是一片平坦的滩地——或者说，几千年前曾是滩地的地方。赫尔曼·罗伊希之地的整条海岸，在东半球下沉，在西半球抬升。我们撇开溪流转了个方向，发觉风力更猛了，而且是从左边而不是正面朝我们刮来。再往前，我们已经到了右手那座山的岬角上，听得见海在前方和两侧轰吼。汤姆对那条 V 形峡湾的判断没错，我想。

===Original===
We began running into scattered trees now, and when we got around the point of the mountain we entered another valley.

Trees, like everything else on Fenris, are considerably different from anything analogous on normal planets. They aren’t tall, the biggest not more than fifteen feet high, but they are from six to eight feet thick, with all the branches at the top, sprouting out in all directions and reminding me of pictures of Medusa. The outside bark is a hard shell, which grows during the beginning of our four hot seasons a year. Under that will be more bark, soft and spongy, and this gets more and more dense toward the middle; and then comes the hardwood core, which may be as much as two feet thick.

===Chinese===
这时我们开始撞见零零星星的树，绕过山嘴之后，便进入了另一道山谷。

树，和芬尼斯上的其他一切一样，跟正常行星上的同类大有分别。它们不高，最高的不过十五英尺，树干却有六到八英尺粗，枝条全长在顶端，朝四面八方支棱出来，让我想起美杜莎（Medusa）的画像。外层的树皮是一层硬壳，在我们一年四个炎热季节的开头生长。硬壳底下还有树皮，松软如海绵，越往中间越致密；再往里就是硬木芯，最厚能有两英尺。

===Original===
“One thing, we have firewood,” Murell said, looking at them.

“What’ll we cut it with; our knives?” I wanted to know.

===Chinese===
「有一点是现成的：我们有柴火了，」穆雷尔望着那些树说。

「拿什么砍呢？用我们的小刀？」我问道。

===Original===
“Oh, we have a sonocutter on the boat,” Ramón Llewellyn said. “We can chop these things into thousand-pound chunks and float them to camp with the lifters. We could soak the spongy stuff on the outside with water and let it freeze, and build a hut out of it, too.” He looked around, as far as the light penetrated the driving snow. “This wouldn’t be a bad place to camp.”

*Not if we’re going to try to work on the boat*, I thought. And packing Dominic, with his broken leg, down over that waterfall was something I didn’t want to try, either. I didn’t say anything. Wait till we got back to the boat. It was too cold and windy here to argue, and besides, we didn’t know what Abe and his party might have found upstream.

===Chinese===
「哦，艇上有声波切割器，」拉蒙·卢埃林说。「可以把这些东西切成一千磅一块，用升举器浮运回营地。还可以把外面那层海绵似的软料浸上水，让它冻起来，再拿它搭一座小屋。」他朝四下里望了望，目光所及，尽是扑面的风雪。「在这儿扎营倒不算坏。」

*要是还想对那条艇动工，这儿可不成*，我想。再说，把断了腿的多米尼克背下那道瀑布，也不是我情愿尝试的事。我什么也没说。等回到艇上再讲。这里又冷、风又大，犯不上抬杠，何况我们还不知道艾布那一队往上游会找出什么来。

# Dedication / 献词

===Original===
For Betty and Vall, with loving remembrance

===Chinese===
谨以挚爱的怀念，献给贝蒂（Betty）与瓦尔（Vall）。

## Elementary, My Dear Kivelson / 基本演绎法，我亲爱的基维尔森

===Original===
Before we left the lighted elevator car, we took a quick nose count. Besides the Kivelsons, there were five *Javelin* men—Ramón Llewellyn, Abdullah Monnahan, Abe Clifford, Cesário Vieira, and a whitebeard named Piet Dumont. Al Devis had been with us when we crashed the door out of the meeting room, but he’d fallen by the way. We had a couple of flashlights, so, after sending the car down to Bottom Level, we picked our way up the zigzag iron stairs to the catwalk, under the seventy-foot ceiling, and sat down in the dark.

Joe Kivelson was fretting about what would happen to the rest of his men.

===Chinese===
趁着还没走出亮着灯的电梯车，我们赶紧点了点人头。除了基维尔森父子，还有五名标枪号（*Javelin*）的船员——拉蒙·卢埃林（Ramón Llewellyn）、阿卜杜拉·莫纳汉（Abdullah Monnahan）、艾布·克利福德（Abe Clifford）、塞萨里奥·维埃拉（Cesário Vieira），外加一个叫皮特·杜蒙（Piet Dumont）的白胡子老头。撞开会议室大门的时候，阿尔·德维斯（Al Devis）本来也跟我们在一处，可惜半道掉了队。我们手里有两支手电，于是把电梯车打发回底层（Bottom Level），便沿之字形铁梯摸黑往上爬，爬到七十英尺高的顶棚下面的栈桥上，坐进了黑暗里。

乔·基维尔森（Joe Kivelson）正为他其余手下的境遇焦躁不安。

===Original===
“Fine captain I am, running out and leaving them!”

“If they couldn’t keep up, that’s their tough luck,” Oscar Fujisawa told him. “You brought out all you could. If you’d waited any longer, none of us would have gotten out.”

“They won’t bother with them,” I added. “You and Tom and Oscar, here, are the ones they want.”

===Chinese===
「我算哪门子船长，自己逃出来，把他们全撂下了！」

「他们要是跟不上，那就只能怪自己倒霉，」奥斯卡·藤泽（Oscar Fujisawa）对他说。「能带出来的，你都带出来了。再耽搁下去，咱们一个也出不来。」

「他们不会去找那些人的麻烦，」我补充道。「他们要的是你、汤姆（Tom），还有这位奥斯卡。」

===Original===
Joe was still letting himself be argued into thinking he had done the right thing when we saw the lights of a lorry coming from uptown at ceiling level. A moment later, it backed to the catwalk, and Bish Ware stuck his head out from the pilot’s seat.

“Where do you gentlemen wish to go?” he asked.

“To the *Javelin*,” Joe said instantly.

“Huh-uh,” Oscar disagreed. “That’s the first place they’ll look. That’ll be all right for Ramón and the others, but if they catch you and Tom, they’ll shoot you and call it self-defense, or take you in and beat both of you to a jelly. This’ll blow over in fifteen or twenty hours, but I’m not going anywhere near my ship, now.”

===Chinese===
乔还在被大家你一言我一语地劝得相信自己没做错，这时我们看见一辆卡车的灯火贴着顶棚高度从上城方向驶来。片刻之后，它倒到栈桥边，比什·韦尔（Bish Ware）从驾驶座里探出头来。

「各位先生想去哪儿？」他问。

「去标枪号，」乔脱口而出。

「不行，」奥斯卡不同意。「他们头一个要搜的就是那儿。拉蒙他们几个回去还不要紧，可要是叫他们逮住你和汤姆，要么开枪把你们打了，再报个正当防卫，要么把你们抓进去，把你们俩揍得稀烂。这场风波过十五二十个小时就会平息，眼下我可不愿意靠近我自己那条船半步。」

===Original===
“Drop us off on Second Level Down, about Eighth Street and a couple of blocks from the docks,” the mate, Llewellyn, said. “We’ll borrow some weapons from Patel the Pawnbroker and then circulate around and see what’s going on. But you and Joe and Oscar had better go underground for a while.”

“The *Times*,” I said. “We have a whole pillar-building to ourselves; we could hide half the population.”

That was decided upon. We all piled into the lorry, and Bish took it to an inconspicuous place on the Second Level and let down. Ramón Llewellyn and the others got out. Then we went up to Main City Level. We passed within a few blocks of Hunters’ Hall. There was a lot of noise, but no shooting.

Joe Kivelson didn’t have anything to say, on the trip, but he kept looking at the pilot’s seat in perplexity and apprehension. I think he expected Bish to try to ram the lorry through every building we passed by or over.

===Chinese===
「把我们放在下第二层（Second Level Down）吧，大概第八街（Eighth Street），离船坞隔一两个街区，」大副卢埃林说。「我们先去当铺老板帕特尔（Patel the Pawnbroker）那儿借几件家伙，然后四处转转，看看风色。你和乔、奥斯卡呢，最好先躲起来避一阵风头。」

「去《时报》（*Times*），」我说。「一整栋柱形大楼就归我们一家，藏下半城人都绰绰有余。」

就这么定了。我们全都挤进卡车，比什把它开到下第二层一处不显眼的地方，落了下来。拉蒙·卢埃林和其他人下了车。然后我们升上主城层（Main City Level）。路过猎手会堂（Hunters’ Hall）时，离着只有几个街区。那边喧闹成一片，好在还没动枪。

这一路上乔·基维尔森一言不发，只是又惶惑又提心吊胆地不住打量驾驶座。我猜他是一直在等着比什开车往我们从旁经过或从头顶飞越的每一栋楼里硬撞。

===Original===
We found Dad in the editorial department on the top floor, feeding voice-tape to Julio while the latter made master sheets for teleprinting. I gave him a quick rundown on what had happened that he hadn’t gotten from my radio. Dad cluck-clucked in disapproval, either at my getting into a fight, assaulting an officer, or, literally, throwing money away.

Bish Ware seemed a little troubled. “I think,” he said, “that I shall make a circuit of my diocese, and see what can be learned from my devoted flock. Should I turn up anything significant, I will call it in.”

With that, he went tottering over to the elevator, stumbling on the way and making an unepiscopal remark. I watched him, and then turned to Dad.

===Chinese===
我们在顶层的编辑部里找到了爸爸，他正把口述录音带喂给胡利奥（Julio），由后者制作电传打印的底版。我把无线电里没来得及报告的那部分经过，三言两语给他讲了一遍。爸爸啧啧连声，大不以为然，不知是为了我卷进斗殴、动手袭警，还是为了我那不折不扣字面意义上的「把钱往外扔」。

比什·韦尔看起来有点心事重重。「我想，」他说，「我该到我的教区巡上一圈，看看能从我这群虔敬的羔羊嘴里问出些什么来。一旦发现什么要紧的情况，我会打电话报来。」

说完，他便一步三晃地朝电梯走去，半路绊了一下，迸出一句大大有失主教身份的话。我目送他走远，然后转向爸爸。

===Original===
“Did he have anything to drink after I left?” I asked.

“Nothing but about five cups of coffee.”

I mentally marked that: *Add oddities, Bish Ware.* He’d been at least four hours without liquor, and he was walking as unsteadily as when I’d first seen him at the spaceport. I didn’t know any kind of liquor that would persist like that.

===Chinese===
「我走之后，他喝过什么没有？」我问。

「没什么，也就五杯来咖啡吧。」

我在心里记下一笔：*再添一桩怪事：比什·韦尔。*他至少四个小时滴酒未沾，走起路来却还跟我头一回在太空港见到他时一样摇晃。我可不知道世上有哪种酒，酒劲能像这样经久不散。

===Original===
Julio had at least an hour’s tape to transcribe, so Dad and Joe and Tom and Oscar and I went to the living room on the floor below. Joe was still being bewildered about Bish Ware.

“How’d he manage to come for us?” he wanted to know.

“Why, he was here with me all evening,” Dad said. “He came from the spaceport with Walt and Tom, and had dinner with us. He called a few people from here, and found out about the fake riot and police raid Ravick had cooked up. You’d be surprised at how much information he can pick up around town.”

===Chinese===
胡利奥手里起码还有一小时的磁带要誊录，于是爸爸、乔、汤姆、奥斯卡和我下到楼下一层的起居室去了。乔对比什·韦尔仍旧满肚子疑惑。

「他怎么会想到来接我们的？」他想知道。

「嗐，他一整晚都陪着我呢，」爸爸说。「他跟沃尔特（Walt）和汤姆一道从太空港过来的，还在这儿吃了晚饭。他从这儿给几个人打了电话，就摸清了拉维克（Ravick）一手炮制的那场假暴乱加警察突袭。他在这城里能打听到多少消息，准会叫你大吃一惊。」

===Original===
Joe looked at his son, alarmed.

“Hey! You let him see—” he began.

“The wax on Bottom Level, in the Fourth Ward?” I asked. “He won’t blab about that. He doesn’t blab things where they oughtn’t to be blabbed.”

“That’s right,” Dad backed me up. He was beginning to think of Bish as one of the *Times* staff, now. “We got a lot of tips from him, but nothing we give him gets out.” He got his pipe lit again. “What about that wax, Joe?” he asked. “Were you serious when you made that motion about a price of seventy-five centisols?”

===Chinese===
乔吃惊地望着儿子。

「嘿！你竟让他看见了——」他开口道。

「你是说底层、第四区（Fourth Ward）的那批蜡？」我问。「他不会走漏这个的。不该说的场合，他从不乱说。」

「正是，」爸爸帮我敲边鼓。他如今已渐渐把比什看成《时报》的一员了。「我们从他那儿得过不少线报，可我们交给他的东西，从来没有一件漏出去过。」他重新点着烟斗。「乔，那批蜡是怎么回事？」他问。「你提七十五厘索尔（centisol）的价钱那个动议，是当真的吗？」

===Original===
“I sure was!” Joe declared. “That’s the real price, and always has been, and that’s what we get or Kapstaad doesn’t get any more wax.”

“If Murell can top it, maybe Kapstaad won’t get any more wax, period,” I said. “Who’s he with—Interstellar Import-Export?”

Anybody would have thought a barbwire worm had crawled onto Joe Kivelson’s chair seat under him.

===Chinese===
「当然是当真的！」乔断然道。「这才是实价，从来都是。我们就非卖这个数不可——不然卡普斯塔德（Kapstaad）就一两蜡也别想再拿到。」

「要是穆雷尔（Murell）肯出更高的价，卡普斯塔德说不定就一两蜡都彻底别想拿到了，」我说。「他是哪家的人——星际进出口公司（Interstellar Import-Export）？」

谁看了都会以为，乔·基维尔森的椅子面上不知什么时候爬进了一条铁丝网蠕虫（barbwire worm）。

===Original===
“Where’d you hear that?” he demanded, which is the Galaxy’s silliest question to ask any newsman. “Tom, if you’ve been talking—”

“He hasn’t,” I said. “He didn’t need to. It sticks out a parsec in all directions.” I mentioned some of the things I’d noticed while interviewing Murell, and his behavior after leaving the ship. “Even before I’d talked to him, I wondered why Tom was so anxious to get aboard with me. He didn’t know we’d arranged to put Murell up here; he was going to take him to see that wax, and then take him to the *Javelin*. You were going to produce him at the meeting and have him bid against Belsher, only that tread-snail fouled your lines for you. So then you thought you had to stall off a new contract till he got out of the hospital.”

===Chinese===
「你从哪儿听来的？」他质问道——这可是全银河系拿去问新闻记者的最蠢的一个问题。「汤姆，要是你走漏了风声——」

「他没有，」我说。「他也用不着。这事明摆着朝四面八方伸出去足有一秒差距（parsec）远。」我说起了采访穆雷尔时注意到的几处细节，还有他下船后的种种表现。「还没跟他搭上话，我就纳闷汤姆为什么那么急着想跟我一道上船。他并不知道我们已安排穆雷尔住到这儿来；他原打算先带他去看那批蜡，再带他去标枪号。你们是准备让他在大会上露面，叫他跟贝尔歇（Belsher）竞价，偏偏那只履带蜗牛搅坏了你们的局。于是你们就觉得，非得把新合同拖到他出院再签不可。」

===Original===
The two Kivelsons and Oscar Fujisawa were looking at one another; Joe and Tom in consternation, and Oscar in derision of both of them. I was feeling pretty good. Brother, I thought, Sherlock Holmes never did better, himself.

That, all of a sudden, reminded me of Dr. John Watson, whom Bish perceived to have been in Afghanistan. That was one thing Sherlock H. Boyd hadn’t deduced any answers for. Well, give me a little more time. And more data.

“You got it all figured out, haven’t you?” Joe was asking sarcastically. The sarcasm was as hollow as an empty oil drum.

===Chinese===
基维尔森父子和奥斯卡·藤泽面面相觑；乔和汤姆一脸惊惶，奥斯卡则把两人好一顿讥诮。我心里美滋滋的。老兄，我心想，就算是夏洛克·福尔摩斯（Sherlock Holmes）本人，也不过如此吧。

这冷不丁又让我想起约翰·华生医生（Dr. John Watson）——比什当年不是「一眼看出」他去过阿富汗嘛。而有件事，夏洛克·H·博伊德（Sherlock H. Boyd——这是我给自己起的诨号，把福尔摩斯的大名接到自家姓氏上）至今还没推演出答案。好吧，再给我一点时间。再来一点数据。

「你全都盘算明白了，是吧？」乔话里带刺地问。那点讽刺空得就像一只见了底的油桶。

===Original===
“The *Times*,” Dad was saying, trying not to sound too proud, “has a very sharp reportorial staff, Joe.”

“It isn’t Interstellar,” Oscar told me, grinning. “It’s Argentine Exotic Organics. You know, everybody thought Joe, here, was getting pretty high-toned, sending his daughter to school on Terra. School wasn’t the only thing she went for. We got a letter from her, the last time the Cape Canaveral was in, saying that she’d contacted Argentine Organics and that a man was coming out on the *Peenemünde*, posing as a travel-book author. Well, he’s here, now.”

“You’d better keep an eye on him,” I advised. “If Steve Ravick gets to him, he won’t be much use to you.”

===Chinese===
「《时报》，」爸爸说着，尽量不让自己显得太得意，「有一支非常精明的记者队伍，乔。」

「不是星际进出口公司，」奥斯卡咧嘴笑着对我说。「是阿根廷珍奇有机物公司（Argentine Exotic Organics）。你要知道，当初乔老兄把女儿送去特拉（Terra）上学，人人都觉得他派头越端越高雅。可她去那儿图的并不只是上学。上一班卡纳维拉尔号（*Cape Canaveral*）进港时，我们收到她一封信，说她已经跟阿根廷有机物公司搭上了线，有个男人将搭乘佩内明德号（*Peenemünde*）过来，扮成一个旅行书作家。瞧，他这不来了。」

「你们最好看紧他一点，」我劝道。「要是让史蒂夫·拉维克（Steve Ravick）抢先得了手，他对你们就没多大用处了。」

===Original===
“You think Ravick would really harm Murell?” Dad asked.

He thought so, too. He was just trying to comfort himself by pretending he didn’t.

“What do you think, Ralph?” Oscar asked him. “If we get competitive wax buying, again, seventy-five a pound will be the starting price. I’m not spending the money till I get it, but I wouldn’t be surprised to see wax go to a sol a pound on the loading floor here. And you know what that would mean.”

===Chinese===
「你认为拉维克真会伤害穆雷尔？」爸爸问。

他心里也这么想。他不过是装作不信，好宽慰自己罢了。

「拉尔夫（Ralph），你怎么看？」奥斯卡问他。「要是蜡的收购重新变成多家竞买，七十五厘索尔一磅只是起价。这钱一天不到手，我一天不忙着花，可要是看到这儿的装货场上蜡价涨到一索尔（sol）一磅，我也不会吃惊。那意味着什么，你是明白的。」

===Original===
“Thirty for Steve Ravick,” Dad said. That puzzled Oscar, till I explained that “thirty” is newsese for “the end.” “I guess Walt’s right. Ravick would do anything to prevent that.” He thought for a moment. “Joe, you were using the wrong strategy. You should have let Ravick get that thirty-five centisol price established for the Cooperative, and then had Murell offer seventy-five or something like that.”

===Chinese===
「那就是给史蒂夫·拉维克送『三十』了，」爸爸说。这话把奥斯卡听得一头雾水，直到我解释说，「三十」是新闻行话，意思是「终了」。「我看沃尔特说得对。为了不让这种事发生，拉维克什么都干得出来。」他想了片刻。「乔，你当初用的策略不对。你本该让拉维克替合作社（Cooperative）把三十五厘索尔的价钱定下来，然后再让穆雷尔出七十五厘索尔或者差不多这样的价。」

===Original===
“You crazy?” Joe demanded. “Why, then the Coop would have been stuck with it.”

“That’s right. And as soon as Murell’s price was announced, everybody would drop out of the Cooperative and reclaim their wax, even the captains who owe Ravick money. He’d have nobody left but a handful of thugs and barflies.”

“But that would smash the Cooperative,” Joe Kivelson objected. “Listen, Ralph; I’ve been in the Cooperative all my life, since before Steve Ravick was heard of on this planet. I’ve worked hard for the Cooperative, and—”

===Chinese===
「你疯了吗？」乔反问。「那样合作社不就让这个价钱套死了。」

「一点没错。而穆雷尔的价钱一公布，所有人都会退出合作社，把自己的蜡提走，连欠着拉维克钱的船长们也会走。到头来他手下就剩一小撮打手和泡酒吧的醉鬼了。」

「可那样合作社就砸了呀，」乔·基维尔森反对道。「你听我说，拉尔夫；我这辈子都在合作社里，早在史蒂夫·拉维克在这颗行星上有了名气之前就是。我一直为合作社拼死拼活地干，而且——」

===Original===
*You didn’t work hard enough*, I thought. *You let Steve Ravick take it away from you.* Dad told Joe pretty much the same thing:

“You don’t have a Cooperative, Joe. Steve Ravick has a racket. The only thing you can do with this organization is smash it, and then rebuild it with Ravick and his gang left out.”

Joe puzzled over that silently. He’d been thinking that it was the same Cooperative his father and Simon MacGregor and the other old hunters had organized, and that getting rid of Ravick was simply a matter of voting him out. He was beginning to see, now, that parliamentary procedure wasn’t any weapon against Ravick’s force and fraud and intimidation.

===Chinese===
*你干得还不够卖力*，我心里想。*是你放任史蒂夫·拉维克把它从你手里夺了去。*爸爸对乔说的话，意思也差不多：

「你手上已经没有合作社了，乔。史蒂夫·拉维克手上的是一个巧取豪夺的黑买卖。对眼下这个组织，你唯一的办法就是砸烂它，然后甩开拉维克和他那伙人，重新把它建起来。」

乔默默琢磨着这番话。他一直以为，这还是他父亲和西蒙·麦格雷戈（Simon MacGregor）以及别的老猎手当年组织起来的那个合作社，赶走拉维克无非是表决把他选下去的事。直到此刻他才开始明白，对付拉维克的暴力、欺诈和恐吓，议事程序根本算不上武器。

===Original===
“I think Walt has something,” Oscar Fujisawa said. “As long as Murell’s in the hospital at the spaceport, he’s safe, but as soon as he gets out of Odin Dock & Shipyard territory, he’s going to be a clay pigeon.”

Tom hadn’t been saying anything. Now he cleared his throat.

“On the *Peenemünde*, I was talking about taking Mr. Murell for a trip in the *Javelin*,” he said. “That was while we were still pretending he’d come here to write a book. Maybe that would be a good idea, anyhow.”

“It’s a cinch we can’t let him get killed on us,” his father said. “I doubt if Exotic Organics would send anybody else out, if he was.”

===Chinese===
「我看沃尔特说得在理，」奥斯卡·藤泽说。「穆雷尔只要还住在太空港的医院里，他就是安全的；可他一走出奥丁船坞造船厂（Odin Dock & Shipyard）的地界，就要变成活靶子了。」

汤姆一直没吭声。这时他清了清嗓子。

「在佩内明德号上的时候，我就说起过要请穆雷尔先生乘标枪号跑一趟，」他说。「那还是我们假装他来这儿写书的时候。不管怎么说，这兴许是个好主意。」

「咱们说什么也不能让他死在咱们手里，」他父亲说。「他要是死了，珍奇有机物公司还会不会再派别人来，我可就说不准了。」

===Original===
“Here,” Dad said. “We’ll run the story we have on him in the morning edition, and then correct it and apologize to the public for misleading them and explain in the evening edition. And before he goes, we can have him make an audiovisual for the ’cast, telling everybody who he is and announcing the price he’s offering. We’ll put that on the air. Get enough publicity, and Steve Ravick won’t dare do anything to him.”

Publicity, I thought, is the only weapon Dad knows how to use. He thinks it’s invincible. Me, I wouldn’t bet on what Steve Ravick wouldn’t dare do if you gave me a hundred to one. Ravick had been in power too long, and he was drunker on it than Bish Ware ever got on Baldur honey-rum. As an intoxicant, rum is practically a soft drink beside power.

===Chinese===
「这么办，」爸爸说。「明天晨版先把我们手里关于他的报道登出去，然后在晚版里更正，为误导了公众向大家道歉，并把原委讲清楚。趁他还没走，我们可以让他为播出录一段声像节目，告诉所有人他是谁，宣布他出的价钱。我们把它播出去。宣传声势造得足够大，史蒂夫·拉维克就不敢对他下手了。」

宣传啊，我心想，是爸爸唯一会使的武器。他以为它无往不胜。至于我，就算给我一百比一的赔率，我也不敢押史蒂夫·拉维克有哪些事不敢干。拉维克掌权太久了，权力灌得他醉意之深，连比什·韦尔灌足了巴尔德蜂蜜朗姆酒（Baldur honey-rum）也比不上。论醉人的本事，跟权力摆在一起，朗姆酒几乎就是软饮料。

===Original===
“Well, do you think Ravick’s gotten onto Murell yet?” Oscar said. “We kept that a pretty close secret. Joe and I knew about him, and so did the Mahatma and Nip Spazoni and Corkscrew Finnegan, and that was all.”

“I didn’t even tell Tom, here, till the *Peenemünde* got into radio range,” Joe Kivelson said. “Then I only told him and Ramón and Abdullah and Abe and Hans Cronje.”

“And Al Devis,” Tom added. “He came into the conning tower while you were telling the rest of us.”

===Chinese===
「唉，你看拉维克识破穆雷尔的底了吗？」奥斯卡说。「这事我们捂得相当严。知道的只有我和乔，再加上『圣雄』（the Mahatma）、尼普·斯帕佐尼（Nip Spazoni）和『螺丝锥』芬尼根（Corkscrew Finnegan），就这些人了。」

「连汤姆这儿，我都是等佩内明德号进了无线电通讯范围才告诉的，」乔·基维尔森说。「当时我只跟他说了，另外还有拉蒙、阿卜杜拉、艾布和汉斯·克龙涅（Hans Cronje）。」

「还有阿尔·德维斯，」汤姆补了一句。「你给我们大伙儿讲的时候，他正好进了指挥塔。」

===Original===
The communication screen began buzzing, and I went and put it on. It was Bish Ware, calling from a pay booth somewhere.

“I have some early returns,” he said. “The cops cleared everybody out of Hunters’ Hall except the Ravick gang. Then Ravick reconvened the meeting, with nobody but his gang. They were very careful to make sure they had enough for a legal quorum under the bylaws, and then they voted to accept the new price of thirty-five centisols a pound.”

“That’s what I was afraid of,” Joe Kivelson said. “Did they arrest any of my crew?”

===Chinese===
通讯屏幕嗡嗡响了起来，我走过去接通。是比什·韦尔，从什么地方的付费通话亭打来的。

「我这边有了些初步回报，」他说。「警察把猎手会堂里的人都清了出去，单留下拉维克那伙人。跟着拉维克重新开会，到场的清一色是他自己人。他们还仔细数够了章程规定的法定人数，然后表决通过了三十五厘索尔一磅的新价格。」

「我就怕来这一手，」乔·基维尔森说。「他们抓了我船上的弟兄没有？」

===Original===
“Not that I know of,” Bish said. “They made a few arrests, but turned everybody loose later. They’re still looking for you and your son. As far as I know, they aren’t interested in anybody else.” He glanced hastily over his shoulder, as though to make sure the door of the booth was secure. “I’m with some people, now. I’ll call you back later.”

“Well, that’s that, Joe,” Oscar said, after Bish blanked the screen. “The Ravick Coop’s stuck with the price cut. The only thing left to do is get everybody out of it we can, and organize a new one.”

“I guess that’s so,” Joe agreed. “I wonder, though if Ravick has really got wise to Murell.”

===Chinese===
「据我所知没有，」比什说。「他们抓了几个人，后来又都放了。他们还在搜捕你和你儿子。据我了解，别人他们没兴趣。」他匆匆回头瞥了一眼，像是确认通话亭的门关严实了没有。「我这会儿跟一些人在一起。回头我再打给你。」

「好了，事已至此，乔，」比什把屏幕切断之后，奥斯卡说。「拉维克的合作社这下吃定了这次降价。剩下要做的，就是把能拉的人都拉出来，另组一个新的。」

「我看也是，」乔表示同意。「不过我纳闷，拉维克到底是不是真的摸清了穆雷尔的底细。」

===Original===
“Walt figured it out since the ship got in,” Oscar said. “Belsher’s been on the ship with Murell for six months. Well, call it three; everything speeds up about double in hyperspace. But in three months he ought to see as much as Walt saw in a couple of hours.”

“Well, maybe Belsher doesn’t know what’s suspicious, the way Walt does,” Tom said.

“I’m sure he doesn’t,” I said. “But he and Murell are both in the wax business. I’ll bet he noticed dozens of things I never even saw.”

===Chinese===
「船进港之后，沃尔特就把这事推想出来了，」奥斯卡说。「贝尔歇跟穆雷尔同船待了六个月。就算三个月吧；在超空间（hyperspace）里，一切都差不多加快一倍。可三个月下来，他该看出来的东西，总不会少于沃尔特几个钟头里看出的那些。」

「话虽这么说，兴许贝尔歇不像沃尔特那样，天生知道什么情形可疑，」汤姆说。

「我敢肯定他不知道，」我说。「不过他和穆雷尔都是吃蜡这行饭的。我打赌，他注意到的东西有几十件，我连见都没见到过。」

===Original===
“Then we’d better take awfully good care of Mr. Murell,” Tom said. “Get him aboard as fast as we can, and get out of here with him. Walt, you’re coming along, aren’t you?”

That was what we’d agreed, while Glenn Murell was still the famous travel-book author. I wanted to get out of it, now. There wouldn’t be anything happening aboard the *Javelin*, and a lot happening here in Port Sandor. Dad had the same idea, only he was one hundred percent for my going with Murell. I think he wanted me out of Port Sandor, where I wouldn’t get in the way of any small high-velocity particles of lead that might be whizzing around.

===Chinese===
「那我们就得把穆雷尔先生照料得妥妥帖帖，」汤姆说。「尽快请他上船，带着他离开这儿。沃尔特，你也一起去的，对吧？」

那是格伦·穆雷尔（Glenn Murell）还是大名鼎鼎的旅行书作家时，我们说定了的。可如今我想打退堂鼓了。标枪号上不会有什么动静，桑多尔港（Port Sandor）这边却会有好戏连台。爸爸跟我想的一样，只是他百分之百赞成我跟穆雷尔一起走。我想，他是要把我弄出桑多尔港，省得有什么四处乱飞的小小高速铅粒，跟我狭路相逢。

## Endnotes / 尾注

===Original===
1. Ed. note: “The End.” ↩︎

===Chinese===
1. 编者注：「全书完。」 ↩︎

## Finale / 终章

===Original===
They had Tom Kivelson in a private room at the hospital; he was sitting up in a chair, with a lot of pneumatic cushions around him, and a lunch tray on his lap. He looked white and thin. He could move one arm completely, but the bandages they had loaded him with seemed to have left the other free only at the elbow. He was concentrating on his lunch, and must have thought I was one of the nurses, or a doctor, or something of the sort.

“Are you going to let me have a cigarette and a cup of coffee, when I’m through with this?” he asked.

“Well, I don’t have any coffee, but you can have one of my cigarettes,” I said.

Then he looked up and gave a whoop. “Walt! How’d you get in here? I thought they weren’t going to let anybody in to see me till this afternoon.”

“Power of the press,” I told him. “Bluff, blarney, and blackmail. How are they treating you?”

===Chinese===
医院把汤姆·基维尔森（Tom Kivelson）安置在一间单人病房里；他坐在椅子上，身周垫了一圈充气软垫，腿上搁着午餐托盘。他脸色发白，人也瘦了。一条胳膊活动自如，可缠满全身的绷带似乎只给另一条留出了肘部以下的自由。他正专心对付午餐，八成把我当成了护士、大夫之类的什么人。

「等我把这些吃完了，能赏我一支烟、一杯咖啡吗？」他问。

「这个嘛，咖啡我可没有，烟倒能给你一支，」我说。

他这才抬头看见我，欢呼了一声。「沃尔特（Walt）！你怎么钻进来的？我还当他们要到今天下午才放人进来见我呢。」

「新闻界的力量，」我告诉他。「虚张声势、花言巧语，外加要挟。他们待你怎么样？」

===Original===
“Awful. Look what they gave me for lunch. I thought we were on short rations down on Hermann Reuch’s Land. How’s Father?”

“He’s all right. They took the splint off, but he still has to carry his arm in a sling.”

“Lucky guy; he can get around on his feet, and I’ll bet he isn’t starving, either. You know, speaking about food, I’m going to feel like a cannibal eating carniculture meat, now. My whole back’s carniculture.” He filled his mouth with whatever it was they were feeding him and asked, through it: “Did I miss Steve Ravick’s hanging?”

===Chinese===
「糟透了。瞧瞧他们给我吃的什么午饭。我还当咱们是在赫尔曼·罗伊希之地（Hermann Reuch's Land）勒紧裤腰带过日子呢。我爸怎么样？」

「他没事。夹板拆了，只是胳膊还得用吊带吊着。」

「他可真走运；能自己下地走动，我敢打赌也饿不着。话说回来，一提吃的——往后我再吃肉培（carniculture）肉，准觉得自己在吃人。我整条背都是肉培培出来的。」他往嘴里塞了一大口不知什么玩意儿的饭食，含着问道：「我是不是错过了看史蒂夫·拉维克（Steve Ravick）上绞架？」

===Original===
I was horrified. “Haven’t these people told you anything?” I demanded.

“Nah; they wouldn’t even tell me the right time. Afraid it would excite me.”

So I told him; first who Bish Ware really was, and then who Ravick really was. He gaped for a moment, and then shoveled in more food.

“Go on; what happened?”

I told him how Bish had smuggled Gerrit and Leo Belsher out on Second Level Down and gotten them to the spaceport, where Courtland’s men had been waiting for them.

===Chinese===
我吓了一跳。「这些人什么都没跟你说吗？」我追问。

「才不呢；他们连现在几点都不肯告诉我。生怕把我激动着。」

于是我把事情原原本本讲给他听：先讲比什·韦尔（Bish Ware）究竟是什么人，再讲拉维克（Ravick）究竟是什么人。他张着嘴愣了半晌，随即又往嘴里扒拉起饭来。

「接着说；后来怎么样了？」

我告诉他，比什如何在下第二层（Second Level Down）偷偷把赫里特（Gerrit）和利奥·贝尔歇（Leo Belsher）运出城、送去太空港，科特兰（Courtland）的人正在那儿等着他们。

===Original===
“Gerrit’s going to Terra, and from there to Loki. They want the natives to see what happens to a Terran who breaks Terran law; teach them that our law isn’t just to protect us. Belsher’s going to Terra, too. There was a big ship captains’ meeting; they voted to reclaim their wax and sell it individually to Murell, but to retain membership in the Coop. They think they’ll have to stay in the Coop to get anything that’s gettable out of Gerrit’s and Belsher’s money. Oscar Fujisawa and Cesário Vieira are going to Terra on the *Cape Canaveral* to start suit to recover anything they can, and also to petition for reclassification of Fenris. Oscar’s coming back on the next ship, but Cesário’s going to stay on as the Coop representative. I suppose he and Linda will be getting married.”

“Natch. They’ll both stay on Terra, I suppose. Hey, whattaya know! Cesário’s getting off Fenris without having to die and reincarnate.”

He finished his lunch, such as it was and what there was of it, and I relieved him of the tray and set it on the floor beyond his chair. I found an ashtray and lit a cigarette for him and one for myself, using the big lighter. Tom looked at it dubiously, predicting that sometime I’d push the wrong thing and send myself bye-byes for a couple of hours. I told him how Bish had used it.

===Chinese===
「赫里特要被押去特拉（Terra），再从那里转往洛基（Loki）。他们要让当地土著亲眼看看，一个触犯了特拉法律的特拉人会是什么下场；让他们明白，咱们的法律不只是用来保护咱们自己的。贝尔歇也要押去特拉。船长们开了一次大会，表决决定收回各自的蜡，单独出售给穆雷尔（Murell），但保留合作社的会籍。他们觉得，想从赫里特和贝尔歇的钱财里追讨回什么，就非得留在合作社里不可。奥斯卡·藤泽（Oscar Fujisawa）和塞萨里奥·维埃拉（Cesário Vieira）将乘卡纳维拉尔角号（*Cape Canaveral*）去特拉，提起诉讼，能追回多少追回多少，同时请愿给芬尼斯（Fenris）重新定级。奥斯卡搭下一班船回来，塞萨里奥则留下当合作社的代表。我猜他和琳达（Linda）也快结婚了。」

「可不是嘛。我猜他们俩都会留在特拉了。嘿，你瞧这事儿！塞萨里奥总算不用先死一道再投胎，也能离开芬尼斯了。」

他把那份午餐吃完了——就那么点东西，有什么算什么——我接过托盘，放到他椅子那头外的地板上。我找来烟灰缸，用那支大号打火机给他点了支烟，也给自己点了支。汤姆将信将疑地打量着那玩意儿，预言说早晚有一天我会按错机关，把自己「拜拜」掉几个钟头。我告诉他，比什是怎么使这打火机的。

===Original===
“Bet a lot of people wanted to hang him, too, before they found out who he was and what he’d really done. What’s my father think of Bish, now?”

“Bish Ware is a great and good man, and the savior of Fenris,” I said. “And he was real smart, to keep an act like that up for five years. Your father modestly admits that it even fooled him.”

“Bet Oscar Fujisawa knew it all along.”

“Well, Oscar modestly admits that he suspected something of the sort, but he didn’t feel it was his place to say anything.”

Tom laughed, and then wanted to know if they were going to hang Mort Hallstock. “I hope they wait till I can get out of here.”

===Chinese===
「我打赌，在他身份露馅、大家知道他究竟干了什么之前，想把他送上绞架的人也不少。我爸如今怎么看比什？」

「比什·韦尔是一位伟大而高尚的人，是芬尼斯的救星，」我说。「能把那出戏一口气演足五年，他也真是聪明绝顶。你父亲倒谦虚地承认，连他自己都让这出戏给骗过了。」

「我打赌奥斯卡·藤泽从头到尾就知情。」

「这个嘛，奥斯卡也谦虚地承认，他早疑心有那么回事，只是觉得轮不到他多嘴。」

汤姆笑了，接着又想知道，他们会不会把莫特·霍尔斯特克（Mort Hallstock）也送上绞架。「我盼着他们等我出了院再动手。」

===Original===
“No, Odin Dock & Shipyard claim he’s a political refugee and they won’t give him up. They did loan us a couple of accountants to go over the city books, to see if we could find any real evidence of misappropriation, and whattaya know, there were no city books. The city of Port Sandor didn’t keep books. We can’t even take that three hundred thousand sols away from him; for all we can prove, he saved them out of his five-thousand-sol-a-year salary. He’s shipping out on the *Cape Canaveral*, too.”

“Then we don’t have any government at all!”

“Are you fooling yourself we ever had one?”

“No, but—”

“Well, we have one now. A temporary dictatorship; Bish Ware is dictator. Fieschi loaned him Ranjit Singh and some of his men. The first thing he did was gather up the city treasurer and the chief of police and march them to the spaceport; Fieschi made Hallstock buy them tickets, too. But there aren’t going to be any unofficial hangings. This is a law-abiding planet, now.”

===Chinese===
「不会。奥丁船坞造船厂（Odin Dock & Shipyard）声称他是政治难民，不肯交人。他们倒借给我们两名会计，来查市政账目，看能不能找出挪用公款的实据——你猜怎么着，压根就没有市政账目。桑多尔港（Port Sandor）市里根本不记账。连那三十万索尔（sols），我们都没法从他手里追回来；就我们拿得出的证据而论，那笔钱还是他从一年五千索尔的薪水里省出来的呢。他也要搭卡纳维拉尔角号走。」

「那咱们岂不是压根儿就没有政府！」

「你还在骗自己，说咱们从前有过政府吗？」

「没骗自己，可是——」

「这个嘛，咱们如今有了。一个临时独裁政权；比什·韦尔就是独裁者。菲耶斯基（Fieschi）把手下的兰吉特·辛格（Ranjit Singh）和几个人借给了他。他干的头一件事，就是把市财政官和警察局长押去太空港；菲耶斯基还逼着霍尔斯特克给他们掏了船票钱。不过，谁也不会再被私下里绞死了。这颗行星如今是个守法之地。」

===Original===
A nurse came in, and disapproved of Tom smoking and of me being in the room at all.

“Haven’t you had your lunch yet?” she asked Tom.

He looked at her guilelessly and said, “No; I was waiting for it.”

“Well, I’ll get it,” she said. “I thought the other nurse had brought it.” She started out, and then she came back and had to fuss with his cushions, and then she saw the tray on the floor.

“You did so have your lunch!” she accused.

===Chinese===
一位护士走了进来，对汤姆抽烟不满，对我这个人居然待在病房里，就更加看不顺眼了。

「你的午饭还没吃吗？」她问汤姆。

他一脸无辜地望着她说：「没呢；我正等着呢。」

「那我去给你端来，」她说。「我还以为另一位护士已经送来了呢。」她往外走，又折回来非要把他的软垫摆弄摆弄，接着就看见了地板上的托盘。

「你明明吃过午饭了！」她控诉道。

===Original===
Tom looked at her as innocently as ever. “Oh, you mean these samples? Why, they were good; I’ll take all of them. And a big slab of roast beef, and brown gravy, and mashed potatoes. And how about some ice cream?”

It was a good try; too bad it didn’t work.

“Don’t worry, Tom,” I told him. “I’ll get my lawyer to spring you out of this jug, and then we’ll take you to my place and fill you up on Mrs. Laden’s cooking.”

The nurse sniffed. She suspected, quite correctly, that whoever Mrs. Laden was, she didn’t know anything about scientific dietetics.

===Chinese===
汤姆还是那副清白无辜的表情望着她。「哦，你是说这些样品啊？嗯，味道好得很；这些我全要。再来一大块烤牛肉、棕色肉汁和土豆泥。另外，来点冰淇淋怎么样？」

这一手耍得漂亮；可惜没成。

「别愁，汤姆，」我告诉他。「我会叫我的律师把你从这号子里捞出去，然后带你去我家，让莱登太太（Mrs. Laden）的手艺把你喂得饱饱的。」

护士嗤了一声。她相当有把握地怀疑：不管那位莱登太太是何方神圣，反正对科学配膳一窍不通。

---
===Original===
When I got back to the *Times*, Dad and Julio had had their lunch and were going over the teleprint edition. Julio was printing corrections on blank sheets of plastic and Dad was cutting them out and cementing them over things that needed correcting on the master sheets. I gave Julio a short item to the effect that Tom Kivelson, son of Captain and Mrs. Joe Kivelson, one of the *Javelin* survivors who had been burned in the tallow-wax fire, was now out of all danger, and recovering. Dad was able to scrounge that onto the first page.

There was a lot of other news. The T.F.N. destroyer *Simón Bolivar*, en route from Gimli to pick up the notorious Anton Gerrit, alias Steve Ravick, had come out of hyperspace and into radio range. Dad had talked to the skipper by screen and gotten interviews, which would be telecast, both with him and Detective-Major MacBride of the Colonial Constabulary. The *Simón Bolivar* would not make landing, but go into orbit and send down a boat. Detective-Major MacBride (alias Dr. John Watson) would remain on Fenris to take over local police activities.

More evidence had been unearthed at Hunters’ Hall on the frauds practiced by Leo Belsher and Gerrit-alias-Ravick; it looked as though a substantial sum of money might be recovered, eventually, from the bank accounts and other holdings of both men on Terra. Acting Resident-Agent Gonzalo Ware—Ware, it seemed, really was his right name, but look what he had in front of it—had promulgated more regulations and edicts, and a crackdown on the worst waterfront dives was in progress. I’ll bet the devoted flock was horrified at what their beloved bishop had turned into. Bish would leave his diocese in a lot healthier condition than he’d found it, that was one thing for sure. And most of the gang of thugs and plug-uglies who had been used to intimidate and control the Hunters’ Cooperative had been gathered up and jailed on vagrancy charges; prisoners were being put to work cleaning up the city.

And there was a lot about plans for a registration of voters, and organization of election boards, and a local electronics-engineering firm had been awarded a contract for voting machines. I didn’t think there had ever been a voting machine on Fenris before.

===Chinese===
我回到《时报》（*Times*）时，爸爸和胡利奥（Julio）已经吃过午饭，正在核对电传版（teleprint edition）。胡利奥在空白塑料纸上印出更正条，爸爸把它们剪下来，贴到母版上需要订正的地方。我给了胡利奥一条简讯，大意是：乔·基维尔森（Joe Kivelson）船长夫妇之子、在脂蜡（tallow-wax）大火中被烧伤的标枪号（*Javelin*）幸存船员之一汤姆·基维尔森，现已完全脱离危险，正在康复。爸爸施展手段，把它挤上了头版。

还有一大堆别的新闻。特拉联邦海军（T.F.N.）驱逐舰「西蒙·玻利瓦尔号」（*Simón Bolivar*）正从吉姆利（Gimli）赶来，提拿那个臭名昭著的安东·赫里特（Anton Gerrit）——化名史蒂夫·拉维克——已驶出超空间，进入无线电通话范围。爸爸通过屏幕跟舰长谈过，采得了两份将要上电视播出的访谈：一份来自舰长本人，一份来自殖民地宪兵队（Colonial Constabulary）的侦缉少校（Detective-Major）麦克布莱德（MacBride）。西蒙·玻利瓦尔号不作降落，只进入轨道，放一艘小艇下来。麦克布莱德侦缉少校（化名约翰·华生医生〔Dr. John Watson〕）将留在芬尼斯，接管本地警务。

猎手会堂（Hunters' Hall）又挖出了更多证据，证明利奥·贝尔歇和化名拉维克的赫里特所施的种种欺诈；看来，两人存在特拉的银行账户和其他财产，最终有望追回相当一笔钱款。代理联邦驻行星代理官贡萨洛·韦尔（Gonzalo Ware）——看来「韦尔」倒真是他的本姓，只是瞧瞧他在这姓前头挂的是个什么名号——又颁布了更多规章法令，对码头区几家最不像话的下等酒馆的整治正雷厉风行。我敢打赌，那群虔诚的羔羊眼见他们敬爱的主教变成了如今这般模样，准吓得不轻。有一点是板上钉钉的：比什交出的教区，会远比他接手时健康。还有，那帮一贯被用来威吓、操纵猎手合作社（Hunters' Cooperative）的暴徒和打手，大部分已被缉拿归案，以流浪罪名收监；囚犯们正被派去清扫全城。

还有许多关于选民登记计划、组建选举委员会的报道，本地一家电子工程公司还拿到了投票机的制造合同。我看芬尼斯以前连一台投票机都不曾有过。

===Original===
“The commander of the *Bolivar* says he’ll take your story to Terra with him, and see that it gets to Interworld News,” Dad told me as we were sorting the corrected master sheets and loading them into the photoprint machine, to be sent out on the air. “The *Bolivar*’ll make Terra at least two hundred hours ahead of the *Cape Canaveral*. Interworld will be glad to have it. It isn’t often they get a story like that with the first news of anything, and this’ll be a big story.”

“You shouldn’t have given me the exclusive by-line,” I said. “You did as much work on it as I did.”

“No, I didn’t, either,” he contradicted, “and I knew what I was doing.”

With the work done, I remembered that I hadn’t had anything to eat since breakfast, and I went down to take inventory of the refrigerator. Dad went along with me, and after I had assembled a lunch and sat down to it, he decided that his pipe needed refilling, lit it, poured a cup of coffee and sat down with me.

===Chinese===
「玻利瓦尔号舰长说，他愿意把你的报道随身带去特拉，保证送到星际新闻社（Interworld News），」爸爸告诉我；这时我们正把改好的母版分拣装进光印机（photoprint machine），准备随电波发送出去。「玻利瓦尔号抵达特拉，至少会比卡纳维拉尔角号早两百小时。星际新闻社拿到它准高兴。这样的报道还跟事件的第一手消息一并到手，他们可难得碰上；而这篇准是条重磅新闻。」

「你不该把独家署名给我，」我说。「这篇报道你下的功夫不比我少。」

「我才没有，」他反驳道，「何况我这么做是心里有数的。」

活儿干完，我才想起早饭之后还粒米未进，便下楼去盘点冰箱。爸爸跟了我去，等我拼凑出一份午餐坐下开吃，他认定烟斗该添烟丝了，装上点着，倒了杯咖啡，在我对面坐下。

===Original===
“You know, Walt, I’ve been thinking, lately,” he began.

*Oh-oh*, I thought. When Dad makes that remark, in just that tone, it’s all hands to secure ship for diving.

“We’ve all had to do a lot of thinking, lately,” I agreed.

“Yes. You know, they want me to be mayor of Port Sandor.”

I nodded and waited till I got my mouth empty. I could see a lot of sense in that. Dad is honest and scrupulous and public-spirited; too much so, sometimes, for his own good. There wasn’t any question of his ability, and while there had always been antagonism between the hunter-ship crews and waterfront people and the uptown business crowd, Dad was well liked and trusted by both parties.

===Chinese===
「你知道吧，沃尔特，我最近一直在琢磨一件事，」他开了腔。

*坏了*，我心想。每当爸爸用这种口气说这句话，那就等于吹响了「全体各就固船位、准备下潜」的号子。

「咱们大家最近都琢磨了不少事，」我附和道。

「是啊。你知道吧，他们想让我当桑多尔港市长。」

我点点头，等把嘴里的东西咽下去才开口。我看这事大有道理。爸爸为人诚实、一丝不苟、热心公事；有时候热心得过了头，反而不利于自己。他的能力毋庸置疑，而且虽说猎怪船船员和码头人同城里商界那帮人之间向来不睦，爸爸却两边都受人爱戴、被人信任。

===Original===
“Are you going to take it?” I asked.

“I suppose I’ll have to, if they really want me. Be a sort of obligation.”

That would throw a lot more work on me. Dad could give some attention to the paper as mayor, but not as much as now.

“What do you want me to try to handle for you?” I asked.

“Well, Walt, that’s what I’ve been thinking about,” he said. “I’ve been thinking about it for a long time, and particularly since things got changed around here. I think you ought to go to school some more.”

===Chinese===
「你打算接下吗？」我问。

「他们要是真要我，我想我推不掉。算是尽一份义务吧。」

那就会把更多的担子压到我身上。爸爸当了市长，也还能照应照应报社，只是不可能像现在这样倾注全力了。

「你想让我替你挑起哪一摊？」我问。

「这个嘛，沃尔特，我琢磨的正是这件事，」他说。「我琢磨很久了，尤其是自打这里的局面变了个样以后。我觉得，你该再去念点书。」

===Original===
That made me laugh. “What, back to Hartzenbosch?” I asked. “I could teach him more than he could teach me, now.”

“I doubt that, Walt. Professor Hartzenbosch may be an old maid in trousers, but he’s really a very sound scholar. But I wasn’t thinking about that. I was thinking about your going to Terra to school.”

“Huh?” I forgot to eat, for a moment. “Let’s stop kidding.”

“I didn’t start kidding; I meant it.”

“Well, think again, Dad. It costs money to go to school on Terra. It even costs money to go to Terra.”

===Chinese===
这话逗得我笑了。「什么，再回哈岑博施教授（Professor Hartzenbosch）那儿去念？」我问。「如今他能教我的，还没我能教他的多。」

「这话我可不信，沃尔特。哈岑博施教授也许是个穿长裤的老小姐，可他学问实在扎实。不过我想的不是这个。我想的是送你去特拉上学。」

「啊？」我一时忘了吃东西。「别开玩笑了。」

「玩笑可不是我先开的；我是当真的。」

「那你再好好想想，爸爸。上特拉念书是要花钱的。光去特拉一趟就得花钱。」

===Original===
“We have a little money, Walt. Maybe more than you think we do. And with things getting better, we’ll lease more teleprinters and get more advertising. You’re likely to get better than the price of your passage out of that story we’re sending off on the *Bolivar*, and that won’t be the end of it, either. Fenris is going to be in the news for a while. You may make some more money writing. That’s why I was careful to give you the by-line on that Gerrit story.” His pipe had gone out again; he took time out to relight it, and then added: “Anything I spend on this is an investment. The *Times* will get it back.”

“Yes, that’s another thing; the paper,” I said. “If you’re going to be mayor, you won’t be able to do everything you’re doing on the paper now, and then do all my work too.”

“Well, shocking as the idea may be, I think we can find somebody to replace you.”

“Name one,” I challenged.

===Chinese===
「咱们有一点钱，沃尔特。说不定比你想的多。等日子好起来，我们会租进更多电传印报机，拉来更多广告。咱们随玻利瓦尔号发出去的那篇报道，给你挣回来的多半不止一张船票钱，而且这还只是个开头。芬尼斯还得在新闻里火上一阵子。你写东西，兴许还能再挣几笔。所以我才特意在那篇赫里特的报道上给你署了名。」他的烟斗又灭了；他停下来重新点着，然后补了一句：「我在这个上面花的每一分钱都是投资。《时报》会连本带利收回来。」

「是啊，还有一档子事；报纸，」我说。「你要是当了市长，现在报社里这些活儿就顾不过来了，总不能再加上我这一摊吧。」

「呃，这想法兴许听着吓人，不过我想，我们能找个人来顶替你。」

「说出一个来，」我将了他一军。

===Original===
“Well, Lillian Arnaz, at the Library, has always been interested in newspaper work,” he began.

“A girl!” I hooted. “You have any idea of some of the places I have to go to get stories?”

“Yes. I have always deplored the necessity. But a great many of them have been closed lately, and the rest are being run in a much more seemly manner. And she wouldn’t be the only reporter. I hesitate to give you any better opinion of yourself than you have already, but it would take at least three people to do the work you’ve been doing. When you get back from Terra, you’ll find the *Times* will have a very respectable reportorial staff.”

“What’ll I be, then?” I wondered.

“Editor,” Dad told me. “I’ll retire and go into politics full time. And if Fenris is going to develop the way I believe it will, the editor of the *Times* will need a much better education than I have.”

===Chinese===
「这个嘛，图书馆（the Library）的莉莲·阿纳兹（Lillian Arnaz），一直对新闻这一行很有兴趣，」他开了腔。

「个姑娘家！」我嗤笑道。「你知不知道，为了跑新闻，有些什么地方是我不得不去的？」

「知道。对这份必要，我一向深表遗憾。不过那些地方近来关了一大批，剩下的也正经规矩多了。再说她也不会是唯一的记者。你的自我评价已经够高了，我本不想再往上抬，可你一个人干的那份活儿，起码得三个人才顶得下来。等你从特拉回来，会发现《时报》已经有了一支很体面的记者队伍。」

「那我到时候算什么？」我纳闷。

「主编，」爸爸告诉我。「我就退休，全职从政。而且，要是芬尼斯真朝我相信的那个方向蒸蒸日上，《时报》的主编，受的教育就得比我好得多才行。」

===Original===
I kept on eating, to give myself an excuse for silence. He was right, I knew that. But college on Terra; why, that would be at least four years, maybe five, and then a year for the round trip. …

“Walt, this doesn’t have to be settled right away,” Dad said. “You won’t be going on the *Simón Bolivar*, along with Ravick and Belsher. And that reminds me. Have you talked to Bish lately? He’d be hurt if you didn’t see him before he left.”

===Chinese===
我继续吃着，好给自己找个沉默的借口。他是对的，这我知道。可是上特拉念大学；那一去至少四年，兴许五年，再加上往返路程一年。……

「沃尔特，这事不必马上定下来，」爸爸说。「你又不用跟拉维克、贝尔歇一道乘西蒙·玻利瓦尔号走。对了，这倒提醒我了。你近来跟比什谈过吗？他动身之前你要是不去见见他，他会伤心的。」

---

===Original===
The truth was, I’d been avoiding Bish, and not just because I knew how busy he was. My face felt like a tallow-wax fire every time I thought of how I’d been trying to reform him, and I didn’t quite know what I’d be able to say to him if I met him again. And he seemed to me to be an entirely different person, as though the old Bish Ware, whom I had liked in spite of what I’d thought he was, had died, and some total stranger had taken his place.

But I went down to the Municipal Building. It didn’t look like the same place. The walls had been scrubbed; the floors were free from litter. All the drove of loafers and hangers-on had been run out, or maybe jailed and put to work. I looked into a couple of offices; everybody in them was busy. A few of the old police force were still there, but their uniforms had been cleaned and pressed, they had all shaved recently, and one or two looked as though they liked being able to respect themselves, for a change.

The girl at the desk in the mayor’s outside office told me Bish had a delegation of uptown merchants, who seemed to think that reform was all right in its place but it oughtn’t to be carried more than a few blocks above the waterfront. They were protesting the new sanitary regulations. Then she buzzed Bish on the handphone, and told me he’d see me in a few minutes. After a while, I heard the delegation going down the hall from the private office door. One of them was saying:

“Well, this is what we’ve always been screaming our heads off for. Now we’ve got it good and hard; we’ll just have to get used to it.”

===Chinese===
实情是，我一直在躲着比什，倒不只是因为我知道他忙。每当想起自己从前竟一门心思想要「改造」他，我的脸就烧得像一场脂蜡大火，而且真要再见到他，我也不知道该对他说些什么。在我眼里，他像是换了个人：仿佛那个旧日的比什·韦尔——不管我曾把他当成什么人，我终究喜欢过他——已经死了，顶替他的是一个彻头彻尾的陌生人。

可我还是去了市政大楼（Municipal Building）。那地方看起来已面目一新。墙壁擦洗过了；地板上再无杂物。那一大群闲汉食客被撵了个精光，要不就是蹲了号子被派去干活了。我朝几间办公室里望了望；里面人人都在忙。老警队的人还留着几个，可制服都浆洗熨平了，胡子也新刮过，有一两个看上去挺受用——难得换换样，总算能自尊自爱一回了。

市长外间办公室里坐在写字台后的姑娘告诉我，比什正在接待一批城里商界的代表。这帮人似乎觉得，改革放在它该在的地方未尝不可，只是不该越过码头区往上几个街区再往里推。他们是来抗议新卫生条例的。随后她用手持电话呼了比什，说他几分钟后见我。过了一会儿，我听见那批代表从里间办公室的门那边沿走廊离去，其中一个正说着：

「唉，这不正是咱们一直扯着嗓子嚷嚷要的东西嘛。如今结结实实领受到了；咱们也只好慢慢习惯了。」

===Original===
When I went in, Bish rose from his desk and came to meet me, shaking my hand. He looked and was dressed like the old Bish Ware I’d always known.

“Glad you dropped in, Walt. Find a seat. How are things on the *Times*?”

“You ought to know. You’re making things busy for us.”

“Yes. There’s so much to do, and so little time to do it. Seems as though I’ve heard somebody say that before.”

“Are you going back to Terra on the *Simón Bolivar*?”

===Chinese===
我进去时，比什从办公桌后站起身，迎上前来同我握手。他的模样和穿着，都还是我素来认识的那个老比什·韦尔。

「高兴你过来坐坐，沃尔特。找个位子。《时报》那边怎么样？」

「你还有什么不知道的。你正让咱们忙得团团转呢。」

「是啊。要办的事这么多，办事的时间又这么少。好像听谁这么说过来着。」

「你要搭西蒙·玻利瓦尔号回特拉吗？」

===Original===
“Oh, Allah forbid! I made a trip on a destroyer, once, and once is enough for a lifetime. I won’t even be able to go on the *Cape Canaveral*; I’ll take the *Peenemünde* when she gets in. I’m glad MacBride—Dr. Watson—is going to stop off. He’ll be a big help. Don’t know what I’d have done without Ranjit Singh.”

“That won’t be till after the *Cape Canaveral* gets back from Terra.”

“No. That’s why I’m waiting. Don’t publish this, Walt, I don’t want to start any premature rumors that might end in disappointments, but I’ve recommended immediate reclassification to Class III, and there may be a Colonial Office man on the *Cape Canaveral* when she gets in. Resident-Agent, permanent. I hope so; he’ll need a little breaking in.”

“I saw Tom Kivelson this morning,” I said. “He seems to be getting along pretty well.”

“Didn’t anybody at the hospital tell you about him?” Bish asked.

===Chinese===
「哦，真主可不容！驱逐舰我坐过一回，一辈子一回就够了。我连卡纳维拉尔角号都搭不上；等佩内明德号（*Peenemünde*）进港，我搭它走。我很高兴麦克布莱德——华生医生——要中途留下来。他会帮大忙的。真不知道没有兰吉特·辛格，我该怎么办。」

「那得等卡纳维拉尔号从特拉返航之后了。」

「是啊。所以我才等。这一条先别见报，沃尔特，我不想过早掀起什么到头来叫人空欢喜一场的谣言。不过我已经建议立即把芬尼斯重新划入第三级（Class III），等卡纳维拉尔号进港时，船上或许会来一位殖民部（Colonial Office）的人。联邦驻行星代理官，长期编制。我盼着如此；那位先生也得有个入门磨合的过程。」

「我今天上午去看过汤姆·基维尔森了，」我说。「他好像恢复得挺不错。」

「医院里的人没跟你说他的情况吗？」比什问。

===Original===
I shook my head. He cursed all hospital staffs.

“I wish military security was half as good. Why, Tom’s permanently injured. He won’t be crippled, or anything like that, but there was considerable unrepairable damage to his back muscles. He’ll be able to get around, but I doubt it he’ll ever be able to work on a hunter-ship again.”

I was really horrified. Monster-hunting was Tom’s whole life. I said something like that.

“He’ll just have to make a new life for himself. Joe says he’s going to send him to school on Terra. He thinks that was his own idea, but I suggested it to him.”

“Dad wants me to go to school on Terra.”

===Chinese===
我摇了摇头。他把全天下的医院职员骂了个遍。

「真希望军队的保密纪律有他们一半好。唉，汤姆落下终身损伤了。他不会残废，也不是那一类的事，可他背部肌肉有相当一部分损伤无法修复。走动是不成问题的，但能不能再上猎怪船干活，我看悬。」

这回我是真吓着了。猎怪就是汤姆的全部生命。我说了句差不多意思的话。

「他只好重新给自己造一个人生了。乔说，要送他去特拉上学。他当那是他自己的主意，其实是我在他耳朵边吹的风。」

「爸爸也想让我去特拉念书。」

===Original===
“Well, that’s a fine idea. Tom’s going on the *Peenemünde*, along with me. Why don’t you come with us?”

“That would be great, Bish. I’d like it. But I just can’t.”

“Why not?”

“Well, they want Dad to be mayor, and if he runs, they’ll all vote for him. He can’t handle this and the paper both alone.”

“He can get help on both jobs.”

“Yes, but… Why, it would be years till I got back. I can’t sacrifice the time. Not now.”

===Chinese===
「那敢情好。汤姆要搭佩内明德号走，跟我一道。你何不同我们一起？」

「那再好不过了，比什。我很乐意。可我就是走不开。」

「为什么走不开？」

「这个嘛，他们要爸爸当市长，他一出马，大家都会投他的票。市长和报社，他一个人两头顾不过来。」

「两份差事，他都可以雇人搭手嘛。」

「是啊，可是……你想，我一去就得好几年才回得来。这时间我豁不出去。现在不行。」

===Original===
“I’d say six years. You can spend your voyage time from here cramming for entrance qualifications. Schools don’t bother about academic credits any more; they’re only interested in how much you know. You take four years’ regular college, and a year postgrading, and you’ll have all the formal education you’ll need.”

“But, Bish, I can get that here, at the Library,” I said. “We have every book on film that’s been published since the Year Zero.”

“Yes. And you’d die of old age before you got a quarter through the first film bank, and you still wouldn’t have an education. Do you know which books to study, and which ones not to bother with? Or which ones to read first, so that what you read in the others will be comprehensible to you? That’s what they’ll give you on Terra. The tools, which you don’t have now, for educating yourself.”

I thought that over. It made sense. I’d had a lot of the very sort of trouble he’d spoken of, trying to get information for myself in proper order, and I’d read a lot of books that duplicated other books I’d read, and books I had trouble understanding because I hadn’t read some other book first. Bish had something there. I was sure he had. But six years!

===Chinese===
「我看是六年。从这儿去特拉的航程，正好拿来突击备考入学资格。如今的学校已经不讲究什么学分资历了；它们只关心你肚子里到底有多少货。你正经念四年大学，再进修一年研究生，需要的正规教育就全齐了。」

「可是，比什，这些东西我在图书馆也能学到，」我说。「自元年（Year Zero）以来出版的每一种书，咱们都有缩微胶片版。」

「是啊。可你把头一座胶片库啃完四分之一之前，就该老死了，到头来还是没受过教育。你知道哪些书该钻研、哪些不值一顾吗？知道该先读哪一本，读别的书时才看得懂吗？这些，正是特拉能给你的。是自我教育的工具——如今你手里没有的工具。」

我把这话掂量了一番。有道理。他说的那些难处，我自己就尝过一大堆：想按部就班地给自己汲取知识，却读了许多跟别的书内容重复的书，还有的书读不懂，就因为没先读另外哪一本。比什这话说到点子上了。我敢肯定说到了。可是，六年啊！

===Original===
I said that aloud, and added: “I can’t take the time. I have to be doing things.”

“You’ll do things. You’ll do them a lot better for waiting those six years. You aren’t eighteen yet. Six years is a whole third of your past life. No wonder it seems long to you. But you’re thinking the wrong way; you’re relating those six years to what has passed. Relate them to what’s ahead of you, and see how little time they are. You take ordinary care of yourself and keep out of any more civil wars, and you have sixty more years, at least. Your six years at school are only one-tenth of that. I was fifty when I came here to this Creator’s blunder of a planet. Say I had only twenty more years; I spent a quarter of them playing town drunk here. I’m the one who ought to be in a rush and howling about lost time, not you. I ought to be in such a hurry I’d take the *Simón Bolivar* to Terra and let this place go to—to anywhere you might imagine to be worse.”

===Chinese===
我把这句念叨出了声，又补上一句：「这时间我搭不起。我得干事情。」

「你会干成事的。先等上那六年，你能干得好得多。你还不到十八岁。六年是你过去整整三分之一的人生，怪不得你嫌长。可你这算法就不对了；你是拿那六年跟你已经过完的日子比。拿它们跟你前头的日子比比，看看有多短。你好好保重自己，别再搅和进什么内战，往后的日子起码还有六十年。你上学的六年，不过是其中的十分之一。我到这颗堪称造物主败笔的星球上来的时候，已经五十岁了。就算我只剩二十年吧；我拿其中四分之一的光阴，在这镇上扮醉汉。该火烧眉毛、哀叹虚度光阴的是我，不是你。我要是真那么急，就该搭西蒙·玻利瓦尔号去特拉，随这地方爱怎么着怎么着——随它去你想得到的随便哪个更糟的地方。」

===Original===
“You know, I don’t think you like Fenris.”

“I don’t. If I were a drinking man, this planet would have made a drunkard of me. Now, you forget about these six years chopped out of your busy life. When you get back here, with an education, you’ll be a kid of twenty-four, with a big long life ahead of you and your mind stocked with things you don’t have now that will help you make something—and more important, something enjoyable—out of it.”

===Chinese===
「说起来，我看你不喜欢芬尼斯。」

「是不喜欢。我若是个贪杯的人，这颗星球早把我灌成酒鬼了。好了，把你那忙碌人生里被剜走六年这档子事忘掉吧。等你受了教育回到这里，你才二十四岁，面前还铺着长长的整整一辈子，脑子里装满了你如今没有的东西，能帮着你把这一辈子做出点名堂——更要紧的是，做出点有意思的名堂来。」

---
===Original===
There was a huge crowd at the spaceport to see us off, Tom and Bish Ware and me. Mostly, it was for Bish. If I don’t find a monument to him when I get back, I’ll know there is no such thing as gratitude. There had been a big banquet for us the evening before, and I think Bish actually got a little tipsy.
