Nobody can be sure, though; it might have been just the old actor back in his role. Now they were all crowding around us, as many as could jam in, in the main lounge of the *Peenemünde*. Joe Kivelson and his wife. Dad and Julio and Mrs. Laden, who was actually being cordial to Bish, and who had a bundle for us that we weren’t to open till we were in hyperspace. Lillian Arnaz, the girl who was to take my place as star reporter. We were going to send each other audiovisuals; advice from me on the job, and news from the *Times* from her. Glenn Murell, who had his office open by now and was grumbling that there had been a man from Interstellar Import-Export out on the *Cape Canaveral*, and if the competition got any stiffer the price of tallow-wax would be forced up on him to a sol a pound. And all the *Javelin* hands who had been wrecked with us on Hermann Reuch’s Land, and the veterans of the Civil War, all but Oscar and Cesário, who will be at the dock to meet us when we get to Terra.

===Chinese===
太空港挤满了为我们送行的人——我、汤姆，还有比什·韦尔。主要是为比什送的。等我回来时要是见不到给他立的纪念碑，我就知道世上根本没有感恩这回事了。头天晚上为我们办了一场盛大的宴会，我想比什真有点喝高了。不过谁也说不准；没准儿只是老演员重新入了戏罢了。此刻他们全都围在我们身边，能塞进佩内明德号主休息室的都挤了进来。乔·基维尔森和他夫人；爸爸、胡利奥，还有莱登太太——她对比什居然一团和气，还给我们带了个包裹，叮嘱我们进了超空间才许拆开。莉莲·阿纳兹，即将接替我当王牌记者的姑娘。我们俩说好互相给对方寄声像节目：我给她传经，教她怎么跑新闻；她给我传递《时报》的消息。格伦·穆雷尔（Glenn Murell），事务所如今已经开张，正嘟嘟囔囔抱怨说，卡纳维拉尔号上来过一位星际进出口公司（Interstellar Import-Export）的人，竞争要是再这么加码，他收购脂蜡就得被抬到一索尔一磅了。还有同我们一起在赫尔曼·罗伊希之地遭难的标枪号全体船员，还有那场内战的老战友们——只少了奥斯卡和塞萨里奥，等我们抵达特拉时，他们会在码头上迎我们。

===Original===
I wonder what it’ll be like, on a world where you go to bed every time it gets dark and get up when it gets light, and can go outdoors all the time. I wonder how I’ll like college, and meeting people from all over the Federation, and swapping tall stories about our home planets.

And I wonder what I’ll learn. The long years ahead, I can’t imagine them now, will be spent on the *Times*, and I ought to learn things to fit me for that. But I can’t get rid of the idea about carniculture growth of tallow-wax. We’ll have to do something like that. The demand for the stuff is growing, and we don’t know how long it’ll be before the monsters are hunted out. We know how fast we’re killing them, but we don’t know how many there are or how fast they breed. I’ll talk to Tom about that; maybe between us we can hit on something, or at least lay a foundation for somebody else who will.

===Chinese===
我不知道那会是怎样一番光景：在一个天一黑就上床、天一亮就起身、随时随地都能到户外的世界上生活。也不知道我会不会喜欢上上大学，喜欢上结交来自联邦各地的人，跟他们互相吹嘘各自母星的奇闻。

也不知道我将学到些什么。往后那些漫长的年月——如今我还想象不出——都要花在《时报》上，我理应学些东西，好让自己配得上这份事业。可我总放不下用肉培法培育脂蜡这个念头。这一步，我们迟早总要走。这东西的需求正一天天看涨，而谁也说不好怪兽还能让我们猎多久。我们知道自己宰杀的速度，却不知道它们总数几何、繁殖多快。这事我要跟汤姆好好聊聊；兴许我们俩能碰出点名堂，再不济，也能给将来接手的什么人打个底子。

===Original===
The crowd pushed out and off the ship, and the three of us were alone, here in the lounge of the *Peenemünde*, where the story started and where it ends. Bish says no story ends, ever. He’s wrong. Stories die, and nothing in the world is deader than a dead news story. But before they do, they hatch a flock of little ones, and some of them grow into bigger stories still. What happens after the ship lifts into the darkness, with the pre-dawn glow in the east, will be another, a new, story.

But to the story of how the hunters got an honest cooperative and Fenris got an honest government, and Bish Ware got Anton Gerrit the slaver, I can write1

===Chinese===
人群陆续挤出船去，我们仨独自留了下来，就在佩内明德号这间主休息室里——这个故事从哪里开始，就在哪里收尾。比什说，故事从来不会终结。他错了。故事会死，而世上没有什么比一条寿终正寝的新闻更死气沉沉。不过临死之前，它们会孵出一窝小的，其中一些还会长成更大的故事。等飞船升入黑暗、东方泛起黎明前的微光之后发生的一切，将是另一个故事，一个全新的故事。

至于猎怪手们如何赢得了一个诚实的合作社、芬尼斯如何得到了一个诚实的政府、比什·韦尔又如何拿住了奴隶贩子安东·赫里特——这个故事，我可以写下——

## Main City Level / 主城层

===Original===
The ceiling on Main City Level is two hundred feet high; in order to permit free circulation of air and avoid traffic jams, nothing is built higher than a hundred and fifty feet except the square buildings, two hundred yards apart, which rest on foundations on the Bottom Level and extend up to support the roof. The *Times* has one of these pillar-buildings, and we have the whole thing to ourselves. In a city built for a quarter of a million, twenty thousand people don’t have to crowd very closely on one another. Naturally, we don’t have a top landing stage, but except for the buttresses at the corners and solid central column, the whole street floor is open.

Tom hadn’t said anything after we left the stacks of wax and the men guarding them. We came up a vehicle shaft a few blocks up Broadway, and he brought the jeep down and floated it in through one of the archways. As usual, the place was cluttered with equipment we hadn’t gotten around to repairing or installing, merchandise we’d taken in exchange for advertising, and vehicles, our own and everybody else’s. A couple of mechanics were tinkering on one of them. I decided, for the oomptieth time, to do something about cleaning it up. Say in another two or three hundred hours, when the ships would all be in port and work would be slack, and I could hire a couple of good men to help.

We got Murell’s stuff off the jeep, and I hunted around till I found a hand-lifter.

===Chinese===
主城层（Main City Level）的顶棚高两百英尺；为了让空气自由流通、避免交通拥堵，除了那种每隔两百码一座的方形建筑，任何楼宇都不得高过一百五十英尺——那些方楼坐落在底层的地基上，一路向上延伸，把屋顶撑住。《时报》（*Times*）便拥有这样一座柱楼，整栋都归我们独用。一座按二十五万人规模修起来的城，住着两万人，彼此大可不必挤得太紧。自然，我们没有顶层起降台，不过除了四角的扶壁和实心的中央立柱，整个临街楼面四面敞通。

离开那几垛蜡和看守的人之后，汤姆（Tom）一直没说话。我们顺着一条车行竖井升上来，出口在百老汇（Broadway）大街过去几个街区的地方，他把吉普降下来，飘进一道拱门。跟往常一样，这地方堆满了杂物：还没腾出手修理或安装的设备、拿广告版面换来的各色货物，还有车辆——我们自己家的和别人家的。两个机修工正在其中一辆上敲敲打打。我不知第多少次下定决心，要把这里收拾出个名堂来。比如说，再过两三百个小时，等船统统回港、活计松下来，我就能雇几个好帮手。

我们把穆雷尔（Murell）的行李从吉普上卸下来，我四下翻找，总算寻着一台手提升举器。

===Original===
“Want to stay and have dinner with us, Tom?” I asked.

“Uh?” It took him a second or so to realize what I’d said. “Why, no, thanks, Walt. I have to get back to the ship. Father wants to see me before the meeting.”

“How about you, Bish? Want to take potluck with us?”

“I shall be delighted,” he assured me.

Tom told us goodbye absentmindedly, lifted the jeep, and floated it out into the street. Bish and I watched him go; Bish looked as though he had wanted to say something and then thought better of it. We floated Murell’s stuff and mine over to the elevator beside the central column, and I ran it up to the editorial offices on the top floor.

===Chinese===
「汤姆，留下来跟我们一起吃晚饭吧？」我问。

「啊？」他愣了一两秒，才反应过来我说的是什么。「哦，不了，谢谢，沃尔特（Walt）。我得回船上去。开会之前，父亲要见我。」

「你呢，比什（Bish）？愿意跟我们一起吃顿便饭吗？」

「不胜荣幸，」他向我保证。

汤姆心不在焉地道了别，升起吉普，飘出到街上去了。我和比什目送他离开；比什的神情像是原本想说点什么，又改变了主意。我们把穆雷尔的行李连同我的东西飘运到中央立柱旁的电梯，我开着电梯直上顶楼的编辑室。

===Original===
We came out in a big room, half the area of the floor, full of worktables and radios and screens and photoprinting machines. Dad, as usual, was in a gray knee-length smock, with a pipe jutting out under his ragged mustache, and, as usual, he was stopping every minute or so to relight it. He was putting together the stuff I’d transmitted in for the audiovisual newscast. Over across the room, the rest of the *Times* staff, Julio Kubanoff, was sitting at the composing machine, his peg leg propped up and an earphone on, his fingers punching rapidly at the keyboard as he burned letters onto the white plastic sheet with ultraviolet rays for photographing. Julio was an old hunter-ship man who had lost a leg in an accident and taught himself his new trade. He still wore the beard, now white, that was practically the monster-hunters’ uniform.

“The stuff come in all right?” I asked Dad, letting down the lifter.

“Yes. What do you think of that fellow Belsher?” he asked. “Did you ever hear such an impudent string of lies in your life?” Then, out of the corner of his eye, he saw the lifter full of luggage, and saw somebody with me. “Mr. Murell? Please excuse me for a moment, till I get this blasted thing together straight.” Then he got the film spliced and the sound record matched, and looked up. “Why, Bish? Where’s Mr. Murell, Walt?”

“Mr. Murell has had his initiation to Fenris,” I said. “He got squirted by a tread-snail almost as soon as he got off the ship. They have him at the spaceport hospital; it’ll be 2400 before they get all the poison sweated out of him.”

===Chinese===
出来是一间大屋子，占了整层楼面的一半，摆满工作台、无线电、屏幕和照相印刷机。爸爸照例穿一件灰色及膝罩衫，乱蓬蓬的髭须底下撅着一杆烟斗，而且照例每隔一分钟左右就要停下来重新点一回。他正在剪辑我传送回去的素材，准备声像新闻节目。屋子另一头，《时报》编辑部的其余全班人马——胡利奥·库巴诺夫（Julio Kubanoff）——正坐在排字机前，假腿架着，耳朵上挂一只耳机，手指在键盘上飞快敲击，用紫外线把字母烧印到白色塑料版材上，供拍照制版。胡利奥是猎怪船上的老人手，一次事故里丢了一条腿，自学转了这一行。他至今还蓄着那把如今已经全白的胡子——这种胡子几乎就是猎怪手的制服。

「素材都传到了吧？」我问爸爸，一面把升举器放下来。

「到了。你觉得贝尔歇（Belsher）那家伙怎么样？」他问。「你这辈子听过这么一串厚颜无耻的谎话没有？」接着，他用眼角余光瞥见升举器上堆满行李，又看见我身边有人。「穆雷尔先生？请稍等片刻，容我把这该死的东西接顺了再说。」随后他把胶片接好、对准音轨，才抬起头来。「咦，是比什？沃尔特，穆雷尔先生人呢？」

「穆雷尔先生已经领教过芬尼斯（Fenris）的入门礼了，」我说。「他差不多刚下船，就叫一条履带蜗牛喷了一家伙。他们把他安置在太空港医院；要把他身上的毒全发出来，怎么也得熬到 2400。」

===Original===
I went on to tell him what had happened. Dad’s eyes widened slightly, and he took the pipe out of his mouth and looked at Bish with something very reasonably like respect.

“That was mighty sharp work,” he said. “If you’d been a second slower, we’d be all out of visiting authors. That would have been a nice business; story would have gotten back to Terra, and been most unfortunate publicity for Fenris. And, of course,” he afterthoughted, “most unfortunate for Mr. Murell, too.”

“Well, if you give this any publicity, I would rather you passed my own trifling exploit over in silence,” Bish said. “I gather the spaceport people wouldn’t be too happy about giving the public the impression that their area is teeming with tread-snails, either. They have enough trouble hiring shipping-floor help as it is.”

“But don’t you want people to know what you did?” Dad demanded, incredulously. Everybody wanted their names in print or on ’cast; that was one of his basic articles of faith. “If the public learned about this—” he went on, and then saw where he was heading and pulled up short. It wouldn’t be tactful to say something like, “Maybe they wouldn’t think you were just a worthless old soak.”

===Chinese===
我接着把事情的经过讲给他听。爸爸的眼睛微微睁大，把烟斗从嘴里拿出来，用一种完全称得上敬意的神情望着比什。

「干得真利索，」他说。「你再慢上一秒，我们来访作家的存货就要绝版了。那可就热闹了：消息传回特拉（Terra），对芬尼斯来说将是最不幸的宣传。当然，」他又补上一句，「对穆雷尔先生来说也是最不幸的。」

「这个嘛，这事你们要公开报道的话，我宁愿把我那点微不足道的小小壮举默默略过，」比什说。「依我看，太空港那边的人也不乐意让公众觉得他们那片地界到处爬满履带蜗牛——他们如今招募装卸工就已经够头疼的了。」

「可你难道不想让人们知道你干的事吗？」爸爸难以置信地质问。谁都想让自己的名字见报、上广播——这是他的一条基本信条。「要是公众知道了这件事——」他继续往下说，随即看清了这话要说到哪里去，猛地刹住了。像「没准儿人家就不会觉得你不过是个一无是处的老酒鬼了」这样的话，说出来未免太不知趣。

===Original===
Bish saw where Dad was heading, too, but he just smiled, as though he were about to confer his episcopal blessing.

“Ah, but that would be a step out of character for me,” he said. “I must not confuse my public. Just as a favor to me, Ralph, say nothing about it.”

“Well, if you’d rather I didn’t. … Are you going to cover this meeting at Hunters’ Hall, tonight, Walt?” he asked me.

“Would I miss it?”

===Chinese===
比什也看出了爸爸这话的走向，却只是微微一笑，仿佛正要赐下他主教级的祝福。

「啊，可那样就有失我的本色了，」他说。「我可不能让公众对我的印象混淆起来。就算帮我个忙，拉尔夫（Ralph），这事只字别提。」

「好吧，既然你不愿意我提。……沃尔特，今晚猎手会堂（Hunters' Hall）的那个会，你打算去采访吗？」他问我。

「我能错过吗？」

===Original===
He frowned. “I could handle that myself,” he said. “I’m afraid this meeting’s going to get a little rough.”

I shook my head. “Let’s face it, Dad,” I said. “I’m a little short of eighteen, but you’re sixty. I can see things coming better than you can, and dodge them quicker.”

Dad gave a rueful little laugh and looked at Bish.

“See how it goes?” he asked. “We spend our lives shielding our young and then, all of a sudden, we find they’re shielding us.” His pipe had gone out again and he relit it. “Too bad you didn’t get an audiovisual of Belsher making that idiotic statement.”

===Chinese===
他皱起眉头。「我一个人就应付得了，」他说。「我怕今晚这个会要闹得有点凶。」

我摇摇头。「面对现实吧，爸爸，」我说。「我还差一点满十八，可你都六十了。扑过来的东西，我看得比你准，躲得比你快。」

爸爸苦笑了一声，望向比什。

「瞧见这世道了吧？」他问。「我们操劳一辈子护着小的，冷不丁却发现，他们反过来护起我们来了。」他的烟斗又灭了，重新点上。「可惜你没把贝尔歇说那句蠢话录成声像节目。」

===Original===
“He didn’t even know I was getting a voice-only. All the time he was talking, I was doodling in a pad with a pencil.”

“Synthetic substitutes!” Dad snorted. “Putting a synthetic tallow-wax molecule together would be like trying to build a spaceship with a jackknife and a tack hammer.” He puffed hard on his pipe, and then excused himself and went back to his work.

===Chinese===
「他根本不知道我在录纯音频。他讲话的整段时间里，我都在拿支铅笔在小本子上涂鸦。」

「合成代用品！」爸爸嗤了一声。「要拼出一个合成的脂蜡分子，就好比想用一把折刀加一把小钉锤造出一艘飞船来。」他猛吸了几口烟斗，随后告了个罪，回到自己的活计上去了。

---

===Original===
Editing an audiovisual telecast is pretty much a one-man job. Bish wanted to know if he could be of assistance, but there was nothing either of us could do, except sit by and watch and listen. Dad handled the Belsher thing by making a film of himself playing off the recording, and interjecting sarcastic comments from time to time. When it went on the air, I thought, Ravick wasn’t going to like it. I would have to start wearing my pistol again. Then he made a tape on the landing of the *Peenemünde* and the arrival of Murell, who he said had met with a slight accident after leaving the ship. I took that over to Julio when Dad was finished, along with a tape on the announced tallow-wax price cut. Julio only grunted and pushed them aside. He was setting up the story of the fight in Martian Joe’s—a “local bar,” of course; nobody ever gets shot or stabbed or slashed or slugged in anything else. All the news *is* fit to print, sure, but you can’t give your advertisers and teleprinter customers any worse name than they have already. A paper has to use some judgment.

===Chinese===
剪辑一档声像新闻节目，基本上是单人的活计。比什问能不能帮上什么忙，可我们俩谁都插不上手，只能坐在旁边看着、听着。爸爸处理贝尔歇那段素材的办法，是拍一段他本人边放录音、边插入冷嘲热讽点评的影片。我心想，这节目一播出去，拉维克（Ravick）是不会高兴的。我又得开始随身带枪了。随后他又做了一盘带子，讲佩内明德号（*Peenemünde*）降落和穆雷尔抵达——照他的说法，此人离船之后出了点小事故。爸爸弄完，我就把那盘带子连同宣布脂蜡降价的带子一起拿给胡利奥。胡利奥只哼了一声，把它们推到一旁。他正在排「火星乔」酒吧（Martian Joe's）斗殴那条新闻——当然，得写成「本地一家酒吧」；在任何别的地方，从来没人中过枪、挨过捅、挂过彩、吃过闷棍。没错，凡是新闻确实*都*宜刊登，可你总不能给自己的广告主和电传机订户安上比他们现有名声更臭的名声。办报总得有点分寸。

===Original===
Then Dad and Bish and I went down to dinner. Julio would have his a little later, not because we’re too good to eat with the help but because, around 1830, the help is too busy setting up the next paper to eat with us. The dining room, which is also the library, living room, and general congregating and loafing place, is as big as the editorial room above. Originally, it was an office, at a time when a lot of Fenris Company office work was being done here. Some of the furniture is original, and some was made for us by local cabinetmakers out of native hardwood. The dining table, big enough for two ships’ crews to eat at, is an example of the latter. Then, of course, there are screens and microbook cabinets and things like that, and a refrigerator to save going a couple of hundred feet to the pantry in case anybody wants a snack.

I went to that and opened it, and got out a bulb of concentrated fruit juice and a bottle of carbonated water. Dad, who seldom drinks, keeps a few bottles around for guests. Seems most of our “guests” part with information easier if they have something like the locally made hydroponic potato schnapps inside them for courage.

“You drink Baldur honey-rum, don’t you, Bish?” he said, pawing among the bottles in the liquor cabinet next to the refrigerator. “I’m sure I have a bottle of it. Now wait a minute; it’s here somewhere.”

===Chinese===
随后爸爸、比什和我下楼去吃晚饭。胡利奥的那份要晚一点吃，倒不是因为我们架子太大、不屑与雇员同席，而是因为一到 1830 前后，雇员们正忙着排下一期报纸，腾不出空跟我们一块儿吃。这间饭厅兼作书房、客厅和大伙儿聚堆闲待的地方，跟楼上编辑室一样大。它起初是间办公室，当年芬尼斯公司（Fenris Company）的好些文牍事务就在这里办理。家具有些是原物，有些是本地木匠用本地硬木给我们打的。那张坐得下两船船员进餐的大餐桌，便属于后者的例子。此外自然还有屏幕、微缩书柜之类的物件，外加一台冰箱——省得谁想垫垫肚子，还得跑上一两百英尺去配膳室。

我走过去打开冰箱，取出一小瓶浓缩果汁和一瓶碳酸水。爸爸难得喝酒，却为客人备着几瓶。似乎我们的多数「客人」，肚子里灌上一点本地酿的水培马铃薯烧酒（schnapps）壮胆，吐露起消息来才痛快。

「比什，你喝的是巴尔德蜂蜜朗姆酒（Baldur honey-rum），对吧？」他说着，在冰箱旁的酒柜里翻拣。「我肯定存着一瓶。且慢；就在这儿哪个地方。」

===Original===
When Dad passes on and some medium claims to have produced a spirit communication from him, I will not accept it as genuine without the expression: “Now wait a minute; it’s here somewhere.”

Bish wanted to know what I was fixing for myself, and I told him.

“Never mind the rum, Ralph. I believe,” he said, “that I shall join Walt in a fruit fizz.”

Well, whattaya know! Maybe my stealthy temperance campaign was having results. Dad looked positively startled, and then replaced the bottle he was holding.

===Chinese===
等爸爸百年之后，若真有哪个灵媒声称招来了他的亡灵讯示，其中要是少了「且慢；就在这儿哪个地方」这句口头禅，我是不会认作真迹的。

比什想知道我在给自己调什么，我告诉了他。

「朗姆酒就免了，拉尔夫。我想，」他说，「我就陪沃尔特一起喝杯果子汽水（fruit fizz）吧。」

嘿，你瞧！没准儿我那场悄无声息的禁酒运动真见了成效。爸爸当真吓了一跳，随即把他手里那瓶放了回去。

===Original===
“I believe I’ll make it unanimous,” he said. “Fix me up a fruit fizz, too, Walt.”

I mixed two more fruit fizzes, and we carried them over to the table. Bish sipped at his critically.

“Palatable,” he pronounced it. “Just a trifle on the mild side, but definitely palatable.”

===Chinese===
「那我就凑成全票一致，」他说。「沃尔特，也给我调一杯果子汽水。」

我又调了两杯果子汽水，我们端到桌边。比什挑剔地抿着自己那杯。

「尚属可口，」他品评道。「只是稍嫌淡了些，不过确确实实可口。」

===Original===
Dad looked at him as though he still couldn’t believe the whole thing. Dinner was slow coming. We finished our fizzes, and Bish and I both wanted repeats, and Dad felt that he had to go along. So I made three more. We were finishing them when Mrs. Laden started bringing in the dinner. Mrs. Laden is a widow; she has been with us since my mother died, the year after I was born. She is violently anti-liquor. Reluctantly, she condones Dad taking a snort now and then, but as soon as she saw Bish Ware, her face started to stiffen.

===Chinese===
爸爸望着他，仿佛还是不敢相信这整个儿的事。晚饭迟迟不上。我们喝完汽水，比什和我都要再来一杯，爸爸觉得只好奉陪。于是我又调了三杯。我们快喝完的时候，莱登太太（Mrs. Laden）开始上晚饭。莱登太太是位寡妇，从我母亲去世起就在我家操持——那是我出生后的第二年。她对酒深恶痛绝。爸爸偶尔抿上一小口，她还勉强睁一只眼闭一只眼，可她一看见比什·韦尔，脸色便开始板起来。

===Original===
She put the soup on the table and took off for the kitchen. She always has her own dinner with Julio. That way, while they’re eating he can tell her all the news that’s fit to print, and all the gossip that isn’t.

For the moment, the odd things I’d been noticing about our distinguished and temporarily incapacitated visitor came under the latter head. I told Dad and Bish about my observations, beginning with the deafening silence about Glenn Murell at the library. Dad began popping immediately.

“Why, he must be an impostor!” he exclaimed. “What kind of a racket do you think he’s up to?”

===Chinese===
她把汤端上桌，便径自回厨房去了。她总是和胡利奥一起吃自己那份晚饭。这样一来，两人吃着饭，他就既可以把凡是宜于刊登的新闻讲给她听，也可以把所有不宜刊登的八卦一一道来。

眼下，我在我们这位声名显赫、暂时又动弹不得的访客身上注意到的种种蹊跷，便属于后一类。我把自己的观察讲给爸爸和比什听，从图书馆里关于格伦·穆雷尔（Glenn Murell）那震耳欲聋的一片死寂说起。爸爸立刻就炸了。

「哎呀，他准是个冒名顶替的骗子！」他叫道。「你猜他这是在搞什么鬼名堂？」

===Original===
“Mmm-mm; I wouldn’t say that, not right away,” Bish said. “In the first place, Murell may be his true name and he may publish under a *nom de plume*. I admit, some of the other items are a little suspicious, but even if he isn’t an author, he may have some legitimate business here and, having heard a few stories about this planetary Elysium, he may be exercising a little caution. Walt, tell your father about that tallow-wax we saw, down in Bottom Level Fourth Ward.”

I did, and while I was talking Dad sat with his soup spoon poised halfway to his mouth for at least a minute before he remembered he was holding it.

“Now, that is funny,” he said when I was through. “Why do you suppose … ?”

===Chinese===
「唔唔；这话我还不忙着说死，」比什说。「首先，穆雷尔可能就是他的本名，出书用的兴许是个笔名（nom de plume）。我承认，其余几处是有点可疑；可就算他不是作家，他来这儿也可能有正经生意，又听了不少关于这座行星极乐土（Elysium）的传闻，行事谨慎些也在情理之中。沃尔特，把咱们在底层第四区见到的那批脂蜡，讲给你父亲听听。」

我讲了。我说着的时候，爸爸举着汤匙停在嘴边半空，足足有一分多钟，才想起来手里还握着它。

「唔，这可就有意思了，」我讲完了他说。「你琢磨琢磨，这是为什么……？」

===Original===
“Somebody,” Bish said, “some group of ship captains, is holding wax out from the Cooperative. There’s no other outlet for it, so my guess is that they’re holding it for a rise in price. There’s only one way that could happen, and that, literally, would be over Steve Ravick’s dead body. It could be that they expect Steve’s dead body to be around for a price rise to come in over.”

I was expecting Dad to begin spouting law-and-order. Instead, he hit the table with his fist; not, fortunately, the one that was holding the soup spoon.

“Well, I hope so! And if they do it before the *Cape Canaveral* gets in, they may fix Leo Belsher, too, and then, in the general excitement, somebody might clobber Mort Hallstock, and that’d be grand slam. After the triple funeral, we could go to work on setting up an honest cooperative and an honest government.”

===Chinese===
「某个人，」比什说，「某伙船长，正把蜡扣下来不交合作社。这批蜡没有别的出路，所以我猜，他们是在囤着等涨价。而涨价只有一种可能——不夸张地说，非得踏过史蒂夫·拉维克（Steve Ravick）的尸体不可。也很可能，他们正巴望史蒂夫的尸体横在那儿，好让涨价的行情从上头碾进来。」

我还以为爸爸要开始高谈法律与秩序。谁知他一拳砸在桌子上；所幸砸的不是握汤匙的那只手。

「嘿，我还真盼着呢！要是他们赶在卡纳维拉尔角号（*Cape Canaveral*）进港之前动手，兴许连利奥·贝尔歇（Leo Belsher）也一并料理了；然后趁着一场大乱，没准再有谁撂倒莫特·霍尔斯特克（Mort Hallstock），那就是大满贯了。等三场葬礼办完，我们就可以着手建立一个正经的合作社、一个正经的政府。」

===Original===
“Well, I never expected to hear you advocating lynch law, Dad,” I said.

He looked at me for a few seconds.

“Tell the truth, Walt, neither did I,” he admitted. “Lynch law is a horrible thing; don’t make any mistake about that. But there’s one thing more horrible, and that’s no law at all. And that is the present situation in Port Sandor.

===Chinese===
「哎呀，我可从没想到会听你鼓吹私刑，爸爸，」我说。

他盯着我看了几秒钟。

「说实在的，沃尔特，我自己也没想到，」他承认道。「私刑是可怕的东西；这一点千万别弄错。可还有一样更可怕的，那就是完全无法无天。而那正是桑多尔港（Port Sandor）眼下的局面。

===Original===
“You know what the trouble is, here? We have no government. No legal government, anyhow; no government under Federation law. We don’t even have a Federation Resident-Agent. Before the Fenris Company went broke, it was the government here; when the Space Navy evacuated the colonists, they evacuated the government along with them. The thousand who remained were all too busy keeping alive to worry about that. They didn’t even care when Fenris was reclassified from Class III, uninhabited but inhabitable, to Class II, inhabitable only in artificial environment, like Mercury or Titan. And when Mort Hallstock got hold of the town-meeting pseudo government they put together fifty years ago and turned it into a dictatorship, nobody realized what had happened till it was too late. Lynch law’s the only recourse we have.”

===Chinese===
「你知道这儿的症结在哪儿吗？我们没有政府。不管怎么说，没有合法政府；没有一部依联邦法成立的政府。我们甚至连个联邦驻行星代理官（Resident-Agent）都没有。芬尼斯公司破产之前，它就是这里的政府；太空海军（Space Navy）撤走殖民者的时候，连政府也一并撤走了。留下来的那一千号人全都忙着保命，顾不上操心这个。芬尼斯从第三类行星——无人定居但宜于定居——改划成第二类——只能靠人工环境居住，跟水星或泰坦一样——的时候，他们连关心都懒得关心。等到莫特·霍尔斯特克攫取了五十年前大家拼凑起来的镇民大会式伪政府、把它变成独裁统治，谁都没觉察出了什么事，等觉察过来已经太迟了。私刑是我们仅剩的出路。」

===Original===
“Ralph,” Bish told him, “if anything like that starts, Belsher and Hallstock and Ravick won’t be the only casualties. Between Ravick’s goons and Hallstock’s police, they have close to a hundred men. I won’t deny that they could be cleaned out, but it wouldn’t be a lynching. It would be a civil war.”

“Well, that’s swell!” Dad said. “The Federation Government has never paid us any attention; the Federation planets are scattered over too many million cubic light-years of space for the Government to run around to all of them wiping everybody’s noses. As long as things are quiet here, they’ll continue to do nothing for us. But let a story hit the big papers on Terra, *Revolution Breaks Out on Fenris*—and that’ll be the story I’ll send to Interworld News—and watch what happens.”

===Chinese===
「拉尔夫，」比什对他说，「真要起了那样的头，遭殃的绝不会只有贝尔歇、霍尔斯特克和拉维克几个。拉维克的打手加上霍尔斯特克的警察，他们手里有将近一百号人。我不否认这帮人可以被清算干净，可那就不是私刑了。那是内战。」

「嘿，那才叫好呢！」爸爸说。「联邦政府从来懒得搭理我们；联邦各行星散布在几百万立方光年的太空里，政府可没工夫挨个跑过去给每个人擦鼻涕。只要这儿风平浪静，他们就将继续对我们无所作为。可只要有一条新闻登上特拉的大报——《革命在芬尼斯爆发》（*Revolution Breaks Out on Fenris*）——那条新闻我就会发给星际新闻社（Interworld News）——然后就瞧好吧。」

===Original===
“I will tell you what will happen,” Bish Ware said. “A lot of people will get killed. That isn’t important, in itself. People are getting killed all the time, in a lot worse causes. But these people will all have friends and relatives who will take it up for them. Start killing people here in a faction fight, and somebody will be shooting somebody in the back out of a dark passage a hundred years from now over it. You want this planet poisoned with blood feuds for the next century?”

Dad and I looked at one another. That was something that hadn’t occurred to either of us, and it should have. There were feuds, even now. Half the little settlements on the other islands and on the mainland had started when some group or family moved out of Port Sandor because of the enmity of some larger and more powerful group or family, and half our shootings and knife fights grew out of old grudges between families or hunting crews.

===Chinese===
「我来告诉你会发生什么，」比什·韦尔说。「会有许许多多的人送命。这本身倒不重要。人本来就时时刻刻在送命，为的还是糟糕得多的缘由。可这些死者个个都有亲朋好友，会替他们把仇接下去。今天在这儿的一场派系火并里开了杀戒，一百年后还会有人为此从阴暗的过道里朝人背后打黑枪。你想让这颗行星被血仇毒害下一个世纪吗？」

爸爸和我面面相觑。这是我们俩谁都没想到的，而本来早该想到。仇隙如今就有。其他岛屿和大陆上那些小小的聚居点，有一半是这样来的：某些团伙或家族因为更大、更强的团伙或家族的敌意，迁出了桑多尔港；而本地的枪击和持刀斗殴，也有一半滋生于家族之间或猎怪班组之间的旧怨。

===Original===
“We don’t want it poisoned for the next century with the sort of thing Mort Hallstock and Steve Ravick started here, either,” Dad said.

“Granted.” Bish nodded. “If a civil war’s the only possible way to get rid of them, that’s what you’ll have to have, I suppose. Only make sure you don’t leave a single one of them alive when it’s over. But if you can get the Federation Government in here to clean the mess up, that would be better. Nobody starts a vendetta with the Terran Federation.”

“But how?” Dad asked. “I’ve sent story after story off about crime and corruption on Fenris. They all get the file-and-forget treatment.”

===Chinese===
「我们同样不想让下一个百年，被莫特·霍尔斯特克和史蒂夫·拉维克在这里挑起的那类东西毒化，」爸爸说。

「这话在理。」比什点头。「倘若内战是除掉他们的唯一办法，那你们恐怕也只好打一场。只是务必做到：等一切结束，别给他们留一个活口。不过，要是能把联邦政府请进来收拾这个烂摊子，那就更好了。谁也不敢同特拉联邦（Terran Federation）结世仇。」

「可怎么请？」爸爸问。「关于芬尼斯的犯罪和腐败，我一篇接一篇发过报道。它们统统落得个归档了事的下场。」

===Original===
Mrs. Laden had taken away the soup plates and brought us our main course. Bish sat toying with his fork for a moment.

“I don’t know what you can do,” he said slowly. “If you can stall off the blowup till the *Cape Canaveral* gets in, and you can send somebody to Terra. …”

All of a sudden, it hit me. Here was something that would give Bish a purpose; something to make him want to stay sober.

“Well, don’t say, ‘If *you* can,’ ” I said. “Say, ‘If *we* can.’ You live on Fenris, too, don’t you?”

===Chinese===
莱登太太撤走汤盘，给我们端上了主菜。比什坐着，一时摆弄着手里的餐叉。

「我不知道你们能做什么，」他缓缓说道。「如果你们能把这场爆乱拖到卡纳维拉尔角号进港之后，又能派个人去特拉……」

突然，我灵光一闪。这不正是能赋予比什一个人生目标的东西吗——一个能让他愿意保持清醒的东西。

「哎，可别说什么『要是*你们*能』，」我说。「得说『要是*我们*能』。你不也住在芬尼斯吗？」

## Masks Off / 卸下面具

===Original===
There was dead silence for two or three seconds. If a kitten had sneezed, everybody would have heard it. Then it started, first an inarticulate roar, and then a babel of unprintabilities. I thought I'd heard some bad language from these same men in this room when Leo Belsher's announcement of the price cut had been telecast, but that was prayer meeting to this. Dad was still talking. At least, I saw his lips move in the screen.

"Say that again, Ralph," Oscar Fujisawa shouted.

Dad must have heard him. At least, his lips moved again, but I wasn't a lip reader and neither was Oscar. Oscar turned to the mob—by now, it was that, pure and simple—and roared, in a voice like a foghorn, "**Shut up and listen!**" A few of those closest to him heard him. The rest kept on shouting curses. Oscar waited a second, and then pointed his submachine gun at the ceiling and hammered off the whole clip.

===Chinese===
屋里死寂了两三秒钟。这时候哪怕有只小猫打个喷嚏，人人都听得见。随后就炸开了：先是一声不成腔调的怒吼，接着便是一片没法见诸文字的污言秽语。我还以为，利奥·贝尔歇（Leo Belsher）宣布降价的通告电视播出那天，我已经领教过这屋里同一批人的脏话，可跟眼前这一比，那简直算得上祈祷会了。爸爸还在讲话。至少，我看见屏幕上他的嘴唇在动。

「拉尔夫（Ralph），再说一遍，」奥斯卡·藤泽（Oscar Fujisawa）大声喊道。

爸爸准是听见了。至少，他的嘴唇又动了起来，可我不会读唇，奥斯卡也不会。奥斯卡转向那群暴民——事到如今，这就纯粹是一群暴民了——用雾笛般的嗓音吼道：「**闭嘴，听着！**」离他最近的几个人听到了他的话。其余的人还在破口大骂。奥斯卡等了一秒，然后举起冲锋枪对准天花板，一口气打光了整匣子弹。

===Original===
"Shut up, a couple of hundred of you, and listen!" he commanded, on the heels of the blast. Then he turned to the screen again. "Now, Ralph; what was it you were saying?"

"Hallstock got to the spaceport about half an hour ago," Dad said. "He bought a ticket to Terra. Sigurd Ngozori's here; he called the bank and one of the clerks there told him that Hallstock had checked out his whole account, around three hundred thousand sols. Took some of it in cash and the rest in Banking Cartel drafts. Murell says that his information is that Bish Ware, Steve Ravick and Leo Belsher arrived earlier, about an hour ago. He didn't see them himself, but he talked with spaceport workmen who did."

The men who had crowded up to the screen seemed to have run out of oaths and obscenities now. Oscar was fitting another clip into his submachine gun.

===Chinese===
枪声余音未落，他随即下令：「你们这几百号人，都给我闭嘴，听着！」说完他又转向屏幕。「好了，拉尔夫；你刚才在说什么？」

「莫特·霍尔斯特克（Mort Hallstock）大约半个钟头前到了太空港，」爸爸说。「他买了一张去特拉（Terra）的船票。西格德·恩戈佐里（Sigurd Ngozori）就在我这儿；他给银行打了电话，那边一个职员告诉他，霍尔斯特克把整个账户都提清了，三十万索尔上下。一部分提了现金，其余开成银行卡特尔（Banking Cartel）的汇票。穆雷尔（Murell）说，据他得到的消息，比什·韦尔（Bish Ware）、史蒂夫·拉维克（Steve Ravick）和利奥·贝尔歇到得早些，大概一个钟头前。他本人没有亲眼见到，不过他跟见着的太空港工人谈过。」

刚才涌到屏幕跟前的人们，这会儿似乎把咒骂的词儿都用尽了。奥斯卡正往冲锋枪里压另一匣子弹。

===Original===
"Well, we'll have to go to the spaceport and get them," he said. "And take four ropes instead of three."

"You'll have to fight your way in," Dad told him. "Odin Dock & Shipyard won't let you take people out of their spaceport without a fight. They've all bought tickets by now, and Fieschi will have to protect them."

"Then we'll kick the *blankety-blank* spaceport apart," somebody shouted.

That started it up again. Oscar wondered if getting silence was worth another clip of cartridges, and decided it wasn't. He managed to make himself heard without it.

===Chinese===
「好吧，那我们就得杀上太空港去揪人，」他说。「绳子也得多带一条——带四条，不是三条。」

「那你们就得一路打进去了，」爸爸告诉他。「奥丁船坞造船厂（Odin Dock & Shipyard）绝不会让你们不费一枪一弹就把人从他们的太空港里带走。那几个人如今都买了船票，菲耶斯基（Fieschi）就必须保护他们。」

「那咱们就把那个*混账的*太空港拆个稀巴烂，」有人喊道。

这一嗓子又把炸窝点着了。奥斯卡盘算着，要不要再打一匣子弹换一刻安静，想想还是不值。他没再动枪，硬是让自己被听见了。

===Original===
"We'll do nothing of the kind. We need that spaceport to stay alive. But we will take Ravick and Belsher and Hallstock—"

"And that *etaoin shrdlu* traitor of a Ware!" Joe Kivelson added.

"And Bish Ware," Oscar agreed. "They only have fifty police; we have three or four thousand men."

Three or four thousand undisciplined hunters, against fifty trained, disciplined and organized soldiers, because that was what the spaceport police were. I knew their captain, and the lieutenants. They were old Regular Army, and they ran the police force like a military unit.

===Chinese===
「那种蠢事我们可干不得。我们还指着那座太空港活命呢。不过拉维克、贝尔歇和霍尔斯特克，我们是拿定了——」

「还有韦尔那个 *etaoin shrdlu*（排字工顺手乱敲出的鬼画符，咒骂话）叛徒！」乔·基维尔森（Joe Kivelson）补充道。

「还有比什·韦尔，」奥斯卡表示同意。「他们拢共才五十个警察；我们有三四千人。」

三四千纪律涣散的猎怪手，对上五十名训练有素、纪律严明、组织严密的士兵——太空港警察本来就是这么一支队伍。我认识他们那位上尉，也认识那几位中尉。他们都是正规军出身的老兵，把这支警察队伍当一支军队来带。

===Original===
"I'll bet Ware was working for Ravick all along," Joe was saying.

That wasn't good thinking even for Joe Kivelson. I said:

"If he was working for Ravick all along, why did he tip Dad and Oscar and the Mahatma on the bomb aboard the *Javelin*? That wasn't any help to Ravick."

"I get it," Oscar said. "He never was working for anybody but Bish Ware. When Ravick got into a jam, he saw a way to make something for himself by getting Ravick out of it. I'll bet, ever since he came here, he was planning to cut in on Ravick somehow. You notice, he knew just how much money Ravick had stashed away on Terra? When he saw the spot Ravick was in, Bish just thought he had a chance to develop himself another rich uncle."

===Chinese===
「我敢打赌，韦尔从头到尾都是在给拉维克卖命，」乔正说着。

这想法就算出自乔·基维尔森之口，也不算高明。我说：

「他要是从头到尾给拉维克卖命，那他为什么要向爸爸、奥斯卡和『圣雄』通风报信，说标枪号（*Javelin*）上有炸弹？那可帮不上拉维克半点忙。」

「我明白了，」奥斯卡说。「他从来到处卖命，也只给比什·韦尔一个人卖命。等拉维克陷进坑里，他瞧出了一条道道儿：把拉维克捞出来，自己还能捞上一票。我敢打赌，打他来这儿那天起，就一直盘算着怎么从拉维克身上咬下一块。你们注意到没有，拉维克在特拉藏了多少钱，他一清二楚？等他看清拉维克眼下的处境，比什只当自己等来了机会，可以再给自己培植一位阔叔叔。」

===Original===
I'd been worse stunned than anybody by Dad's news. The worst of it was that Oscar could be right. I hadn't thought of that before. I'd just thought that Ravick and Belsher had gotten Bish drunk and found out about the way the men were posted around Hunters' Hall and the lone man in the jeep on Second Level Down.

Then it occurred to me that Bish might have seen a way of getting Fenris rid of Ravick and at the same time save everybody the guilt of lynching him. Maybe he'd turned traitor to save the rest of us from ourselves.

I turned to Oscar. "Why get excited about it?" I asked. "You have what you wanted. You said yourself that you couldn't care less whether Ravick got away or not, as long as you got him out of the Coop. Well, he's out for good now."

===Chinese===
爸爸带来的消息，我比谁都更受震动。最糟的是，奥斯卡说的可能是对的。这层我先前没想到。我只是想，拉维克和贝尔歇把比什灌醉了，套出了人手围堵猎手会堂（Hunters' Hall）的布防，还有下第二层（Second Level Down）吉普车里那个单独放哨的人。

接着我又想到，比什也许早就看出了一条路子：既能让芬尼斯（Fenris）甩掉拉维克，又免得大家背上私刑处死他的罪疚。也许他当这个叛徒，是为了把我们其余人从我们自己手里救出来。

我转向奥斯卡。「这有什么好激动的？」我问。「你要的已经到手了。你自己说过的，只要能把拉维克弄出合作社，他跑不跑路你压根儿不在乎。喏，如今他跑了个干净。」

===Original===
"That was before the fire," Oscar said. "We didn't have a couple of million sols' worth of wax burned. And Tom Kivelson wasn't in the hospital with half the skin burned off his back, and a coin toss whether he lives or not."

"Yes. I thought you were Tom's friend," Joe Kivelson reproached me.

I wondered how much skin hanging Steve Ravick would grow on Tom's back. I didn't see much percentage in asking him, though. I did turn to Oscar Fujisawa with a quotation I remembered from *Moby Dick*, the book he'd named his ship from.

"*How many barrels will thy vengeance yield thee, even if thou gettest it, Captain Ahab?*" I asked. "*It will not fetch thee much in our Nantucket market.*"

He looked at me angrily and started to say something. Then he shrugged.

===Chinese===
「那是在大火之前，」奥斯卡说。「那时候我们还没烧掉价值几百万索尔的蜡。汤姆·基维尔森（Tom Kivelson）也还没有躺在医院里，背上烧掉了一半的皮，是死是活全看抛硬币落哪面。」

「是啊。我还当你是汤姆的朋友呢，」乔·基维尔森责备我。

我暗自琢磨，把史蒂夫·拉维克绞死，能在汤姆背上长出多少皮来。不过我看，拿这个问题去问他实在没什么油水。我倒是转向奥斯卡·藤泽，引了一句我记得的《白鲸》（*Moby Dick*）里的话——他那条船的船名就是打这本书里来的。

「*你的复仇，纵然得逞，又能给你榨出多少桶油来，亚哈船长？*」我问。「*这在我们南塔开特（Nantucket）市场上，可卖不出几个钱。*」

他恼火地瞪了我一眼，张口想说什么。随即耸了耸肩。

===Original===
"I know, Walt," he said. "But you can't measure everything in barrels of whale oil. Or skins of tallow-wax."

Which was one of those perfectly true statements which are also perfectly meaningless. I gave up. My job's to get the news, not to make it. I wondered if that meant anything, either.

They finally got the mob sorted out, after a lot of time wasted in pillaging Ravick's living quarters on the fourth floor. *However, the troops stopped to loot the enemy's camp.* I'd come across that line fifty to a hundred times in history books. Usually, it had been expensive looting; if the enemy didn't counterattack, they managed, at least, to escape. More to the point, they gathered up all the cannon and machine guns around the place and got them onto contragravity in the street. There must have been close to five thousand men, by now, and those who couldn't crowd onto vehicles marched on foot, and the whole mass, looking a little more like an army than a mob, started up Broadway.

Since it is not proper for reporters to loot on the job, I had gotten outside in my jeep early and was going ahead, swinging my camera back to get the parade behind me. Might furnish a still-shot illustration for somebody's History of Fenris in a century or so.

===Chinese===
「我知道，沃尔特（Walt），」他说。「可你不能把什么都折算成多少桶鲸油。或者多少张脂蜡（tallow-wax）皮。」

这话属于那种绝对正确、同时也绝对空洞的话。我放弃了。我的本分是采访新闻，不是制造新闻。我怀疑这话是不是也一样毫无意义。

他们费了好大的劲才把这伙暴民整顿成型，其间浪费了大量时间洗劫四楼上拉维克的住所。*然而，部队停下来劫掠了敌营。*这句话我在史书上读到过五十到一百回。通常，这种劫掠代价高昂；就算敌人不反扑，至少也能趁机脱身。更要紧的是，他们把这一带所有的火炮和机枪都搜集起来，装上了街里的反重力车。到这时，人数怕是已近五千，挤不上车的便步行前进，整支人马瞧着已比乌合之众多了几分军队的模样，沿着百老汇（Broadway）出发了。

新闻记者在工作时间打劫有失体统，我早早便开着吉普到了外头，走在队伍前面，回身抡着摄影机，把身后的游行队伍收进镜头。说不定还能给百来年后哪位写的《芬尼斯史》配一幅静照插图。

===Original===
Broadway was empty until we came to the gateway to the spaceport area. There was a single medium combat car there, on contragravity halfway to the ceiling, with a pair of 50 mm guns and a rocket launcher pointed at us, and under it, on the roadway, a solitary man in an olive-green uniform stood.

I knew him; Lieutenant Ranjit Singh, Captain Courtland's second-in-command. He was a Sikh. Instead of a steel helmet, he wore a striped turban, and he had a black beard that made Joe Kivelson's blond one look like Tom Kivelson's chin-fuzz. On his belt, along with his pistol, he wore the little kirpan, the dagger all Sikhs carry. He also carried a belt radio, and as we approached he lifted the phone to his mouth and a loudspeaker on the combat car threw his voice at us:

"All right, that's far enough, now. The first vehicle that comes within a hundred yards of this gate will be shot down."

===Chinese===
百老汇一路空空荡荡，直到我们抵达太空港区的大门口。那儿只停着一辆中型战斗车，反重力悬浮在半空，升到离城市穹顶一半的高度，一对 50 毫米炮和一具火箭发射器齐齐对准了我们；车子底下的路面上，孤零零立着一个穿橄榄绿制服的人。

我认得他；兰吉特·辛格（Ranjit Singh）中尉，科特兰（Courtland）上尉的副手。他是个锡克教徒。头上不戴钢盔，裹着一条条纹缠头巾，一部黑胡子把乔·基维尔森那部金胡子衬得跟汤姆下巴上的绒毛似的。腰带上除了手枪，还挂着那柄小小的克尔潘短剑（kirpan）——锡克教徒人人随身佩带的匕首。他还带了部腰带电台，我们走近时，他把话筒举到嘴边，战斗车上的扩音器便把他的声音劈头盖脸砸了过来：

「好了，到此为止。哪辆车再敢靠近这道门一百码以内，就叫它趴下。」

===Original===
One man, and one combat car, against five thousand, with twenty-odd guns and close to a hundred machine guns. He'd last about as long as a pint of trade gin at a Sheshan funeral. The only thing was, before he and the crew of the combat car were killed, they'd wipe out about ten or fifteen of our vehicles and a couple of hundred men, and they would be the men and vehicles in the lead.

Mobs are a little different from soldiers, and our Rebel Army was still a mob. Mobs don't like to advance into certain death, and they don't like to advance over the bodies and wreckage of their own forward elements. Neither do soldiers, but soldiers will do it. Soldiers realize, when they put on the uniform, that some day they may face death in battle, and if this is it, this is it.

I got the combat car and the lone soldier in the turban—that would look good in anybody's history book—and moved forward, taking care that he saw the *Times* lettering on the jeep and taking care to stay well short of the deadline. I let down to the street and got out, taking off my gun belt and hanging it on the control handle of the jeep. Then I walked forward.

===Chinese===
一个人，一辆战斗车，对上五千人、二十来门炮、近百挺机枪。他撑的时间，大约跟一品脱贸易金酒摆在一场上舍尚人（Sheshan）的葬礼上差不多长。唯一的问题是，在他和战斗车乘员被干掉之前，他们能报销我们十几辆车、一两百号人——而且报销的偏偏是打头阵的人和车。

暴民跟士兵总有点不一样，我们这支造反大军到这会儿还是一群暴民。暴民不爱朝着必死无疑的地方冲，也不爱踩着自己先头队伍的尸首和残骸往前推。士兵其实也不爱，可士兵做得到。士兵穿上军装时就明白，有朝一日自己可能会在战场上面对死亡——真到了那一步，那就是那一步。

我把战斗车和那个裹缠头巾的孤身士兵拍了下来——这画面搁进谁的史书里都不丢份——然后驱车向前，既留心让他看清吉普车身上《时报》（*Times*）的字样，也留心同那条死线（deadline）保持足够的距离。我把车降到街面，跨出车来，解下枪带挂在吉普的操纵杆上。然后步行上前。

===Original===
"Lieutenant Ranjit," I said, "I'm representing the *Times*. I have business inside the spaceport. I want to get the facts about this. It may be that when I get this story, these people will be satisfied."

"We will, like Nifflheim!" I heard Joe Kivelson bawling, above and behind me. "We want the men who started the fire my son got burned in."

"Is that the Kivelson boy's father?" the Sikh asked me, and when I nodded, he lifted the phone to his lips again. "Captain Kivelson," the loudspeaker said, "your son is alive and under skin-grafting treatment here at the spaceport hospital. His life is not, repeat not, in danger. The men you are after are here, under guard. If any of them are guilty of any crimes, and if you can show any better authority than an armed mob to deal with them, they may, may, I said, be turned over for trial. But they will not be taken from this spaceport by force, as long as I or one of my men remains alive."

===Chinese===
「兰吉特中尉，」我说，「我代表《时报》。我在太空港里有点事务要办。我要把这件事的真相弄清楚。等我把事实拿到手，这些人也许就心满意足了。」

「想得美，除非尼弗尔海姆（Nifflheim）结了冰！」我听见乔·基维尔森在我上方身后吼叫。「我们要的就是那几个放火的人——我儿子就是在那场火里烧成这样的。」

「这位是基维尔森那孩子的父亲？」锡克人问我。我点了点头，他又把话筒举到唇边。扩音器响起：「基维尔森船长，令郎还活着，正在太空港医院接受植皮治疗。他的生命没有——重复一遍，没有——危险。你要找的那些人都在这里，有人看守。他们当中若有谁犯了什么罪，而你们又能拿出比一支武装暴民更像样的权威来处置他们，他们也许，也许——我说的是也许——会被移交审判。但是，只要我或者我手下还有一个人活着，就绝不容许任何人用武力把他们从这座太空港里带走。」

===Original===
"That's easy. We'll get them afterward," Joe Kivelson shouted.

"Somebody may. You won't," Ranjit Singh told him. "Van Steen, hit that ship's boat first, and hit it at the first hostile move anybody in this mob makes."

"Yes, sir. With pleasure," another voice replied.

Nobody in the Rebel Army, if that was what it still was, had any comment to make on that. Lieutenant Ranjit turned to me.

"Mr. Boyd," he said. None of this sonny-boy stuff; Ranjit Singh was a man of dignity, and he respected the dignity of others. "If I admit you to the spaceport, will you give these people the facts exactly as you learn them?"

===Chinese===
「那容易。回头我们再收拾他们，」乔·基维尔森喊道。

「有人也许可以。你们不行，」兰吉特·辛格告诉他。「范·斯蒂恩（Van Steen），先瞄准那条船的小艇，这群人里谁敢有第一个敌对动作，就开火。」

「是，长官。乐意效劳，」另一个声音答道。

造反大军里——如果它还配叫大军的话——没人对此发表什么意见。兰吉特中尉转向我。

「博伊德（Boyd）先生，」他说。没有半句「小家伙」之类的口气；兰吉特·辛格是个有尊严的人，也尊重他人的尊严。「如果我放你进太空港，你肯不肯把你了解到的事实，原原本本讲给这些人听？」

===Original===
"That's what the *Times* always does, Lieutenant." Well, almost all the facts almost always.

"Will you people accept what this *Times* reporter tells you he has learned?"

"Yes, of course." That was Oscar Fujisawa.

"I won't!" That was Joe Kivelson. "He's always taking the part of that old rumpot of a Bish Ware."

"Lieutenant, that remark was a slur on my paper, as well as myself," I said. "Will you permit Captain Kivelson to come in along with me? And somebody else," I couldn't resist adding, "so that people will believe him?"

===Chinese===
「《时报》从来就是这么办的，中尉。」嗯，差不多所有的事实，差不多从来都是。

「你们各位，愿意相信这位《时报》记者通报给你们的消息吗？」

「愿意，当然。」那是奥斯卡·藤泽。

「我不愿意！」那是乔·基维尔森。「他老是护着比什·韦尔那个老酒坛子。」

「中尉，这话既玷辱了本报，也玷辱了我本人，」我说。「您可否准许基维尔森船长随我一同进来？再带上另一位，」我忍不住补了一句，「好叫大家肯信他的话？」

===Original===
Ranjit Singh considered that briefly. He wasn't afraid to die—I believe he was honestly puzzled when he heard people talking about fear—but his job was to protect some fugitives from a mob, not to die a useless hero's death. If letting in a small delegation would prevent an attack on the spaceport without loss of life and ammunition—or maybe he reversed the order of importance—he was obliged to try it.

"Yes. You may choose five men to accompany Mr. Boyd," he said. "They may not bring weapons in with them. Sidearms," he added, "will not count as weapons."

After all, a kirpan was a sidearm, and his religion required him to carry that. The decision didn't make me particularly happy. Respect for the dignity of others is a fine thing in an officer, but like journalistic respect for facts, it can be carried past the point of being a virtue. I thought he was overestimating Joe Kivelson's self-control.

Vehicles in front began grounding, and men got out and bunched together on the street. Finally, they picked their delegation: Joe Kivelson, Oscar Fujisawa, Casmir Oughourlian the shipyard man, one of the engineers at the nutrient plant, and the Reverend Hiram Zilker, the Orthodox-Monophysite preacher. They all had pistols, even the Reverend Zilker, so I went back to the jeep and put mine on. Ranjit Singh had switched his radio off the speaker and was talking to somebody else. After a while, an olive-green limousine piloted by a policeman in uniform and helmet floated in and grounded. The six of us got into it, and it lifted again.

===Chinese===
兰吉特·辛格略作思量。他不怕死——我相信他听见旁人谈论恐惧时，是真心感到困惑——但他的职责是保护几个逃亡者不受暴民伤害，而不是白白死出一场英雄殉难。要是放进一小队代表，就能既不折损人命、又不耗费弹药地保住太空港免遭攻打——说不定他把这两样的轻重次序正颠倒着排——那他就义不容辞，值得一试。

「可以。你们可以挑选五个人，陪同博伊德先生，」他说。「他们不得携带武器入内。随身佩枪，」他补充道，「不算武器。」

毕竟，克尔潘短剑也算随身佩枪，而他的宗教要求他佩着它。这个裁决让我不怎么痛快。尊重他人的尊严，在军官身上是种美德，可就像新闻记者尊重事实一样，一旦过了头，就不再是美德了。我觉得他高估了乔·基维尔森的自制力。

前头的车辆纷纷落地，人们下了车，在街上聚作一团。最后，代表选了出来：乔·基维尔森、奥斯卡·藤泽、造船厂的卡斯米尔·乌古尔良（Casmir Oughourlian）、营养剂厂的一名工程师，还有正统一性论派（Orthodox-Monophysite）的传道人希拉姆·齐尔克（Hiram Zilker）牧师。人人都有手枪，连齐尔克牧师也不例外，于是我也折回吉普，把我那把戴上。兰吉特·辛格已把电台从扩音器上切换开，正在跟别人通话。过了一会儿，一辆橄榄绿大型轿车，由一名穿制服、戴钢盔的警察驾驶，飘了进来，落了地。我们六个人上了车，车又升了起来。

===Original===
The car let down in a vehicle hall in the administrative area, and the police second lieutenant, Chris Xantos, was waiting alone, armed only with the pistol that was part of his uniform and wearing a beret instead of a helmet. He spoke to us, and ushered us down a hallway toward Guido Fieschi's office.

I get into the spaceport administrative area about once in twenty or so hours. Oughourlian is a somewhat less frequent visitor. The others had never been there, and they were visibly awed by all the gleaming glass and brightwork, and the soft lights and the thick carpets. *All Port Sandor ought to look like this*, I thought. It could, and maybe now it might, after a while.

There were six chairs in a semicircle facing Guido Fieschi's desk, and three men sitting behind it. Fieschi, who had changed clothes and washed since the last time I saw him, sat on the extreme right. Captain Courtland, with his tight mouth under a gray mustache and the quadruple row of medal ribbons on his breast, was on the left. In the middle, the seat of honor, was Bish Ware, looking as though he were presiding over a church council to try some rural curate for heresy.

===Chinese===
车在行政区的一座车辆厅里落了下来，警察少尉克里斯·克桑托斯（Chris Xantos）独自候在那里，只带着制服配发的那把手枪，头上没戴钢盔，戴了顶贝雷帽。他向我们讲了几句，便引着我们沿一条走廊朝圭多·菲耶斯基（Guido Fieschi）的办公室走去。

我大约每隔二十来个小时就能进一趟太空港的行政区。乌古尔良来得稍少些。其余几位从没进来过，满眼的锃亮玻璃和闪光的金属件、柔和的灯光、厚实的地毯，看得他们肃然生畏。*整个桑多尔港（Port Sandor）都该是这副模样*，我心想。这是做得到的，兴许如今过了这一阵，真能做到了。

圭多·菲耶斯基的办公桌前，六把椅子摆成半圆形，桌子后面坐着三个人。菲耶斯基坐在最右边，自上次见面以来，他已经换过衣服、梳洗过了。科特兰上尉坐在左边，灰白胡子底下抿着一张紧绷的嘴，胸前别着四排勋表。正中间的主位上是比什·韦尔，那副神情，仿佛正在主持一场教务会议，审理一桩乡间助理牧师涉嫌异端的案子。

===Original===
As soon as Joe Kivelson saw him, he roared angrily:

"There's the dirty traitor who sold us out! He's the worst of the lot; I wouldn't be surprised if—"

Bish looked at him like a bishop who has just been contradicted on a point of doctrine by a choirboy.

"Be quiet!" he ordered. "I did not follow this man you call Ravick here to this … this running-hot-and-cold Paradise planet, and I did not spend five years fraternizing with its unwashed citizenry and creating for myself the role of town drunkard of Port Sandor, to have him taken from me and lynched after I have arrested him. People do not lynch my prisoners."

===Chinese===
乔·基维尔森一看见他，便怒吼起来：

「就是这个人，把我们出卖了的肮脏叛徒！他是这一伙里最坏的一个；要是我听说——我也一点不会吃惊——」

比什望着他，那眼神活像一位主教，刚被唱诗班的男孩驳倒了一条教义。

「安静！」他喝令道。「我一路追到这个……这个忽冷忽热的『极乐土』行星上，追你们称作拉维克的这个人，又花五年工夫跟这行星上不修边幅的居民打成一片，给自己挣下桑多尔港市镇酒鬼的名头，可不是为了在逮捕他之后，眼睁睁让人把他从我手里抢走、私刑绞死。谁也不许私刑处死我的囚犯。」

===Original===
"And who in blazes are you?" Joe demanded.

Bish took cognizance of the question, if not the questioner.

"Tell them, if you please, Mr. Fieschi," he said.

"Well, Mr. Ware is a Terran Federation Executive Special Agent," Fieschi said. "Captain Courtland and I have known that for the past five years. As far as I know, nobody else was informed of Mr. Ware's position."

After that, you could have heard a gnat sneeze.

===Chinese===
「活见鬼，你到底是什么人？」乔质问。

比什理会了这个问题——如果说没理会提问之人。

「劳驾，请你告诉他们，菲耶斯基先生，」他说。

「是这样，韦尔先生是特拉联邦（Terran Federation）的行政特别探员（Executive Special Agent），」菲耶斯基说。「科特兰上尉和我知晓此事已有五年。据我所知，其他任何人都不曾被告知韦尔先生的身份。」

这句话之后，连蠓虫打个喷嚏你都听得见。

===Original===
Everybody knows about Executive Special Agents. There are all kinds of secret agents operating in the Federation—Army and Navy Intelligence, police of different sorts, Colonial Office agents, private detectives, Chartered Company agents. But there are fewer Executive Specials than there are inhabited planets in the Federation. They rank, *ex officio*, as Army generals and Space Navy admirals; they have the privilege of the floor in Parliament, they take orders from nobody but the President of the Federation. But very few people have ever seen one, or talked to anybody who has.

And Bish Ware—*good ol' Bish; he'sh everybodysh frien'*—was one of them. And I had been trying to make a man of him and reform him. I'd even thought, if he stopped drinking, he might make a success as a private detective—at Port Sandor, on Fenris! I wondered what color my face had gotten now, and I started looking around for a crack in the floor, to trickle gently and unobtrusively into.

And it should have been obvious to me, maybe not that he was an Executive Special, but that he was certainly no drunken barfly. The way he'd gone four hours without a drink, and seemed to be just as drunk as ever. That was right—just as drunk as he'd ever been; which was to say, cold sober. There was the time I'd seen him catch that falling bottle and set it up. No drunken man could have done that; a man's reflexes are the first thing to be affected by alcohol. And the way he shot that tread-snail. I've seen men who could shoot well on liquor, but not quick-draw stuff. That calls for perfect coordination. And the way he went into his tipsy act at the *Times*—veteran actor slipping into a well-learned role.

===Chinese===
行政特别探员是什么人，人人都知道。联邦境内活动的密探五花八门——陆军和海军的情报部门、各式各样的警察、殖民部（Colonial Office）的探员、私家侦探、特许公司（Chartered Company）的代理人。可行政特别探员的人数，比联邦境内有人居住的行星还少。他们*依职*享有陆军将官和太空海军（Space Navy）将官的军衔；在议会（Parliament）享有发言权；只听联邦总统一人的号令。但极少有人亲眼见过一位，连跟见过的人交谈过的都极少。

而比什·韦尔——*好老比什，他系人人都嘚朋油*（good ol' Bish; he'sh everybodysh frien'）——竟然就是其中一员。而我，先前还一直想把他扶成个正派人，改造他。我甚至想过，只要他把酒戒了，当私家侦探没准能成一番事业——在桑多尔港，在芬尼斯！我不知道自己这会儿脸红成了什么颜色，只顾四下里找条地缝，好悄没声儿地、不动声色地渗进去。

这些我本该早看出来的——倒未必看出他是行政特别探员，至少该看出他绝不是什么醉醺醺的酒吧常客。瞧他能四个钟头滴酒不沾，看起来却跟往常一样醉。正是如此——跟往常任何时候一样「醉」；也就是说，清醒透顶。还有一回，我亲眼见他接住那只坠落的瓶子，把它稳稳立好。醉汉绝办不到；人的反应能力，最先受酒精影响的就是它。再看他射死那条履带蜗牛（tread-snail）的身手。喝着酒还能打准的人我见过，可那种拔枪急射的本事没见过。那需要完美无缺的协调。还有他在《时报》进入醉态的那副做派——老戏骨滑进一个烂熟于心的角色。

===Original===
He drank, sure. He did a lot of drinking. But there are men whose systems resist the effects of alcohol better than others, and he must have been an exceptional example of the type, or he'd never have adopted the sort of cover personality he did. It would have been fairly easy for him. Space his drinks widely, and never take a drink unless he *had* to, to maintain the act. When he was at the *Times* with just Dad and me, what did he have? A fruit fizz.

Well, at least I could see it after I had my nose rubbed in it. Joe Kivelson was simply gaping at him. The Reverend Zilker seemed to be having trouble adjusting, too. The shipyard man and the chemical engineer weren't saying anything, but it had kicked them for a loss, too. Oscar Fujisawa was making a noble effort to be completely unsurprised. Oscar is one of our better poker players.

"I thought it might be something like that," he lied brazenly. "But, Bish … Excuse me, I mean, Mr. Ware …"

"Bish, if you please, Oscar."

"Bish, what I'd like to know is what you wanted with Ravick," he said. "They didn't send any Executive Special Agent here for five years to investigate this tallow-wax racket of his."

===Chinese===
酒他当然喝。而且喝得不少。可有些人的体质天生比别人扛得住酒精，他想必是这一类里的佼佼者，否则绝不敢用他那种掩护人设。对他来说，这并不算难：把每杯酒的间隔拉得远远的，除非为了维持演技*不得不*喝，否则滴酒不沾。他在《时报》，屋里只有爸爸和我的时候，喝的是什么？一杯果子汽水。

唉，好歹等事实摁着我的鼻子蹭过一遍之后，我总算看明白了。乔·基维尔森只顾张着嘴瞪他。齐尔克牧师似乎一时也拐不过这个弯来。造船厂那位和化学工程师一言不发，可这事同样把他们砸懵了。奥斯卡·藤泽正做着高尚的努力，务求显得毫不意外。奥斯卡是本地数得着的扑克好手之一。

「我就琢磨着，八成是这么回事，」他厚着脸皮撒谎。「不过，比什……失礼了，我是说，韦尔先生……」

「叫我比什就好，奥斯卡。」

「比什，我想知道的是，你追拉维克所为何来，」他说。「联邦绝不会为了查他这点脂蜡骗局，就派一位行政特别探员在这儿耗上五年。」

===Original===
"No. We have been looking for him for a long time. Fifteen years, and I've been working on it that long. You might say, I have made a career of him. Steve Ravick is really Anton Gerrit."

Maybe he was expecting us to leap from our chairs and cry out, "Aha! The infamous Anton Gerrit! Brought to book at last!" We didn't. We just looked at one another, trying to connect some meaning to the name. It was Joe Kivelson, of all people, who caught the first gleam.

"I know that name," he said. "Something on Loki, wasn't it?"

Yes; that was it. Now that my nose was rubbed in it again, I got it.

"The Loki enslavements. Was that it?" I asked. "I read about it, but I never seem to have heard of Gerrit."

===Chinese===
「不是。我们找他已经很久了。十五年了，我也追了他十五年。可以说，我把自己的毕生事业都押在他身上了。史蒂夫·拉维克的本名，是安东·赫里特（Anton Gerrit）。」

他大概指望我们从椅子上跳起来，齐声惊呼：「啊哈！那个恶名昭彰的安东·赫里特！终于落网伏法了！」我们没有。我们只是面面相觑，拼命想给这个名字拴上一点意义。偏偏是乔·基维尔森，第一个捕捉到了那道微光。

「这名字我知道，」他说。「洛基（Loki）上的什么事，对吧？」

对；正是。这回事实又摁着我的鼻子蹭了一遍，我也想起来了。

「洛基奴役事件。是那个吗？」我问。「我读到过那段新闻，可赫里特这个名字，我好像从来没听说过。」

===Original===
"He was the mastermind. The ones who were caught, fifteen years ago, were the underlings, but Ravick was the real Number One. He was responsible for the enslavement of from twenty to thirty thousand Lokian natives, gentle, harmless, friendly people, most of whom were worked to death in the mines."

No wonder an Executive Special would put in fifteen years looking for him. You murder your grandmother, or rob a bank, or burn down an orphanage with the orphans all in bed upstairs, or something trivial like that, and if you make an off-planet getaway, you're reasonably safe. Of course there's such a thing as extradition, but who bothers? Distances are too great, and communication is too slow, and the Federation depends on every planet to do its own policing.

But enslavement's something different. The Terran Federation is a government of and for—if occasionally not by—all sapient peoples of all races. The Federation Constitution guarantees equal rights to all. Making slaves of people, human or otherwise, is a direct blow at everything the Federation stands for. No wonder they kept hunting fifteen years for the man responsible for the Loki enslavements.

===Chinese===
「他是主谋。十五年前落网的那些都是喽啰，拉维克才是真正的头号人物。两三万洛基土著被奴役，罪责在他——那是个温和、无害、友善的种族，其中大多数人被活活累死在矿上。」

难怪一位行政特别探员肯为他耗上十五年。你谋害亲祖母也好，抢银行也好，把孤儿院一把火烧塌、孤儿全睡在楼上的床里也好，诸如此类的鸡毛蒜皮，只要你逃出这颗行星，就相当安全。引渡制度当然有，可谁耐烦去折腾？距离太远，通讯太慢，联邦本就指着每颗行星自己管好自家的治安。

但奴役是另一回事。特拉联邦是一个属于一切种族中一切智慧种族、并为其服务的政府——虽说偶尔并非由他们当家做主。联邦宪法（Federation Constitution）保障人人权利平等。把人变成奴隶，无论是不是人类，都是对联邦立国之本的迎头一击。难怪他们锲而不舍追捕了十五年，非揪出洛基奴役事件的元凶不可。

===Original===
"Gerrit got away, with a month's start. By the time we had traced him to Baldur, he had a year's start on us. He was five years ahead of us when we found out that he'd gone from Baldur to Odin. Six years ago, nine years after we'd started hunting for him, we decided, from the best information we could get, that he had left Odin on one of the local-stop ships for Terra, and dropped off along the way. There are six planets at which those Terra-Odin ships stop. We sent a man to each of them. I drew this prize out of the hat.

"When I landed here, I contacted Mr. Fieschi, and we found that a man answering to Gerrit's description had come in on the *Peenemünde* from Odin seven years before, about the time Gerrit had left Odin. The man who called himself Steve Ravick. Of course, he didn't look anything like the pictures of Gerrit, but facial surgery was something we'd taken for granted he'd have done. I finally managed to get his fingerprints."

Special Agent Ware took out a cigar, inspected it with the drunken oversolemnity he'd been drilling himself into for five years, and lit it. Then he saw what he was using and rose, holding it out, and I went to the desk and took back my lighter-weapon.

===Chinese===
「赫里特逃走时，抢先了我们一个月。等我们追查到巴尔德星（Baldur），他已抢先了一年。等查明他已从巴尔德去了奥丁星（Odin），他又抢先了五年。六年前——那时我们追捕他已是第九个年头——根据我们所能弄到的最可靠情报，我们判定他已搭上奥丁飞特拉的某条沿途停靠船，中途下了船。那种特拉—奥丁航线沿途停靠的行星共有六颗。我们每颗行星派了一个人。我抽签抽中了这份大奖。

「我在此地着陆后，便联络了菲耶斯基先生，我们查到，七年前有个与赫里特外貌描述相符的人，搭乘佩内明德号（*Peenemünde*）从奥丁抵达本地——时间恰在赫里特离开奥丁前后。此人自称史蒂夫·拉维克。当然，他的长相跟赫里特的照片毫无相似之处，但他做过面部整容手术，这一点我们本就料定了。最后，我终于设法弄到了他的指纹。」

韦尔特别探员掏出一支雪茄，带着他磨炼了五年的醉汉式过分郑重端详了一番，点着了。随后他看清了手里用的是个什么物件，站起身，把它递了过来，我走到桌前，取回了我那件打火机武器（lighter-weapon）。

===Original===
"Thank you, Walt. I wouldn't have been able to do this if I hadn't had that. Where was I? Oh, yes. I got Gerrit-alias-Ravick's fingerprints, which did not match the ones we had on file for Gerrit, and sent them in. It was eighteen months later that I got a reply on them. According to his fingerprints, Steve Ravick was really a woman named Ernestine Coyón, who had died of acute alcoholism in the free public ward of a hospital at Paris-on-Baldur fourteen years ago."

"Why, that's incredible!" the Reverend Zilker burst out, and Joe Kivelson was saying: "Steve Ravick isn't any woman. …"

"Least of all one who died fourteen years ago," Bish agreed. "But the fingerprints were hers. A pauper, dying in a public ward of a big hospital. And a man who has to change his identity, and who has small, woman-sized hands. And a crooked hospital staff surgeon. You get the picture now?"

===Chinese===
「谢谢你，沃尔特。刚才要没这物件，我还真点不着。我讲到哪儿了？哦，对。我取到了赫里特——化名拉维克——的指纹，跟我们档里赫里特的存档指纹对不上，便把它们呈报了上去。过了十八个月，我才等到回音。按指纹的结论，史蒂夫·拉维克其实是个女人，名叫埃内斯蒂娜·科永（Ernestine Coyón），十四年前就死于急性酒精中毒，死在巴尔德星巴黎城（Paris-on-Baldur）一家医院的免费公共病房里。」

「哎呀，这太不可思议了！」齐尔克牧师脱口而出，乔·基维尔森也在嚷嚷：「史蒂夫·拉维克怎么可能是女人。……」

「更不可能是十四年前就死了的女人，」比什附和道。「可那指纹确实是她的。一个贫民，死在大医院的公共病房里。再有一个必须改换身份的男人，偏偏长着一双女人般的小手。再加上一个贪赃枉法的医院外科医师。现在，你们明白是怎么回事了吧？」

===Original===
"They're doing the same thing on Tom's back, right here," I told Joe. "Only you can't grow fingerprints by carniculture, the way you can human tissue for grafting. They had to have palm and finger surfaces from a pair of real human hands. A pauper, dying in a free-treatment ward, her body shoved into a mass-energy converter." Then I thought of something else. "That showoff trick of his, crushing out cigarettes in his palm," I said.

Bish nodded commendingly. "Exactly. He'd have about as much sensation in his palms as I'd have wearing thick leather gloves. I'd noticed that.

"Well, six months going, and a couple of months waiting on reports from other planets, and six months coming, and so on, it wasn't until the *Peenemünde* got in from Terra, the last time, that I got final confirmation. Dr. Watson, you'll recall."

===Chinese===
「就在这儿，他们正在汤姆背上做着同样的事，」我对乔说。「只不过，指纹没法像移植用的人体组织那样，用肉培（carniculture）技术培育出来。他们必须搞到一双真正人手上的掌面和指面。一个贫民，死在免费医疗病房里，尸体被塞进质能转换炉（mass-energy converter）。」接着我又想起一件事。「还有他那手炫技——用掌心掐灭香烟，」我说。

比什赞许地点了点头。「正是。他那两只掌心的知觉，跟戴着厚皮手套差不多。这一点，我早就注意到了。

「就这样，去程半年，等各行星的回音又几个月，回程再半年，如此这般，直到上一班佩内明德号从特拉抵港，我才拿到最后的确证。你还记得吧，那位『约翰·华生医生』（Dr. Watson）。」

===Original===
"Who, you perceived, had been in Afghanistan," I mentioned, trying to salvage something. Showing off. The one I was trying to impress was Walt Boyd.

"You caught that? Careless of me," Bish chided himself. "What he gave me was a report that they had finally located a man who had been a staff surgeon at this hospital on Baldur at the time. He's now doing a stretch for another piece of malpractice he was unlucky enough to get caught at later. We will not admit making deals with any criminals, in jail or out, but he is willing to testify, and is on his way to Terra now. He can identify pictures of Anton Gerrit as those of the man he operated on fourteen years ago, and his testimony and Ernestine Coyón's fingerprints will identify Ravick as that man. With all the Colonial Constabulary and Army Intelligence people got on Gerrit on Loki, simple identification will be enough. Gerrit was proven guilty long ago, and it won't be any trouble, now, to prove that Ravick is Gerrit."

"Why didn't you arrest him as soon as you got the word from your friend from Afghanistan?" I wanted to know.

===Chinese===
「那位，依『你看』，去过阿富汗的约翰·华生医生，」我提了一句，想多少挽回一点颜面。班门弄斧了一把。而这卖弄想折服的看客，正是沃尔特·博伊德本人。

「你听出来了？我太大意了，」比什自责道。「我那位朋友交给我的，是一份报告：他们终于找到了一个人，案发当时正是巴尔德那家医院的外科主治医师。他如今正在坐牢，罪名是后来另一起行医不法——算他倒霉才东窗事发。我们对内对外都不会承认跟任何罪犯——在押的也罢、不在押的也罢——做过交易，不过他愿意出庭作证，目前正在押赴特拉途中。他能指认安东·赫里特的照片，正是他十四年前动手术的那个人；他的证词，再加上埃内斯蒂娜·科永的指纹，就能把拉维克认定为此人。凭殖民地宪兵队（Colonial Constabulary）和陆军情报部门当初在洛基搜集到的赫里特罪证，单凭指认就已足够。赫里特的罪状早已坐实，如今要证明拉维克就是赫里特，不费吹灰之力。」

「你那位『阿富汗朋友』一有音讯，为什么不立刻逮捕他？」我很想知道。

===Original===
"Good question; I've been asking myself that," Bish said, a trifle wryly. "If I had, the *Javelin* wouldn't have been bombed, that wax wouldn't have been burned, and Tom Kivelson wouldn't have been injured. What I did was send my friend, who is a Colonial Constabulary detective, to Gimli, the next planet out. There's a Navy base there, and always at least a couple of destroyers available. He's coming back with one of them to pick Gerrit up and take him to Terra. They ought to be in in about two hundred and fifty hours. I thought it would be safer all around to let Gerrit run loose till then. There's no place he could go.

"What I didn't realize, at the time, was what a human H-bomb this man Murell would turn into. Then everything blew up at once. Finally, I was left with the choice of helping Gerrit escape from Hunters' Hall or having him lynched before I could arrest him." He turned to Kivelson. "In the light of what you knew, I don't blame you for calling me a dirty traitor."

"But how did I know …" Kivelson began.

"That's right. You weren't supposed to. That was before you found out. You ought to have heard what Gerrit and Belsher—as far as I know, that is his real name—called me after they found out, when they got out of that jeep and Captain Courtland's men snapped the handcuffs on them. It even shocked a hardened sinner like me."

===Chinese===
「问得好；这话我也一直在问自己，」比什说，语气带着几分苦涩。「我要是当机立断，标枪号就不会挨炸，那批蜡就不会烧掉，汤姆·基维尔森也不会受伤。我实际做的，是把我那位朋友——他是殖民地宪兵队的侦探——派去了外圈下一颗行星吉姆利（Gimli）。那儿有个海军基地，常年至少泊着两艘可以调遣的驱逐舰。他会押着其中一艘回来，提走赫里特，解往特拉。他们大约二百五十个小时后该到。我想，在那之前放赫里特在外头逍遥，对各方都更稳妥。反正他无路可逃。

「我当时万万没料到的是，穆雷尔这个人竟会变成一颗人形氢弹。结果一切轰然引爆。到最后，我只剩下两条路：要么帮赫里特逃出猎手会堂，要么眼睁睁看着他在我来得及逮捕之前被人私刑绞死。」他转向基维尔森。「凭你当时知道的情况，你骂我肮脏叛徒，我不怪你。」

「可我哪知道……」基维尔森开了口。

「说得对。你原本就不该知道。那是你知情之前的事了。你真该听听赫里特和贝尔歇——据我所知，贝尔歇倒是他真名——识破之后是怎么骂我的，就在他们下了那辆吉普、科特兰上尉的人咔嗒给他们铐上手铐的时候。连我这样一个积年老罪人，都让他们骂得心惊。」

===Original===
There was a lot more of it. Bish had managed to get into Hunters' Hall just about the time Al Devis and his companion were starting the fire Ravick—Gerrit—had ordered for a diversion. The whole gang was going to crash out as soon as the fire had attracted everybody away. Bish led them out onto the Second Level Down, sleep-gassed the lone man in the jeep, and took them to the spaceport, where the police were waiting for them.

As soon as I'd gotten everything, I called the *Times*. I'd had my radio on all the time, and it had been coming in perfectly. Dad, I was happy to observe, was every bit as flabbergasted as I had been at who and what Bish Ware was. He might throw my campaign to reform Bish up at me later on, but at the moment he wasn't disposed to, and I was praising Allah silently that I hadn't had a chance to mention the detective agency idea to him. That would have been a little too much.

"What are they doing about Belsher and Hallstock?" he asked.

===Chinese===
后面的事还多着呢。阿尔·德维斯（Al Devis）和他那名同伙动手放火——那是拉维克，也就是赫里特，下令放的调虎离山之火——差不多正当那个时候，比什设法进了猎手会堂。那一伙人本打算等大火把人统统引开，就全体夺门而逃。比什领着他们出来，到了下第二层，用麻醉瓦斯放倒了吉普里那个单独放哨的人，把他们送到太空港，警察正在那里候着他们。

等我把一切都弄到了手，便呼叫《时报》。我的电台一直开着，收讯始终清清楚楚。我很欣慰地看到，对于比什·韦尔是何等人物、干的是什么营生，爸爸跟我先前一样目瞪口呆。日后他兴许会把我那场改造比什的运动翻出来数落我，不过眼下他没这个意思，我也正在心里默默感赞真主：幸亏我一直没机会跟他提那个侦探社的点子。那可就有点太过了。

「贝尔歇和霍尔斯特克那边怎么处置？」他问。

===Original===
"Belsher goes back to Terra with Ravick. Gerrit, I mean. That's where he collected his cut on the tallow-wax, so that is where he'd have to be tried. Bish is convinced that somebody in Kapstaad Chemical must have been involved, too. Hallstock is strictly a local matter."

"That's about what I thought. With all this interstellar back-and-forth, it'll be a long time before we'll be able to write thirty under the story."

"Well, we can put thirty under the Steve Ravick story," I said.

===Chinese===
「贝尔歇随拉维克一起押回特拉。我是说赫里特。他那份脂蜡分成是在那边收的，所以审判也必须在那边进行。比什确信，卡普斯塔德化学公司（Kapstaad Chemical）里头必然也有人参了一脚。霍尔斯特克则纯属本地事务。」

「跟我想到一块儿去了。这一来一去的星际官司，够拖上好一阵子，我们才能在这篇报道末尾写上『三十』（thirty，新闻行话，意为『终了』）。」

「话虽这么说，史蒂夫·拉维克这篇，咱们眼下就能写上『三十』了，」我说。

===Original===
Then it hit me. The Steve Ravick story was finished; that is, the local story of racketeer rule in the Hunters' Cooperative. But the Anton Gerrit story was something else. That was Federation-wide news; the end of a fifteen-year manhunt for the most wanted criminal in the known Galaxy. And who had that story, right in his hot little hand? Walter Boyd, the ace—and only—reporter for the mighty *Port Sandor Times*.

"Yes," I continued. "The Ravick story's finished. But we still have the Anton Gerrit story, and I'm going to work on it right now."

===Chinese===
跟着，我忽然醒过神来。史蒂夫·拉维克的报道是完了；也就是说，猎手合作社（Hunters' Cooperative）里骗子集团当家的这段本地故事完了。可安东·赫里特的报道是另一回事。那是轰遍全联邦的大新闻；追捕已知银河系头号通缉犯的十五年大搜捕，至此落下帷幕。而这条新闻，正攥在谁滚烫的小手心里？沃尔特·博伊德——威风凛凛的《桑多尔港时报》（*Port Sandor Times*）的王牌——也是唯一的——记者。

「是啊，」我接着说。「拉维克这篇是完了。可安东·赫里特这篇还在我们手里，我这就去动笔。」

## Mayday, Mayday / 梅戴，梅戴

===Original===
Getting a ship's boat berthed inside the ship in the air is tricky work under the best of conditions; the way the wind was blowing by now, it would have been like trying to thread a needle inside a concrete mixer. We submerged after the ship and went in underwater. Then we had to wait in the boat until the ship rose above the surface and emptied the water out of the boat berth. When that was done and the boat berth was sealed again, the ship went down seventy fathoms and came to rest on the bottom, and we unsealed the boat and got out.

There was still the job of packing the wax into skins, but that could wait. Everybody was tired and dirty and hungry. We took turns washing up, three at a time, in the little ship's latrine which, for some reason going back to sailing-ship days on Terra, was called the “head.” Finally the whole sixteen of us gathered in the relatively comfortable wardroom under the after gun turret.

Comfortable, that is, to the extent that everybody could find a place to sit down, or could move about without tripping over somebody else. There was a big pot of coffee, and everybody had a plate or bowl of hot food. There’s always plenty of hot food to hand on a hunter-ship; no regular mealtimes, and everybody eats, as he sleeps, when he has time. This is the only time when a whole hunter crew gets together, after a monster has been killed and cut up and the ship is resting on the bottom and nobody has to stand watch.

Everybody was talking about the killing, of course, and the wax we had in the hold, and counting the money they were going to get for it, at the new eighty-centisol price.

===Chinese===
即便在最好的条件下，把船载小艇在半空里收进船腹也是件棘手的活计；照如今这风势，那就好比想在混凝土搅拌机里穿针引线。我们跟着大船下潜，从水底进了艇库。之后只得待在小艇里干等，等大船浮出水面，把艇库里的水排空。这一套完成、艇库重新封好之后，大船下潜到七十英寻，坐底停稳，我们这才开启小艇，钻了出来。

把蜡装进皮囊的活儿还悬着，不过不急在一时。人人都又累又脏又饿。我们轮流到那间小小的船用盥洗间里洗漱，一次进去三个——不知出于什么可以一直追溯到特拉帆船时代的缘由，那地方被叫作「船头」（head）。最后，我们全体十六人在艉炮塔下方那间相对舒适的军官餐室里聚了齐。

说舒适，也就是人人都找得着地方坐、起身走动不至于绊到旁人的程度。一大壶咖啡摆在那里，人人手里都端着一盘或一碗热食。猎怪船上的热食向来管够；没有定时的饭点，人人吃饭同睡觉一样，得空就吃。猎杀了一头怪兽、宰割停当、大船坐底、无人需要值更——全体猎怪船员像这样聚到一处，一船之中也就只此一回。

大伙儿的话题当然离不开这场猎杀，离不开舱里的那些蜡，人人都在按新出的八十厘索尔牌价，盘算这批货能换回多少钱。

===Original===
“Well, I make it about fourteen tons,” Ramón Llewellyn, who had been checking the wax as it went into the hold, said. He figured mentally for a moment, and added, “Call it twenty-two thousand sols.” Then he had to fall back on a pencil and paper to figure shares.

I was surprised to find that he was reckoning shares for both Murell and myself.

“Hey, do we want to let them do that?” I whispered to Murell. “We just came along for the ride.”

“I don’t want the money,” he said. “These people need every cent they can get.”

So did I, for that matter, and I didn’t have salary and expense account from a big company on Terra. However, I hadn’t come along in the expectation of making anything out of it, and a newsman has to be careful about the outside money he picks up. It wouldn’t do any harm in the present instance, but as a practice it can lead to all kinds of things, like playing favorites, coloring news, killing stories that shouldn’t be killed. We do enough of that as it is, like playing down the tread-snail business for Bish Ware and the spaceport people, and never killing anybody except in a “local bar.” It’s hard to draw a line on that sort of thing.

“We’re just guests,” I said. “We don’t work here.”

===Chinese===
「依我看，大约十四吨，」拉蒙·卢埃林（Ramón Llewellyn）说，蜡进舱时一直是他在清点。他心算片刻，又补上一句：「就算两万二千索尔（sols）吧。」可接下来要算各人的份成，他就只好求助于纸笔了。

我惊讶地发现，他把我跟穆雷尔（Murell）两个人的份成也算了进去。

「喂，咱们能让他们这么算吗？」我悄声问穆雷尔。「我们不过是搭个顺风船。」

「这钱我不能要，」他说。「这些人挣的每一个子儿都用得着。」

说实在的，我又何尝不缺钱，况且我也没有特拉哪家大公司给我开薪水、给我报销开销。不过我随船出来，本来就没指望从中捞到什么，而记者这一行，对捡进腰包的外快必须慎之又慎。就眼下这一回而言，收下并无大碍；可一旦成了惯例，什么祸根都能埋下——偏袒一方、粉饰新闻、枪毙不该枪毙的稿子。这类事我们如今干的已经不少了，比如替比什·韦尔（Bish Ware）和太空港那班人把履带蜗牛（tread-snail）事件轻描淡写，比如从不把人写死，除非死在「本地酒吧」里。这种线，实在难划。

「我们只是客人，」我说。「我们不在这儿当差。」

===Original===
“The dickens you are,” Joe Kivelson contradicted. “Maybe you came aboard as guests, but you’re both part of the crew now. I never saw a prettier shot on a monster than Walt made—took that thing’s head off like a chicken on a chopping block—and he did a swell job of covering for the cutting-up. And he couldn’t have done that if Murell hadn’t handled the boat the way he did, and that was no easy job.”

“Well, let’s talk about that when we get to port,” I said. “Are we going right back, or are we going to try for another monster?”

“I don’t know,” Joe said. “We could stow the wax, if we didn’t get too much, but if we stay out, we’ll have to wait out the wind and by then it’ll be pretty cold.”

“The longer we stay out, the more the cruise’ll cost,” Abdullah Monnahan, the engineer, said, “and the expenses’ll cut into the shares.”

“Tell the truth, I’m sort of antsy to get back,” Joe Kivelson said. “I want to see what’s going on in Port Sandor.”

“So am I,” Murell said. “I want to get some kind of office opened, and get into business. What time will the *Cape Canaveral* be getting in? I want a big cargo, for the first time.”

===Chinese===
「你们才不是什么客人，」乔·基维尔森（Joe Kivelson）驳道。「上船的时候兴许是客人，可如今你们俩都算船上的人了。我从没见过比沃尔特（Walt）那一炮更漂亮的猎怪射击——像剁砧板上的鸡头似的，一下就把那家伙的脑袋削了下来——宰割全程的报道，他也干得漂亮极了。而要不是穆雷尔把小艇操持得那么利落，这些活儿他一样也干不成，那可不是桩轻省差事。」

「这个嘛，等回了港再谈，」我说。「我们这就直接打道回府，还是再试着猎一头？」

「说不准，」乔说。「要是猎得不多，蜡还塞得进舱；可要是留在外头，就得把大风熬过去，到那时天就冷得够呛了。」

「在外头多待一个钟头，这一趟就多花一个钟头的钱，」轮机员阿卜杜拉·莫纳汉（Abdullah Monnahan）说，「开销都要从份成里扣。」

「说实话，我这心里已经有点发急想回去了，」乔·基维尔森说。「我想看看桑多尔港（Port Sandor）眼下到底成了什么局面。」

「我也一样，」穆雷尔说。「我想先弄出个办事处来，把生意做起来。卡纳维拉尔角号（*Cape Canaveral*）什么时候进港？头一趟，我就想装一大船货。」

===Original===
“Oh, not for four hundred hours, at the least,” I said. “The spaceships always try to miss the early-dark and early-daylight storms. It’s hard to get a big ship down in a high wind.”

“That’ll be plenty of time, I suppose,” Murell said. “There’s all that wax you have stored, and what I can get out of the Cooperative stores from crews that reclaim it. But I’m going to have a lot to do.”

“Yes,” I agreed. “Dodging bullets, for one.”

“Oh, I don’t expect any trouble,” Murell said. “This fellow Ravick’s shot his round.”

He was going to say something else, but before he could say it there was a terrific roar forward. The whole ship bucked like a recoiling gun, throwing everybody into a heap, and heeled over to starboard. There were a lot of yells, particularly from those who had been splashed with hot coffee, and somebody was shouting something about the magazines.

===Chinese===
「哦，最少也得四百个小时，」我说。「宇宙飞船总是尽量避开入夜之初和破晓之初的风暴。顶着大风让一艘大船降落，可不是容易事。」

「我想时间够宽裕了，」穆雷尔说。「你们存着的那些蜡是现成的，我还能从合作社（Coop）货栈里，向各船伙计收购他们提取的寄存蜡。不过我要办的事有一大堆。」

「是啊，」我附和道。「头一件就是躲子弹。」

「哦，我不觉得还会出什么乱子，」穆雷尔说。「这位拉维克（Ravick）那一枪已经放过了。」

他正要说点别的，话还没出口，船首方向炸开一声惊天动地的巨响。整条船像后坐的大炮似的猛地一颠，把所有人摔作一堆，随即向右舷倾侧。喊叫声乱成一团，被热咖啡泼了一身的人叫得尤其响，还有人在嚷嚷什么弹药舱。

===Original===
“The magazines are aft, you dunderhead,” Joe Kivelson told him, shoving himself to his feet. “Stay put, everybody; I’ll see what it is.”

He pulled open the forward door. An instant later, he had slammed it shut and was dogging it fast.

“Hull must be ruptured forward; we’re making water. It’s spouting up the hatch from the engine room like a geyser,” he said. “Ramón, go see what it’s like in the boat berth. The rest of you, follow him, and grab all the food and warm clothing you can. We’re going to have to abandon.”

He stood by the doorway aft, shoving people through and keeping them from jamming up, saying: “Take it easy, now; don’t crowd. We’ll all get out.” There wasn’t any panic. A couple of men were in the doorway of the little galley when I came past, handing out cases of food. As nothing was coming out at the instant, I kept on, and on the way back to the boat-berth hatch, I pulled down as many parkas and pairs of overpants as I could carry, squeezing past Tom, who was collecting fleece-lined hip boots. Each pair was buckled together at the tops; a hunter always does that, even at home ashore.

===Chinese===
「弹药舱在船尾，你这木头脑袋，」乔·基维尔森一边撑着站起来一边对他说。「都待在原地别动；我去看看怎么回事。」

他拉开了前部舱门。转瞬之间，他已砰地把门撞上，飞快地扳动门扣扣死。

「船首部位的船壳准是裂了；船正在进水。水像间歇泉一样顺着机舱舱口往上喷，」他说。「拉蒙，去艇库看看那边什么情形。其余的人，跟着他去，能抢多少食物和保暖衣物就抢多少。我们得弃船。」

他守在通往船尾的门洞旁，把人一个个往里推，不叫他们挤成一团，嘴里说着：「别慌，慢点儿；不要挤。咱们都出得去。」没有人惊慌失措。我打小厨房门口经过时，两个汉子正往外交付成箱的食物。眼下他们手里一时没递出东西，我便接着往前走；折回艇库舱口的路上，我顺手扯下风雪大衣和罩裤，能抱多少抱多少，侧身挤过正在收拢羊毛衬里及胯长靴的汤姆（Tom）。每双长靴都在靴口处两两扣在一起；猎怪手向来有这个习惯，哪怕上了岸在家也一样。

===Original===
Ramón had the hatch open, and had opened the top hatch of the boat, below. I threw my double armload of clothing down through it and slid down after, getting out of the way of the load of boots Tom dumped ahead of him. Joe Kivelson came down last, carrying the ship’s log and some other stuff. A little water was trickling over the edge of the hatch above.

“It’s squirting up from below in a dozen places,” he said, after he’d sealed the boat. “The whole front of the ship must be blown out.”

“Well, now we know what happened to Simon MacGregor’s *Claymore*,” I said, more to myself than to anybody else.

Joe and Hans Cronje, the gunner, were getting a rocket out of the locker, detaching the harpoon and fitting on an explosive warhead. He stopped, while he and Cronje were loading it into the after launcher, and nodded at me.

===Chinese===
拉蒙已经打开了舱口，也打开了下方小艇的顶舱口。我把满抱的衣物统统扔了下去，人跟着滑下去，闪身避开汤姆先撂下来的那一堆长靴。乔·基维尔森最后一个下来，带着航海日志和一些别的东西。上方舱口的边缘，已经有细细的水流往下淌了。

「水正从下面十几个窟窿往外喷，」他封好小艇之后说。「整条船的前半截准是炸飞了。」

「好哇，西蒙·麦格雷戈（Simon MacGregor）那条阔剑号（*Claymore*）遭了什么，如今我们总算知道了，」我说，与其说是讲给谁听，不如说是自言自语。

乔和炮手汉斯·克龙涅（Hans Cronje）正从储柜里往外取一枚火箭，卸下鱼叉，换装爆破弹头。趁他和克龙涅把火箭往艉部发射筒里装的当口，他停了停手，朝我点了点头。

===Original===
“That’s what I think, too,” he said. “Everybody grab onto something; we’re getting the door open.”

I knew what was coming and started hugging a stanchion as though it were a long-lost sweetheart, and Murell, who didn’t but knew enough to imitate those who did, hugged it from the other side. The rocket whooshed out of the launcher and went off with a deafening bang outside. For an instant, nothing happened, and I told Murell not to let go. Then the lock burst in and the water, at seventy fathoms’ pressure, hit the boat. Abdullah had gotten the engines on and was backing against it. After a little, the pressure equalized and we went out the broken lock stern first.

We circled and passed over the *Javelin*, and then came back. She was lying in the ooze, a quarter over on her side, and her whole bow was blown out to port. Joe Kivelson got the square box he had brought down from the ship along with the log, fussed a little with it, and then launched it out the disposal port. It was a radio locator. Sometimes a lucky ship will get more wax than the holds’ capacity; they pack it in skins and anchor it on the bottom, and drop one of those gadgets with it. It would keep on sending a directional signal and the name of the ship for a couple of years.

“Do you really think it was sabotage?” Murell was asking me. Blowing up a ship with sixteen men aboard must have seemed sort of extreme to him. Maybe that wasn’t according to Terran business ethics. “Mightn’t it have been a power unit?”

===Chinese===
「我也是这么想的，」他说。「都抓紧点什么东西；我们要开舱门了。」

我知道接下来是什么，一把抱住一根立柱，抱得像搂着失散多年的老情人；穆雷尔虽然不明就里，倒也识相，照着懂行的人学，从另一侧把柱子抱住了。火箭嗖地窜出发射筒，在外头炸出一声震耳欲聋的巨响。一时之间什么也没发生，我叮嘱穆雷尔千万别撒手。随后舱门被冲开，七十英寻水压的海水砸到艇身上。阿卜杜拉已经发动引擎，正顶着水流开倒车。过了一小会儿，压力终于扯平，我们艇尾在前，从撞破的舱门里冲了出去。

我们兜了个圈子，从标枪号（*Javelin*）上方驶过，又折了回来。她歪在软泥里，船身朝一侧倾过了四分之一，整个船头在左舷方向被炸飞了。乔·基维尔森取出他随航海日志一道带下船的那只方匣子，摆弄了几下，从排放口把它发射了出去。那是一具无线电定位器（radio locator）。有时候运气好的船猎到的蜡会超出货舱容量，他们便把蜡装进皮囊，锚沉在海底，随匣子丢下这么一个装置。往后一两年里，它会不断发出定向信号，报出船名。

「你当真认为这是蓄意破坏？」穆雷尔在问我。把一条载着十六个人的船炸掉，在他看来未免有点过分。特拉人的生意经里，兴许没有这一条。「会不会是动力单元出了岔子？」

===Original===
“No. Power units don’t blow, and if one did, it would vaporize the whole ship and a quarter of a cubic mile of water around her. No, that was old fashioned country-style chemical explosive. Cataclysmite, probably.”

“Ravick?” he asked, rather unnecessarily.

“You know how well he can get along without you and Joe Kivelson, and here’s a chance to get along without both of you together.” Everybody in the boat was listening, so I continued: “How much do you know about this fellow Devis, who strained his back at the last moment?”

“Engine room’s where he could have planted something,” Joe Kivelson said.

“He was in there by himself for a while, the morning after the meeting,” Abdullah Monnahan added.

===Chinese===
「不会。动力单元不爆炸；真要是爆了，整条船连同她周围四分之一立方英里的海水都会被气化。不，那是老式的、乡土风味的化学炸药。多半是灾变素（cataclysmite）。」

「拉维克干的？」他问，这话其实问得多余。

「你清楚，没了你和乔·基维尔森，他的日子能过得多滋润；如今正好有个机会，把你们俩一并打发掉。」艇上人人都在听，我便接着往下说：「那个临到最后关头闪了腰的德维斯（Devis），你们对他知多少？」

「机舱正是他能做手脚的地方，」乔·基维尔森说。

「开会过后的第二天早上，他一个人在里头待过一阵，」阿卜杜拉·莫纳汉补充道。

===Original===
“And he disappeared between the meeting room and the elevator, during the fight,” Tom mentioned. “And when he showed up, he hadn’t been marked up any. I’d have thought he’d have been pretty badly beaten—unless they knew he was one of their own gang.”

“We’re going to look Devis up when we get back,” somebody said pleasantly.

“If we get back,” Ramón Llewellyn told him. “That’s going to take some doing.”

“We have the boat,” Hans Cronje said. “It’s a little crowded, but we can make it back to Port Sandor.”

“I hope we can,” Abe Clifford, the navigator, said. “Shall we take her up, Joe?”

“Yes, see what it’s like on top,” the skipper replied.

===Chinese===
「还有，斗殴进行的时候，他从会议室到电梯之间那一段就没了人影，」汤姆提到。「等他再露面，身上连块淤青都没挂。我原以为他准得挨一顿狠揍——除非人家早知道他是自己一伙的。」

「等回了港，我们要好好『拜访』一下德维斯，」有人和颜悦色地说。

「得先回得去再说，」拉蒙·卢埃林对他说。「那可不是件容易事。」

「艇还在咱们手里，」汉斯·克龙涅说。「挤是挤了点，可总能回到桑多尔港。」

「但愿如此，」领航员艾布·克利福德（Abe Clifford）说。「乔，咱们升上去瞧瞧？」

「好，看看上头是什么光景，」船长答道。

===Original===
Going up, we passed a monster at about thirty fathoms. It stuck its neck out and started for us. Monnahan tilted the boat almost vertical and put on everything the engines had, lift and drive parallel. An instant later, we broke the surface and shot into the air.

The wind hit the boat as though it had been a ping-pong ball, and it was several seconds, and bad seconds at that, before Monnahan regained even a semblance of control. There was considerable bad language, and several of the crew had bloody noses. Monnahan tried to get the boat turned into the wind. A circuit breaker popped, and red lights blazed all over the instrument panel. He eased off and let the wind take over, and for a while we were flying in front of it like a rifle bullet. Gradually, he nosed down and submerged.

“Well, that’s that.” Joe Kivelson said, when we were back in the underwater calm again. “We’ll have to stay under till the wind’s over. Don’t anybody move around or breathe any deeper than you have to. We’ll have to conserve oxygen.”

===Chinese===
上浮途中，我们在大约三十英寻深处经过了一头怪兽。它探出脖子，朝我们直扑过来。莫纳汉把艇身几乎竖了起来，引擎开到极限，升举与驱动并联齐发。眨眼工夫，我们冲破海面，射入空中。

风撞上小艇，把它当乒乓球似的打；过了好几秒——而且是难熬的几秒——莫纳汉才勉强找回一丝一毫的操控。舱里骂声不绝，好几名船员鼻血都磕了出来。莫纳汉想把艇头调转顶风。断路器啪地跳了闸，仪表板上红灯一片狂闪。他放缓了劲头，任凭风接手摆布，有那么一阵子，我们像一颗出膛的步枪子弹，被风推着飞。渐渐地，他压低艇首，潜入了水中。

「唉，事已至此。」重新回到水下的平静之后，乔·基维尔森说。「在风过去之前，我们只能待在下面。谁也别乱动，呼吸能多浅就多浅。我们得省着氧气用。」

===Original===
“Isn’t the boat equipped with electrolytic gills?” Murell asked.

“Sure, to supply oxygen for a maximum of six men. We have sixteen in here.”

“How long will our air last, for sixteen of us?” I asked.

“About eight hours.”

It would take us fifty to get to Port Sandor, running submerged. The wind wouldn’t even begin to fall in less than twenty.

===Chinese===
「这艇上没装电解鳃（electrolytic gills）吗？」穆雷尔问。

「装是装了，顶多供六个人的氧气。我们这里头可塞了十六个。」

「我们十六个人，这点空气撑得了多久？」我问。

「八个钟头左右。」

而潜航回桑多尔港，要五十个小时。这场风不到二十个小时，连转弱的苗头都不会有。

===Original===
“We can go south, to the coast of Hermann Reuch’s Land,” Abe Clifford, the navigator, said. “Let me figure something out.”

He dug out a slide rule and a pencil and pad and sat down with his back to the back of the pilot’s seat, under the light. Everybody watched him in a silence which Joe Kivelson broke suddenly by bellowing:

“Dumont! You light that pipe and I’ll feed it to you!”

Old Piet Dumont grabbed the pipe out of his mouth with one hand and pocketed his lighter with the other.

===Chinese===
「我们可以往南走，去赫尔曼·罗伊希之地（Hermann Reuch's Land）的海岸，」领航员艾布·克利福德说。「让我来盘算盘算。」

他掏出计算尺、铅笔和便笺，背靠着飞行员座椅的椅背，就着灯光坐定。人人都盯着他，舱内一片寂静，忽然被乔·基维尔森的一声怒吼打破：

「杜蒙（Dumont）！你再敢点那烟斗，我就把它塞进你嘴里喂你吃！」

老皮特·杜蒙（Piet Dumont）一手薅下嘴里的烟斗，另一手把打火机揣回了兜里。

===Original===
“Gosh, Joe; I guess I just wasn’t thinking …” he began.

“Well, give me that pipe.” Joe put it in the drawer under the charts. “Now you won’t have it handy the next time you don’t think.”
