After a while, Abe Clifford looked up. “Ship’s position I don’t have exactly; somewhere around East 25° Longitude, South 20° Latitude. I can’t work out our present position at all, except that we’re somewhere around South 30° Latitude. The locator signal is almost exactly north-by-northeast of us. If we keep it dead astern, we’ll come out in Sancerre Bay, on Hermann Reuch’s Land. If we make that, we’re all right. We’ll be in the lee of the Hacksaw Mountains, and we can surface from time to time to change air, and as soon as the wind falls we can start for home.”

Then he and Abdullah and Joe went into a huddle, arguing about cruising speed submerged. The results weren’t so heartening.

===Chinese===
「哎呀，乔；我想我一时没过脑子……」他开了腔。

「行了，烟斗给我。」乔把它收进了海图底下的抽屉。「这下好了，下回你再不过脑子，它也不在手边了。」

过了一阵，艾布·克利福德抬起头来。「沉船的位置我拿不准；大致在东经二十五度、南纬二十度一带。我们眼下的位置根本推算不出来，只知道差不多在南纬三十度上下。定位器信号几乎正在我们的正北北东方向。让它始终对着艇尾正后方，我们就能驶到赫尔曼·罗伊希之地的桑塞尔湾（Sancerre Bay）。只要到了那儿，我们就都好办了。我们在钢锯山脉（Hacksaw Mountains）的背风面，可以不时浮上来换换空气，风一停就能动身回家。」

接着他跟阿卜杜拉、乔凑作一堆，争论水下巡航速度该定多少。争出来的结果不那么令人宽心。

===Original===
“It looks like a ten-hour trip, submerged,” Joe said. “That’s two hours too long, and there’s no way of getting more oxygen out of the gills than we’re getting now. We’ll just have to use less. Everybody lie down and breathe as shallowly as possible, and don’t do anything to use energy. I’m going to get on the radio and see what I can raise.”

*Big chance*, I thought. These boat radios were only used for communicating with the ship while scouting; they had a strain-everything range of about three hundred miles. Hunter-ships don’t crowd that close together when they’re working. Still, there was a chance that somebody else might be sitting it out on the bottom within hearing. So Abe took the controls and kept the signal from the wreck of the *Javelin* dead astern, and Joe Kivelson began speaking into the radio:

“Mayday, Mayday, Mayday, Mayday. Captain Kivelson, *Javelin*, calling. My ship was wrecked by an explosion; all hands now in scout boat, proceeding toward Sancerre Bay, on course south-by-southwest from the wreck. Locator signal is being broadcast from the *Javelin*. Other than that, we do not know our position. Calling all craft, calling Mayday.”

===Chinese===
「潜航过去，看来得十个钟头，」乔说。「这就多出两个钟头了，而电解鳃眼下供出的氧气，一丝一毫也多不出来。我们只能少用。全体躺下，呼吸尽量放浅，别干任何耗费力气的事。我这就上电台，看看能呼到谁。」

*好大的指望*，我心想。这种艇载电台，原本只用来在外出侦察时和大船通话；把浑身解数全算上，通信距离也就三百英里上下。猎怪船干活时，彼此可不会挤得那么近。不过，兴许另有一条船也正坐底熬风、恰好在我们听得见的地方，这也难说。于是艾布操起操纵器，让标枪号残骸发出的信号始终对着艇尾正后方，乔·基维尔森则对着电台开了口：

「梅戴（Mayday），梅戴，梅戴，梅戴。标枪号船长基维尔森呼叫。我船遭爆炸失事；全体人员现已转入侦察艇（scout boat），正循南南西航向自沉船驶往桑塞尔湾。标枪号正在播发定位器信号。除此之外，我方不知自身位置。呼叫所有船只，呼叫梅戴。」

===Original===
He stopped talking. The radio was silent except for an occasional frying-fat crackle of static. Then he began over again.

I curled up, trying to keep my feet out of anybody’s face and my face clear of anybody else’s feet. Somebody began praying, and somebody else told him to belay it, he was wasting oxygen. I tried to go to sleep, which was the only practical thing to do. I must have succeeded. When I woke again, Joe Kivelson was saying, exasperatedly:

“Mayday, Mayday, Mayday, Mayday …”

===Chinese===
他停了下来。电台里一片死寂，只偶尔响起几声煎油似的静电噼啪。接着，他又从头呼叫起来。

我蜷起身子，尽量不让自己的脚杵到谁的脸上，也不让自己的脸挡了谁的脚。有人开始祷告，立刻有人叫他打住，说他白白糟蹋氧气。我设法入睡，这是唯一实际可行的正事。想必是真睡着了。等我再醒来时，乔·基维尔森正又急又恼地念叨着：

「梅戴，梅戴，梅戴，梅戴……」

## Meeting Out of Order / 会议失序

===Original===
Dad called the spaceport hospital, after dinner, and talked to Doc Rojansky. Murell was asleep, and in no danger whatever. They’d given him a couple of injections and a sedative, and his system was throwing off the poison satisfactorily. He’d be all right, but they thought he ought to be allowed to rest at the hospital for a while.

By then, it was time for me to leave for Hunters’ Hall. Julio and Mrs. Laden were having their dinner, and Dad and Bish went up to the editorial office. I didn’t take a car. Hunters’ Hall was only a half dozen blocks south of the *Times*, toward the waterfront. I carried my radio-under-false-pretense slung from my shoulder, and started downtown on foot.

The business district was pretty well lighted, both from the ceiling and by the stores and restaurants. Most of the latter were in the open, with small kitchen and storage buildings. At a table at one of them I saw two petty officers from the *Peenemünde* with a couple of girls, so I knew the ship wasn’t leaving immediately. Going past the Municipal Building, I saw some activity, and an unusually large number of police gathered around the vehicle port. Ravick must have his doubts about how the price cut was going to be received, and Mort Hallstock was mobilizing his storm troopers to give him support in case he needed it. I called in about that, and Dad told me fretfully to be sure to stay out of trouble.

===Chinese===
晚饭后，爸爸给太空港医院打了电话，同罗扬斯基医生（Doc Rojansky）谈了谈。穆雷尔（Murell）睡着了，毫无危险。他们给他打了几针、用了镇静剂，他的身体正在顺利地把毒排出去。他会没事的，不过他们认为，还是该让他在医院里休养一阵。

这时候，我也该动身去猎手会堂（Hunters' Hall）了。胡利奥（Julio）和莱登太太（Mrs. Laden）正在吃晚饭，爸爸和比什（Bish）上编辑部的楼去了。我没叫车。猎手会堂离《时报》（*Times*）不过六个街区，在南边、朝着滨水区。我把那台靠谎话蒙来的电台挎在肩上，步行往城中心去。

商业区照明相当不错，头顶的城市顶棚和各家店铺、餐馆都亮着灯。餐馆大多是露天的，只带着小小的厨房和储藏小屋。在其中一家的桌旁，我看见佩内明德号（*Peenemünde*）上的两名军士陪着两个姑娘，由此我知道这船一时半会儿还不会走。路过市政大楼（Municipal Building）时，我瞧见那里有些动静，车港四周聚着多得反常的警察。看来拉维克（Ravick）对降价会遭到什么反响心里没底，莫特·霍尔斯特克（Mort Hallstock）正把他的冲锋队动员起来，以备需要时给他撑腰。我用电台汇报了这一情况，爸爸焦躁地叮嘱我千万要置身事外、别惹祸上身。

===Original===
Hunters’ Hall was a four-story building, fairly substantial as buildings that don’t have to support the roof go, with a landing stage on top and a vehicle park underneath. As I came up, I saw a lot of cars and jeeps and ships’ boats grounded in and around it, and a crowd of men, almost all of them in boat-clothes and wearing whiskers, including quite a few characters who had never been out in a hunter-ship in their lives but were members in the best of good standing of the Cooperative. I also saw a few of Hallstock’s uniformed thugs standing around with their thumbs in their gun belts or twirling their truncheons.

I took an escalator up to the second floor, which was one big room, with the escalators and elevators in the rear. It was the social room, decorated with photos and models and solidigraphs of hunter-ships, photos of record-sized monsters lashed alongside ships before cutting-up, group pictures of ships’s crews, monster tusks, dried slashers and halberd fish, and a whole monster head, its tusked mouth open. There was a big crowd there, too, at the bar, at the game machines, or just standing around in groups talking.

I saw Tom Kivelson and his father and Oscar Fujisawa, and went over to join them. Joe Kivelson is just an outsize edition of his son, with a blond beard that’s had thirty-five years’ more growth. Oscar is skipper of the *Pequod*﻿—he wouldn’t have looked baffled if Bish Ware called him Captain Ahab﻿—and while his family name is Old Terran Japanese, he had blue eyes and red hair and beard. He was almost as big as Joe Kivelson.

===Chinese===
猎手会堂是一座四层建筑，以无须承托城市顶棚的房屋而论，算是相当结实了，顶层有个起降台，底下有个停车场。我走近时，看见大批轿车、吉普和船载小艇停在楼里楼外，还有一大群汉子，几乎个个一身船上装束、满脸络腮胡，其中不乏这辈子从没上过猎怪船出过海、却在合作社（Cooperative）里挂着顶好信誉的会员角色。我还看见霍尔斯特克的几个制服打手站在四周，大拇指勾在枪带里，或是转着警棍。

我乘自动扶梯上了二楼。那是一整间大厅，扶梯和电梯都在后部。这是会员社交厅，装点着猎怪船的照片、模型和立体显像（solidigraph），有破纪录尺寸的怪兽宰割前拴缚在船侧的照片，有各船船员的合影，有怪兽长牙、晒干的砍杀兽（slasher）和戟鱼（halberd fish），还有一整颗怪兽头，长牙支棱着的嘴大张着。这里也聚着一大群人，有的在吧台，有的在游戏机前，也有的只是成群站着聊天。

我看见了汤姆·基维尔森（Tom Kivelson）和他父亲，还有奥斯卡·藤泽（Oscar Fujisawa），便走过去加入他们。乔·基维尔森（Joe Kivelson）就是他儿子放大了一号的版本，一部金色的胡子多长了三十五个年头。奥斯卡是裴廓德号（*Pequod*）的船长——就算比什·韦尔（Bish Ware）当面叫他亚哈船长（Captain Ahab），他也不会露出半点困惑——他家姓氏虽是古特拉的日本姓，人却长着蓝眼睛、红头发红胡子。块头跟乔·基维尔森不相上下。

===Original===
“Hello, Walt,” Joe greeted me. “What’s this Tom’s been telling me about Bish Ware shooting a tread-snail that was going to sting Mr. Murell?”

“Just about that,” I said. “That snail must have crawled out from between two stacks of wax as we came up. We never saw it till it was all over. It was right beside Murell and had its stinger up when Bish shot it.”

“He took an awful chance,” Kivelson said. “He might of shot Mr. Murell.”

I suppose it would look that way to Joe. He is the planet’s worst pistol shot, so according to him nobody can hit anything with a pistol.

===Chinese===
「你好啊，沃尔特（Walt），」乔跟我打招呼。「汤姆跟我说比什·韦尔开枪打死了一条要蜇穆雷尔先生的履带蜗牛，是怎么回事？」

「就是这么回事，」我说。「那蜗牛准是趁我们上船的当口，从两垛蜡之间爬出来的。事情结束时我们才看见它。比什开枪的时候，它就在穆雷尔身边，毒刺都已经竖起来了。」

「他这是在玩命，」基维尔森说。「他没准会把穆雷尔先生打着了。」

我琢磨着，在乔眼里自然是这样。他是全行星枪法最臭的手枪射手，所以照他看来，谁也别想用手枪打中任何东西。

===Original===
“He wouldn’t have taken any chance not shooting,” I said. “If he hadn’t, we’d have been running the Murell story with black borders.”

Another man came up, skinny, red hair, sharp-pointed nose. His name was Al Devis, and he was Joe Kivelson’s engineer’s helper. He wanted to know about the tread-snail shooting, so I had to go over it again. I hadn’t anything to add to what Tom had told them already, but I was the *Times*, and if the *Times* says so it’s true.

“Well, I wouldn’t want any drunk like Bish Ware shooting around me with a pistol,” Joe Kivelson said.

That’s relative, too. Joe doesn’t drink.

===Chinese===
「可他不开枪才是真玩命，」我说。「他要是没打，我们登穆雷尔的报道就得加黑框了。」

又一个人凑了过来，瘦精精的，红头发，尖鼻子。他叫阿尔·德维斯（Al Devis），是乔·基维尔森的轮机助手。他想了解打死履带蜗牛的经过，我只好又讲了一遍。汤姆已经对他们讲过的那些，我实在添不出什么新东西，可我代表着《时报》，而《时报》说了，那就是事实。

「反正，我可不愿让比什·韦尔这样的酒鬼在我旁边开枪，」乔·基维尔森说。

这话也是相对的。乔滴酒不沾。

===Original===
“Don’t kid yourself, Joe,” Oscar told him. “I saw Bish shoot a knife out of a man’s hand, one time, in One Eye Swanson’s. Didn’t scratch the guy; hit the blade. One Eye has the knife, with the bullet mark on it, over his back bar, now.”

“Well, was he drunk then?” Joe asked.

“Well, he had to hang onto the bar with one hand while he fired with the other.” Then he turned to me. “How is Murell, now?” he asked.

I told him what the hospital had given us. Everybody seemed much relieved. I wouldn’t have thought that a celebrated author of whom nobody had ever heard before would be the center of so much interest in monster-hunting circles. I kept looking at my watch while we were talking. After a while, the *Times* newscast came on the big screen across the room, and everybody moved over toward it.

===Chinese===
「别自欺欺人了，乔，」奥斯卡对他说。「有一回，我在『独眼斯旺森』（One Eye Swanson's）亲眼见比什把一个人手里的刀打了出去。那人一根汗毛没伤着；子弹打在刀刃上。那把带着弹痕的刀，如今就挂在独眼柜台后面的墙上。」

「那么，他那时候醉没醉？」乔问。

「这么说吧，他开枪的时候，一只手得抓着吧台才站得稳。」说着他转向我。「穆雷尔现在怎么样了？」他问。

我把医院通报的情况告诉了他。大家似乎都大大松了口气。我真没想到，一位从前谁也没听说过的鼎鼎大名的作家，居然会成为猎怪界上下如此关注的中心。谈话间我不停地看表。过了一会儿，房间那头的大屏幕上开始播《时报》的新闻节目，人人都朝那边挪了过去。

===Original===
They watched the *Peenemünde* being towed down and berthed, and the audiovisual interview with Murell. Then Dad came on the screen with a record player in front of them, and gave them a playoff of my interview with Leo Belsher.

Ordinary bad language I do not mind. I’m afraid I use a little myself, while struggling with some of the worn-out equipment we have at the paper. But when Belsher began explaining about how the price of wax had to be cut again, to thirty-five centisols a pound, the language those hunters used positively smelled. I noticed, though, that a lot of the crowd weren’t saying anything at all. They would be Ravick’s boys, and they would have orders not to start anything before the meeting.

===Chinese===
大家看着佩内明德号被拖下来停入泊位，看着对穆雷尔的声像采访。接着爸爸出现在屏幕上，面前摆着一台唱机，给他们播放了我采访利奥·贝尔歇（Leo Belsher）的录音。

寻常的粗话我并不介意。跟报社里那些破旧设备较劲的时候，恐怕我自己也免不了来上几句。可等贝尔歇开始解释蜡价为什么必须再降、降到三十五厘索尔（centisol）一磅时，那些猎怪手骂出来的话简直都冒臭气了。不过我注意到，人群里有好些个一言不发。那准是拉维克的人，领了吩咐，开会前不许先动手惹事。

===Original===
“Wonder if he’s going to try to give us that stuff about substitutes?” Oscar said.

“Well, what are you going to do?” I asked.

“I’ll tell you what we’re not going to do,” Joe Kivelson said. “We’re not going to take his price cut. If he won’t pay our price, he can use his [deleted by censor] substitutes.”

“You can’t sell wax anywhere else, can you?”

===Chinese===
「不知道他会不会又拿代用品那套货色来糊弄我们？」奥斯卡说。

「那你们打算怎么办？」我问。

「我告诉你我们不打算干什么，」乔·基维尔森说。「我们不打算接受他压的价。他要是不肯按我们的价钱付，就让他用他那〔脏话已遭新闻检查官删节〕的代用品好了。」

「你们也没别的地方可以卖蜡，不是吗？」

===Original===
“Is that so, we can’t?” Joe started.

Before he could say anything else, Oscar was interrupting:

“We can eat for a while, even if we don’t sell wax. Sigurd Ngozori’ll carry us for a while and make loans on wax. But if the wax stops coming in, Kapstaad Chemical’s going to start wondering why.﻿ ﻿…”

By this time, other *Javelin* men came drifting over﻿—Ramón Llewellyn, the mate, and Abdullah Monnahan, the engineer, and Abe Clifford, the navigator, and some others. I talked with some of them, and then drifted off in the direction of the bar, where I found another hunter captain, Mohandas Gandhi Feinberg, whom everybody simply called the Mahatma. He didn’t resemble his namesake. He had a curly black beard with a twisted black cigar sticking out of it, and nobody, after one look at him, would have mistaken him for any apostle of nonviolence.

===Chinese===
「哦，是吗，就没别的地方？」乔冒起火来。

他话还没说完，奥斯卡就插了进来：

「就算卖不出蜡，我们也能撑上一阵子。西格德·恩戈佐里（Sigurd Ngozori）会先让我们赊着账，还会收蜡放款。可要是蜡断了来路，卡普斯塔德化学公司（Kapstaad Chemical）就要犯嘀咕了：这是为什么？……」

这时候，标枪号（*Javelin*）上的其他人也陆陆续续聚了过来——大副拉蒙·卢埃林（Ramón Llewellyn）、轮机师阿卜杜拉·莫纳汉（Abdullah Monnahan）、领航员艾布·克利福德（Abe Clifford），还有几个别的。我同他们中几位聊了聊，随后朝吧台那边晃了过去，在那里又碰上一位猎怪船船长，莫汉达斯·甘地·范伯格（Mohandas Gandhi Feinberg），大家干脆都叫他「圣雄」（the Mahatma）。他跟他那位同名者毫无相似之处。他生着一部黑色卷胡子，胡子里斜叼着一支扭弯了的黑雪茄，任何人只要看他一眼，就不会把他误认成什么非暴力主义的使徒。

===Original===
He had a proposition he was enlisting support for. He wanted balloting at meetings to be limited to captains of active hunter-ships, the captains to vote according to expressed wishes of a majority of their crews. It was a good scheme, though it would have sounded better if the man who was advocating it hadn’t been a captain himself. At least, it would have disenfranchised all Ravick’s permanently unemployed “unemployed hunters.” The only trouble was, there was no conceivable way of getting it passed. It was too much like trying to curtail the powers of Parliament by act of Parliament.

The gang from the street level started coming up, and scattered in twos and threes around the hall, ready for trouble. I’d put on my radio when I’d joined the Kivelsons and Oscar, and I kept it on, circulating around and letting it listen to the conversations. The Ravick people were either saying nothing or arguing that Belsher was doing the best he could, and if Kapstaad wouldn’t pay more than thirty-five centisols, it wasn’t his fault. Finally, the call bell for the meeting began clanging, and the crowd began sliding over toward the elevators and escalators.

The meeting room was on the floor above, at the front of the building, beyond a narrow hall and a door at which a couple of Ravick henchmen wearing guns and sergeant-at-arms brassards were making everybody check their knives and pistols. They passed me by without getting my arsenal, which consisted of a sleep-gas projector camouflaged as a jumbo-sized lighter and twenty sols in two rolls of forty quarter sols each. One of these inside a fist can make a big difference.

===Chinese===
他正为一个提案四处拉支持。他主张把会议表决权限定在现役猎怪船船长范围内，船长按船员多数明白表达的意愿投票。这主意不坏，只是由他这个当船长的人自己提出来，听上去就没那么动听了。至少，这办法能把拉维克那帮永远失业的「失业猎手」统统排除在投票之外。唯一的麻烦是，根本想不出任何办法能让它通过。这就好比想靠议会法案来削减议会的权力。

地面层来的那伙人开始上楼了，三三两两散布在大厅各处，一副随时准备闹事的架势。我加入基维尔森父子那边时就把电台打开了，一直开着，在各处转来转去，让它听着各方谈话。拉维克的人要么一言不发，要么就争辩说贝尔歇已经尽了力，卡普斯塔德不肯出三十五厘索尔以上的价，不能怪他。最后，开会的铃声当当地响了起来，人群开始朝电梯和扶梯涌去。

会议室在再上一层，位于大楼前部，穿过一道窄廊、经一扇门进去；门口站着拉维克的两个佩枪的喽啰，戴着纠察（sergeant-at-arms）袖章，让每个人把刀子和手枪寄存了才能进。他们放我过去时没动我的军火库——那里面有一具伪装成超大号打火机的催眠气体喷射器（sleep-gas projector），还有二十索尔（sol）的硬币，分成两卷，每卷四十枚四分之一索尔。其中一卷攥进拳头里，能顶大用。

===Original===
Ravick and Belsher and the secretary of the Coop, who was a little scrawny henpecked-husband type who never had an opinion of his own in his life, were all sitting back of a big desk on a dais in front. After as many of the crowd who could had found seats and the rest, including the Press, were standing in the rear, Ravick pounded with the chunk of monster tusk he used for a gavel and called the meeting to order.

“There’s a bunch of old business,” he said, “but I’m going to rule that aside for the moment. We have with us this evening our representative on Terra, Mr. Leo Belsher, whom I wish to present. Mr. Belsher.”

Belsher got up. Ravick started clapping his hands to indicate that applause was in order. A few of his zombies clapped their hands; everybody else was quiet. Belsher held up a hand.

===Chinese===
拉维克、贝尔歇，还有合作社的秘书——一个干瘦矮小、受气包式的小男人，一辈子没有过自己的主见——都坐在前头讲台后一张大桌子后面。等能找到座位的人都落了座，其余的人（包括新闻界）在后面站着，拉维克用他那截当议事槌使的怪兽长牙敲了敲，宣布开会。

「旧议案倒是有一大堆，」他说，「但我现在裁定，暂且搁置不议。今晚，我们驻特拉（Terra）的代表利奥·贝尔歇先生与我们同在，我愿将他介绍给大家。贝尔歇先生。」

贝尔歇站起身。拉维克带头鼓起掌来，示意大家该鼓掌了。他那几个行尸走肉般的跟班拍了拍手；其余的人都默不作声。贝尔歇举起一只手。

===Original===
“Please don’t applaud,” he begged. “What I have to tell you isn’t anything to applaud about.”

“You’re tootin’ well right it isn’t!” somebody directly in front of me said, very distinctly.

“I’m very sorry to have to bring this news to you, but the fact is that Kapstaad Chemical Products, Ltd., is no longer able to pay forty-five centisols a pound. This price is being scaled down to thirty-five centisols. I want you to understand that Kapstaad Chemical wants to give you every cent they can, but business conditions no longer permit them to pay the old price. Thirty-five is the absolute maximum they can pay and still meet competition﻿—”

“Aaah, knock it off, Belsher!” somebody shouted. “We heard all that rot on the screen.”

===Chinese===
「请别鼓掌，」他恳求道。「我要告诉大家的事，没有什么值得鼓掌的。」

「你说得一点儿不错，本来就不值得！」我正前方有人一字一顿、清清楚楚地说。

「我非常抱歉，不得不把这个消息带给你们：卡普斯塔德化学产品有限公司（Kapstaad Chemical Products, Ltd.）再也付不起四十五厘索尔一磅的价钱了。这个价格将下调到三十五厘索尔。我要你们明白，卡普斯塔德化学公司愿意付给你们它所能付出的每一分钱，但行情已经不容许他们再照旧价付款了。三十五厘索尔是他们的绝对极限，再高就无法应付竞争——」

「啊——省省吧，贝尔歇！」有人吼道。「屏幕上那一套鬼话我们都听过了。」

===Original===
“How about our contract?” somebody else asked. “We do have a contract with Kapstaad, don’t we?”

“Well, the contract will have to be renegotiated. They’ll pay thirty-five centisols or they’ll pay nothing.”

“They can try getting along without wax. Or try buying it somewhere else!”

“Yes; those wonderful synthetic substitutes!”

“Mr. Chairman,” Oscar Fujisawa called out. “I move that this organization reject the price of thirty-five centisols a pound for tallow-wax, as offered by, or through, Leo Belsher at this meeting.”

===Chinese===
「那我们的合同怎么说？」另有人问。「我们跟卡普斯塔德是有合同的，不是吗？」

「这个嘛，合同只好重新谈了。他们要么按三十五厘索尔付，要么一个子儿也不付。」

「他们可以试试没有蜡怎么过活。要么试试上别处买去！」

「是啊；还有那些妙不可言的合成代用品呢！」

「主席先生，」奥斯卡·藤泽扬声说道。「我动议：本组织否决由利奥·贝尔歇在本次会议上提出或经其转达的脂蜡（tallow-wax）每磅三十五厘索尔的价格。」

===Original===
Ravick began clamoring that Oscar was out of order, that Leo Belsher had the floor.

“I second Captain Fujisawa’s motion,” Mohandas Feinberg said.

“And Leo Belsher doesn’t have the floor; he’s not a member of the Cooperative,” Tom Kivelson declared. “He’s our hired employee, and as soon as this present motion is dealt with, I intend moving that we fire him and hire somebody else.”

“I move to amend Captain Fujisawa’s motion,” Joe Kivelson said. “I move that the motion, as amended, read, ‘﻿—and stipulate a price of seventy-five centisols a pound.’”

“You’re crazy!” Belsher almost screamed.

Seventy-five was the old price, from which he and Ravick had been reducing until they’d gotten down to forty-five.

===Chinese===
拉维克嚷嚷起来，说奥斯卡不合程序，说发言权在利奥·贝尔歇手里。

「我附议藤泽船长的动议，」莫汉达斯·范伯格说。

「再说利奥·贝尔歇也没有发言权；他不是合作社的成员，」汤姆·基维尔森宣告。「他是我们雇来的雇员。眼下这个动议一处理完，我就打算动议把他解雇，另请高明。」

「我动议修正藤泽船长的动议，」乔·基维尔森说。「我动议，修正后的动议措辞为：『——并规定价格为每磅七十五厘索尔。』」

「你们疯了！」贝尔歇几乎是尖叫起来。

七十五厘索尔本是老价钱，后来他跟拉维克一路往下压，一直压到了四十五。

===Original===
Just at that moment, my radio began making a small fuss. I unhooked the handphone and brought it to my face.

“Yeah?”

It was Bish Ware’s voice: “Walt, get hold of the Kivelsons and get them out of Hunters’ Hall as fast as you can,” he said. “I just got a tip from one of my﻿ ﻿… my parishioners. Ravick’s going to stage a riot to give Hallstock’s cops an excuse to raid the meeting. They want the Kivelsons.”

“Roger.” I hung up, and as I did I could hear Joe Kivelson shouting: “You think we don’t get any news on this planet? Tallow-wax has been selling for the same price on Terra that it did eight years ago, when you two crooks started cutting the price. Why, the very ship Belsher came here on brought the quotations on the commodity market﻿—”

===Chinese===
就在这当口，我的电台轻轻闹起了动静。我解下手持话机举到脸边。

「喂？」

话机里传来比什·韦尔的声音：「沃尔特，找到基维尔森父子，尽快把他们带出猎手会堂，」他说。「我刚从我的一位……我的一位『教民』那儿得了风声。拉维克要制造一场暴乱，好给霍尔斯特克的警察一个冲击会场的借口。他们要的就是基维尔森父子。」

「明白。」我挂了话机，就在这时，听见乔·基维尔森正吼道：「你们当我们这行星上收不到新闻？八年前你们两个骗子开始压价的时候，脂蜡在特拉卖什么价，如今还卖什么价。哼，贝尔歇来的时候坐的那条船，运来的大宗商品市场行情单上就写着呢——」

===Original===
I edged through the crowd till I was beside Oscar Fujisawa. I decided the truth would need a little editing; I didn’t want to use Bish Ware as my source.

“Oscar, Dad just called me,” I told him. “A tip came in to the *Times* that Ravick’s boys are going to fake a riot and Hallstock’s cops are going to raid the meeting. They want Joe and Tom. You know what they’ll do if they get hold of them.”

“Shot while resisting arrest. You sure this is a good tip, though?”

Across the room, somebody jumped to his feet, kicking over a chair.

===Chinese===
我从人群里侧身穿行，挤到奥斯卡·藤泽身旁。我认定实话需要稍加编辑；我不想把比什·韦尔供出来当消息来源。

「奥斯卡，爸爸刚给我来了电话，」我告诉他。「《时报》收到线报，说拉维克的人要假造一场暴乱，霍尔斯特克的警察就借机冲击会场。他们要抓乔和汤姆。你知道落到他们手里会是什么下场。」

「『拒捕时被击毙』。不过，你确定这线报可靠？」

房间那头，有人一跃而起，踢翻了一把椅子。

===Original===
“That’s a double two-em-dashed lie, you *etaoin shrdlu* so-and-so!” somebody yelled.

“Who are you calling a so-and-so, you thus-and-so-ing such-and-such?” somebody else yelled back, and a couple more chairs got smashed and a swirl of fighting started.

“Yes, it is,” Oscar decided. “Let’s go.”

We started plowing through the crowd toward where the Kivelsons and a couple more of the *Javelin* crew were clumped. I got one of the rolls of quarter sols into my right fist and let Oscar go ahead. He has more mass than I have.

It was a good thing I did, because before we had gone ten feet, some character got between us, dragged a two-foot length of inch-and-a-half high-pressure hose out of his pant leg, and started to swing at the back of Oscar’s head. I promptly clipped him behind the ear with a fist full of money, and down he went. Oscar, who must have eyes in the back of his head, turned and grabbed the hose out of his hand before he dropped it, using it to clout somebody in front of him. Somebody else came pushing toward us, and I was about to clip him, too, when he yelled, “Watch it, Walt; I’m with it!” It was Cesário Vieira, another *Javelin* man; he’s engaged to Linda Kivelson, Joe’s daughter and Tom’s sister, the one going to school on Terra.

===Chinese===
「这是打了双倍破折号的弥天大谎，你这个 *etaoin shrdlu*（铅字盘顺手敲出的乱字行，代脏话）的混账东西！」有人吼道。

「你骂谁混账东西，你这个如此这般、这般如此的货色？」另一个人回骂道，跟着又有几把椅子被砸烂，一场混战旋风似的卷了起来。

「可靠，」奥斯卡当机立断。「走吧。」

我们开始从人群里犁出一条路，朝基维尔森父子和标枪号另外几名船员扎堆的地方推进。我把一卷四分之一索尔硬币塞进右拳，让奥斯卡走在头里。他块头比我大。

亏得我这么做了，因为我们还没走出十英尺，就有个家伙横插到我们中间，从裤腿里抽出一截两英尺长、一英寸半粗的高压软管，朝奥斯卡的后脑勺抡去。我立刻用攥满钱的拳头照他耳后敲了一记，他应声倒地。奥斯卡准是后脑勺长了眼睛，转身抢在他撒手之前夺下软管，顺势敲了前面某人一下。又有人朝我们挤过来，我正要也赏他一记，那人却嚷道：「留神，沃尔特；我是自己人！」是塞萨里奥·维埃拉（Cesário Vieira），也是标枪号上的人；他跟琳达·基维尔森（Linda Kivelson）订了婚——乔的女儿、汤姆的妹妹，就是正在特拉上学的那位。

===Original===
Then we had reached Tom and Joe Kivelson. Oscar grabbed Joe by the arm.

“Come on, Joe; let’s get moving,” he said. “Hallstock’s Gestapo are on the way. They have orders to get you dead or alive.”

“Like blazes!” Joe told him. “I never chickened out on a fight yet, and﻿—”

That’s what I’d been afraid of. Joe is like a Zarathustra *veldtbeest*; the only tactics he knows is a headlong attack.

“You want to get your crew and your son killed, and yourself along with them?” Oscar asked him. “That’s what’ll happen if the cops catch you. Now are you coming, or will I have to knock you senseless and drag you out?”

===Chinese===
这时我们已经到了汤姆和乔·基维尔森跟前。奥斯卡一把抓住乔的胳膊。

「快走，乔；咱们得挪窝了，」他说。「霍尔斯特克的盖世太保已经出动了。他们接到的命令是不管死活都要拿到你。」

「休想！」乔答道。「我打起架来还从来没当过逃兵，而且——」

我就怕他这个。乔活像一头查拉图斯特拉（Zarathustra）的草原兽（*veldtbeest*）；他懂的唯一战术就是不管不顾地猛冲。

「你想让你手下船员和你儿子都送命，连你自己也搭上？」奥斯卡问他。「警察抓住你，就是这个下场。现在你是自己走，还是要我把你打昏了拖出去？」

===Original===
Fortunately, at that moment somebody took a swing at Joe and grazed his cheek. It was a good thing that was all he did; he was wearing brass knuckles. Joe went down a couple of feet, bending at the knees, and caught this fellow around the hips with both hands, straightening and lifting him over his head. Then he threw him over the heads of the people in front of him. There were yells where the human missile landed.

“That’s the stuff, Joe!” Oscar shouted. “Come on, we got them on the run!”

That, of course, converted a strategic retreat into an attack. We got Joe aimed toward the doors and before he knew it, we were out in the hall by the elevators. There were a couple of Ravick’s men, with sergeant-at-arms arm bands, and two city cops. One of the latter got in Joe’s way. Joe punched him in the face and knocked him back about ten feet in a sliding stagger before he dropped. The other cop grabbed me by the left arm.

I slugged him under the jaw with my ten-sol right and knocked him out, and I felt the wrapping on the coin roll break and the quarters come loose in my hand. Before I could drop them into my jacket pocket and get out the other roll, one of the sergeants at arms drew a gun. I just hurled the handful of coins at him. He dropped the pistol and put both hands to his face, howling in pain.

I gave a small mental howl myself when I thought of all the nice things I could have bought for ten sols. One of Joe Kivelson’s followers stooped and scooped up the fallen pistol, firing a couple of times with it. Then we all rushed Joe into one of the elevators and crowded in behind him, and as I turned to start it down I could hear police sirens from the street and also from the landing stage above. In the hall outside the meeting room, four or five of Ravick’s free-drink mercenaries were down on all fours scrabbling for coins, and the rest of the pursuers from the meeting room were stumbling and tripping over them. I wished I’d brought a camera along, too. The public would have loved a shot of that. I lifted the radio and spoke into it:

===Chinese===
幸好就在这时，有人朝乔挥了一拳，擦过他的面颊。亏得他也只挥了这一下；他手上还戴着铜指虎呢。乔膝盖一弯、身子矮下去两英尺，双手搂住那家伙的胯部，随即挺身把他举过头顶，朝前面众人的头顶外扔了出去。这枚「人体炮弹」落地的所在，顿时响起一片嚎叫。

「好样的，乔！」奥斯卡吼道。「快，他们已经被咱们打跑啦！」

这一来，战略撤退当然就变成了进攻。我们把乔对准大门，等他回过神来，我们已经出了会议室，站在电梯旁的廊道里。那儿有拉维克的两个人，戴着纠察袖章，还有两名本市警察。其中一个警察挡了乔的路。乔一拳打在他脸上，把他打得踉踉跄跄倒滑出十来英尺才倒下。另一个警察抓住了我的左臂。

我用攥着十索尔硬币的右拳朝他下巴底下结结实实来了一记，把他打昏过去，同时觉得手心里纸卷破了，硬币散了一手。还没等我把它们塞进外衣口袋、再掏出另一卷，一个纠察抽出了枪。我干脆把满把硬币朝他掷了过去。他撒了手枪，双手捂脸，痛得直嚎。

一想到那十索尔原本能置办多少好东西，我自己也在心里小小嚎了一声。乔·基维尔森的一名手下俯身捞起掉落的枪，用它开了两枪。接着我们大家一起把乔拥进一部电梯，挤在他身后；我转身启动电梯下行时，听得见街面上和顶层起降台都响起了警笛。会议室外的廊道里，拉维克用免费酒水雇来的那四五个雇佣打手正趴在地上抢硬币，从会议室追出来的其他人则被他们绊得跌跌撞撞。我真后悔没把照相机也带上。读者们准爱看这么一张照片。我举起电台，对着它说道：

===Original===
“This is Walter Boyd, returning you now to the regular entertainment program.”

A second later, the thing whistled at me. As the car started down and the doors closed I lifted the handphone. It was Bish Ware again.

“We’re going down in the elevator to Second Level Down,” I said. “I have Joe and Tom and Oscar Fujisawa and a few of the *Javelin* crew with me. The place is crawling with cops now.”

“Go to Third Level Down and get up on the catwalk on the right,” Bish said. “I’ll be along to pick you up.”

===Chinese===
「这里是沃尔特·博伊德（Walter Boyd），现在把您送回常规的娱乐节目。」

一秒钟后，那玩意儿冲我发出一声呼哨。电梯开始下行、门合拢时，我又举起了手持话机。还是比什·韦尔。

「我们正乘电梯下到下第二层（Second Level Down），」我说。「乔、汤姆、奥斯卡·藤泽，还有标枪号的几名船员都跟我在一起。这地方现在到处是警察。」

「去下第三层（Third Level Down），上右边的高架步道等着，」比什说。「我马上开车去接你们。」

===Original===
“Roger. We’ll be looking for you.”

The car stopped at Second Level Down. I punched a button and sent it down another level. Joe Kivelson, who was dabbing at his cheek with a piece of handkerchief tissue, wanted to know what was up.

“We’re getting a pickup,” I told him. “Vehicle from the *Times*.”

I thought it would save arguments if I didn’t mention who was bringing it.

===Chinese===
「明白。我们等你。」

电梯停在了下第二层。我按了个按钮，让它再往下降一层。乔·基维尔森正用一片纸巾按着脸颊，想知道这是唱的哪一出。

「有人来接应我们，」我告诉他。「《时报》派来的车。」

我想，不提开车来的是谁，可以省掉一场口舌。

## Monster Killing / 猎杀怪兽

===Original===
There was a man in the turret, waiting to help me. He had a clip of five rounds in the gun, the searchlight on, and the viewscreen tuned to the forward pickup. After checking the gun and loading the chamber, I looked in that, and in the distance, lighted by the boat above and the searchlight of the *Javelin*, I saw a long neck with a little head on the end of it weaving about. We were making straight for it, losing altitude and speed as we went.

Then the neck dipped under the water and a little later reappeared, coming straight for the advancing light. The forward gun went off, shaking the ship with its recoil, and the head ducked under again. There was a spout from the shell behind it.

===Chinese===
炮塔里有个人等着帮我。他已经给炮装好了一个五发弹夹，探照灯开着，屏幕调到了舰首摄像的画面。我检查过火炮、上了膛，凑到屏幕前望去：远处，在上方小艇和标枪号（*Javelin*）探照灯的照耀下，我看见一条长长的脖子和末端一颗小脑袋正在来回摆动。我们径直朝它逼近，边下降边减速。

随即那脖子扎进水里，不多时又冒了出来，径直朝渐近的灯光冲来。舰首炮打响了，后坐力震得全船直晃，那颗脑袋又缩回水下。炮弹在它身后激起一股水柱。

===Original===
I took my eyes from the forward screen and looked out the rear window, ready to shove my face into the sight-mask. An instant later, the head and neck reappeared astern of us. I fired, without too much hope of hitting anything, and then the ship was rising and circling.

As soon as I’d fired, the monster had sounded, headfirst. I fired a second shot at his tail, in hope of crippling his steering gear, but that was a clean miss, too, and then the ship was up to about five thousand feet. My helper pulled out the partly empty clip and replaced it with a full one, giving me five and one in the chamber.

*If I’d been that monster*, I thought, *I’d have kept on going till I was a couple of hundred miles away from this place*; but evidently that wasn’t the way monsters thought, if thinking is what goes on inside a brain cavity the size of a quart bottle in a head the size of two oil drums on a body as big as the ship that was hunting him. He’d found a lot of gulpers and funnelmouths, and he wasn’t going to be chased away from his dinner by somebody shooting at him.

===Chinese===
我把视线从舰首屏幕挪开，望向后窗，随时准备把脸贴进瞄准面罩（sight-mask）。说时迟那时快，那颗脑袋连着脖子在我们船尾方向再度露头。我开了一炮，本没抱多大命中的指望，随后飞船便拉升绕圈了。

我的炮声一响，那怪兽便头朝下潜了下去。我朝它的尾巴又补了一炮，想打坏它的转向「舵」，谁知又是一发干干净净的脱靶，这时飞船已爬到约五千英尺高。帮手抽出打剩的弹夹，换上一整夹，给我备下弹夹五发、膛内一发。

我想，换了我是那头怪兽，准会一路游下去，直到离这地方两三百英里开外才停；可怪兽显然不这么想——如果在那颗两只油桶大的脑袋里、在那只有一夸脱瓶子大的脑腔里进行的过程也算得上思考的话；而这颗脑袋，安在一具和追猎它的飞船一般庞大的躯体上。它已经寻着了一大群囫吞鱼（gulpers）和漏斗嘴鱼（funnelmouths），可不想让人放几炮，就把自己从晚餐旁赶走。

===Original===
I wondered why they didn’t eat screwfish, instead of the things that preyed on them. Maybe they did and we didn’t know it. Or maybe they just didn’t like screwfish. There were a lot of things we didn’t know about sea-monsters.

For that matter, I wondered why we didn’t grow tallow-wax by carniculture. We could grow any other animal matter we wanted. I’d often thought of that.

The monster wasn’t showing any inclination to come to the surface again, and finally Joe Kivelson’s voice came out of the intercom:

===Chinese===
我纳闷，它们为什么不吃螺鱼（screwfish），偏去吃那些以螺鱼为食的东西。也许它们也吃，只是我们不知道。又也许它们干脆就是不爱吃螺鱼。关于海怪，我们不知道的事还多着呢。

再说，我也纳闷，我们为什么不用肉培法培育脂蜡（tallow-wax）。别的动物性材料，想要什么我们都能培出来。这个问题我早就想过不止一回。

那怪兽丝毫没有再浮上来的意思，终于，乔·基维尔森（Joe Kivelson）的声音从内部通话器里传了出来：

===Original===
“Run in the guns and seal ports. Secure for submersion. We’re going down and chase him up.”

My helper threw the switch that retracted the gun and sealed the gun port. I checked that and reported, “After gun secure.” Hans Cronje’s voice, a moment later, said, “Forward gun secure,” and then Ramón Llewellyn said, “Ship secure; ready to submerge.”

Then the *Javelin* began to settle, and the water came up over the window. I didn’t know what the radar was picking up. All I could see was the screen and the window; water lighted for about fifty feet in front and behind. I saw a cloud of screwfish pass over and around us, spinning rapidly as they swam as though on lengthwise axis﻿—they always spin counterclockwise, never clockwise. A couple of funnelmouths were swimming after them, overtaking and engulfing them.

===Chinese===
「把炮收进来，封死炮眼。各处固定，准备下潜。我们下去，把它赶上来。」

帮手扳下收回火炮、封闭炮眼的开关。我检查完毕，报告：「艉炮固定完毕。」片刻后，汉斯·克龙涅（Hans Cronje）的声音响起：「舰首炮固定完毕。」接着拉蒙·卢埃林（Ramón Llewellyn）说：「全船固定完毕；准备下潜。」

随后标枪号开始下沉，海水漫过舷窗。雷达上究竟收到了什么，我无从知道。入眼的只有屏幕和窗外——前后各约五十英尺的海水被照得透亮。只见一大团螺鱼从我们头顶和四周游过，它们一边游一边急速旋转，仿佛绕着纵轴打转——它们永远逆时针转，从不顺时针。几条漏斗嘴鱼尾随其后，追上便整口吞下。

===Original===
Then the captain yelled, “Get set for torpedo,” and my helper and I each grabbed a stanchion. A couple of seconds later it seemed as though King Neptune himself had given the ship a poke in the nose; my hands were almost jerked loose from their hold. Then she swung slowly, nosing up and down, and finally Joe Kivelson spoke again:

“We’re going to surface. Get set to run the guns out and start shooting as soon as we’re out of the water.”

“What happened?” I asked my helper.

“Must have put the torp right under him and lifted him,” he said. “He could be dead or stunned. Or he could be live and active and spoiling for a fight.”

===Chinese===
接着船长吼了一声：「准备抗鱼雷冲击！」我和帮手各抓住一根立柱。两三秒后，只觉得像是海王尼普顿（King Neptune）他老人家亲手朝船鼻子上捅了一记，我的双手差点被震脱。随后船缓缓打转，船头一起一伏，终于，乔·基维尔森又开口了：

「我们要上浮了。准备好，一出水面就伸出炮，马上开火。」

「刚才是怎么回事？」我问帮手。

「准是鱼雷正好在它底下炸开，把它掀了起来，」他说。「它兴许死了，兴许震晕了。也可能照样活蹦乱跳，正憋着一股劲要打架呢。」

===Original===
That last could be trouble. The *Times* had run quite a few stories, some with black borders, about ships that had gotten into trouble with monsters. A hunter-ship is heavy and it is well-armored﻿—install hyperdrive engines in one, and you could take her from here to Terra﻿—but a monster is a tough brute, and he has armor of his own, scales an inch or so thick and tougher than sole leather. A lot of chair seats around Port Sandor are made of single monster scales. A monster strikes with its head, like a snake. They can smash a ship’s boat, and they’ve been known to punch armor-glass windows out of their frames. I didn’t want the window in front of me coming in at me with a monster head the size of a couple of oil drums and full of big tusks following it.

===Chinese===
最后那种情形可就麻烦了。《时报》（*Times*）刊登过不少报道，讲的是跟怪兽打交道吃了亏的船，有几则还镶着黑框。猎怪船船体重、装甲厚——真给它装上超空间引擎（hyperdrive engines），大可以从这里一路开到特拉（Terra）——可怪兽也是条结实的莽汉，而且自有它的装甲：一身鳞片足有一英寸上下厚，比鞋底革还坚韧。桑多尔港（Port Sandor）一带不少座椅的面子，就是拿整片怪兽鳞做的。怪兽出击用的是脑袋，活像一条蛇。它们能砸烂船上的小艇，还有过把装甲玻璃窗整个从窗框里顶出来的记录。我可不想让面前这扇窗带着一颗两油桶大小、满口大长牙的怪兽脑袋，一道砸到我身上。

===Original===
The *Javelin* came up fast, but not as fast as the monster, which seemed to have been injured only in his disposition. He was on the surface already, about fifty yards astern of us, threshing with his forty-foot wing-fins, his neck arched back to strike. I started to swing my gun for the chest shot Joe Kivelson had recommended as soon as it was run out, and then the ship was swung around and tilted up forward by a sudden gust of wind. While I was struggling to get the sights back on the monster, the ship gave another lurch and the cross hairs were right on its neck, about six feet below the head. I grabbed the trigger, and as soon as the shot was off, took my eyes from the sights. I was just a second too late to see the burst, but not too late to see the monster’s neck jerk one way out of the smoke puff and its head fly another. A second later, the window in front of me was splashed with blood as the headless neck came down on our fantail.

===Chinese===
标枪号上浮得飞快，可仍不及那头怪兽——它看来只在脾气上受了点伤。它早已浮在水面，在我们船尾约五十码开外，四十英尺长的翼鳍劈啪拍打，长脖子向后弯成一张弓，蓄势待击。炮一伸出去，我便转动炮身，去找乔·基维尔森叮嘱过的那记朝胸口打的炮，不料一阵骤风把船吹得横转过去、船头高高翘起。我正竭力把准星重新压回怪兽身上，船又猛地一晃，十字线竟不偏不倚落在它脖颈上，离脑袋约六英尺。我一把扣下扳机，炮一响便把眼睛从瞄准镜上移开。爆炸的烟团我晚了一秒没能看到，却正好看到怪兽的脖子从烟里往一边猛抽、脑袋朝另一边飞出去。一秒之后，那条无头的脖子砸在我们船尾甲板上，血浆溅了我一窗。

===Original===
Immediately, two rockets jumped from the launcher over the gun turret, planting a couple of harpoons, and the boat, which had been circling around since we had submerged, dived into the water and passed under the monster, coming up on the other side dragging another harpoon line. The monster was still threshing its wings and flogging with its headless neck. It takes a monster quite a few minutes to tumble to the fact that it’s been killed. My helper was pounding my back black and blue with one hand and trying to pump mine off with the other, and I was getting an ovation from all over the ship. At the same time, a couple more harpoons went into the thing from the ship, and the boat put another one in from behind.

I gathered that shooting monsters’ heads off wasn’t at all usual, and hastened to pass it off as pure luck, so that everybody would hurry up and deny it before they got the same idea themselves.

===Chinese===
霎时间，两枚火箭从炮塔上方的发射器里蹿出，钉上了两支鱼叉；自我们下潜起一直在空中盘旋的小艇一头扎进水里，从怪兽身下穿过，到另一侧才浮出水面，拖曳着又一根鱼叉索。怪兽仍在扑打双翼，无头的脖子还在来回抽甩。怪兽得花上好几分钟，才能咂摸明白自己已经死了。我的帮手一只手把我的背捶得青一块紫一块，另一只手又把我的手摇得快要脱臼，全船上下喝彩声四起。与此同时，船上又朝那家伙身上射进两支鱼叉，小艇再从它身后补上一支。

我听得出来，一炮轰下怪兽脑袋这种事并不多见，便赶忙一口咬定这纯属手气，好让人人急着否认——赶在他们自己回过味来、觉得这话不假之前。

===Original===
We hadn’t much time for ovations, though. We had a very slowly dying monster, and before he finally discovered that he was dead, a couple of harpoons got pulled out and had to be replaced. Finally, however, he quieted down, and the boat swung him around, bringing the tail past our bow, and the ship cut contragravity to specific-gravity level and settled to float on top of the water. The boat dived again, and payed out a line that it brought up and around and up again, lashing the monster fast alongside.

“All right,” Kivelson was saying, out of the intercom. “Shooting’s over. All hands for cutting-up.”

===Chinese===
不过，庆贺的工夫可不多。我们守着一头死得极慢的怪兽，在它终于发现自己死了之前，又有几支鱼叉被挣脱，只得重新补射。它总算渐渐安分下来，小艇把它拨转过来，让尾巴从我们船头外拖过，飞船将反重力收回到比重一档，落到水面上漂浮。小艇再度下潜，放出一根缆绳，绕上去、兜过来、再收上去，把怪兽牢牢捆贴在船侧。

「好了，」基维尔森的声音从内部通话器里传来。「射击结束。全体动手宰割。」

===Original===
I pulled on a parka and zipped it up and went out onto the deck. Everybody who wasn’t needed at engines or controls was there, and equipment was coming up from below﻿—power saws and sonocutters and even a solenoid jackhammer. There were half a dozen floodlights, on small contragravity lifters; they were run up on lines fifty feet above the ship’s deck. By this time it was completely dark and fine snow was blowing. I could see that Joe Kivelson was anxious to get the cutting-up finished before the wind got any worse.

“Walt, can you use a machine gun?” he asked me.

I told him I could. I was sure of it; a machine gun is fired in a rational and decent manner.

===Chinese===
我套上风雪大衣，拉上拉链，走到甲板上。凡是引擎和操纵岗位上用不着的人都在，装备正从下面往上运——动力锯、声波切割器（sonocutter），甚至还有一台电磁风镐（solenoid jackhammer）。另有六盏泛光灯，装在小型的反重力升举器上，顺着吊索升到甲板上空五十英尺处。这时候天已全黑，细雪纷飞。我看得出来，乔·基维尔森巴不得赶在风势更坏之前把宰割干完。

「沃尔特，你会使机枪吗？」他问我。

我说会。这个我有把握；机枪打起来是有理性、有体面的。

===Original===
“Well, all right. Suppose you cover for us from the boat,” he said. “Mr. Murell can pilot for you. You never worked at cutting-up before, and neither did he. You’d be more of a hindrance than a help and so would he. But we do need a good machine gunner. As soon as we start throwing out waste, we’ll have all the slashers and halberd fish for miles around. You just shoot them as fast as you see them.”

He was courteous enough not to add: “And don’t shoot any of the crew.”

The boat came in and passed out the lines of its harpoons, and Murell and I took the places of Cesário Vieira and the other man. We went up to the nose, and Murell took his place at the controls, and I got back of the 7 mm machine gun and made sure that there were plenty of extra belts of ammo. Then, as we rose, I pulled the goggles down from my hood, swung the gun away from the ship, and hammered off a one-second burst to make sure it was working, after which I settled down, glad I had a comfortable seat and wasn’t climbing around on that monster.

===Chinese===
「那好。你就在小艇上给我们打掩护，」他说。「穆雷尔（Murell）先生可以替你驾驶。你没干过宰割活儿，他也没干过。你们俩与其说帮得上忙，不如说更碍事。不过我们倒真缺一名好机枪手。我们一开始往外抛废料，方圆多少英里的砍杀兽（slasher）和长戟鱼（halberd fish）就都得招来。你只管见着就打，越快越好。」

他总算很有涵养，没把这句补出来：「还有，别把船员给打死了。」

小艇靠拢过来，交出艇上那些鱼叉索，我和穆雷尔接替了塞萨里奥·维埃拉（Cesário Vieira）和另一名船员。我们走到艇首，穆雷尔在操纵位上坐定，我则趴到那挺 7 毫米机枪后面，确认备用弹链备得足足的。随后，趁小艇上升，我把护目镜从风帽上拉下来，将枪口转离飞船，哒哒哒打了一个一秒钟点射试枪，试完便安顿下来——庆幸自己有个舒舒服服的座位，不必到那头怪兽身上爬来爬去。

===Original===
They began knocking scales loose with the jackhammer and cutting into the leathery skin underneath with sonocutters. The sea was getting heavy, and the ship and the attached monster had begun to roll.

“That’s pretty dangerous work,” Murell said. “If a man using one of those cutters slipped.﻿ ﻿…”

“It’s happened,” I told him. “You met our peg-legged compositor, Julio. That was how he lost his leg.”

“I don’t blame them for wanting all they can get for tallow-wax.”

===Chinese===
大伙儿开始用风镐震松鳞片，再用声波切割器割开底下那层皮革般的皮。海况越来越坏，飞船和拴在一起的怪兽开始摇晃颠簸。

「这活儿相当危险，」穆雷尔说。「要是哪个使切割器的人脚下一滑……」

「出过这种事，」我告诉他。「你见过我们那位装木腿的排字工胡利奥（Julio）吧。他那条腿就是这么丢的。」

「他们想靠脂蜡尽量多换些钱，我不怪他们。」

===Original===
They had the monster opened down the belly, and were beginning to cut loose big chunks of the yellow tallow-wax and throw them into cargo nets and swing them aboard with lifters, to be chucked down the cargo hatches. I was only able to watch that for a minute or so and tell Murell what was going on, and then the first halberd fish, with a spearlike nose and sharp ridges of the nearest thing to bone you find on Fenris, came swimming up. I swung the gun on the leader and gave him a second of fire, and then a two-second burst on the ones behind. Then I waited for a few seconds until the survivors converged on their dead and injured companions and gave them another burst, which wiped out the lot of them.

It was only a couple of seconds after that that the first slasher came in, shiny as heat-blued steel and waving four clawed tentacles that grew around its neck. It took me a second or so to get the sights on him. He stopped slashing immediately. Slashers are smart; you kill them and they find it out right away.

===Chinese===
他们已把怪兽顺着肚皮剖开，开始割下一大块一大块黄色的脂蜡，扔进货网，用升举器吊上船，倒进货舱口。这一幕我只看了一分钟上下，一边给穆雷尔讲解这是怎么回事，随后第一批长戟鱼就游上来了——长着标枪似的长吻，身上支着锐利的棱脊，那是你在芬尼斯（Fenris）所能找到的最接近骨头的东西。我把枪口转向领头那条，喂了它一秒火力，再朝后面的来一记两秒点射。然后我等了几秒，待幸存的聚到死伤同伴周围，又补上一梭子，把整群都报销了。

那之后不过两三秒，第一条砍杀兽就到了，浑身油光锃亮，赛过烧蓝的钢，脖子四周生着的四条带爪触手四下乱舞。我用了大约一秒把准星压上它。它立刻就不砍不杀了。砍杀兽聪明得很：你把它打死了，它当场就明白过来。

===Original===
Before long, the water around the ship and the monster was polluted with things like that. I had to keep them away from the men, now working up to their knees in water, and at the same time avoid massacring the crew I was trying to protect, and Murell had to keep the boat in position, in spite of a steadily rising wind, and every time I had to change belts, there’d be a new rush of things that had to be shot in a hurry. The ammunition bill for covering a cutting-up operation is one of the things that runs up expenses for a hunter-ship. The ocean bottom around here must be carpeted with machine-gun brass.

Finally, they got the job done, and everybody went below and sealed ship. We sealed the boat and went down after her. The last I saw, the remains of the monster, now stripped of wax, had been cast off, and the water around it was rioting with slashers and clawbeaks and halberd fish and similar marine unpleasantnesses.

===Chinese===
没多久，飞船和怪兽四周的水里就挤满了这类货色，简直成了祸害。我得把它们挡在干活的人外面——这会儿大家已经泡在齐膝深的水里干活——同时又不能把我本要保护的船员也给扫倒；穆雷尔得顶着越刮越紧的风，把小艇稳在阵位上；而且每回我一换弹链，必有一批新的东西蜂拥而至，非得火速开火不可。掩护一场宰割作业的弹药账单，正是猎怪船开销居高不下的原因之一。这一带的海底，怕是早让机枪弹壳铺成了毯子。

最后，活儿总算干完，众人下舱封船。我们也封好小艇，跟着下潜。我看到的最后一眼是：剥净了蜡的怪兽残骸已被放开，周围的海水里，砍杀兽、钩喙鱼（clawbeaks）、长戟鱼和诸如此类讨人嫌的海中丑类，正闹腾得不可开交。

## Reporter Working / 记者工作中

===Original===
Bish came over and greeted us solemnly.

“Good afternoon, gentlemen. Captain Ahab, I believe,” he said, bowing to Tom, who seemed slightly puzzled; the education Tom had been digging out for himself was technical rather than literary. “And Mr. Pulitzer. Or is it Horace Greeley?”

“Lord Beaverbrook, your Grace,” I replied. “Have you any little news items for us from your diocese?”

Bish teetered slightly, getting out a cigar and inspecting it carefully before lighting it.

“We‑el,” he said carefully, “my diocese is full to the hatch covers with sinners, but that’s scarcely news.” He turned to Tom. “One of your hands on the *Javelin* got into a fight in Martian Joe’s, a while ago. Lumped the other man up pretty badly.” He named the Javelin crewman, and the man who had been pounded. The latter was one of Steve Ravick’s goons. “But not fatally, I regret to say,” Bish added. “The local Gestapo are looking for your man, but he made it aboard Nip Spazoni’s *Bulldog*, and by this time he’s halfway to Hermann Reuch’s Land.”

===Chinese===
比什走了过来，一本正经地向我们打招呼。

「午安，二位先生。我想，这位是亚哈船长（Captain Ahab）吧，」他向汤姆欠身说道；汤姆显得有点茫然——他自个儿苦啃出来的那点教育偏在技术，不沾文学。「这位呢，是普利策先生（Mr. Pulitzer）。还是该称霍勒斯·格里利（Horace Greeley）？」

「比弗布鲁克勋爵（Lord Beaverbrook）在此，主教大人，」我答道。「您的教区里可有什么小小的新闻条目，赏给我们两条？」

比什身子微微晃了晃，掏出一支雪茄，仔细端详一番才点上。

「呃——是，」他字斟句酌地说，「我这教区里罪人满得齐了舱口盖，可那算不上新闻。」他转向汤姆。「不久前，你标枪号（*Javelin*）上有个船员在『火星乔』酒吧（Martian Joe's）跟人动了一场手，把对方结结实实揍了个鼻青脸肿。」他说出了那个标枪号船员的名字，还有挨揍的那位。后者是史蒂夫·拉维克（Steve Ravick）手下的一名打手。「可惜没揍出人命，在下深表遗憾，」比什补上一句。「本地盖世太保正在找你那个人，不过他已经上了尼普·斯帕佐尼（Nip Spazoni）的斗牛犬号（*Bulldog*），这会儿只怕已经走到去赫尔曼·罗伊希之地（Hermann Reuch's Land）的半道上了。」

===Original===
“Isn’t Nip going to the meeting, tonight?” Tom asked.

Bish shook his head. “Nip is a peace-loving man. He has a well-founded suspicion that peace is going to be in short supply around Hunters’ Hall this evening. You know, of course, that Leo Belsher’s coming in on the *Peenemünde* and will be there to announce another price cut. The new price, I understand, will be thirty-five centisols a pound.”

*Seven hundred sols a ton*, I thought; *why, that would barely pay ship expenses.*

===Chinese===
「尼普今晚不去开会吗？」汤姆问。

比什摇了摇头。「尼普是个爱好和平的人。他有充分的理由怀疑，今晚猎手会堂（Hunters' Hall）一带，和平怕是要供不应求。你们当然知道，利奥·贝尔歇（Leo Belsher）搭佩内明德号（*Peenemünde*）来，将到场宣布再一次降价。据我所知，新价是三十五厘索尔一磅。」

*一吨七百索尔*，我心想；*唉，这价钱连船上的开销都勉强才付得起。*

===Original===
“Where did you get that?” Tom asked, a trifle sharply.

“Oh, I have my spies and informers,” Bish said. “And even if I hadn’t, it would figure. The only reason Leo Belsher ever comes to this Eden among planets is to negotiate a new contract, and who ever heard of a new contract at a higher price?”

That had all happened before, a number of times. When Steve Ravick had gotten control of the Hunters’ Cooperative, the price of tallow-wax, on the loading floor at Port Sandor spaceport, had been fifteen hundred sols a ton. As far as Dad and I could find out, it was still bringing the same price on Terra as it always had. It looked to us as if Ravick and Leo Belsher, who was the Coop representative on Terra, and Mort Hallstock were simply pocketing the difference. I was just as sore about what was happening as anybody who went out in the hunter-ships. Tallow-wax is our only export. All our imports are paid for with credit from the sale of wax.

===Chinese===
「这消息你从哪儿弄来的？」汤姆问，口气有点冲。

「哦，我有我的密探和线人，」比什说。「就算没有，掐指也算得出来。利奥·贝尔歇之所以肯光临这座众行星间的伊甸园，唯一的缘故就是来谈一份新合同，可谁听说过越谈价越高的新合同？」

这种种早已发生过不止一回。史蒂夫·拉维克把猎手合作社（Hunters' Cooperative）抓到手的时候，脂蜡（tallow-wax）在桑多尔港（Port Sandor）太空港装货平台上的价钱还是一吨一千五百索尔。据爸爸和我所能查到的，它在特拉（Terra）上一直照老价钱出售，从没变过。在我们看来，拉维克、身任合作社驻特拉代表的利奥·贝尔歇，再加上莫特·霍尔斯特克（Mort Hallstock），分明是把差价径直揣进了自家腰包。对眼下这局面，我跟任何一个出海猎怪的船员一样，憋着一肚子火。脂蜡是我们唯一的出口货。我们全部的进口，都靠卖蜡的进项来支付。

===Original===
It isn’t really wax, and it isn’t tallow. It’s a growth on the Jarvis’s sea-monster; there’s a layer of it under the skin, and around organs that need padding. An average-sized monster, say a hundred and fifty feet long, will yield twelve to fifteen tons of it, and a good hunter kills about ten monsters a year. Well, at the price Belsher and Ravick were going to cut from, that would run a little short of a hundred and fifty thousand sols for a year. If you say it quick enough and don’t think, that sounds like big money, but the upkeep and supplies for a hunter-ship are big money, too, and what’s left after that’s paid off is divided, on a graduated scale, among ten to fifteen men, from the captain down. A hunter-boat captain, even a good one like Joe Kivelson, won’t make much more in a year than Dad and I make out of the *Times*.

Chemically, tallow-wax isn’t like anything else in the known Galaxy. The molecules are huge; they can be seen with an ordinary optical microscope, and a microscopically visible molecule is a curious-looking object, to say the least. They use the stuff to treat fabric for protective garments. It isn’t anything like collapsium, of course, but a suit of waxed coveralls weighing only a couple of pounds will stop as much radiation as half an inch of lead.

Back when they were getting fifteen hundred a ton, the hunters had been making good money, but that was before Steve Ravick’s time.

===Chinese===
它其实不是蜡，也不是油脂。它是贾维斯海怪（Jarvis's sea-monster）身上长出来的东西；皮下垫着一层，需要衬垫的器官四周也裹着一层。一头中等个头的怪兽——就算一百五十英尺长吧——能出十二到十五吨，一个好猎怪手一年大约猎杀十头。嗯，按贝尔歇和拉维克即将从那儿往下砍的价钱算，一年下来略少于十五万索尔。这笔数目，只要说得够快、脑子不去细想，听着还挺像笔大钱；可猎怪船的维护和补给同样是笔大钱，刨掉这些之后剩下的，再按级差递减，分给从船长往下的十到十五个人。一条猎怪船的船长，哪怕是乔·基维尔森（Joe Kivelson）那样的好手，一年挣的也比我爸爸和我从《时报》（*Times*）里挣的多不出多少。

论化学性质，脂蜡在已知银河系里独一无二。它的分子极大，用一台普通光学显微镜就能看见；而一个显微镜下可见的分子长什么模样，起码可以说是个稀罕物件。人们拿这种东西处理织物，制作防护服。它当然远比不上坍缩素（collapsium），可一套只重两三磅的涂蜡连体工作服，挡起辐射来抵得上半英寸厚的铅板。

当年一吨还卖一千五百的时候，猎怪手们挣的确实是好钱，可那是史蒂夫·拉维克上台之前的事了。

===Original===
It was slightly before mine, too. Steve Ravick had showed up on Fenris about twelve years ago. He’d had some money, and he’d bought shares in a couple of hunter-ships and staked a few captains who’d had bad luck and got them in debt to him. He also got in with Morton Hallstock, who controlled what some people were credulous enough to take for a government here. Before long, he was secretary of the Hunters’ Cooperative. Old Simon MacGregor, who had been president then, was a good hunter, but he was no businessman. He came to depend very heavily on Ravick, up till his ship, the *Claymore*, was lost with all hands down in Fitzwilliam Straits. I think that was a time bomb in the magazine, but I have a low and suspicious mind. Professor Hartzenbosch has told me so repeatedly. After that, Steve Ravick was president of the Coop. He immediately began a drive to increase the membership. Most of the new members had never been out in a hunter-ship in their lives, but they could all be depended on to vote the way he wanted them to.

First, he jacked the price of wax up, which made everybody but the wax buyers happy. Everybody who wasn’t already in the Coop hurried up and joined. Then he negotiated an exclusive contract with Kapstaad Chemical Products, Ltd., in South Africa, by which they agreed to take the entire output for the Coop. That ended competitive wax buying, and when there was nobody to buy the wax but Kapstaad, you had to sell it through the Cooperative or you didn’t sell it at all. After that, the price started going down. The Cooperative, for which read Steve Ravick, had a sales representative on Terra, Leo Belsher. He wrote all the contracts, collected all the money, and split with Ravick. What was going on was pretty generally understood, even if it couldn’t be proven, but what could anybody do about it?

===Chinese===
那段好日子，也稍稍早于我记事的年月。史蒂夫·拉维克大约十二年前出现在芬尼斯（Fenris）。他手头有些钱，买下了几条猎怪船的股份，又资助了几个正背霉运的船长，让他们欠下他的债。他还攀上了莫顿·霍尔斯特克（Morton Hallstock）——此人把持着一套玩意儿，轻信的人真会拿它当作这里的政府。不久，他便当上了猎手合作社的秘书。当年的老社长西蒙·麦格雷戈（Simon MacGregor）是位好猎手，却不是生意人。他对拉维克的倚重与日俱增，直到他的阔剑号（*Claymore*）在菲茨威廉海峡（Fitzwilliam Straits）连人带船全部沉没。我一直觉得，那是弹药舱里叫人安了定时炸弹，不过我这人心思阴暗、疑神疑鬼，哈岑博施教授（Professor Hartzenbosch）已屡次三番如此告诫过我。在那之后，史蒂夫·拉维克便坐上了合作社社长的位子。他随即发起一场扩员运动。新社员大多这辈子从没出海猎过怪，但个个都靠得住：他要他们怎么投票，他们就怎么投。

第一步，他先把蜡价抬了上去，蜡的买主以外人人称快。还没入社的人赶紧忙不迭地加入。接着，他又与南非的卡普斯塔德化学制品公司（Kapstaad Chemical Products, Ltd.）谈成一份独家合同，对方同意包销合作社的全部产出。竞购脂蜡的局面就此完结，而当世上只剩卡普斯塔德一家买蜡，你不通过合作社，就一两也卖不出去。打那以后，价钱便开始走低。合作社——这词儿读作史蒂夫·拉维克——在特拉设有一位销售代表，就是利奥·贝尔歇。合同全由他一手起草，货款全经他一手收拢，再同拉维克坐地分赃。这里面玩的是什么勾当，大家心里都相当明白，哪怕拿不出证据；可又能拿它怎么办呢？

===Original===
Maybe somebody would try to do something about it at the meeting this evening. I would be there to cover it. I was beginning to wish I owned a bulletproof vest.

Bish and Tom were exchanging views on the subject, some of them almost printable. I had my eyes to my binoculars, watching the tugs go up to meet the *Peenemünde*.

“What we need for Ravick, Hallstock and Belsher,” Tom was saying, “is about four fathoms of harpoon line apiece, and something to haul up to.”

That kind of talk would have shocked Dad. He is very strong for law and order, even when there is no order and the law itself is illegal. I’d always thought there was a lot of merit in what Tom was suggesting. Bish Ware seemed to have his doubts, though.

===Chinese===
也许今晚的会上，就会有人想法子治一治这事。我会到场采访。我都开始希望自己有件防弹背心了。

比什和汤姆正就这个题目交换看法，其中有些话差不多够得上见报。我贴着双筒望远镜，看拖船迎上佩内明德号。

「对付拉维克、霍尔斯特克和贝尔歇这三位，」汤姆正说着，「一人配上约莫四英寻捕鲸索就够了，再找根能往上吊的横梁。」

这种话要是让爸爸听见，准会大受震动。他是极讲法治秩序的人，哪怕秩序荡然无存、连法律本身都不合法的时节也照讲不误。而我一向觉得，汤姆的主张大有可取之处。不过比什·韦尔（Bish Ware）似乎心存疑虑。

===Original===
“Mmm, no; there ought to be some better way of doing it than that.”

“Can you think of one?” Tom challenged.

I didn’t hear Bish’s reply. By that time, the tugs were almost to the ship. I grabbed up the telephoto camera and aimed it. It has its own power unit, and transmits directly. In theory, I could tune it to the telecast station and put what I was getting right on the air, and what I was doing was transmitting to the *Times*, to be recorded and ’cast later. Because it’s not a hundred percent reliable, though, it makes its own audiovisual record, so if any of what I was sending didn’t get through, it could be spliced in after I got back.

===Chinese===
「嗯，不；总该有个比那强些的办法。」

「你想得出来吗？」汤姆将军道。

比什怎么回答的，我没听见。那时候，拖船差不多已经贴到飞船跟前。我抓起长焦相机（telephoto camera），对准了它。相机自带动力单元，直接发送。理论上，我可以把它调到电视广播台，把眼前的画面直接播出去；而我此刻做的，是把信号发回《时报》，先录下来，日后播出。不过它并非百分之百可靠，所以它还自制一份声像记录（audiovisual），这样万一我发回去的内容有没能送达的，等我回去还可以补剪进去。

===Original===
I got some footage of the tugs grappling the ship, which was now completely weightless, and pulling her down. Through the finder, I could see that she had her landing legs extended; she looked like a big overfed spider being hauled in by a couple of gnats. I kept the butt of the camera to my shoulder, and whenever anything interesting happened, I’d squeeze the trigger. The first time I ever used a real submachine gun had been to kill a blue slasher that had gotten into one of the ship pools at the waterfront. I used three one-second bursts, and threw bits of slasher all over the place, and everybody wondered how I’d gotten the practice.

A couple more boats, pushers, went up to help hold the ship against the wind, and by that time she was down to a thousand feet, which was half her diameter. I switched from the shoulder-stock telephoto to the big tripod job, because this was the best part of it. The ship was weightless, of course, but she had mass and an awful lot of it. If anybody goofed getting her down, she’d take the side of the landing pit out, and about ten percent of the population of Fenris, including the ace reporter for the *Times*, along with it.

At the same time, some workmen and a couple of spaceport cops had appeared, taken out a section of railing and put in a gate. The *Peenemünde* settled down, turned slowly to get her port in line with the gate, and lurched off contragravity and began running out a bridge to the promenade. I got some shots of that, and then began packing my stuff back in the hamper.

===Chinese===
拖船钩住此刻已完全失重的飞船，把它往下拽，我拍下了几个镜头。透过取景器，我看见她已伸出了着陆支架，活像一只喂得脑满肠肥的大蜘蛛，正被几只小蚊子拖回家去。我把相机的枪托抵在肩窝，一有值得拍的场面就扣一下扳机。我头一回用真正的冲锋枪，是去宰一条窜进滨水区船池的蓝色砍杀兽（slasher）。我打了三个一秒钟的点射，把砍杀兽的碎块崩得四下乱飞，人人都在纳闷，我这套手法是从哪儿练出来的。

又有两条推船升了上来，帮着把飞船顶在风里；这时她已降到一千英尺，恰是她直径的一半。我从托肩式长焦换成大三脚架上那台重型机，因为最精彩的段落到了。飞船固然没有重量，可她有质量，而且大得吓人。真要有人在她降落时捅了娄子，她就会把降落坑一侧的坑壁齐齐削塌，顺带捎走芬尼斯大约百分之十的人口，连《时报》的王牌记者也在其内。

与此同时，一些工人和两名太空港警察已经到场，拆下一截栏杆，装上了一道闸门。佩内明德号落定下来，缓缓转身，让舷门对准闸门，随即关掉反重力（contragravity），猛地一沉，开始向外伸出一座栈桥，搭向散步长廊。这几幕我也拍了几张，然后动手把家伙什装回器材箱。

===Original===
“You going aboard?” Tom asked. “Can I come along? I can carry some of your stuff and let on I’m your helper.”

*Glory be*, I thought; *I finally got that apprentice.*

“Why, sure,” I said. “You tow the hamper; I’ll carry this.” I got out what looked like a big camera case and slung it over my shoulder. “But you’ll have to take me out on the *Javelin*, sometime, and let me shoot a monster.”

He said it was a deal, and we shook on it. Then I had another idea.

===Chinese===
「你要上船去？」汤姆问。「能捎上我吗？我可以帮你背些器材，装作是你的帮手。」

*谢天谢地*，我心想；*我这学徒总算盼来了。*

「哎呀，当然行，」我说。「你拖着器材箱，我拿这个。」我取出一件看着像大号相机箱的东西，挎上肩。「不过回头你得带我上标枪号出一趟海，让我也猎一头怪兽。」

他说一言为定，我们握手成交。接着我又生出一个主意。

===Original===
“Bish, suppose you come with us, too,” I said. “After all, Tom and I are just a couple of kids. If you’re with us, it’ll look a lot more big-paperish.”

That didn’t seem to please Tom too much. Bish shook his head, though, and Tom brightened.

“I’m dreadfully sorry, Walt,” Bish said. “But I’m going aboard, myself, to see a friend who is en route through to Odin. A Dr. Watson; I have not seen him for years.”

I’d caught that name, too, when we’d gotten the passenger list. Dr. John Watson. Now, I know that all sorts of people call themselves Doctor, and Watson and John aren’t too improbable a combination, but I’d read *Sherlock Holmes* long ago, and the name had caught my attention. And this was the first, to my knowledge, that Bish Ware had ever admitted to any off-planet connections.

===Chinese===
「比什，你也一道去吧，」我说。「毕竟汤姆和我不过是两个半大孩子。有你在场，看着就更像大报的排场了。」

这话似乎让汤姆不太痛快。不过比什摇了摇头，汤姆顿时眉开眼笑。

「万分抱歉，沃尔特（Walt），」比什说。「不过我自己也要上船，去见一位中途过境、续程去奥丁星（Odin）的朋友。一位华生医生（Dr. Watson）；我好些年没见过他了。」

拿到旅客名单那会儿，这个名字我也留意到了。约翰·华生医生（Dr. John Watson）。当然，我知道自称「博士」的人形形色色，华生配约翰也算不上太不可能的组合，可我多年前就读过《福尔摩斯探案》（*Sherlock Holmes*），这名字便上了我的心。而且据我所知，这是比什·韦尔头一回承认自己在行星之外还有什么瓜葛。

===Original===
We started over to the gate. Hallstock and Ravick were ahead of us. So was Sigurd Ngozori, the president of the Fidelity & Trust, carrying a heavy briefcase and accompanied by a character with a submachine gun, and Adolf Lautier and Professor Hartzenbosch. There were a couple of spaceport cops at the gate, in olive-green uniforms that looked as though they had been sprayed on, and steel helmets. I wished we had a city police force like that. They were Odin Dock & Shipyard Company men, all former Federation Regular Army or Colonial Constabulary. The spaceport wasn’t part of Port Sandor, or even Fenris; the Odin Dock & Shipyard Company was the government there, and it was run honestly and efficiently.

They knew me, and when they saw Tom towing my hamper they cracked a few jokes about the new *Times* cub reporter and waved us through. I thought they might give Bish an argument, but they just nodded and let him pass, too. We all went out onto the bridge, and across the pit to the equator of the two-thousand-foot globular ship.

We went into the main lounge, and the captain introduced us to Mr. Glenn Murell. He was fairly tall, with light gray hair, prematurely so, I thought, and a pleasant, noncommittal face. I’d have pegged him for a businessman. Well, I suppose authoring is a business, if that was his business. He shook hands with us, and said:

“Aren’t you rather young to be a newsman?”

I started to burn on that. I get it all the time, and it burns me all the time, but worst of all on the job. Maybe I am only going-on-eighteen, but I’m doing a man’s work, and I’m doing it competently.

===Chinese===
我们朝闸门走去。霍尔斯特克和拉维克走在我们前头。西格德·恩戈佐里（Sigurd Ngozori）也在——他是忠信信托公司（Fidelity & Trust）的总裁，提着一只沉甸甸的公文包，身边跟着个挎冲锋枪的角色——在场的还有阿道夫·洛蒂埃（Adolf Lautier）和哈岑博施教授。闸门口站着两名太空港警察，橄榄绿的制服紧绷得像喷漆喷上去的，头上顶着钢盔。我真希望我们的市警也有这副成色。他们都是奥丁船坞造船厂（Odin Dock & Shipyard）公司的人，清一色前特拉联邦正规军（Federation Regular Army）或殖民地宪兵队（Colonial Constabulary）出身。太空港不属于桑多尔港，甚至不属于芬尼斯；在那一亩三分地上，奥丁船坞造船厂公司就是政府，而且办得又清廉又高效。

他们都认得我，见汤姆拖着我的器材箱，就拿《时报》新来的见习记者打趣了几句，挥手放我们过去。我本以为他们会同比什多费几句口舌，可他们也只点了点头，便放他通行了。我们一道走上栈桥，横过降落坑，来到这艘两千英尺直径的球形飞船的赤道处。

我们走进主休息厅，船长把我们介绍给格伦·穆雷尔先生（Mr. Glenn Murell）。他个子挺高，一头浅灰头发——我想是白得早了些——一张讨人喜欢却莫测高深的脸。换我来猜，我会把他认作生意人。嗯，我想写书也算一门生意，倘若那算他的本行的话。他跟我们握了手，说道：

「干新闻记者，你不嫌太年轻了点吗？」

我一听这话火就往上冒。这话我时时听、时时冒火，而最冒火的莫过于当着正事听。也许我确实才刚要满十八，可我干的是大人的活儿，而且干得不含糊。

===Original===
“Well, they grow up young on Fenris, Mr. Murell,” Captain Marshak earned my gratitude by putting in. “Either that or they don’t live to grow up.”

Murell unhooked his memophone and repeated the captain’s remark into it. Opening line for one of his chapters. Then he wanted to know if I’d been born on Fenris. I saw I was going to have to get firm with Mr. Murell, right away. The time to stop that sort of thing is as soon as it starts.

“Who,” I wanted to know, “is interviewing whom? You’ll have at least five hundred hours till the next possible ship out of here; I only have two and a half to my next deadline. You want coverage, don’t you? The more publicity you get, the easier your own job’s going to be.”

Then I introduced Tom, carefully giving the impression that while I handled all ordinary assignments, I needed help to give him the full V.I.P. treatment. We went over to a quiet corner and sat down, and the interview started.

===Chinese===
「唉，芬尼斯的孩子长大得早，穆雷尔先生，」马沙克船长（Captain Marshak）插了这么一句，赢得了我一份感激。「不早长大，就活不到长大。」

穆雷尔摘下他的备忘录音机（memophone），把船长这句话对着它复述了一遍。正好拿去当某一章的开场白。接着他又想知道我是不是生在芬尼斯。我看出来了，我得立刻对穆雷尔先生硬起来。这类苗头，就得在刚冒头的时候掐灭。

「请问，」我反问，「到底是谁在采访谁？离下一班可能离港的飞船，您起码还有五百个小时；离我的下一次截稿，我只有两个半钟头。您想要报道，不是吗？您的风头出得越足，您自己的差事就越好办。」

接着我把汤姆介绍给他，字斟句酌地留下这么个印象：寻常的采访任务我独自全包，但若要给他全套的贵宾待遇，就少不得帮手。我们走到一个安静角落坐下，采访开始了。

===Original===
The camera case I was carrying was a snare and a deceit. Everybody knows that reporters use recorders in interviews, but it never pays to be too obtrusive about them, or the subject gets recorder-conscious and stiffens up. What I had was better than a recorder; it was a recording radio. Like the audiovisuals, it not only transmitted in to the *Times*, but made a recording as insurance against transmission failure. I reached into a slit on the side and snapped on the switch while I was fumbling with a pencil and notebook with the other hand, and started by asking him what had decided him to do a book about Fenris.

After that, I fed a question every now and then to keep him running, and only listened to every third word. The radio was doing a better job than I possibly could have. At the same time, I was watching Steve Ravick, Morton Hallstock and Leo Belsher at one side of the room, and Bish Ware at the other. Bish was within ear-straining range. Out of the corner of my eye, I saw another man, younger in appearance and looking like an Army officer in civvies, approach him.

“My dear Bishop!” this man said in greeting.

As far as I knew, that nickname had originated on Fenris. I made a mental note of that.

===Chinese===
我背的那只相机箱，其实是个幌子，也是个骗局。谁都知道记者采访要用录音机，但把录音机摆得太露绝无好处，否则采访对象一惦记它，人就僵了。我这件东西比录音机强：它是一台录音无线电台。跟那些声像设备一样，它不但把信号发进《时报》，还自行录上一份，以防发送失灵。我把手伸进侧边的开口，咔哒打开开关，另一只手则装模作样地摆弄铅笔和采访本，开口头一个问题便问他：是什么拿定了主意，要写一本关于芬尼斯的书。

此后，我隔三差五抛个问题，让他一直说下去，自己三个字里只听一个字。那台无线电台干的差事，任凭我怎么也比不上。与此同时，我一面留意房间一侧的史蒂夫·拉维克、莫顿·霍尔斯特克和利奥·贝尔歇，一面留意另一侧的比什·韦尔。比什离我不远不近，正处在竖起耳朵勉强听得清的范围。我用眼角瞥见另一个男人朝他走去，那人看上去年纪轻些，像个穿了便装的陆军军官。

「我亲爱的主教！」这人开口招呼道。

据我所知，那个绰号正是发源于芬尼斯。我心里默默记下了这一笔。

===Original===
“How are you?” Bish replied, grasping the other’s hand. “You have been in Afghanistan, I perceive.”

That did it. I told you I was an old *Sherlock Holmes* reader; I recognized that line. This meeting was prearranged, neither of them had ever met before, and they needed a recognition code. Then I returned to Murell, and decided to wonder about Bish Ware and “Dr. Watson” later.

It wasn’t long before I was noticing a few odd things about Murell, too, which confirmed my original suspicions of him. He didn’t have the firm name of his alleged publishers right, he didn’t know what a literary agent was and, after claiming to have been a newsman, he consistently used the expression “news service.” I know, everybody says that﻿—everybody but newsmen. They always call a news service a “paper,” especially when talking to other newsmen.

Of course, there isn’t any paper connected with it, except the pad the editor doodles on. What gets to the public is photoprint, out of a teleprinter. As small as our circulation is, we have four or five hundred of them in Port Sandor and around among the small settlements in the archipelago, and even on the mainland. Most of them are in bars and cafés and cigar stores and places like that, operated by a coin in a slot and leased by the proprietor, and some of the big hunter-ships like Joe Kivelson’s *Javelin* and Nip Spazoni’s *Bulldog* have them.

===Chinese===
「别来无恙？」比什握住对方的手，应道。「依我看，您到过阿富汗。」

这一下全露底了。我说过，我是读《福尔摩斯探案》长大的老读者；这句话我认得出来。这场会面是事先安排的，两人此前从未谋面，他们需要一套识别暗号。随后我把心思收回到穆雷尔身上，决定把比什·韦尔和那位「华生医生」的疑团留待以后再琢磨。

没过多久，我也开始留意到穆雷尔的几处蹊跷，印证了我最初对他的怀疑。他自报的那家出版社，字号都没说对；他不知道文学经纪人（literary agent）是什么；而且既然自称当过新闻记者，却一口一个「新闻社」（news service）。我知道，人人都这么说——人人，除了真报人。真报人张口总把新闻社叫「报纸」，跟同行说话时尤其如此。

当然，如今的报纸跟纸不沾边，除了编辑涂鸦用的那沓便笺。送到公众手里的是光印件（photoprint），从电传印报机（teleprinter）里吐出来。我们的发行量虽小，这类机器在桑多尔港和群岛间的小居民点、甚至大陆上，也铺了四五百台。大多数摆在酒吧、咖啡馆、雪茄店之类的去处，投币开动，由店主租营；一些大猎怪船，比如乔·基维尔森的标枪号和尼普·斯帕佐尼的斗牛犬号，船上也装着。

===Original===
But long ago, back in the First Centuries, Pre-Atomic and Atomic Era, they were actually printed on paper, and the copies distributed and sold. They used printing presses as heavy as a spaceship’s engines. That’s why we still call ourselves the Press. Some of the old papers on Terra, like *La Prensa* in Buenos Aires, and the *Melbourne Times*, which used to be the *London Times* when there was still a London, were printed that way originally.

Finally I got through with my interview, and then shot about fifteen minutes of audiovisual, which would be cut to five for the ’cast. By this time Bish and “Dr. Watson” had disappeared, I supposed to the ship’s bar, and Ravick and his accomplices had gotten through with their conspiracy to defraud the hunters. I turned Murell over to Tom, and went over to where they were standing together. I’d put away my pencil and pad long ago with Murell; now I got them out ostentatiously as I approached.

“Good day, gentlemen,” I greeted them. “I’m representing the *Port Sandor Times*.”

“Oh, run along, sonny; we haven’t time to bother with you,” Hallstock said.

===Chinese===
但很久以前，早在最初的几个世纪——前原子时代和原子时代——报纸还真印在纸上，一份份发行售卖。他们用的印刷机，沉重得赛过飞船的引擎。所以我们至今还自称「报界」（the Press）。特拉上有些老报纸，比如布宜诺斯艾利斯的《新闻报》（*La Prensa*），比如《墨尔本时报》（*Melbourne Times*）——在伦敦还存在的年头，它原叫《伦敦时报》（*London Times*）——起初都是那么印的。

采访总算做完了，接着我又拍了约十五分钟的声像，播出时会剪成五分钟。这时比什和「华生医生」已经不见了，我猜是上了船上的酒吧；拉维克和他的同党，也把坑害猎手的密谋商量完毕。我把穆雷尔交给汤姆，自己朝他们聚在一起站立的地方走去。跟穆雷尔周旋时，我早把铅笔和便笺本收了起来；这会儿走近了，又大张旗鼓地掏了出来。

「日安，几位先生，」我向他们致意。「我代表《桑多尔港时报》（*Port Sandor Times*）。」

「哦，一边玩去吧，小家伙；我们没工夫搭理你，」霍尔斯特克说。

===Original===
“But I want to get a story from Mr. Belsher,” I began.

“Well, come back in five or six years, when you’re dry behind the ears, and you can get it,” Ravick told me.

“Our readers aren’t interested in the condition of my ears,” I said sweetly. “They want to read about the price of tallow-wax. What’s this about another price cut? To thirty-five centisols a pound, I understand.”

“Oh, Steve, the young man’s from the news service, and his father will publish whatever he brings home,” Belsher argued. “We’d better give him something.” He turned to me. “I don’t know how this got out, but it’s quite true,” he said. He had a long face, like a horse’s. At least, he looked like pictures of horses I’d seen. As he spoke, he pulled it even longer and became as doleful as an undertaker at a ten-thousand-sol funeral.

===Chinese===
「可我想从贝尔歇先生这儿拿条新闻，」我开口道。

「那好，过个五六年，等你耳朵后面的湿气晾干了再来，包你能拿到，」拉维克告诉我。

「我们的读者对我耳朵的状况不感兴趣，」我甜甜地说。「他们想读的是脂蜡的价钱。听说又要降价了？我了解到的说法是，降到三十五厘索尔一磅。」

「哎呀，史蒂夫，这年轻人是新闻社的，他从家里带回来什么，他父亲就会登什么，」贝尔歇争辩道。「咱们最好还是给他点东西。」他转向我。「我不知道这消息是怎么走漏的，不过确有其事，」他说。他生着一张长脸，像马的脸。至少，像我见过的马的图片。说话间，他把脸拉得更长了，愁得像出席万索尔葬礼的殡仪老板。

===Original===
“The price has gone down, again. Somebody has developed a synthetic substitute. Of course, it isn’t anywhere near as good as real Fenris tallow-wax, but try and tell the public that. So Kapstaad Chemical is being undersold, and the only way they can stay in business is cut the price they have to pay for wax.﻿ ﻿…”

It went on like that, and this time I had real trouble keeping my anger down. In the first place, I was pretty sure there was no substitute for Fenris tallow-wax, good, bad or indifferent. In the second place, it isn’t sold to the gullible public, it’s sold to equipment manufacturers who have their own test engineers and who have to keep their products up to legal safety standards. He didn’t know this balderdash of his was going straight to the *Times* as fast as he spouted it; he thought I was taking it down in shorthand. I knew exactly what Dad would do with it. He’d put it on telecast in Belsher’s own voice.

Maybe the monster-hunters would start looking around for a rope, then.

When I got through listening to him, I went over and got a short audiovisual of Captain Marshak of the *Peenemünde* for the ’cast, and then I rejoined Tom and Murell.

===Chinese===
「价钱又跌了。有人研发出了一种合成替代品。当然，它远远比不上真正的芬尼斯脂蜡，可你试试把这道理讲给公众听。卡普斯塔德化学公司（Kapstaad Chemical）的生意叫人压了价，他们要维持下去，唯一的办法就是削减收蜡的进价……」

他就这么一路说下去，这一回我可是费了真力气才把火气压住。其一，我相当有把握，芬尼斯脂蜡根本没有替代品，好的、坏的、不好不坏的都没有。其二，脂蜡并不是卖给好糊弄的公众的，而是卖给设备制造商的，那些厂家有自己的测试工程师，产品必须达到法定的安全标准。他不知道，自己这通胡话正随着他喷吐的速度，一路直通《时报》；他还当我在用速记一字字记下来呢。爸爸会拿它派什么用场，我一清二楚。他会用贝尔歇本人的嗓音，把它送上电视广播。

到那时候，猎怪手们说不定就该开始四处寻摸绳子了。

听他讲究，我走过去给佩内明德号的马沙克船长拍了一小段声像，预备播出，然后回到汤姆和穆雷尔身边。

===Original===
“Mr. Murell says he’s staying with you at the *Times*,” Tom said. He seemed almost as disappointed as Professor Hartzenbosch. I wondered, for an incredulous moment, if Tom had been trying to kidnap Murell away from me. “He wants to go out on the *Javelin* with us for a monster-hunt.”

“Well, that’s swell!” I said. “You can pay off on that promise to take me monster-hunting, too. Right now, Mr. Murell is my big story.” I reached into the front pocket of my “camera” case for the handphone, to shift to two-way. “I’ll call the *Times* and have somebody come up with a car to get us and Mr. Murell’s luggage.”

“Oh, I have a car. Jeep, that is,” Tom said. “It’s down on the Bottom Level. We can use that.”

Funny place to leave a car. And I was sure that he and Murell had come to some kind of an understanding, while I was being lied to by Belsher. I didn’t get it. There was just too much going on around me that I didn’t get, and me, I’m supposed to be the razor-sharp newshawk who gets everything.

===Chinese===
「穆雷尔先生说，他要住在你们《时报》那儿，」汤姆说。他那副失望劲儿，几乎赶得上哈岑博施教授。有一瞬间，我简直难以置信地疑心起来：汤姆是不是一直想把穆雷尔从我身边拐走。「他想跟我们上标枪号出海猎怪。」

「哎呀，那敢情好！」我说。「你正好把带我出海猎怪的那个许诺也一并兑现。眼下，穆雷尔先生就是我的头号新闻。」我把手伸进「相机」箱的前袋，取出手持话机（handphone），切换成双向通话。「我这就呼叫《时报》，叫人开辆车上来接我们，连穆雷尔先生的行李一起拉上。」

「哦，我有车。确切说，一辆吉普，」汤姆说。「就停在底层（Bottom Level）。咱们用那辆就行。」

把车撂在那么个地方，可真有意思。而且我敢肯定，就在我听贝尔歇撒谎的工夫，他和穆雷尔已经达成了某种默契。我没看透。我周围没看透的事实在太多，而我这个人，本该是那把锋利如剃刀、什么都抓得住的新闻鹰隼（newshawk）。

## Tallow-Wax Fire / 脂蜡大火

===Original===
Now that we were out of the traffic jam, I could poke along and use the camera myself. The wax was stacked in piles twenty feet high, which gave thirty feet of clear space above them, but the section where they had been piled was badly cut up by walls and full of small extra columns to support the weight of the pulp plant above and the carniculture vats on the level over that. However, the piles themselves weren’t separated by any walls, and the fire could spread to the whole stock of wax. There were more men and vehicles on the job than room for them to work. I passed over the heads of the crowd around the edges and got onto a comparatively unobstructed side where I could watch and get views of the fire fighters pulling down the big skins of wax and loading them onto contragravity skids to be hauled away. It still wasn’t too hot to work unshielded, and they weren’t anywhere near the burning stacks, but the fire seemed to be spreading rapidly. The dredger and the three shielded derricks hadn’t gotten into action yet.

I circled around clockwise, dodging over, under and around the skids and lorries hauling wax out of danger. They were taking them into the section through which I had brought the jeep a few minutes before, and just dumping them on top of the piles of mineral nutrients.

The operation seemed to be directed from an improvised headquarters in the area that had been cleared of ammunition. There were a couple of view screens and a radio, operated by women. I saw one of the teachers I’d gone to school to a few years ago, and Joe Kivelson’s wife, and Oscar Fujisawa’s current girlfriend, and Sigurd Ngozori’s secretary, and farther off there was an equally improvised coffee-and-sandwich stand. I grounded the jeep, and Murell and I got out and went over to the headquarters. Joe Kivelson seemed to be in charge.
===Chinese===
我们既已脱出车阵，就能慢悠悠地往前蹭，亲自操起摄影机了。蜡码成二十英尺高的堆，堆顶上还留着三十英尺的净空；只是堆蜡的这片地段被一道道隔墙切割得七零八落，还密布着许多加设的小立柱，用来支撑上面木浆厂的重量和再上一层肉培培养槽的重量。然而蜡堆彼此之间并没有任何隔墙，一场火便能蔓延到全部存蜡。现场的人和车辆之多，超出了施展得开的余地。我从四下围拢的人群头顶上方掠过，转到一侧相对没有遮挡的地方，既能观战，又能拍到消防员把一整皮一整皮的脂蜡拽下来、装上反重力载板运走的镜头。这时无遮蔽作业还不算太热，他们离燃烧的蜡堆也还远着呢，可火势似乎正在迅速蔓延。那台挖泥船和三台带护罩的吊车，都还没有投入行动。

我顺着顺时针方向绕行，在抢运蜡出险的载板和卡车的上上下下、里里外外躲闪腾挪。那些蜡被运进几分钟前我驾吉普穿过的那片区域，就那么直接撂在一堆堆矿物养料顶上。

整个行动似乎由一处设在已清空弹药地带的临时指挥部调度。那里有几台屏幕和一部无线电，都由妇女们操作。我看见一位几年前教过我的老师，还有乔·基维尔森的妻子、奥斯卡·藤泽眼下的女友、西格德·恩戈佐里的秘书；再远些，还有一个同样临时凑合的咖啡三明治摊。我把吉普降落地面，同穆雷尔一道下车，朝指挥部走去。坐镇主事的似乎是乔·基维尔森。

===Original===
I have, I believe, indicated here and there that Joe isn’t one of our mightier intellects. There are a lot of better heads, but Joe can be relied upon to keep his, no matter what is happening or how bad it gets. He was sitting on an empty box, his arm in a now-filthy sling, and one of Mohandas Feinberg’s crooked black cigars in his mouth. Usually, Joe smokes a pipe, but a cigar’s less bother for a temporarily one-armed man. Standing in front of him, like a schoolboy in front of the teacher, was Mayor Morton Hallstock.

“But, Joe, they simply won’t!” His Honor was wailing. “I did talk to Mr. Fieschi; he says he knows this is an emergency, but there’s a strict company directive against using the spaceport area for storage of anything but cargo that has either just come in or is being shipped out on the next ship.”

“What’s this all about?” Murell asked.

“Fieschi, at the spaceport, won’t let us store this wax in the spaceport area,” Joe said. “We got to get it stored somewhere; we need a lot of floor space to spread this fire out on, once we get into it. We have to knock the burning wax cylinders apart, and get them separated enough so that burning wax won’t run from one to another.”
===Chinese===
我想，我在前文各处大概已经多少暗示过：乔算不上我们这里智力顶拔尖的人物。脑瓜比他好使的多的是，可不管出了什么事、局面坏到什么地步，乔这人靠得住，总能稳得住自己那颗头脑。他坐在一只空箱子上，吊胳膊的悬带已经脏得不成样子，嘴里叼着一支莫汉达斯·范伯格那种歪歪扭扭的黑雪茄。乔平素抽烟斗，可对一个暂时只剩一条胳膊的人来说，雪茄省事得多。站在他面前的是市长莫顿·霍尔斯特克（Morton Hallstock），那副架势活像站在老师跟前的学童。

「可是，乔，他们就是死活不肯呀！」市长大人正哀嚎。「我确实跟菲耶斯基先生谈过了；他说他知道这是紧急状况，可公司有严格的指令：太空港区只准存放刚运抵的货物、或下一班船就要运出的货物，其余一概不行。」

「这是怎么回事？」穆雷尔问。

「太空港的菲耶斯基不肯让我们把这些蜡存进太空港区，」乔说。「我们总得找个地方存放；一旦真正跟这场火干上，我们需要一大片地面把它摊开来。必须把烧着的蜡筒砸散，让它们彼此隔出足够的距离，烧熔的蜡才不会从这一根流到那一根。」

===Original===
“Well, why can’t we store it in the spaceport area?” Murell wanted to know. “It is going out on the next ship. I’m consigning it to Exotic Organics, in Buenos Aires.” He turned to Joe. “Are those skins all marked to indicate who owns them?”

“That’s right. And any we gather up loose, from busted skins, we can figure some way of settling how much anybody’s entitled to from them.”

“All right. Get me a car and run me to the spaceport. Call them and tell them I’m on the way. I’ll talk to Fieschi myself.”

“Martha!” Joe yelled to his wife. “Car and driver, quick. And then call the spaceport for me; get Mr. Fieschi or Mr. Mansour on screen.”
===Chinese===
「哦？那为什么不能存进太空港区？」穆雷尔想弄个明白。「这批蜡下一班船就要运走。我正给布宜诺斯艾利斯的珍奇有机物公司办理托运。」他转向乔。「这些蜡皮全都标了物主记号吗？」

「标了。至于从破皮里散落出来、我们随手收拢的零蜡，总能想出个办法，算清各家该分多少。」

「好。给我备辆车，送我去太空港。先打电话告诉他们我在路上。我亲自去跟菲耶斯基谈。」

「玛莎（Martha）！」乔朝他妻子喊道。「车和司机，快点。然后替我接通太空港；把菲耶斯基先生或曼苏尔先生（Mr. Mansour）叫上屏幕。」

===Original===
Inside two minutes, a car came in and picked Murell up. By that time, Joe was talking to somebody at the spaceport. I called the paper, and told Dad that Murell was buying the wax for his company as fast as it was being pulled off the fire, at eighty centisols a pound. He said that would go out as a special bulletin right away. Then I talked to Morton Hallstock, and this time he wasn’t giving me any of the run-along-sonny routine. I told him, rather hypocritically, what a fine thing he’d done, getting that equipment from Hunters’ Hall. I suspect I sounded as though I were mayor of Port Sandor and Hallstock, just seventeen years old, had done something the grownups thought was real smart for a kid. If so, he didn’t seem to notice. Somebody connected with the press was being nice to him. I asked him where Steve Ravick was.

“Mr. Ravick is at Hunters’ Hall,” he said. “He thought it would be unwise to make a public appearance just now.” Oh, brother, what an understatement! “There seems to be a lot of public feeling against him, due to some misconception that he was responsible for what happened to Captain Kivelson’s ship. Of course, that is absolutely false. Mr. Ravick had absolutely nothing to do with that. He wasn’t anywhere near the *Javelin*.”

“Where’s Al Devis?” I asked.
===Chinese===
不出两分钟，一辆车驶进来，接走了穆雷尔。这时乔已经在跟太空港的什么人通话了。我给报社挂了电话，告诉爸爸：穆雷尔正以每磅八十厘索尔的价钱，代表他的公司把抢下火场的蜡照单全收。他说这条消息马上就作为特别公报发出去。随后我去跟莫顿·霍尔斯特克搭话，这一回他可再没拿「一边玩去吧，小家伙」那套来搪塞我。我相当口是心非地夸了他几句，说他从猎手会堂调来装备，干了一件漂亮事。我怀疑自己那口气听上去像是：我成了桑多尔港的市长，而霍尔斯特克只是个十七岁的小年轻，干了件大人们觉得「对个孩子来说真够机灵」的事。就算真是那样，他倒也没听出来。好歹有个报界的人对他客客气气。我问他史蒂夫·拉维克在哪儿。

「拉维克先生在猎手会堂，」他说。「他认为眼下这时节公开露面是不明智的。」嚯，老天，这话可说得真够轻描淡写的！「公众对他似乎颇有些情绪，因为有一种误解，以为基维尔森船长的船出的事该由他负责。当然啦，那纯属子虚乌有。拉维克先生与那件事绝对毫无干系。他当时根本就不在标枪号附近。」

「阿尔·德维斯在哪儿？」我问。

===Original===
“Who? I don’t believe I know him.”

After Hallstock got into his big black air-limousine and took off, Joe Kivelson gave a short laugh.

“I could have told him where Al Devis is,” he said. “No, I couldn’t, either,” he corrected himself. “That’s a religious question, and I don’t discuss religion.”

I shut off my radio in a hurry. “Who got him?” I asked.
===Chinese===
「谁？我想我不认识这么个人。」

霍尔斯特克上了他那辆大型黑色空中轿车飞走之后，乔·基维尔森短促地笑了一声。

「我本可以告诉他阿尔·德维斯在哪儿，」他说。「不对，其实我也说不上来，」他又改了口。「那是个宗教问题，我不讨论宗教。」

我赶紧关掉了自己的无线电。「是谁干掉的他？」我问。

===Original===
Joe named a couple of men from one of the hunter-ships.

“Here’s what happened. There were six men on guard here; they had a jeep with a 7 mm machine gun. About an hour ago, a lorry pulled in, with two men in boat-clothes on it. They said that Pierre Karolyi’s *Corinne* had just come in with a hold full of wax, and they were bringing it up from the docks, and where should they put it? Well, the men on guard believed that; Pierre’d gone off into the twilight zone after the *Helldiver* contacted us, and he could have gotten a monster in the meantime.

“Well, they told these fellows that there was more room over on the other side of the stacks, and the lorry went up above the stacks and started across, and when they were about the middle, one of the men in it threw out a thermoconcentrate bomb. The lorry took off, right away. The only thing was that there were two men in the jeep, and one of them was at the machine gun. They’d lifted to follow the lorry over and show them where to put this wax, and as soon as the bomb went off, the man at the gun grabbed it and caught the lorry in his sights and let go. This fellow hadn’t been covering for cutting-up work for years for nothing. He got one burst right in the control cabin, and the lorry slammed into the next column foundation. After they called in an alarm on the fire the bomb had started, a couple of them went to see who’d been in the lorry. The two men in it were both dead, and one of them was Al Devis.”
===Chinese===
乔点出了其中一艘猎怪船上两名船员的名字。

「事情经过是这样：这儿原先有六个人守卫，配一辆架着 7 毫米机枪的吉普。大约一个钟头前，一辆卡车开了进来，车上是两个穿船员装束的人。他们说，皮埃尔·卡罗利（Pierre Karolyi）的科琳号（*Corinne*）刚进港，满舱的蜡，他们正把货从船坞运上来，问该卸在哪儿？守卫的人信了；自从地狱潜水员号跟我们联系上之后，皮埃尔就驶进晨昏带去了，这中间他确实可能已经猎到了一头怪兽。

「于是守卫告诉那两位，蜡堆另一侧那头地方更宽敞；卡车便升到蜡堆上方，开始横穿过去。刚到差不多中段，车里一个人扔出一枚集热剂炸弹，卡车立刻逃之夭夭。只可惜吉普上有两个人，其中一个正守在机枪后面。他们本来已经升空跟在卡车后面，要指点这批蜡卸在何处；炸弹一响，守机枪的那位一把攥住机枪，把卡车套进瞄具，就搂了扳机。这位仁兄这些年为宰割作业打掩护，可不是白当的。他一梭子正中操纵舱，卡车便一头撞上旁边那根立柱的基座。他们先就炸弹引起的大火报了警，随后有两个人过去查看卡车里坐的是什么人。车里两人都死了，其中一个就是阿尔·德维斯。」

===Original===
“Pity,” I said. “I’d been looking forward to putting a recording of his confession on the air. Where is this lorry now?”

Joe pointed toward the burning wax piles. “Almost directly on the other side. We have a couple of men guarding it. The bodies are still in it.
