We don’t want any tampering with it till it can be properly examined; we want to have the facts straight, in case Hallstock tries to make trouble for the men who did the shooting.”

I didn’t know how he could. Under any kind of Federation law at all, a man killed committing a felony﻿—and bombing and arson ought to qualify for that﻿—is simply bought and paid for; his blood is on nobody’s head but his own. Of course, a small matter like legality was always the least of Mort Hallstock’s worries.

“I’ll go get some shots of it,” I said, and then I snapped on my radio and called the story in.
===Chinese===
「可惜，」我说。「我还一直巴望着把他的认罪录音播出去呢。那辆卡车现在在哪儿？」

乔朝烧着的蜡堆一指。「差不多正在正对面。我们派了两个人守着，尸首还在车里。在正式勘验之前，我们不想让它被人做任何手脚；我们要把事实弄得一清二楚，以防霍尔斯特克想给开枪的那两个人找麻烦。」

我不明白他还能怎么找。按随便哪一种联邦法律，一个人在犯重罪时被击毙——投弹和纵火总归够得上重罪了吧——那都是自作自受、愿买愿卖；这笔血账除了记在他自己头上，跟谁都不相干。当然，合法性这样的小事，向来最不入莫特·霍尔斯特克的法眼。

「我去拍几个镜头，」我说，随即啪地拧开无线电，把这条新闻口播给了报社。

===Original===
Dad had already gotten it, from fire-alarm center, but he hadn’t heard that Devis was one of the deceased arsonists. Like me, he was very sorry to hear about it. Devis as Devis was no loss, but alive and talking he’d have helped us pin both the wax fire and the bombing of the *Javelin* on Steve Ravick. Then I went back and got in the jeep.

They were beginning to get in closer to the middle of the stacks where the fire had been started. There was no chance of getting over the top of it, and on the right there were at least five hundred men and a hundred vehicles, all working like crazy to pull out unburned wax. Big manipulators were coming up and grabbing as many of the half-ton sausages as they could, and lurching away to dump them onto skids or into lorries or just drop them on top of the bags of nutrient stacked beyond. Jeeps and cars would dart in, throw grapnels on the end of lines, and then pull away all the wax they could and return to throw their grapnels again. As fast as they pulled the big skins down, men with hand-lifters like the ones we had used at our camp to handle firewood would pick them up and float them away.

That seemed to be where the major effort was being made, at present, and I could see lifter-skids coming in with big blower fans on them. I knew what the strategy was, now; they were going to pull the wax away to where it was burning on one side, and then set up the blowers and blow the heat and smoke away on that side. That way, on the other side more men could work closer to the fire, and in the long run they’d save more wax.
===Chinese===
爸爸已经从火警中心得知了消息，只是还不知道德维斯是那两个已死的纵火犯之一。跟我一样，他闻讯也十分惋惜。德维斯这个人本身死不足惜，可他若是活着还肯开口，本可以帮我们把脂蜡大火和标枪号被炸这两桩事，一齐钉死在史蒂夫·拉维克头上。随后我返身回到吉普上。

人们开始向最初起火的中部蜡堆逼近。从堆顶上空越过去是没指望了；右边少说有五百个人、一百辆车，全都发了疯似的往外抢运没烧着的蜡。大型操纵机开上来，一次抓起尽可能多的半吨重蜡肠，摇摇晃晃地运走，卸到载板上，或倒进卡车，或者干脆撂到外围堆着的成袋养料顶上。吉普和小汽车一次次疾冲进去，甩出绳端拴着的抓钩，能拖走多少蜡就拖走多少，然后折回来再甩一次钩。大皮大皮的蜡一拽下来，拿着手提式升举器的人——跟我们在营地搬柴火用的那种一样——便把它们托起来，飘运出去。

眼下主力似乎正下在这一带；我看见升举滑车载着大鼓风机开了进来。到这时我明白他们的方略了：先把蜡从燃烧着的那一侧抢开，再把鼓风机架起来，把热浪和浓烟朝那一侧吹散。这样，另一侧就能有更多的人贴近火线作业，长远算下来，能多抢回不少蜡。

===Original===
I started around the wax piles to the left, clockwise, to avoid the activity on the other side, and before long I realized that I’d have done better not to have. There was a long wall, ceiling-high, that stretched off uptown in the direction of the spaceport, part of the support for the weight of the pulpwood plant on the level above, and piled against it was a lot of junk machinery of different kinds that had been hauled in here and dumped long ago and then forgotten. The wax was piled almost against this, and the heat and smoke forced me down.

I looked at the junk pile and decided that I could get through it on foot. I had been keeping up a running narration into my radio, and I commented on all this salvageable metal lying in here forgotten, with our perennial metal shortages. Then I started picking my way through it, my portable audiovisual camera slung over my shoulder and a flashlight in my hand. My left hand, of course; it’s never smart to carry a light in your right, unless you’re left-handed.

The going wasn’t too bad. Most of the time, I could get between things without climbing over them. I was going between a broken-down press from the lumber plant and a leaky 500-gallon pressure cooker from the carniculture nutrient plant when I heard something moving behind me, and I was suddenly very glad that I hadn’t let myself be talked into leaving my pistol behind.
===Chinese===
我从左边、也是顺时针方向绕蜡堆而行，为的是避开那边的大忙特忙；没过多久我就发觉，不走这边反倒更好。那里有一道齐天花板高的长墙，朝上城、朝太空港的方向逶迤而去，是承载上一层木浆木材厂重量的支撑之一；墙根底下堆着一大堆各色破烂机器，多年前拖进来一撂，从此无人问津。蜡几乎贴着这堆东西码放，热浪和浓烟把我逼得降了下来。

我打量那堆破烂，断定步行可以穿过去。我一直对着无线电做连贯的现场解说，便就这满坑满谷被遗忘的可回收金属发了通议论——要知道我们这儿常年闹金属短缺。随后我开始在破烂堆里择路穿行，肩上挎着便携式视听摄像机，一只手拿着手电。当然是左手；把手电拿在右手从来算不得聪明，除非你是个左撇子。

路倒不算难走。多数时候，我不必攀上翻下，从物件之间的空当里就能钻过去。我正走在木材厂一台报废的压榨机和肉培营养剂厂一口漏水的 500 加仑压力蒸煮锅之间，忽然听见背后有什么东西在动，顿时大大庆幸自己没听人劝说、把手枪留下。

===Original===
It was a thing the size of a ten-gallon keg, with a thick tail and flippers on which it crawled, and six tentacles like small elephants’ trunks around a circular mouth filled with jagged teeth halfway down the throat. There are a dozen or so names for it, but mostly it is called a meat-grinder.

The things are always hungry and try to eat anything that moves. The mere fact that I would be as poisonous to it as any of the local flora or fauna would be to me made no difference; this meat-grinder was no biochemist. It was coming straight for me, all its tentacles writhing.

I had had my Sterberg out as soon as I’d heard the noise. I also remembered that my radio was on, and that I was supposed to comment on anything of interest that took place around me.
===Chinese===
那东西有一只十加仑小桶那么大，长着一条粗尾巴和几片靠以爬行的鳍肢；一张圆嘴四周伸出六条触手，活像小象的鼻子，喉咙往里一半排满了锯齿状的獠牙。它的名堂有一打之多，但人们多半管它叫绞肉机（meat-grinder）。

这类东西永远饿着肚子，见什么动就想吃什么。我对它的毒性，不亚于本地任何动植物对我的毒性——这一层事实对它毫无影响；这台绞肉机可不是生物化学家。它径直朝我扑来，六条触手一齐扭动。

一听见响动，我的斯特贝格就已经拔在手里了。我还想起自己的无线电开着，而且照规矩，周围发生的任何有意思的事，我都该解说一番。

===Original===
“Here’s a meat-grinder, coming right for me,” I commented in a voice not altogether steady, and slammed three shots down its tooth-studded gullet. Then I scored my target, at the same time keeping out of the way of the tentacles. He began twitching a little. I fired again. The meat-grinder jerked slightly, and that was all.

“Now I’m going out and take a look at that lorry.” I was certain now that the voice was shaky.

The lorry﻿—and Al Devis and his companion﻿—had come to an end against one of the two-hundred-foot masonry and concrete foundations the columns rest on. It had hit about halfway up and folded almost like an accordion, sliding down to the floor. With one thing and another, there is a lot of violent death around Port Sandor. I don’t like to look at the results. It’s part of the job, however, and this time it wasn’t a pleasant job at all.
===Chinese===
「现场出现一台绞肉机，正径直朝我冲来，」我用一条不算太稳的嗓子解说着，随即朝它那镶满利齿的喉咙结结实实连轰三枪。这一下我打中了目标，同时还躲开了那些触手。它开始有点抽搐。我又补了一枪。绞肉机轻轻一挺，就此完事。

「现在我出去看看那辆卡车。」这时我敢肯定，自己的声音是发着抖的。

那辆卡车——连同阿尔·德维斯和他的同伴——在立柱所倚仗的一座两百英尺砖石混凝土基座上走到了尽头。它差不多撞在半腰上，折得几乎像架手风琴，一路滑坠到地面。这样那样的事情多了，桑多尔港一带横死的事向来不少。我不爱看这种下场。可这也是这份差事的一部分，而这一回，实在算不上愉快。

===Original===
The two men who were guarding the wreck and contents were sitting on a couple of boxes, smoking and watching the firefighting operation.

I took the partly empty clip out of my pistol and put in a full one on the way back, and kept my flashlight moving its circle of light ahead and on both sides of me. That was foolish, or at least unnecessary. If there’d been one meat-grinder in that junk pile, it was a safe bet there wasn’t anything else. Meat-grinders aren’t popular neighbors, even for tread-snails. As I approached the carcass of the grinder I had shot I found a ten-foot length of steel rod and poked it a few times. When it didn’t even twitch, I felt safe in walking past it.

I got back in the jeep and returned to where Joe Kivelson was keeping track of what was going on in five screens, including one from a pickup on a lifter at the ceiling, and shouting orders that were being reshouted out of loudspeakers all over the place. The Odin Dock & Shipyard equipment had begun coming out; lorries picking up the wax that had been dumped back from the fire and wax that was being pulled off the piles, and material-handling equipment. They had a lot of small fork-lifters that were helping close to the fire.
===Chinese===
看守残骸及车内物件的两个人坐在几只箱子上，一边抽烟，一边瞧着救火作业。

回去的路上，我退出手枪里打剩小半的弹匣，换上一匣满的；手电的光圈始终在我前方和两侧游动。这其实是犯傻，至少是多余。那堆破烂里倘若有过一台绞肉机，十拿九稳就不会再藏着别的活物。绞肉机这种街坊谁都不待见，连履带蜗牛也不例外。走近我击毙的那台绞肉机的尸首时，我捡起一根十英尺长的钢条，朝它捅了几下。见它连动都不动，我才放心从旁边走过去。

我回到吉普上，返回乔·基维尔森坐镇的地方；他正盯着五台屏幕掌控全局——其中一台的画面来自天花板上一具升举器上架着的摄像机——同时吼出一道道命令，四下里的扩音器又把这些命令复吼出去。奥丁船坞造船厂的设备开始出动了：卡车装载从火线后方倒运出来的蜡和正从蜡堆上抢下的蜡，还有各种物料搬运机械。他们还有不少小型叉式升举器，在贴近火线的地方帮着干。

===Original===
A lot of the wax was getting so soft that it was hard to handle, and quite a few of the plastic skins had begun to split from the heat. Here and there I saw that outside piles had begun to burn at the bottom, from burning wax that had run out underneath. I had moved around to the right and was getting views of the big claw-derricks at work picking the big sausages off the tops of piles, and while I was swinging the camera back and forth, I was trying to figure just how much wax there had been to start with, and how much was being saved. Each of those plastic-covered cylinders was a thousand pounds; one of the claw-derricks was picking up two or three of them at a grab.﻿ ﻿…

I was still figuring when shouts of alarm on my right drew my head around. There was an uprush of flame, and somebody began screaming, and I could see an ambulance moving toward the center of excitement and firemen in asbestos suits converging on a run. One of the piles must have collapsed and somebody must have been splashed. I gave an involuntary shudder. Burning wax was hotter than melted lead, and it stuck to anything it touched, worse than napalm. I saw a man being dragged out of further danger, his clothes on fire, and asbestos-suited firemen crowding around to tear the burning garments from him. Before I could get to where it had happened, though, they had him in the ambulance and were taking him away. I hoped they’d get him to the hospital before he died.

Then more shouting started around at the right as a couple more piles began collapsing. I was able to get all of that﻿—the wax sausages sliding forward, the men who had been working on foot running out of danger, the flames shooting up, and the gush of liquid fire from below. All three derricks moved in at once and began grabbing wax cylinders away on either side of it.
===Chinese===
许多蜡已经软得难以搬弄，好些塑料外皮更被烤得绽裂开来。东一处西一处，我看见外围的蜡堆底部也开始烧了，是从底下流出去的燃蜡引燃的。我已转到右边，正拍摄大型爪式吊车作业、从堆顶上吊走大根蜡肠的镜头；镜头来回摇动之际，我试着估算原本总共有多少蜡、眼下又能抢回多少。那种塑料包覆的蜡筒，每一根足足一千磅；有一台爪式吊车一抓就是两三根。……

我还在估算，右边一阵惊呼把我的头拽了过去。一股火焰腾空而起，有人尖叫起来；我看见一辆救护车朝出事的中心驶去，穿石棉服的消防员一路小跑，从四面围拢过去。准是哪一座蜡堆塌了，泼着了什么人。我不由自主打了个寒噤。烧着的蜡比熔化的铅还烫，沾上什么就死粘住什么，比凝固汽油还狠。我看见一个人被拖离更深的险境，衣服烧着了，穿石棉服的消防员一拥而上，撕掉他身上着火的衣物。可是没等我赶到出事地点，他们已经把他抬进救护车运走了。我只盼他们能赶在他断气之前送进医院。

接着右边又掀起一片喊声——又有几座蜡堆开始坍塌。整个过程我都拍到了：蜡肠向前滑泻，徒步作业的人们奔出险境，火焰蹿上半空，液态的火从底下喷涌而出。三台吊车一齐开进去，开始从火头两侧抢开蜡筒。

===Original===
Then I saw Guido Fieschi, the Odin Dock & Shipyard’s superintendent, and caught him in my camera, moving the jeep toward him.

“Mr. Fieschi!” I called. “Give me a few seconds and say something.”

He saw me and grinned.
===Chinese===
随后我看见了圭多·菲耶斯基——奥丁船坞造船厂的总监——便把他摄入镜头，驾着吉普朝他挪过去。

「菲耶斯基先生！」我喊道。「给我几秒钟，讲两句吧。」

他看见我，咧嘴一笑。

===Original===
“I just came out to see how much more could be saved,” he said. “We have close to a thousand tons on the shipping floor or out of danger here and on the way in, and it looks as though you’ll be able to save that much more. That’ll be a million and a half sols we can be sure of, and a possible three million, at the new price. And I want to take this occasion, on behalf of my company and of Terra-Odin Spacelines, to welcome a new freight shipper.”

“Well, that’s wonderful news for everybody on Fenris,” I said, and added mentally, “with a few exceptions.” Then I asked if he’d heard who had gotten splashed.

“No. I know it happened; I passed the ambulance on the way out. I certainly hope they get to work on him in time.”
===Chinese===
「我就是出来看看还能再抢回多少，」他说。「我们已有将近一千吨——或是运到了装运层，或是在这边脱了险，或是正在运进途中——照眼下这架势，你们还能再抢回同样这么多。按新价钱，那就是一百五十万索尔十拿九稳，三百万索尔也有指望。我还想借这个机会，代表敝公司和特拉-奥丁星际航运公司（Terra-Odin Spacelines），欢迎一位新的货运托运主顾。」

「哎呀，这对芬尼斯的每一个人都是特大的好消息，」我说，心里还补了半句：「除了寥寥几个例外。」接着我问他，听没听说被泼着的人是谁。

「没有。我知道出了这事；出来的时候跟那辆救护车打了个照面。我衷心希望他们来得及给他救治。」

===Original===
Then more wax started sliding off the piles, and more fire came running out at the bottom. Joe Kivelson’s voice, out of the loudspeakers all around, was yelling:

“Everybody away from the front! Get the blowers in; start in on the other side!”
===Chinese===
接着，更多的蜡开始从堆上滑落，更多的火从堆底奔流而出。乔·基维尔森的声音从四面的扩音器里吼了出来：

「全体离开正面！鼓风机开进来；从另一侧下手！」

## The Beacon Light / 信标之光

===Original===
We all said, "Shooting!" and, "The machine gun!" as though we had to tell each other what it was.

"Something's attacking them," Cesário guessed.

"Oh, there isn't anything to attack them now," Abe said. "All the critters are dug in for the winter. I'll bet they're just using it to chop wood with."

That could be; a few short bursts would knock off all the soft wood from one of those big billets and expose the hard core. Only why didn't they use the cutter? It was at the boat now.

"We better go see what it is," Cesário insisted. "It might be trouble."

===Chinese===
我们都嚷了起来：「是枪声！」「是机枪！」好像非得互相告诉一声那是什么似的。

「有什么东西在袭击他们，」塞萨里奥（Cesário）猜道。

「哦，这会儿可没有什么东西能袭击他们了，」艾布（Abe）说。「那些活物全都钻进洞里猫冬了。我敢打赌，他们不过是拿它来劈柴罢了。」

这倒有可能；对着那种粗大的柴段扫上几个短点射，就能把软木统统崩掉，露出硬木芯子。可他们为什么不用切割器呢？那玩意儿如今就在艇上。

「咱们最好过去看看是怎么回事，」塞萨里奥坚持道。「别是出了乱子。」

===Original===
None of us was armed; we'd never thought we'd need weapons. There are quite a few Fenrisian land animals, all creepers or crawlers, that are dangerous, but they spend the extreme hot and cold periods in burrows, in almost cataleptic sleep. It occurred to me that something might have burrowed among the rocks near the camp and been roused by the heat of the fire.

We hadn't carried a floodlight with us—there was no need for one in the moonlight. Of the two at camp, one was pointed up the ravine toward us, and the other into the air. We began yelling as soon as we caught sight of them, not wanting to be dusted over lightly with 7 mm's before anybody recognized us. As soon as the men at the camp heard us, the shooting stopped and they started shouting to us. Then we could distinguish words.

"Come on in! We made contact!"

===Chinese===
我们谁都没带武器；压根儿没想到会用得着。芬尼斯的陆上动物为数不少，全是些爬的蠕的，其中几种还挺危险，不过一到酷暑酷寒时节，它们便钻进地洞，睡得近乎僵死。我忽然想到，没准儿有什么东西在营地附近的岩堆里打洞藏身，被火烤出来的热气惊醒了。

我们没带泛光灯——月光之下用不着。营地的两盏，一盏顺着峡谷朝我们这边照，另一盏直指天空。我们一望见灯光就扯开嗓子喊了起来，可不想还没等有人认出我们，先被 7 毫米机枪随手扫上一片。营地的人一听见我们喊，枪声立时停了，他们也朝我们叫喊起来。渐渐地，能听清字句了。

「快进来！我们联络上了！」

===Original===
We pushed into the hut, where everybody was crowded around the underhatch of the boat, which was now the side door. Abe shoved through, and I shoved in after him. Newsman's conditioned reflex; get to where the story is. I even caught myself saying, "Press," as I shoved past Abdullah Monnahan.

"What happened?" I asked, as soon as I was inside. I saw Joe Kivelson getting up from the radio and making place for Abe. "Who did you contact?"

"The Mahatma; *Helldiver*," he said. "Signal's faint, but plain; they're trying to make a directional fix on us. There are about a dozen ships out looking for us: *Helldiver*, *Pequod*, *Bulldog*, *Dirty Gertie* …" He went on naming them.

"How did they find out?" I wanted to know. "Somebody pick up our Mayday while we were cruising submerged?"

===Chinese===
我们挤进棚子，所有人都围在艇的下舱口边——那如今成了侧门。艾布从人缝里挤过去，我跟在他后头往里钻。新闻记者的条件反射：新闻在哪儿，人就往哪儿钻。我甚至发觉，自己从阿卜杜拉·莫纳汉（Abdullah Monnahan）身边挤过时，嘴里还顺口蹦出一句「记者，借光」。

「出什么事了？」我一进到里面就问。只见乔·基维尔森（Joe Kivelson）正从电台前起身，给艾布让座。「你们跟谁联络上了？」

「『圣雄』（the Mahatma），地狱潜水员号（*Helldiver*）上的，」他说。「信号微弱，但听得清楚；他们正设法对我们定向定位。外面大约有十几条船在找我们：地狱潜水员号、裴廓德号（*Pequod*）、斗牛犬号（*Bulldog*）、脏格蒂号（*Dirty Gertie*）……」他还一个个数下去。

「他们是怎么知道的？」我追问道。「是我们在水下潜航的时候，有谁收到了我们的梅戴（Mayday）呼救？」

===Original===
Abe Clifford was swearing into the radio. "No, of course not. We don't know where in Nifflheim we are. All the instruments in the boat were smashed."

"Well, can't you shoot the stars, Abe?" The voice—I thought it was Feinberg's—was almost as inaudible as a cat's sneeze.

"Sure we can. If you're in range of this makeshift set, the position we'd get would be practically the same as yours," Abe told him. "Look, there's a floodlight pointed straight up. Can you see that?"

"In all this moonlight? We could be half a mile away and not see it."

===Chinese===
艾布·克利福德（Abe Clifford）正对着电台骂骂咧咧。「不，当然不是。我们连自己身在尼弗尔海姆（Nifflheim）的哪个鬼角落都不清楚。艇上的仪表全砸烂了。」

「那么，艾布，你们就不能观星定位吗？」那个声音——我琢磨是范伯格（Feinberg）的——微弱得几乎像猫打喷嚏，听不真切。

「当然能。只要你们在这台临时拼凑的电台收得到的地方，我们定出的位置和你们定出的，实际上是同一个，」艾布告诉他。「听着，我们有一盏泛光灯直指天空。你们看得见吗？」

「顶着这么亮的月光？我们哪怕离它只有半英里，也瞧不见。」

===Original===
"We've been firing with a 7 mm," the navigator said.

"I know; I heard it. On the radio. Have you got any rockets? Maybe if you shot one of them up we could see it."

"Hey, that's an idea! Hans, have we another rocket with an explosive head?"

Cronje said we had, and he and another man got it out and carried it from the boat. I repeated my question to Joe Kivelson.

"No. Your Dad tried to call the *Javelin* by screen; that must have been after we abandoned ship. He didn't get an answer, and put out a general call. Nip Spazoni was nearest, and he cruised around and picked up the locator signal and found the wreck, with the boat berth blown open and the boat gone. Then everybody started looking for us."

===Chinese===
「我们一直在用 7 毫米机枪打，」领航员说。

「我知道；听见了。从电台上听见的。你们有火箭吗？要是朝天上放一枚，我们没准儿看得见。」

「嘿，这倒是个主意！汉斯（Hans），咱们还有一枚带爆破弹头的火箭吗？」

克龙涅（Cronje）说有，他便和另一个汉子把它取了出来，搬出艇外。我把刚才的问题又向乔·基维尔森问了一遍。

「不是。你爸爸试过用屏幕呼叫标枪号（*Javelin*）；那准是在我们弃船之后。他没得到回音，便发出了全频道呼叫。尼普·斯帕佐尼（Nip Spazoni）离得最近，他驾船巡了一圈，收到定位信标的信号，找到了失事的船——只见艇库被炸开了口子，艇却不见了。于是人人都出动来找我们。」

===Original===
Feinberg was saying that he'd call the other ships and alert them. If the *Helldiver* was the only ship we could contact by radio, the odds were that if they couldn't see the rocket from Feinberg's ship, nobody else could. The same idea must have occurred to Abe Clifford.

"You say you're all along the coast. Are the other ships west or east of you?"

"West, as far as I know."

"Then we must be way east of you. Where are you now?"

"About five hundred miles east of Sancerre Bay."

That meant we must be at least a thousand miles east of the bay. I could see how that happened. Both times the boat had surfaced, it had gone straight up, lift and drive operating together. There is a constant wind away from the sunlight zone at high level, heated air that has been lifted, and there is a wind at a lower level out of the dark zone, coming in to replace it. We'd gotten completely above the latter and into the former.

===Chinese===
范伯格正说要呼叫其他船只，给他们提个醒。要是地狱潜水员号是我们唯一能用无线电联络上的船，那么十有八九：从范伯格的船上望不见火箭，别的船也望不见。艾布·克利福德显然也想到了这一层。

「你说你们都在沿海一带。别的船在你们的西边还是东边？」

「西边，据我所知。」

「那我们准是在你们老远的东边了。你们现在在哪儿？」

「桑塞尔湾（Sancerre Bay）以东约五百英里。」

这么说，我们至少在桑塞尔湾以东一千英里之外了。这是怎么闹的，我心里也有数。艇那两回浮出水面，都是径直往上蹿，升举-驱动引擎一并开动。高空中有一股恒定的风，从日照地带吹离——那是被抬升起来的暖气；低空又有一股风，从黑暗地带吹进来补位。我们已经完全升到后一股风的上面，进到前一股里头去了。

===Original===
There was some yelling outside, and then I could hear Hans Cronje:

"Rocket's ready for vertical launching. Ten seconds, nine, eight, seven, six, five, four, three, two, one; rocket off!"

There was a whoosh outside. Clifford, at the radio, repeated: "Rocket off!" Then it banged, high overhead. "Did you see it? he asked.

"Didn't see a thing," Feinberg told him.

===Chinese===
外头有人喊了几嗓子，接着我听见汉斯·克龙涅的声音：

「火箭已备妥，准备垂直发射。十秒、九、八、七、六、五、四、三、二、一——发射！」

外面嗖地一声啸响。守着电台的克利福德复诵：「火箭发射！」跟着，高高的头顶上轰然一响。「你们看见了吗？」他问。

「什么也没看见，」范伯格告诉他。

===Original===
"Hey, I know what they would see!" Tom Kivelson burst out. "Say we go up and set the woods on fire?"

"Hey, that's an idea. Listen, Mahatma; we have a big forest of flowerpot trees up on a plateau above us. Say we set that on fire. Think you could see it?"

"I don't see why not, even in this moonlight. Wait a minute, till I call the other ships."

Tom was getting into warm outer garments. Cesário got out the arc torch, and he and Tom and I raced out through the hut and outdoors. We hastened up the path that had been tramped and dragged to the waterfall, got the lifters off the logs, and used them to help ourselves up over the rocks beside the waterfall.

===Chinese===
「嘿，我知道有什么他们准看得见！」汤姆·基维尔森（Tom Kivelson）脱口而出。「咱们上那边去，把树林点着怎么样？」

「嘿，这主意好。听着，圣雄；我们头顶上的高原上有一大片花盆树（flowerpot tree）林子。咱们把它点着。你看你们能瞧见吗？」

「我看没有瞧不见的道理，哪怕月光这么亮。稍等一下，我招呼一声别的船。」

汤姆往身上套保暖的外层衣物。塞萨里奥取来电弧炬（arc torch），他和汤姆跟我三个冲出棚子、冲到露天里。我们顺着那条被踩踏、拖柴碾出来的小道疾步赶到瀑布跟前，把升举器（lifter）从圆木上卸下来，借它们的力翻上瀑布旁边的岩壁。

===Original===
We hadn't bothered doing anything with the slashings, except to get them out of our way, while we were working. Now we gathered them into piles among the trees, placing them to take advantage of what little wind was still blowing, and touched them off with the arc torch. Soon we had the branches of the trees burning, and then the soft outer wood of the trunks. It actually began to get uncomfortably hot, although the temperature was now down around minus 90° Fahrenheit.

Cesário was using the torch. After he got all the slashings on fire, he started setting fire to the trees themselves, going all around them and getting the soft outer wood burning. As soon as he had one tree lit, he would run on to another.

"This guy's a real pyromaniac," Tom said to me, wiping his face on the sleeve of his father's parka which he was wearing over his own.

===Chinese===
干活那阵子，枝条废料我们一直懒得打理，只求挪开不碍事。这会儿我们把它们归拢成一堆堆，码在树丛中间，摆放的位置还借了那点没停透的风势，再拿电弧炬一一点着。不多时，树枝烧起来了，跟着是树干外层的软木。尽管气温这时已降到零下 90 华氏度上下，居然热得叫人发慌。

塞萨里奥操着那支电弧炬。把枝条废料全点着之后，他又动手去点那些树本身，绕着树身转圈，把外层的软木引燃。一棵树点着了，他就奔向下一棵。

「这家伙真是个纵火狂，」汤姆对我说，一边抬袖子抹脸——那件风雪大衣是他爹的，套在他自己那件外头。

===Original===
"Sure I am," Cesário took time out to reply. "You know who I was about fifty reincarnations ago? Nero, burning Rome." Theosophists never hesitated to make fun of their religion, that way. The way they see it, a thing isn't much good if it can't stand being made fun of. "And look at the job I did on Moscow, a little later."

"Sure; I remember that. I was Napoleon then. What I'd have done to you if I'd caught you, too."

"Yes, and I know what he was in another reincarnation," Tom added. "Mrs. O'Leary's cow!"

===Chinese===
「可不，我就是，」塞萨里奥抽空答道。「你们知道五十次转世之前我是谁？尼禄（Nero），火烧罗马的那位。」神智学者（Theosophist）向来不忌讳这样打趣自己的宗教。照他们看，一样东西若经不起人家打趣，就算不得有多好。「再瞧瞧稍晚一点，我在莫斯科干下的那桩活计。」

「可不；我还记得呢。我那会儿正是拿破仑（Napoleon）。要是当年叫我逮住你，也有你好看的。」

「对啦，我还知道他另一次转世是谁，」汤姆补上一句。「奥利里太太的母牛（Mrs. O'Leary's cow，传说中踢翻油灯、烧掉芝加哥的那头牛）！」

===Original===
Whether or not Cesário really had had any past astral experience, he made a good job of firebugging on this forest. We waited around for a while, far enough back for the heat to be just comfortable and pleasant, until we were sure that it was burning well on both sides of the frozen stream. It even made the double moonlight dim, and it was sending up huge clouds of fire-reddened smoke, and where the fire didn't light the smoke, it was black in the moonlight. There wouldn't be any excuse for anybody not seeing that. Finally, we started back to camp.

As soon as we got within earshot, we could hear the excitement. Everybody was jumping and yelling. "They see it! They see it!"

The boat was full of voices, too, from the radio:

"*Pequod* to *Dirty Gertie*, we see it, too, just off our port bow … Yes, *Bulldog*, we see your running lights; we're right behind you … *Slasher* to *Pequod*: we can't see you at all. Fire a flare, please …"

===Chinese===
且不论塞萨里奥前世当真有过什么星界经历，这回他在林子里纵火，活儿干得实在漂亮。我们在退得够远的地方守了一阵——那距离刚好叫热度暖得舒服受用——直到确信冰封的溪流两岸都烧得旺了才罢。烈火甚至把双月交辉的月光都映得黯淡了，腾起一大团一大团被火映红的烟；火光照不到的烟，在月光下黑沉沉的。谁要看不见这场火，那可没有半点借口了。最后，我们动身返回营地。

刚走到听得见动静的地方，我们就听见了那片欢腾。人人又蹦又喊：「他们看见了！他们看见了！」

电台里的说话声也灌满了艇舱：

「裴廓德号呼叫脏格蒂号，我们也看见了，就在我们左舷船头外……对，斗牛犬号，看见你们的航行灯了；我们紧跟在你们后头……砍杀者号（*Slasher*）呼叫裴廓德号：我们压根儿看不见你们。请放一发信号弹……」

===Original===
I pushed in to the radio. "This is Walter Boyd, *Times* representative with the *Javelin* castaways," I said. "Has anybody a portable audiovisual pickup that I can use to get some pictures in to my paper with?"

That started general laughter among the operators on the ships that were coming in.

"We have one, Walt," Oscar Fujisawa's voice told me. "I'm coming in ahead in the *Pequod* scout boat; I'll bring it with me."

"Thanks, Oscar," I said. Then I asked him: "Did you see Bish Ware before you left port?"

"I should say I did!" Oscar told me. "You can thank Bish Ware that we're out looking for you now. Tell you about it as soon as we get in."

===Chinese===
我挤到电台跟前。「这里是沃尔特·博伊德（Walter Boyd），《时报》（*Times*）随标枪号落难者的代表，」我说。「谁手头有便携式声像拍摄机，借我拍些画面传回报社？」

这话引得正朝这儿赶来的各船报务员一片哄笑。

「我们有一台，沃尔特，」奥斯卡·藤泽（Oscar Fujisawa）的声音对我说。「我乘裴廓德号的侦察艇打头先过来，到时候给你带来。」

「多谢了，奥斯卡，」我说。接着又问他：「你们出港前见到比什·韦尔（Bish Ware）了吗？」

「岂止见到！」奥斯卡告诉我。「如今我们能出来找你们，就得谢谢比什·韦尔。等我们一到，细细讲给你听。」

## The Rescue / 营救

===Original===
The scout boat from the *Pequod* came in about thirty minutes later, from up the ravine where the forest fire was sending up flame and smoke. It passed over the boat and the hut beside it and the crowd of us outside, and I could see Oscar in the machine gunner's seat aiming a portable audiovisual telecast camera. After he got a view of us, cheering and waving our arms, the boat came back and let down. We ran to it, all of us except the man with the broken leg and a couple who didn't have enough clothes to leave the fire, and as the boat opened I could hear Oscar saying:

"Now I am turning you over to Walter Boyd, the *Times* correspondent with the *Javelin* castaways."

He gave me the camera when he got out, followed by his gunner, and I got a view of them, and of the boat lifting and starting west to guide the ships in. Then I shut it off and said to him:

===Chinese===
裴廓德号（*Pequod*）的侦察艇约莫半个钟头后飞了进来，来自山谷上头——那儿的林火还在腾着火苗和浓烟。它从我们的小艇、艇旁的小屋，还有屋外我们这一大群人的头顶掠过，我看见奥斯卡（Oscar）坐在机枪射手席上，端着一台便携式声像转播摄像机。他拍下了我们欢呼挥臂的镜头，随后小艇折返回来，降落下去。我们全都朝它跑去，只有断腿的那位和一两个衣裳不够、离不开火堆的人没去。艇门开启时，我听见奥斯卡正在说：

「现在，我把镜头交给沃尔特·博伊德（Walter Boyd），与标枪号（*Javelin*）落难船员同在的《时报》（*Times*）特派记者。」

他出艇时把摄像机交到了我手里，他的机枪手跟着下了艇。我拍了拍他们俩，又拍了小艇腾空而起、朝西飞去给船队引路的画面。然后我关掉机器，对他说：

===Original===
"What's this about Bish Ware? You said he was the one who started the search."

"That's right," Oscar said. "About thirty hours after you left port, he picked up some things that made him think the *Javelin* had been sabotaged. He went to your father, and he contacted me—Mohandas Feinberg and I still had our ships in port—and started calling the *Javelin* by screen. When he couldn't get response, your father put out a general call to all hunter-ships. Nip Spazoni reported boarding the *Javelin*, and then went searching the area where he thought you'd been hunting, picked up your locator signal, and found the *Javelin* on the bottom with her bow blown out and the boat berth open and the boat gone. We all figured you'd head south with the boat, and that's where we went to look."

"Well, Bish Ware; he was dead drunk, last I heard of him," Joe Kivelson said.

"Aah, just an act," Oscar said. "That was to fool the city cops, and anybody else who needed fooling. It worked so well that he was able to crash a party Steve Ravick was throwing at Hunters' Hall, after the meeting. That was where he picked up some hints that Ravick had a spy in the *Javelin* crew. He spent the next twenty or so hours following that up, and heard about your man Devis straining his back. He found out what Devis did on the *Javelin*, and that gave him the idea that whatever the sabotage was, it would be something to the engines. What did happen, by the way?"

===Chinese===
「比什·韦尔（Bish Ware）到底是怎么回事？你说过，最先发起这场搜寻的就是他。」

「没错，」奥斯卡说。「你们出港后大约三十个钟头，他察觉到一些情况，疑心标枪号遭到了蓄意破坏。他去找了你父亲，又联络了我——莫汉达斯·范伯格（Mohandas Feinberg）和我那会儿都还没出港——然后开始用屏幕呼叫标枪号。叫不应，你父亲便向全体猎怪船发出了总呼。尼普·斯帕佐尼（Nip Spazoni）报称登上了标枪号，随后去他估摸你们作业的海域搜索，收到了你们定位信标的信号，在海底找到了标枪号：船头被炸飞，艇库洞开，小艇不见了。我们都料定你们会驾小艇往南去，便到南边找了。」

「哼，比什·韦尔；我最后听到他的消息时，他已经烂醉如泥了，」乔·基维尔森（Joe Kivelson）说。

「嗐，那全是装的，」奥斯卡说。「装给城里的警察看，也装给别的什么需要蒙骗的人看。这一着灵光得很，会后他竟得以闯进史蒂夫·拉维克（Steve Ravick）在猎手会堂（Hunters' Hall）摆下的酒会。就是在那儿，他得到一点风声：拉维克在标枪号船员里埋了坐探。此后二十来个钟头，他顺着这条线一路追查，听说了你们那位阿尔·德维斯（Al Devis）扭伤了腰的事。他弄清了德维斯在标枪号上管什么差事，由此想到：不管那破坏手脚做在哪儿，总归要出在引擎上。对了，究竟出的是什么事？」

===Original===
A couple of us told him, interrupting one another. He nodded.

"That was what Nip Spazoni thought when he looked at the ship. Well, after that he talked to your father and to me, and then your father began calling and we heard from Nip."

You could see that it absolutely hurt Joe Kivelson to have to owe his life to Bish Ware.

"Well, it's lucky anybody listened to him," he grudged. "I wouldn't have."

"No, I guess maybe you wouldn't," Oscar told him, not very cordially. "I think he did a mighty sharp piece of detective work, myself."

===Chinese===
我们几个人抢着讲给他听，你一言我一语，互相打岔。他点了点头。

「尼普·斯帕佐尼察看那条船的时候，也是这么想的。后来他去找你父亲和我商量，随后你父亲开始呼叫，我们就收到了尼普的消息。」

谁都看得出来，要欠下比什·韦尔一条命，这事实在让乔·基维尔森难受得厉害。

「唉，总算还有人肯听他的，也算走运，」他酸溜溜地说。「换了我，可不会听。」

「是啊，想来你多半是不会听的，」奥斯卡对他说，口吻不怎么热络。「要我说，他这一番侦探功夫，干得漂亮极了。」

===Original===
I nodded, and then, all of a sudden, another idea, under *Bish Ware, Reformation of*, hit me. Detective work; that was it. We could use a good private detective agency in Port Sandor. Maybe I could talk him into opening one. He could make a go of it. He had all kinds of contacts, he was handy with a gun, and if he recruited a couple of tough but honest citizens who were also handy with guns and built up a protective and investigative organization, it would fill a long-felt need and at the same time give him something beside Baldur honey-rum to take his mind off whatever he was drinking to keep from thinking about. If he only stayed sober half the time, that would be a fifty percent success.

Ramón Llewellyn was wanting to know whether anybody'd done anything about Al Devis.

"We didn't have time to bother with any Al Devises," Oscar said. "As soon as Bish figured out what had happened aboard the *Javelin*, we knew you'd need help and need it fast. He's keeping an eye on Al for us till we get back."

"That's if he doesn't get any drunker and forget," Joe said.

Everybody, even Tom, looked at him in angry reproach.

"We better find out what he drinks and buy you a jug of it, Joe," Oscar's gunner told him.

===Chinese===
我点了点头，随后，冷不丁地，又一个念头——该归在*比什·韦尔，改邪归正*条目底下的——击中了我。侦探功夫；就是这个。桑多尔港（Port Sandor）正用得上一家像样的私家侦探社。没准我能说动他开一家。他准能经营得红火。他各路人面的关系都有，枪法又利落；再招募一两个枪法同样利落、身手泼辣却行事正派的公民，办起一个护卫兼侦查的机构，既填补了本地久感匮乏的一桩需要，又给了他一样东西，让他除了巴尔德蜂蜜朗姆酒（Baldur honey-rum）之外另有寄托，好把心思从那些他一直靠灌酒才不去想的事情上挪开。哪怕他只有一半时间是清醒的，那也算百分之五十的成功了。

拉蒙·卢埃林（Ramón Llewellyn）则想知道，阿尔·德维斯那边，有没有人对他采取了什么行动。

「我们可没工夫去搭理什么阿尔·德维斯，」奥斯卡说。「比什一琢磨透标枪号上出了什么事，我们就知道你们需要救援，而且十万火急。他正替我们盯着阿尔，等我们回去。」

「那得指望他别喝得更凶、把这茬给忘了，」乔说。

人人都对他怒目而视、面带责备，连汤姆（Tom）也不例外。

「乔，咱们最好打听打听他喝的是哪一种，也给你买上一壶，」奥斯卡的机枪手对他说。

===Original===
The *Helldiver*, which had been closest to us when our signal had been picked up, was the first ship in. She let down into the ravine, after some maneuvering around, and Mohandas Feinberg and half a dozen of his crew got off with an improvised stretcher on a lifter and a lot of blankets. We got our broken-leg case aboard, and Abdullah Monnahan, and the man with the broken wrist. There were more ships coming, so the rest of us waited. Joe Kivelson should have gone on the *Helldiver*, to have his broken arm looked at, but a captain's always the last man off, so he stayed.

Oscar said he'd take Tom and Joe, and Glenn Murell and me, on the *Pequod*. I was glad of that. Oscar and his mate and his navigator are all bachelors, and they use the *Pequod* to throw parties on when they're not hunting, so it is more comfortably fitted than the usual hunter-ship. Joe decided not to try to take anything away from the boat. He was going to do something about raising the *Javelin*, and the salvage ship could stop here and pick everything up.

"Well, one thing," Oscar told him. "Bring that machine gun, and what small arms you have. I think things are going to get sort of rough in Port Sandor, in the next twenty or so hours."

===Chinese===
我们的信号被收到时，地狱潜水员号（*Helldiver*）离我们最近，头一个赶到。它在空中盘旋挪转了几番，降进山谷，莫汉达斯·范伯格带着六名船员下了船，抬着一副架在升举器上的临时担架，还抱来一大摞毯子。我们把断腿的伤员送上船，又送走了阿卜杜拉·莫纳汉（Abdullah Monnahan）和断了手腕的那位。后面的船还多着，其余的人便接着等。乔·基维尔森本该随地狱潜水员号走，让人给他那条断臂瞧一瞧，可船长永远是最后一个离船的人，于是他留下了。

奥斯卡说，汤姆和乔，还有格伦·穆雷尔（Glenn Murell）和我，由裴廓德号带走。我心里乐意。奥斯卡和他的大副、领航员都是单身汉，不猎怪的时候，就拿裴廓德号来开宴会，所以船上陈设得比寻常猎怪船舒适。乔决定不从那艘小艇上带走任何东西。他打算张罗打捞标枪号的事，打捞船可以中途停在这里，把一切物件都收走。

「对了，有一件事，」奥斯卡对他说。「把那挺机枪带上，你们手头有什么轻武器也都带上。我看接下来这二十来个钟头，桑多尔港的场面要变得相当凶险。」

===Original===
I was beginning to think so, myself. The men who had gotten off the *Helldiver*, and the ones who got off Corkscrew Finnegan's *Dirty Gertie* and Nip Spazoni's *Bulldog* were all talking about what was going to have to be done about Steve Ravick. Bombing *Javelin* would have been a good move for Ravick, if it had worked. It hadn't, though, and now it was likely to be the thing that would finish him for good.

It wasn't going to be any picnic, either. He had his gang of hoodlums, and he could count on Morton Hallstock's twenty or thirty city police; they'd put up a fight, and a hard one. And they were all together, and the hunter fleet was coming in one ship at a time. I wondered if the Ravick-Hallstock gang would try to stop them at the water front, or concentrate at Hunters' Hall or the Municipal Building to stand siege. I knew one thing, though. However things turned out, there was going to be an awful lot of shooting in Port Sandor before it was over.

===Chinese===
我自己也渐渐这么想了。地狱潜水员号上下来的那些人，还有从「螺丝锥」芬尼根（Corkscrew Finnegan）的脏格蒂号（*Dirty Gertie*）和尼普·斯帕佐尼的斗牛犬号（*Bulldog*）上下来的那些人，个个都在谈论该怎么收拾史蒂夫·拉维克。炸掉标枪号，对拉维克本是一步好棋——倘若得手的话。可惜不曾得手，如今这一着多半倒要送他彻底完蛋。

再说，这事也决计不会是一场轻松的郊游。他手底下有一帮暴徒打手，还指望得上莫顿·霍尔斯特克（Morton Hallstock）手下二三十名城市警察；他们会顽抗，而且是死硬的顽抗。何况他们抱成一团，而猎怪船队却是一艘接一艘才赶得到。我捉摸不透，拉维克-霍尔斯特克这伙人是打算在滨水区拦截各船，还是收缩到猎手会堂或市政大楼（Municipal Building）里凭险固守。但有一件事我是清楚的。无论事态怎样收场，了结之前，桑多尔港免不了要响上一场规模惊人的枪战。

===Original===
Finally, everybody had been gotten onto one ship or another but Oscar and his gunner and the Kivelsons and Murell and myself. Then the *Pequod*, which had been circling around at five thousand feet, let down and we went aboard. The conning tower was twice as long as usual on a hunter-ship, and furnished with a lot of easy chairs and a couple of couches. There was a big combination view and communication screen, and I hurried to that and called the *Times*.

Dad came on, as soon as I finished punching the wavelength combination. He was in his shirt sleeves, and he was wearing a gun. I guess we made kind of a show of ourselves, but, after all, he'd come within an ace of being all out of family, and I'd come within an ace of being all out, period. After we got through with the happy reunion, I asked him what was the situation in Port Sandor. He shook his head.

"Not good, Walt. The word's gotten around that there was a bomb planted aboard the *Javelin*, and everybody's taking just one guess who did it. We haven't expressed any opinions one way or another, yet. We've been waiting for confirmation."

"Set for recording," I said. "I'll give you the story as far as we know it."

He nodded, reached one hand forward out of the picture, and then nodded again. I began with our killing the monster and going down to the bottom after the cutting-up, and the explosion. I told him what we had seen after leaving the ship and circling around it in the boat.

===Chinese===
最后，除了奥斯卡和他的机枪手、基维尔森父子，还有穆雷尔和我，其余的人都被这条那条船接走了。随后，一直在五千英尺高空盘旋的裴廓德号降了下来，我们登了船。它的指挥塔比寻常猎怪船上的长出一倍，摆着许多安乐椅和两张长沙发。还有一面观景与通讯两用的大屏幕，我赶忙走到跟前，呼叫《时报》。

我一按完波长组合码，爸爸（Dad）就出现在屏幕上。他只穿着衬衫，腰里还挎着一杆枪。我估摸我们爷儿俩这副亮相挺够看头的，不过话说回来，他差一点就落得家里人一个不剩，我差一点就干脆一了百了。阖家团圆的喜庆话说罢，我问他桑多尔港眼下是什么局面。他摇了摇头。

「不妙，沃尔特。标枪号上被人安了炸弹的说法已经传开，是谁干的，人人猜的都是同一个名字。我们暂且还没表态——往哪边说的态都没表。我们一直在等确证。」

「调到录制，」我说。「我把我们迄今所知的经过讲给你听。」

他点了点头，一只手伸到画面外头，随后又点了点头。我从我们猎杀怪兽、宰割完毕后下潜坐底讲起，讲到那场爆炸。又把我们弃船后驾着小艇绕船一圈所见的情形，一一讲给了他。

===Original===
"The condition of the hull looked very much like the effect of a charge of high explosive exploding in the engine room," I finished.

"We got some views of it, transmitted in by Captain Spazoni, of the *Bulldog*," he said. "Captain Courtland, of the Spaceport Police, has expressed the opinion that it could hardly be anything but a small demolition bomb. Would you say accident can be ruled out?"

"I would. There was nobody in the engine room at the time; we were resting on the bottom, and all hands were in the wardroom."

"That's good enough," Dad said. "We'll run it as 'very convincing and almost conclusive' evidence of sabotage." He'd shut off the recorder for that. "Can I get the story of how you abandoned ship and landed, now?"

His hand moved forward, and the recorder went on again. I gave a brief account of our experiences in the boat, the landing and wreck, and our camp, and the firewood cutting, and how we had repaired the radio. Joe Kivelson talked for a while, and so did Tom and Glenn Murell. I was going to say something when they finished, and I sat down on one of the couches. I distinctly remember leaning back and relaxing.

===Chinese===
「从船壳的残损状况看，非常像一份烈性炸药在机舱里爆炸所致，」我最后总结道。

「我们收到了它的一些图像，是斗牛犬号的斯帕佐尼船长传回来的，」他说。「太空港警察（Spaceport Police）的科特兰（Courtland）上尉已经发表了看法：那除了是一枚小型爆破炸弹，几乎不可能是别的。依你看，意外事故能不能排除？」

「能排除。当时机舱里一个人也没有；我们正坐底停着，全体人手都在军官餐室里。」

「这就够了，」爸爸说。「我们会以『极有说服力、几乎一锤定音』的破坏证据来刊发。」后面这句话，他是先关了录音器才说的。「现在可以给我讲讲你们弃船和登陆的经过了吗？」

他的手又朝前一伸，录音器重新开动。我简略讲了我们在小艇上的经历、登陆和失事、我们的营地、砍柴的事，还有我们怎么修好了电台。乔·基维尔森讲了一阵，汤姆和格伦·穆雷尔也讲了。他们讲完，我还想说点什么，便在一张长沙发上坐了下来。我清清楚楚地记得，自己往后一靠，浑身松快了下来。

===Original===
The next thing I knew, Oscar Fujisawa's mate was shaking me awake.

"We're in sight of Port Sandor," he was telling me.

I mumbled something, and then sat up and found that I had been lying down and that somebody had thrown a blanket over me. Tom Kivelson was still asleep under a blanket on the other couch, across from me. The clock over the instrument panel had moved eight G.S. hours. Joe Kivelson wasn't in sight, but Glenn Murell and Oscar were drinking coffee. I went to the front window, and there was a scarlet glow on the horizon ahead of me.

That's another sight Cesário Vieria will miss, if he takes his next reincarnation off Fenris. Really, it's nothing but damp, warm air, blown up from the exhaust of the city's main ventilation plant, condensing and freezing as it hits the cold air outside, and floodlighted from below. I looked at it for a while, and then got myself a cup of coffee and when I had finished it I went to the screen.

===Chinese===
再睁开眼时，是奥斯卡·藤泽（Oscar Fujisawa）的大副在把我摇醒。

「桑多尔港已经望得见了，」他正对我说。

我含含糊糊应了一声，坐起身来，才发现自己方才一直躺着，身上不知什么时候让人搭了条毯子。汤姆·基维尔森还在我对面那张长沙发上，盖着毯子睡得正沉。仪表板上方的时钟，已经走过了八个银河标准（G.S.）小时。乔·基维尔森不见人影，格伦·穆雷尔和奥斯卡正在喝咖啡。我走到前窗跟前，前方地平线上腾着一道猩红的光晕。

这一景，塞萨里奥·维埃拉（Cesário Vieira）又要错过了——倘若他下一回转世投胎，投到芬尼斯（Fenris）以外的地方去的话。说穿了，那不过是从城市主通风站的排气口里吹上来的湿暖空气，一撞上外头的冷空气便凝结、冻结，再叫下方的泛光灯照得通亮。我望了一阵，给自己弄了一杯咖啡，喝完便走向屏幕。

===Original===
It was still tuned to the *Times*, and Mohandas Feinberg was sitting in front of it, smoking one of his twisted black cigars. He had a big 10 mm Sterberg stuffed into the waistband of his trousers.

"You guys poked along," he said. "I always thought the *Pequod* was fast. We got in three hours ago."

"Who else is in?"

"Corkscrew and some of his gang are here at the *Times*, now. *Bulldog* and *Slasher* just got in a while ago. Some of the ships that were farthest west and didn't go to your camp have been in quite a while. We're having a meeting here. We are organizing the Port Sandor Vigilance Committee and Renegade Hunters' Cooperative."

===Chinese===
屏幕还停在《时报》的频道上，莫汉达斯·范伯格正坐在屏幕前，叼着一支他那种扭弯的黑雪茄，裤腰里还别着一支大号的 10 毫米斯特贝格（Sterberg）。

「你们这帮人可真能磨蹭，」他说。「我一直当裴廓德号是条快船呢。我们三个钟头前就到了。」

「还有谁到了？」

「『螺丝锥』带着他几个弟兄，这会儿就在时报社。斗牛犬号和砍杀者号（*Slasher*）也是前脚刚到。几条原先待在最西边、没去你们营地的那船，回来可有阵子了。我们正在这儿开会呢。我们要成立桑多尔港自警委员会（Vigilance Committee）和叛徒猎手合作社（Renegade Hunters' Cooperative）。」

## The Ship from Terra / 来自特拉的飞船

===Original===
I went through the gateway, towing my equipment in a contragravity hamper over my head. As usual, I was wondering what it would take, short of a revolution, to get the city of Port Sandor as clean and tidy and well lighted as the spaceport area. I knew Dad’s editorials and my sarcastic news stories wouldn’t do it. We’d been trying long enough.

The two girls in bikinis in front of me pushed on, still gabbling about the fight one of them had had with her boyfriend, and I closed up behind the half dozen monster-hunters in long trousers, ankle boots and short boat-jackets, with big knives on their belts. They must have all been from the same crew, because they weren’t arguing about whose ship was fastest, had the toughest skipper, and made the most money. They were talking about the price of tallow-wax, and they seemed to have picked up a rumor that it was going to be cut another ten centisols a pound. I eavesdropped shamelessly, but it was the same rumor I’d picked up, myself, a little earlier.

“Hi, Walt,” somebody behind me called out. “Looking for some news that’s fit to print?”

===Chinese===
我穿过闸口，装备装在一只反重力提箱（contragravity hamper）里，顶在头顶上拖着走。跟平常一样，我又在心里琢磨：除了来一场革命，还得下多大功夫，才能让桑多尔港（Port Sandor）城区变得跟太空港区一样干净、整洁、灯火通明。我心里清楚，靠爸爸的社论和我那些挖苦文章是办不成的。我们试得也够久了。

前头两个穿比基尼的姑娘只管往前挤，嘴里还叽叽喳喳，说着其中一个跟她男朋友吵的那一架。我则跟紧了前面那六个猎怪手：他们穿着长裤、短帮靴和船用短夹克，腰带上挎着大刀。他们准是同一条船上的，因为并没有争谁家的船最快、谁的船长最横、谁挣的钱最多。他们聊的是脂蜡（tallow-wax）的价钱，而且像是听到了风声：蜡价每磅还要再压低十厘索尔（centisol）。我毫不客气地偷听了一场，可惜跟我自己稍早些听到的传闻一模一样。

身后有人喊：「嗨，沃尔特（Walt）。找『适合见报』的新闻来了？」

===Original===
I turned my head. It was a man of about thirty-five with curly brown hair and a wide grin. Adolf Lautier, the entertainment promoter. He and Dad each owned a share in the Port Sandor telecast station, and split their time between his music and drama-films and Dad’s newscasts.

“All the news is fit to print, and if it’s news the *Times* prints it,” I told him. “Think you’re going to get some good thrillers this time?”

He shrugged. I’d just asked that to make conversation; he never had any way of knowing what sort of films would come in. The ones the *Peenemünde* was bringing should be fairly new, because she was outbound from Terra. He’d go over what was aboard, and trade one for one for the old films he’d shown already.

===Chinese===
我回过头去。是个三十五岁上下的汉子，一头棕色卷发，咧着大大的笑脸——阿道夫·洛蒂埃（Adolf Lautier），娱乐推广人。他和爸爸在桑多尔港电视台（telecast station）里各占一份股份，播出时间由他的音乐片、剧情片和爸爸的新闻节目对半分。

「凡是新闻都适合见报；只要是新闻，《时报》（*Times*）就登，」我告诉他。「觉得这趟能进到几部好惊险片吗？」

他耸了耸肩。我这么问不过是没话找话；来的是些什么片子，他从来无从预知。佩内明德号（*Peenemünde*）这趟捎来的应该相当新，因为她是从特拉（Terra）启程外航的一班。他要把船上的片子过一遍目，一比一地换下他已经放过的旧片。

===Original===
“They tell me there’s a real Old-Terran-style Western been showing on Völund that ought to be coming our way this time,” he said. “It was filmed in South America, with real horses.”

That would go over big here. Almost everybody thought horses were as extinct as dinosaurs. I’ve seen so-called Westerns with the cowboys riding Freyan *oukry*. I mentioned that, and then added:

“They’ll think the old cattle towns like Dodge and Abilene were awful sissy places, though.”

“I suppose they were, compared to Port Sandor,” Lautier said. “Are you going aboard to interview the distinguished visitor?”

===Chinese===
「听人说，沃隆德（Völund）那边一直在放一部正宗古特拉风味的西部片，这趟该转到我们这儿了，」他说。「是在南美洲拍的，用的真马。」

这片子在这儿准能轰动。这地方几乎人人都以为马跟恐龙一样早就绝了种。我就看过所谓的西部片，里头牛仔骑的是弗蕾亚星（Freya）的欧克里兽（*oukry*）。我把这话说了一遍，又补上一句：

「不过，他们会觉得道奇（Dodge）、阿比林（Abilene）那些老牛镇，简直是娘们儿气得没法看。」

「跟桑多尔港一比，怕也真够软绵绵的，」洛蒂埃说。「你要上船去采访那位贵宾吗？」

===Original===
“Which one?” I asked. “Glenn Murell or Leo Belsher?”

Lautier called Leo Belsher something you won’t find in the dictionary but which nobody needs to look up. The hunters, ahead of us, heard him and laughed. They couldn’t possibly have agreed more. He was going to continue with the fascinating subject of Mr. Leo Belsher’s ancestry and personal characteristics, and then bit it off short. I followed his eyes, and saw old Professor Hartzenbosch, the principal of the school, approaching.

“Ah, here you are, Mr. Lautier,” he greeted. “I trust that I did not keep you waiting.” Then he saw me. “Why, it’s Walter Boyd. How is your father, Walter?”

===Chinese===
「哪一位？」我问。「格伦·穆雷尔（Glenn Murell），还是利奥·贝尔歇（Leo Belsher）？」

洛蒂埃冲口送了利奥·贝尔歇一个称呼，你在词典里查不着，可谁也用不着查。走在我们前头的几个猎怪手听见了，都笑起来。他们再赞成不过了。他正要沿着「贝尔歇先生的血统与人品」这一引人入胜的话题继续发挥，却猛地刹住了话头。我顺着他的目光望去，看见老哈岑博施教授（Professor Hartzenbosch）——学校的校长——正走过来。

「啊，您在这儿呢，洛蒂埃先生，」他打招呼道。「但愿没有让您久等。」随后他看见了我。「哎呀，这不是沃尔特·博伊德（Walter Boyd）嘛。沃尔特，令尊可安好？」

===Original===
I assured him as to Dad’s health and inquired about his own, and then asked him how things were going at school. As well as could be expected, he told me, and I gathered that he kept his point of expectation safely low. Then he wanted to know if I were going aboard to interview Mr. Murell.

“Really, Walter, it is a wonderful thing that a famous author like Mr. Murell should come here to write a book about our planet,” he told me, very seriously, and added, as an afterthought: “Have you any idea where he intends staying while he is among us?”

“Why, yes,” I admitted. “After the *Peenemünde* radioed us their passenger list, Dad talked to him by screen, and invited him to stay with us. Mr. Murell accepted, at least until he can find quarters of his own.”

There are a lot of good poker players in Port Sandor, but Professor Jan Hartzenbosch is not one of them. The look of disappointment would have been comical if it hadn’t been so utterly pathetic. He’d been hoping to lasso Murell himself.

===Chinese===
我向他担保爸爸身体安好，又问候了他本人的健康，接着问起学校的近况。他告诉我说，跟所能指望的差不多好；我听出了弦外之音——他把「所能指望」稳稳地压得很低。然后他想知道，我是不是要上船去采访穆雷尔先生。

「说真的，沃尔特，穆雷尔先生这样一位名作家肯到我们这儿来，写一本关于我们这颗行星的书，实在是一件大好事，」他一脸郑重地对我说，随后又像临时想起似的补了一句：「他在我们中间盘桓期间打算下榻何处，您可有所耳闻？」

「哦，知道，」我承认。「佩内明德号把旅客名单电告我们之后，爸爸就跟他屏幕通话，请他住到我们家去。穆雷尔先生答应了——至少先住到找着他自己的住处为止。」

桑多尔港打扑克的高手多的是，扬·哈岑博施（Jan Hartzenbosch）教授却不在其列。他脸上那副失望的神情，若不是凄惶得实在过头，本该算得上滑稽。他原是指望亲自把穆雷尔套进自己家里去的。

===Original===
“I wonder if Mr. Murell could spare time to come to the school and speak to the students,” he said, after a moment.

“I’m sure he could. I’ll mention it to him, Professor,” I promised.

Professor Hartzenbosch bridled at that. The great author ought to be coming to his school out of respect for him, not because a seventeen-year-old cub reporter sent him. But then, Professor Hartzenbosch always took the attitude that he was conferring a favor on the *Times* when he had anything he wanted publicity on.

===Chinese===
「不知道穆雷尔先生能不能拨冗到学校来，给学生们讲一讲，」过了一会儿，他说。

「准能。我会跟他提的，教授，」我应承道。

这话叫哈岑博施教授挂了火。大作家上他的学校，理当是出于对他的敬重，而不是由一个十七岁的毛头实习记者打发去的。不过话说回来，哈岑博施教授素来的态度是：凡是他有事想要见报的，那就是赏了《时报》天大的脸面。

===Original===
The elevator door opened, and Lautier and the professor joined in the push to get into it. I hung back, deciding to wait for the next one so that I could get in first and get back to the rear, where my hamper wouldn’t be in people’s way. After a while, it came back empty and I got on, and when the crowd pushed off on the top level, I put my hamper back on contragravity and towed it out into the outdoor air, which by this time had gotten almost as cool as a bake-oven.

I looked up at the sky, where everybody else was looking. The *Peenemünde* wasn’t visible; it was still a few thousand miles off-planet. Big ragged clouds were still blowing in from the west, very high, and the sunset was even brighter and redder than when I had seen it last, ten hours before. It was now about 1630.

Now, before anybody starts asking just who’s crazy, let me point out that this is not on Terra, nor on Baldur nor Thor nor Odin nor Freya, nor any other rational planet. This is Fenris, and on Fenris the sunsets, like many other things, are somewhat peculiar.

===Chinese===
电梯门开了，洛蒂埃和教授随着人潮往里挤。我落在后头，决定等下一趟，好抢先进去、一直退到最里面，免得提箱碍着别人。过了一阵，电梯空着上来了，我上了去；到顶层，人群一哄而下，我重新开启提箱的反重力，拖着它走进户外——这会儿那户外空气已经凉快得差不多赶得上烤炉了。

我抬头望天，别人望着的也是同一片天。佩内明德号还看不见；她仍在行星之外几千英里处。大团大团破絮似的云仍从西边、从很高的地方涌过来，晚霞比我十个钟头前上回看见时更亮、更红。此刻大约是 1630 时。

好了，趁各位还没开始问这到底是谁疯了，容我指出：这里不是特拉，不是巴尔德星（Baldur），不是托尔星（Thor），不是奥丁星（Odin），不是弗蕾亚星，也不是任何一颗讲道理的行星。这里是芬尼斯（Fenris），而在芬尼斯，日落跟许多别的事情一样，都有些古怪。

---

===Original===
Fenris is the second planet of a G4 star, six hundred and fifty light-years to the Galactic southwest of the Sol System. Everything else equal, it should have been pretty much Terra type; closer to a cooler primary and getting about the same amount of radiation. At least, that’s what the book says. I was born on Fenris, and have never been off it in the seventeen years since.

Everything else, however, is not equal. The Fenris year is a trifle shorter than the Terran year we use for Atomic Era dating, eight thousand and a few odd Galactic Standard hours. In that time, Fenris makes almost exactly four axial rotations. This means that on one side the sun is continuously in the sky for a thousand hours, pouring down unceasing heat, while the other side is in shadow. You sleep eight hours, and when you get up and go outside—in an insulated vehicle, or an extreme-environment suit—you find that the shadows have moved only an inch or so, and it’s that much hotter. Finally, the sun crawls down to the horizon and hangs there for a few days—periods of twenty-four G.S. hours—and then slides slowly out of sight. Then, for about a hundred hours, there is a beautiful unfading sunset, and it’s really pleasant outdoors. Then it gets darker and colder until, just before sunrise, it gets almost cold enough to freeze CO2. Then the sun comes up, and we begin all over again.

You are picking up the impression, I trust, that as planets go, Fenris is nobody’s bargain. It isn’t a real hell-planet, and spacemen haven’t made a swear word out of its name, as they have with the name of fluorine-atmosphere Nifflheim, but even the Reverend Hiram Zilker, the Orthodox-Monophysite preacher, admits that it’s one of those planets the Creator must have gotten a trifle absentminded with.

===Chinese===
芬尼斯是一颗 G4 型恒星的第二颗行星，位于太阳系（Sol System）朝银河西南方六百五十光年处。其他条件相同的话，它本该差不多是特拉型的行星：离一颗较冷的恒星更近，接收的辐射总量大体相当。至少，书上是这么说的。我生在芬尼斯，这十七年来从没离开过。

然而，其他条件并不相同。芬尼斯年比我们用来作原子纪元（Atomic Era）纪年的特拉年略短一点，折合八千挂零的银河标准小时（Galactic Standard hours）。在这段时间里，芬尼斯几乎恰好完成四次绕轴自转。这就意味着，行星的一面要一连一千个钟头太阳悬在天上，源源不断地倾泻热浪，另一面则沉在阴影里。你睡上八个钟头，起床出门——坐隔热车，或者穿极端环境防护服（extreme-environment suit）——会发现影子才挪了一寸上下，天却又热了那么几分。最后，太阳磨蹭到地平线上，一挂就是好几个「天」——每个「天」合二十四银河标准小时——然后才缓缓滑没。接着，大约一百个钟头里，天上是一场美得永不褪色的日落，待在户外当真惬意。再往后天越来越黑、越来越冷，到日出前那一刻，冷得几乎能把二氧化碳冻起来。然后太阳升起，我们重新再来一遍。

我相信各位已经听出点眉目了：就行星而论，芬尼斯绝不是什么划算货。它算不上真正的地狱行星，宇航员们也没把它的名字变成一句咒骂——不像那颗氟大气层的尼弗尔海姆（Nifflheim）。不过，连正统一性论派（Orthodox-Monophysite）传道人希拉姆·齐尔克牧师（the Reverend Hiram Zilker）也承认，这类行星，创造主造它们的时候准是走了那么一小会儿神。

===Original===
The chartered company that colonized it, back at the end of the Fourth Century a.e., went bankrupt in ten years, and it wouldn’t have taken that long if communication between Terra and Fenris hadn’t been a matter of six months each way. When the smash finally came, two hundred and fifty thousand colonists were left stranded. They lost everything they’d put into the company, which, for most of them, was all they had. Not a few lost their lives before the Federation Space Navy could get ships here to evacuate them.

But about a thousand, who were too poor to make a fresh start elsewhere and too tough for Fenris to kill, refused evacuation, took over all the equipment and installations the Fenris Company had abandoned, and tried to make a living out of the planet. At least, they stayed alive. There are now twenty-odd thousand of us, and while we are still very poor, we are very tough, and we brag about it.

There were about two thousand people—ten percent of the planetary population—on the wide concrete promenade around the spaceport landing pit. I came out among them and set down the hamper with my telecast cameras and recorders, wishing, as usual, that I could find some ten or twelve-year-old kid weak-minded enough to want to be a reporter when he grew up, so that I could have an apprentice to help me with my junk.

===Chinese===
早在原子纪元第四世纪末殖民这颗行星的那家特许公司，撑了十年就破了产；要不是特拉与芬尼斯之间通一次消息单程就得六个月，它连这十年也撑不了。大崩盘终于来临时，二十五万殖民者被晾在了原地。他们投进公司的家当全打了水漂，而那笔家当，对多数人来说就是全部身家。联邦太空海军（Space Navy）调船来撤人之前，不少人连命也搭了进去。

可大约有一千人，穷得到别处没法重新起家，又硬朗得连芬尼斯都弄不死，拒绝撤离，接手了芬尼斯公司（Fenris Company）撂下的全部设备和设施，试着从这颗行星身上谋生计。至少，他们活了下来。如今我们有两万多人，依旧穷得很，却也硬朗得很，而且逢人就夸这份硬朗。

太空港降落坑四周宽阔的混凝土步道上，大约聚了两千人——全行星人口的百分之十。我走到他们中间，放下装着电视摄像机和录音机的提箱，照例盼着能找个十来岁、脑子不够灵光、长大后想当记者的孩子，好收个学徒帮我搬这些破烂。

===Original===
As the star—and only—reporter of the greatest—and only—paper on the planet, I was always on hand when either of the two ships on the Terra-Odin milk run, the *Peenemünde* and the *Cape Canaveral*, landed. Of course, we always talk to them by screen as soon as they come out of hyperspace and into radio range, and get the passenger list, and a speed-recording of any news they are carrying, from the latest native uprising on Thor to the latest political scandal on Venus. Sometimes the natives of Thor won’t be fighting anybody at all, or the Federation Member Republic of Venus will have some nonscandalous politics, and either will be the man-bites-dog story to end man-bites-dog stories. All the news is at least six months old, some more than a year. A spaceship can log a light-year in sixty-odd hours, but radio waves still crawl along at the same old 186,000 mps.

I still have to meet the ships. There’s always something that has to be picked up personally, usually an interview with some V.I.P. traveling through. This time, though, the big story coming in on the *Peenemünde* was a local item. Paradox? Dad says there is no such thing. He says a paradox is either a verbal contradiction, and you get rid of it by restating it correctly, or it’s a structural contradiction, and you just call it an impossibility and let it go at that. In this case, what was coming in was a real live author, who was going to write a travel book about Fenris, the planet with the four-day year. Glenn Murell, which sounded suspiciously like a *nom de plume*, and nobody here had ever heard of him.

That was odd, too. One thing we can really be proud of here, besides the toughness of our citizens, is our public library. When people have to stay underground most of the time to avoid being fried and/or frozen to death, they have a lot of time to kill, and reading is one of the cheaper and more harmless and profitable ways of doing it. And travel books are a special favorite here. I suppose because everybody is hoping to read about a worse place than Fenris. I had checked on Glenn Murell at the library. None of the librarians had ever heard of him, and there wasn’t a single mention of him in any of the big catalogues of publications.

===Chinese===
作为这颗行星上最伟大——也是唯一——一家报纸的明星——也是仅有——的记者，跑特拉—奥丁星固定航线的两艘船——佩内明德号和卡纳维拉尔角号（*Cape Canaveral*）——无论哪艘降落，我回回必到。当然，她们一出超空间（hyperspace）进入无线电范围，我们总立刻同她们屏幕通话，拿到旅客名单，再快速录下她们捎来的新闻——从托尔星土著最新的叛乱到金星（Venus）最新的政治丑闻，应有尽有。有时候托尔星的土著压根没跟谁开仗，或者金星这个联邦成员共和国的政治居然清白无丑闻，随便哪桩，都是「人咬狗」新闻里登峰造极的货色。所有新闻起码是半年前的旧闻，有些还超过一年。飞船六十来个钟头就能跑完一光年，无线电波却仍旧以老一套的每秒 186,000 英里慢吞吞地爬。

可我还是得去接船。总有些东西得亲自去取，通常是采访哪位过路要人。不过这一回，佩内明德号捎来的大新闻却是条本地消息。悖论？爸爸说天下没这回事。他说，所谓悖论，要么是措辞上的自相矛盾，换一种正确的说法就能消掉；要么是结构上的矛盾，那就干脆管它叫「不可能」，到此为止。这回要来的，是一位如假包换的活作家，要为芬尼斯——一年只有四天的那颗行星——写一本游记。格伦·穆雷尔，这名字听着就让人疑心是个笔名（*nom de plume*），而且此地谁也没听说过他。

这就怪了。这地方除了市民的硬朗之外，真正拿得出手炫耀的，还有我们的公共图书馆。人们为了不被烤死兼或冻死，大部分时间只能待在地下，有的是闲工夫要打发，而读书是其中比较便宜、比较无害也比较有进益的法子之一。游记类在这儿又格外受欢迎。我猜是因为人人都巴望读到一处比芬尼斯更糟的地方。我在图书馆查证过格伦·穆雷尔：哪一位管理员都没听说过他，几大出版物总目录里也寻不见他半个名字。

===Original===
The first and obvious conclusion would be that Mr. Glenn Murell was some swindler posing as an author. The only objection to that was that I couldn’t quite see why any swindler would come to Fenris, or what he’d expect to swindle the Fenrisians out of. Of course, he could be on the lam from somewhere, but in that case why bother with all the cover story? Some of our better-known citizens came here dodging warrants on other planets.

I was still wondering about Murell when somebody behind me greeted me, and I turned around. It was Tom Kivelson.

Tom and I are buddies, when he’s in port. He’s just a shade older than I am; he was eighteen around noon, and my eighteenth birthday won’t come till midnight, Fenris Standard Sundial Time. His father is Joe Kivelson, the skipper of the *Javelin*; Tom is sort of junior engineer, second gunner, and about third harpooner. We went to school together, which is to say a couple of years at Professor Hartzenbosch’s, learning to read and write and put figures together. That is all the schooling anybody on Fenris gets, although Joe Kivelson sent Tom’s older sister, Linda, to school on Terra. Anybody who stays here has to dig out education for himself. Tom and I were still digging for ours.

Each of us envied the other, when we weren’t thinking seriously about it. I imagined that sea-monster hunting was wonderfully thrilling and romantic, and Tom had the idea that being a newsman was real hot stuff. When we actually stopped to think about it, though, we realized that neither of us would trade jobs and take anything at all for boot. Tom couldn’t string three sentences—no, one sentence—together to save his life, and I’m just a town boy who likes to live in something that isn’t pitching end-for-end every minute.

===Chinese===
头一个也是顺理成章的结论是：这位格伦·穆雷尔先生是个冒充作家的骗子。唯一说不通的是，我实在想不出哪个骗子肯跑到芬尼斯来，也想不出他指望从芬尼斯人（Fenrisians）身上骗走什么。当然，他也可能是在哪儿犯了案逃出来的，可那样的话，又何必费心编一整套说辞？我们几位大名鼎鼎的市民，当初就是躲着别颗行星的通缉令来的这儿。

我正为穆雷尔的事纳闷，身后有人跟我打招呼，我转过身去。是汤姆·基维尔森（Tom Kivelson）。

汤姆跟我是哥们儿——趁他在港里的时候。他比我大一丁点儿：他满十八岁是在晌午前后，我的十八岁生日却要等到午夜，按芬尼斯标准日晷时间（Fenris Standard Sundial Time）算。他父亲是标枪号（*Javelin*）船长乔·基维尔森（Joe Kivelson）；汤姆在船上算是见习轮机员、第二炮手，外加大概第三鱼叉手（harpooner）。我们是一块儿上的学——也就是说，在哈岑博施教授的学校里念了两年，学认字、写字和算数。这就是任何一个芬尼斯人所能受到的全部学校教育，尽管乔·基维尔森把汤姆的姐姐琳达（Linda）送去了特拉上学。谁要是留在这儿，学问就得自己刨。汤姆和我还在刨我们那一份。

我们俩互相羡慕——在没认真细想的时候。我想象着猎海怪这一行惊险刺激又浪漫，汤姆则认定当新闻记者才真叫威风。可真静下心来一想，我们都明白：谁也不会拿自己的行当去换对方的，白饶上什么都一样。汤姆打死也连不成三个句子——不对，一个句子——而我不过是个城里孩子，喜欢住在不会时时刻刻前仰后翻的东西里。

===Original===
Tom is about three inches taller than I am, and about thirty pounds heavier. Like all monster-hunters, he’s trying to grow a beard, though at present it’s just a blond chin-fuzz. I was surprised to see him dressed as I was, in shorts and sandals and a white shirt and a light jacket. Ordinarily, even in town, he wears boat-clothes. I looked around behind him, and saw the brass tip of a scabbard under the jacket. Any time a hunter-ship man doesn’t have his knife on, he isn’t wearing anything else. I wondered about his being in port now. I knew Joe Kivelson wouldn’t bring his ship in just to meet the *Peenemünde*, with only a couple of hundred hours’ hunting left till the storms and the cold.

“I thought you were down in the South Ocean,” I said.

“There’s going to be a special meeting of the Coop,” he said. “We only heard about it last evening,” by which he meant after 1800 of the previous Galactic Standard day. He named another hunter-ship captain who had called the *Javelin* by screen. “We screened everybody else we could.”

===Chinese===
汤姆比我高大约三英寸，重大约三十磅。跟所有猎怪手一样，他正试着蓄络腮胡，虽然眼下不过是下巴上一层金黄的绒毛。看见他穿得跟我一样——短裤、凉鞋、白衬衫加一件薄夹克——我颇感意外。平日里，哪怕进城，他也一身船员装束。我往他身后一扫，瞧见夹克底下露出刀鞘的黄铜鞘尖。猎怪船上的人哪一回要是没挎着刀，那准是他身上什么都没穿。我纳闷他这会儿怎么会在港里。我知道乔·基维尔森绝不会单单为了接佩内明德号就把船开回来——离风暴和严寒来临，猎季只剩两三百个钟头了。

「我还以为你在南大洋（South Ocean）呢。」我说。

「合作社（Coop）要开一次特别会议，」他说。「我们昨天傍晚才听说。」——他这话的意思，是上一个银河标准日的 1800 时以后。他还报出了另一位猎怪船船长的名字，就是那位用屏幕呼叫过标枪号的。「能屏幕通知到的，我们都通知了。」

===Original===
That was the way they ran things in the Hunters’ Cooperative. Steve Ravick would wait till everybody had their ships down on the coast of Hermann Reuch’s Land, and then he would call a meeting and pack it with his stooges and hooligans, and get anything he wanted voted through. I had always wondered how long the real hunters were going to stand for that. They’d been standing for it ever since I could remember anything outside my own playpen, which, of course, hadn’t been too long.

I was about to say something to that effect, and then somebody yelled, “There she is!” I took a quick look at the radar bowls to see which way they were pointed and followed them up to the sky, and caught a tiny twinkle through a cloud rift. After a moment’s mental arithmetic to figure how high she’d have to be to catch the sunlight, I relaxed. Even with the telephoto, I’d only get a picture the size of a pinhead, so I fixed the position in my mind and then looked around at the crowd.

Among them were two men, both well dressed. One was tall and slender, with small hands and feet; the other was short and stout, with a scrubby gray-brown mustache. The slender one had a bulge under his left arm, and the short-and-stout job bulged over the right hip. The former was Steve Ravick, the boss of the Hunters’ Cooperative, and his companion was the Honorable Morton Hallstock, mayor of Port Sandor and consequently the planetary government of Fenris.

===Chinese===
猎手合作社（Hunters’ Cooperative）办事向来就是这个路数。史蒂夫·拉维克（Steve Ravick）总要等到大家的船全都下到赫尔曼·罗伊希之地（Hermann Reuch’s Land）沿岸，才召集会议，用他的傀儡和打手把会场塞满，想通过什么就通过什么。我一向纳闷，真正的猎手们还能忍这口气到几时。从我记事起——记围栏以外的事起——他们就一直在忍；当然，我记事也没多久。

我正想说几句这个意思的话，忽然有人喊：「在那儿！」我飞快瞄了一眼那几口雷达碗形天线，看它们朝着哪个方向，顺着往天上望去，从一道云缝里捉住了一点极小的闪光。心里稍一估算她得多高才晒得着太阳，我便放宽了心。就算用长焦相机，也只能拍出针头大的一小点，于是我记牢方位，转而打量起周围的人群。

人堆里有两位男士，衣着都讲究。一位高瘦，手脚都小；另一位矮胖，蓄着一把稀稀拉拉的灰褐色八字胡。瘦子左腋下鼓着一块，矮胖子右边胯上鼓着一块。前者是猎手合作社的头头史蒂夫·拉维克，后者那位同伴，则是尊敬的莫顿·霍尔斯特克（Morton Hallstock）——桑多尔港市长，顺理成章地也就是芬尼斯的行星政府。

===Original===
They had held their respective positions for as long as I could remember anything at all. I could never remember an election in Port Sandor, or an election of officers in the Coop. Ravick had a bunch of goons and triggermen—I could see a couple of them loitering in the background—who kept down opposition for him. So did Hallstock, only his wore badges and called themselves police.

Once in a while, Dad would write a blistering editorial about one or the other or both of them. Whenever he did, I would put my gun on, and so would Julio Kubanoff, the one-legged compositor who is the third member of the *Times* staff, and we would take turns making sure nobody got behind Dad’s back. Nothing ever happened, though, and that always rather hurt me. Those two racketeers were in so tight they didn’t need to care what the *Times* printed or ’cast about them.

Hallstock glanced over in my direction and said something to Ravick. Ravick gave a sneering laugh, and then he crushed out the cigarette he was smoking on the palm of his left hand. That was a regular trick of his. Showing how tough he was. Dad says that when you see somebody showing off, ask yourself whether he’s trying to impress other people, or himself. I wondered which was the case with Steve Ravick.

===Chinese===
打我记事起，这二位就一直各踞其位。我从来不记得桑多尔港搞过什么选举，也不记得合作社改选过什么职员。拉维克养着一帮打手和枪手——我就瞧得见他们中的两个在后面晃荡——替他把反对的声气摁下去。霍尔斯特克同样养着一帮，只不过他那帮人佩着警徽，自称警察。

隔三差五，爸爸会写一篇火辣辣的社论，骂骂这个、骂骂那个，或者两个一块儿骂。每逢这时候，我就带上枪，胡利奥·库巴诺夫（Julio Kubanoff）也带上——他是《时报》员工里的第三名、独腿排字工——我们俩轮班盯梢，别让人摸到爸爸背后去。不过回回都天下太平，这倒总让我有点伤自尊。那两个行霸的地基打得实在太牢，根本犯不着在乎《时报》登他们什么、播他们什么。

霍尔斯特克朝我这边瞟了一眼，跟拉维克嘀咕了句什么。拉维克嗤笑一声，接着把抽着的香烟摁灭在自己的左手掌心里。这是他的保留节目：亮一亮自己有多横。爸爸说过，看见谁卖弄的时候，先问问自己：他是想唬住别人，还是想唬住他自己？我琢磨着史蒂夫·拉维克属于哪一种。

===Original===
Then I looked up again. The *Peenemünde* was coming down as fast as she could without overheating from atmosphere friction. She was almost buckshot size to the naked eye, and a couple of tugs were getting ready to go up and meet her. I got the telephoto camera out of the hamper, checked it, and aimed it. It has a shoulder stock and handgrips and a trigger like a submachine gun. I caught the ship in the finder and squeezed the trigger for a couple of seconds. It would be about five minutes till the tugs got to her and anything else happened, so I put down the camera and looked around.

Coming through the crowd, walking as though the concrete under him was pitching and rolling like a ship’s deck on contragravity in a storm, was Bish Ware. He caught sight of us, waved, overbalanced himself and recovered, and then changed course to starboard and bore down on us. He was carrying about his usual cargo, and as usual the manifest would read, *Baldur honey-rum, from Harry Wong’s bar*.

Bish wasn’t his real name. Neither, I suspected, was Ware. When he’d first landed on Fenris, some five years ago, somebody had nicknamed him the Bishop, and before long that had gotten cut to one syllable. He looked like a bishop, or at least like what anybody who’s never seen a bishop outside a screenplay would think a bishop looked like. He was a big man, not fat, but tall and portly; he had a ruddy face that always wore an expression of benevolent wisdom, and the more cargo he took on the wiser and more benevolent he looked.

===Chinese===
接着我重又抬头。佩内明德号正以不致因大气摩擦而过热的极限速度向下俯冲。肉眼望去，她差不多有一颗铅弹大，几艘拖船正准备升空去接她。我从提箱里取出长焦相机，检查一遍，举枪瞄准。这相机有枪托、握把和扳机，活脱脱一支冲锋枪。我在取景框里套住那艘船，扣了两三秒扳机。拖船赶到她跟前、再有别的动静，大约还得五分钟，于是我放下相机，四下里张望。

人群里正走来一个身影，那走法，仿佛脚底下的混凝土是一片风暴中靠反重力悬浮的船甲板，正前俯后颠——正是比什·韦尔（Bish Ware）。他一眼望见我们，挥了挥手，身子一晃又稳住，随即朝右舷转向，朝我们压了过来。他身上载着照例的那批货，舱单照例会写作：巴尔德蜂蜜朗姆酒（*Baldur honey-rum*），购自「哈利·黄」酒吧（Harry Wong’s）。

比什不是他的真名。我疑心韦尔也不是。约莫五年前他初登芬尼斯那阵子，有人给他起了个绰号叫「主教」，没多久就裁得只剩一个音节了。他长得像位主教——至少，像没在银幕以外见过主教的人心目中主教该有的模样。他块头大，不肥，是那种高挑魁伟的身量；一张红润的脸上一贯挂着慈祥睿智的神情，而且身上载的货越多，那睿智和慈祥就越发深厚。

===Original===
He had iron-gray hair, but he wasn’t old. You could tell that by the backs of his hands; they weren’t wrinkled or crepy and the veins didn’t protrude. And drunk or sober—though I never remembered seeing him in the latter condition—he had the fastest reflexes of anybody I knew. I saw him, once, standing at the bar in Harry Wong’s, knock over an open bottle with his left elbow. He spun half around, grabbed it by the neck and set it up, all in one motion, without spilling a drop, and he went on talking as though nothing had happened. He was quoting Homer, I remembered, and you could tell that he was thinking in the original ancient Greek and translating to Lingua Terra as he went.

He was always dressed as he was now, in a conservative black suit, the jacket a trifle longer than usual, and a black neckcloth with an Uller organic-opal pin. He didn’t work at anything, but quarterly—once every planetary day—a draft on the Banking Cartel would come in for him, and he’d deposit it with the Port Sandor Fidelity & Trust. If anybody was unmannerly enough to ask him about it, he always said he had a rich uncle on Terra.

When I was a kid—well, more of a kid than I am now—I used to believe he really was a bishop—unfrocked, of course, or ungaitered, or whatever they call it when they give a bishop the heave-ho. A lot of people who weren’t kids still believed that, and they blamed him on every denomination from Anglicans to Zen Buddhists, not even missing the Satanists, and there were all sorts of theories about what he’d done to get excommunicated, the mildest of which was that somewhere there was a cathedral standing unfinished because he’d hypered out with the building fund. It was generally agreed that his ecclesiastical organization was paying him to stay out there in the boondocks where he wouldn’t cause them further embarrassment.

===Chinese===
他一头铁灰色头发，人却不老。看手背就知道：不起皱、不干瘪起皮，青筋也不凸。而且无论醉着醒着——虽然我不记得见过他后一种状态——他的反应之快，是我认识的人里的头一份。有一回我亲眼见他站在「哈利·黄」的吧台边，左胳膊肘碰翻了一只开着盖的瓶子。他半边身子一旋，一把攥住瓶颈，把它立定——一气呵成，一滴不洒——嘴上的话头都没断，仿佛什么也没发生过。我记得他当时正引用着荷马（Homer），而且你看得出来，他是一边用古希腊原文思考，一边随口译成地球语（Lingua Terra）的。

他永远穿得跟现在一个样：一套老派黑西装，上衣比常式略长一截，颈间一条黑领巾，别一枚乌勒尔（Uller）有机欧泊胸针。他什么都不做，可是每一季度——即每行星日一回——就有一张银行卡特尔（Banking Cartel）开出的汇票寄到他名下，他再把它存进桑多尔港忠信信托公司（Fidelity & Trust）。谁要是不识相问起，他总是回答说，特拉有位有钱的伯父。

我小时候——嗯，比我现在还小的时候——真相信他就是位主教，当然是给革了圣职的那种，或者叫褫了圣衣，或者随便他们管那叫什么，总之是让教会扫地出门的那种。许多并非孩子的人至今仍信这个，还把他往各家教派头上推——从圣公会一路推到禅宗佛教徒，连撒旦教徒都没漏掉——至于他到底干了什么才被开除教籍，更是说法五花八门，其中最轻的一条是：某处有一座大教堂至今烂着尾，因为他卷着建堂基金超空间跑路了。大家的公论是：他所属的那家教会付着钱，让他待在那片穷乡僻壤，免得再给教会丢人现眼。

===Original===
I was pretty sure, myself, that he was being paid by somebody, probably his family, to stay out of sight. The colonial planets are full of that sort of remittance men.

Bish and I were pretty good friends. There were certain old ladies, of both sexes and all ages, of whom Professor Hartzenbosch was an example, who took Dad to task occasionally for letting me associate with him. Dad simply ignored them. As long as I was going to be a reporter, I’d have to have news sources, and Bish was a dandy. He knew all the disreputable characters in town, which saved me having to associate with all of them, and it is sad but true that you get very few news stories in Sunday school. Far from fearing that Bish would be a bad influence on me, he rather hoped I’d be a good one on Bish.

I had that in mind, too, if I could think of any way of managing it. Bish had been a good man, once. He still was, except for one thing. You could tell that before he’d started drinking, he’d really been somebody, somewhere. Then something pretty bad must have happened to him, and now he was here on Fenris, trying to hide from it behind a bottle. Something ought to be done to give him a shove up on his feet again. I hate waste, and a man of the sort he must have been turning himself into the rumpot he was now was waste of the worst kind.

It would take a lot of doing, though, and careful tactical planning. Preaching at him would be worse than useless, and so would simply trying to get him to stop drinking. That would be what Doc Rojansky, at the hospital, would call treating the symptoms. The thing to do was make him want to stop drinking, and I didn’t know how I was going to manage that. I’d thought, a couple of times, of getting him to work on the *Times*, but we barely made enough money out of it for ourselves, and with his remittance he didn’t need to work. I had a lot of other ideas, now and then, but every time I took a second look at one, it got sick and died.

===Chinese===
我自己倒是蛮有把握：确有人在付钱让他隐姓埋名，多半是他家里。殖民行星上多的是这路人——家里按期汇钱来，只求他们待在别处、别回来碍眼。

比什跟我是相当要好的朋友。城里有些「老太太」——男女皆有、各年龄段俱全，哈岑博施教授即是一例——时不时数落爸爸，说不该放我跟这种人厮混。爸爸一律置之不理。我既然要吃记者这碗饭，总得有消息来源，比什就是个上等来源。城里那些不三不四的人物他全都熟，省得我挨个去结交；何况，主日学校（Sunday school）里出不了几条新闻——这话虽然丧气，却是实情。爸爸非但不担心比什把我带坏，反倒巴望着我能把比什带好。

这层意思我也记在心上——只要能想出个办法来。比什本是个了不起的人，从前是；如今也还是，只除了一件事。看得出来，在端起酒瓶之前，他在什么地方确实是个有分量的人物。后来准是出了什么很不堪的事，如今他就待在芬尼斯，躲在一只酒瓶子后头。总该有人拉他一把，让他重新站直了。我痛恨糟蹋，而他那样的人把自己作践成如今这副酒鬼模样，是糟蹋里最恶劣的一种。

不过这得下大功夫，还得有周密的战术谋划。对他讲道，比白费口舌还要糟；光劝他戒酒也一样。医院的罗扬斯基医生（Doc Rojansky）管那叫「治标不治本」。要紧的是让他自己想戒，可我不知道这该怎么着手。我想过几回，请他到《时报》来做事，可报纸挣的钱连我们自己都不宽裕，而他有汇款，也犯不着做事。我陆陆续续还有过不少别的主意，可每一条，只要再看上第二眼，就蔫了，死了。

## The Treason of Bish Ware / 比什·韦尔的背叛

===Original===
I wanted to find out who had been splashed, but Joe Kivelson was too busy directing the new phase of the fight to hand out casualty reports to the press, and besides, there were too many things happening all at once that I had to get. I went around to the other side where the incendiaries had met their end, moving slowly as close to the face of the fire as I could get and shooting the burning wax flowing out from it. A lot of equipment, including two of the three claw-derricks and a dredger﻿—they’d brought a second one up from the waterfront﻿—were moving to that side. By the time I had gotten around, the blowers had been maneuvered into place and were ready to start. There was a lot of back-and-forth yelling to make sure that everybody was out from in front, and then the blowers started.

It looked like a horizontal volcanic eruption; burning wax blowing away from the fire for close to a hundred feet into the clear space beyond. The derricks and manipulators and the cars and jeeps with grapnels went in on both sides, snatching and dragging wax away. Because they had the wind from the blowers behind them, the men could work a lot closer, and the fire wasn’t spreading as rapidly. They were saving a lot of wax; each one of those big sausages that the lifters picked up and floated away weighed a thousand pounds, and was worth, at the new price, eight hundred sols.
===Chinese===
我想弄清被蜡火溅伤的到底是谁，可乔·基维尔森（Joe Kivelson）正忙着指挥扑救的新阶段，腾不出空向报界发布伤亡通报；再说，一时之间要抓的镜头也实在太多。我绕到那几个纵火犯丧命的另一侧去，尽量贴近火线的正面缓缓移动，一路拍摄从火中流淌出来的燃烧的蜡。大批装备正朝那一侧调动，三台爪式吊车（claw-derrick）去了两台，还有一台挖掘机——他们又从滨水区调上来第二台。等我绕到的时候，鼓风机已经操纵就位，随时可以开工。人们前前后后扯着嗓子互相喊话，确认没有谁还留在风机正面，随后鼓风机开动了。

那景象活像一场横向喷发的火山：燃烧的蜡被风力吹离火场，飞出将近一百英尺，落进外圈的空地。吊车、操纵机（manipulator），还有装着抓钩的汽车和吉普从两翼开进去，把蜡抢的抢、拖的拖。有鼓风机吹出的风在背后顶着，火势蔓延得不那么快了。他们抢下了不少蜡：升举器（lifter）吊起、飘运走的那一条条大蜡肠，每条足有一千磅重，按新价计算值八百索尔（sol）。

===Original===
Finally, they got everything away that they could, and then the blowers were shut down and the two dredge shovels moved in, scooping up the burning sludge and carrying it away, scattering it on the concrete. I would have judged that there had been six or seven million sols’ worth of wax in the piles to start with, and that a little more than half of it had been saved before they pulled the last cylinder away.

The work slacked off; finally, there was nothing but the two dredges doing anything, and then they backed away and let down, and it was all over but standing around and watching the scattered fire burn itself out. I looked at my watch. It was two hours since the first alarm had come in. I took a last swing around, got the spaceport people gathering up wax and hauling it away, and the broken lake of fire that extended downtown from where the stacks had been, and then I floated my jeep over to the sandwich-and-coffee stand and let down, getting out. *Maybe*, I thought, *I could make some kind of deal with somebody like Interworld News on this.* It would make a nice thrilling feature-program item. Just a little slice of life from Fenris, the Garden Spot of the Galaxy.
===Chinese===
最后，能抢的都抢出来了，鼓风机随即关闭，两台挖掘机的铲斗开了进去，把燃烧的蜡泥铲起运走，撒在混凝土场地上摊薄晾灭。依我看，这几垛蜡起初总值六七百万索尔，到最后一个蜡筒拖走时，抢救下来的略过半数。

活计渐渐稀落下来；到后来只剩那两台挖掘机还在忙，随后它们也倒退着落了地，一切就此收场——只消站在四周，看着撒开的火苗自行烧尽便是。我看了看表，距第一声警报传来已过了两个钟头。我兜了最后一圈，拍下太空港的人收拾残蜡、装车运走的场面，拍下从蜡垛原址一路蔓延向下城的破碎火湖，然后把吉普飘到三明治咖啡摊前，降落出舱。*没准*，我心想，*我能拿这个跟星际新闻社（Interworld News）之类做成一笔交易。*这可是个惊险刺激的好专题节目素材。不过是芬尼斯——银河系头号花园胜地——生活的一个小小切片罢了。

===Original===
I got myself a big *zhoumy*-loin sandwich with hot sauce and a cup of coffee, made sure that my portable radio was on, and circulated among the fire fighters, getting comments. Everybody had been a hero, natch, and they were all very unbashful about admitting it. There was a great deal of wisecracking about Al Devis buying himself a ringside seat for the fire he’d started. Then I saw Cesário Vieira and joined him.

“Have all the fire you want, for a while?” I asked him.

“Brother, and how! We could have used a little of this over on Hermann Reuch’s Land, though. Have you seen Tom around anywhere?”

“No. Have you?”

“I saw him over there, about an hour ago. I guess he stayed on this side. After they started blowing it, I was over on Al Devis’s side.” He whistled softly. “Was that a mess!”
===Chinese===
我给自己买了份大号辣酱卓米（zhoumy）腰肉三明治，外加一杯咖啡，确认便携电台开着，便在救火汉子们中间转悠着采录评论。不必说，人人都是英雄，而且个个都毫不害臊地承认这一点。阿尔·德维斯（Al Devis）给自己那场亲手放起的大火买了个前排看位——这类俏皮话说了一箩筐。随后我看见了塞萨里奥·维埃拉（Cesário Vieira），便凑到他身边去。

「这一阵子，火总够你瞧的了吧？」我问他。

「老兄，何止是够！不过当初在赫尔曼·罗伊希之地（Hermann Reuch's Land）要是也能有这么一场就好了。你在这儿哪儿见过汤姆（Tom）没有？」

「没有。你呢？」

「一个来钟头前，我在那边还见过他。我看他是留在这一侧了。他们开始吹火之后，我就到阿尔·德维斯那侧去了。」他轻轻打了个呼哨。「那叫一个乱哟！」

===Original===
There was still a crowd at the fire, but they seemed all to be townspeople. The hunters had gathered where Joe Kivelson had been directing operations. We finished our sandwiches and went over to join them. As soon as we got within earshot, I found that they were all in a very ugly mood.

“Don’t fool around,” one man was saying as we came up. “Don’t even bother looking for a rope. Just shoot them as soon as you see them.”

*Well*, I thought, *a couple of million sols’ worth of tallow-wax, in which they all owned shares, was something to get mean about.* I said something like that.
===Chinese===
火场边上还围着一大群人，但看上去全是城里街坊。猎手们则聚在乔·基维尔森先前指挥作战的地方。我们吃完三明治，便过去同他们会合。刚走到听得见说话的距离，我就发觉他们个个情绪凶戾。

「别婆婆妈妈的，」我们走近时，一个人正这么说。「连绞索都犯不上找。一见面就崩了他们。」

*也是*，我心想，*好几百万索尔的脂蜡（tallow-wax），人人都有股份在里面，这确实值得凶相毕露。*我便顺着这意思说了几句。

===Original===
“It’s not that,” another man said. “It’s Tom Kivelson.”

“What about him?” I asked, alarmed.

“Didn’t you hear? He got splashed with burning wax,” the hunter said. “His whole back was on fire; I don’t know whether he’s alive now or not.”
===Chinese===
「不是为这个，」另一个人说。「是为汤姆·基维尔森。」

「他怎么了？」我心中一惊，问道。

「你没听说吗？他被燃烧的蜡泼溅着了，」那猎手说。「整个背都烧起来了；他眼下是死是活，我说不上。」

===Original===
So that was who I’d seen screaming in agony while the firemen tore his burning clothes away. I pushed through, with Cesário behind me, and found Joe Kivelson and Mohandas Feinberg and Corkscrew Finnegan and Oscar Fujisawa and a dozen other captains and ships’ officers in a huddle.

“Joe,” I said, “I just heard about Tom. Do you know anything yet?”

Joe turned. “Oh, Walt. Why, as far as we know, he’s alive. He was alive when they got him to the hospital.”
===Chinese===
原来我先前看见的那个痛得尖叫的人就是他——消防汉子们把他烧着的衣服撕下来的时候。我从人堆里挤过去，塞萨里奥跟在身后，只见乔·基维尔森、莫汉达斯·范伯格（Mohandas Feinberg）、「螺丝锥」芬尼根（Corkscrew Finnegan）、奥斯卡·藤泽（Oscar Fujisawa），还有十几位船长和船上干部围作一团。

「乔，」我说，「我刚听说汤姆的事。有什么确切消息了吗？」

乔转过身来。「哦，是沃尔特（Walt）。这个嘛，就我们所知，他还活着。他们把他送进医院的时候，人还活着。」

===Original===
“That’s at the spaceport?” I unhooked my handphone and got Dad. He’d heard about a man being splashed, but didn’t know who it was. He said he’d call the hospital at once. A few minutes later, he was calling me back.

“He’s been badly burned, all over the back. They’re preparing to do a deep graft on him. They said his condition was serious, but he was alive five minutes ago.”

I thanked him and hung up, relaying the information to the others. They all looked worried. When the screen girl at a hospital tells you somebody’s serious, instead of giving you the well-as-can-be-expected routine, you know it is serious. Anybody who makes it alive to a hospital, these days, has an excellent chance, but injury cases do die, now and then, after they’ve been brought in. They are the “serious” cases.
===Chinese===
「医院在太空港那边吧？」我摘下手持话机（handphone），接通爸爸（Dad）。他听说了有人被蜡火溅伤，却不知道伤的是谁。他说马上给医院挂电话。几分钟后，他回了电。

「他烧伤得很重，整个背部都是。他们正准备给他做深度植皮。院方说伤势危重，不过五分钟前人还活着。」

我谢过他，挂断通话，把消息转告众人。人人面有忧色。要是医院里管屏幕的姑娘告诉你某人「危重」，而不是来上一套「恢复得还算符合预期」的官样文章，那你就知道真是危重了。如今这年月，凡是活着进医院的人，救回的机会都极大，可也偶尔确有伤员送进去之后死掉的。那便是「危重」病例。

===Original===
“Well, I don’t suppose there’s anything we can do,” Joe said heavily.

“We can clean up on the gang that started this fire,” Oscar Fujisawa said. “Do it now; then if Tom doesn’t make it, he’s paid for in advance.”

Oscar, I recalled, was the one who had been the most impressed with Bish Ware’s argument that lynching Steve Ravick would cost the hunters the four million sols they might otherwise be able to recover, after a few years’ interstellar litigation, from his bank account on Terra. That reminded me that I hadn’t even thought of Bish since I’d left the *Times*. I called back. Dad hadn’t heard a word from him.
===Chinese===
「唉，咱们大概是什么忙也帮不上了，」乔的话音沉甸甸的。

「咱们可以找那帮放火的家伙算总账，」奥斯卡·藤泽说。「现在就动手；这样，就算汤姆挺不过去，账也预先讨回来了。」

我记得，当初比什·韦尔（Bish Ware）有套说辞——私刑吊死史蒂夫·拉维克（Steve Ravick），猎手们就别想追回那四百万索尔；留着他的命打上几年星际官司，或许还能从他在特拉（Terra）的银行账户里追回这笔钱——奥斯卡对这套说辞最是服气。这倒提醒了我：自打离开《时报》（*Times*），我压根儿没想起过比什。我回拨过去。爸爸没听到他半点音讯。

===Original===
“What’s the situation at Hunters’ Hall?” I asked.

“Everything’s quiet there. The police left when Hallstock commandeered that firefighting equipment. They helped the shipyard men get it out, and then they all went to the Municipal Building. As far as I know, both Ravick and Belsher are still in Hunters’ Hall. I’m in contact with the vehicles on guard at the approaches; I’ll call them now.”

I relayed that. The others nodded.
===Chinese===
「猎手会堂（Hunters' Hall）那边形势如何？」我问。

「那儿一切平静。霍尔斯特克（Hallstock）征用那批消防装备的时候，警察就撤了。他们帮船厂的人把装备弄了出去，然后全都去了市政大楼（Municipal Building）。据我所知，拉维克和贝尔歇（Belsher）都还在猎手会堂里。我同守在各条通道口的车保持着联系；我这就呼他们。」

我把这话转达了。众人点头。

===Original===
“Nip Spazoni and a few others are bringing men and guns up from the docks and putting a cordon around the place on the Main City Level,” Oscar said. “Your father will probably be hearing that they’re moving into position now.”

He had. He also said that he had called all the vehicles on the First and Second Levels Down; they all reported no activity in Hunters’ Hall except one jeep on Second Level Down, which did not report at all.

Everybody was puzzled about that.
===Chinese===
「尼普·斯帕佐尼（Nip Spazoni）和另外几个人正从船坞带人扛枪上来，在主城层（Main City Level）那一带布下封锁圈，」奥斯卡说。「他们这会儿正进入位置，你父亲多半就要听到报告了。」

他果然听到了。他还说，他已呼遍下第一层（First Level Down）和下第二层（Second Level Down）的所有车辆；它们都报告猎手会堂里毫无动静，唯独下第二层的一辆吉普压根儿没有回音。

人人都觉得这事蹊跷。

===Original===
